In hoc capite potissimum observabis, quid sit manducare carnem Christi, scilicet credere in Christum crucifixum, simul mortificari, & confidere quod in morte vivificet. Haec de isto capite &c.

## 7:1 Argument
CHAPTER SEVENTH.

Latin:

CAPUT SEPTIMUM.

## 7:1
For he did not wish to walk in Judea.

The first point, concerning the flight of Christ in every affliction. This is one most serious question, whether it pleases God that we are afflicted, that is, whether God approves this ministry of ours. Likewise, whether He approves that we are exposed to death, etc. For it is necessary that all our actions proceed from faith, life, death, peace, and adversity, that is, it is necessary that hearts be certain that what we do pleases God, that God approves our ministry, afflictions, death, etc., otherwise we shall not endure in temptation. And our afflictions will be of such a kind as was that of Manasseh sacrificing his sons, or of the priests of Baal hating themselves. But how shall we be certain that it pleases God through the calling? Where we are compelled by the command of God to obey those who call, lest we attempt anything with foolish zeal, as Saul attacked the Gibeonites. And here Christ awaits the calling.

Latin:

Non enim volebat in Iudaeam ambu-lare.

Primus locus, de fuga Christi in omni afflictione. Haec una gravissima quaestio est, placeat ne deo, quod affligimur, hoc est utrum deus probet hoc nostrum ministerium. Item, utrum probet, quod obiicimur morti &c. Oportet enim omnia nostra ex fide progredi, vitam, mortem, pacem, & adversitatem, id est oportet corda certa esse, deo placere quod agimus, deum probare nostrum ministerium, afflictiones, mortem &c. alioquin in tentatione non durabimus. Et erunt afflictiones nostrae eiusmodi, qualis fuit Manassae immolantis filios suos, aut sacerdotum Baal odientium se. Qui autem certi erimus, quod placeat deo per vocationem? Ubi mandato dei cogimur obedire vocantibus, ne quid stulto zelo tentemus, sicut Saul invasit Gabaonitas. Et hic Christus expectat vocationem.

## 7:2
The feast of tabernacles of the Jews.

The second point of this chapter. Lev. 23. the feast of tabernacles is described, that it may be a memorial of the departure from Egypt, when He preserved them in the desert in a tabernacle the Lord. In which type it is first to be considered, that the Lord in this instituted monuments of the leading out from Egypt, that He might recall into memory the promises made to the fathers. To Abraham the land was promised, and the seed in whom all nations should be blessed. Jacob prophesied that it would come to pass, that the scepter should not be taken away from Judah, until He should come who is the expectation of the nations. In this the people received the land, that they might await the promised Christ from his seed. Therefore when they are admonished of the departure from Egypt, they are admonished of this: Behold into this land you have been sent, where the kingdom may endure, until Christ shall come who may save you. Thus the whole law and ceremonies are directed into Christ. The promise was, that it would come to pass that all nations should be blessed. The law was given which did not bless, but cursed. Wherefore the law itself is not that word, by which Israel might be blessed. Therefore another word remained. Now this also was signified, that because tabernacles were still to be used, they had not yet received the promise made to the fathers. Just as to the Hebrews it is disputed concerning the sabbath-rest and the land.

Latin:

Iudaeorum scenopegia.

Secundus locus huius capitis. Levi. 23. describitur festum tabernaculorum, ut sit memoriale profectionis ex Aegypto, cum in deserto in tabernaculo servarit eos dominus. In quo typo primum considerantum est, dominum in hoc instituisse monumenta eductionis ex Aegypto, ut revocet in memoriam promissiones patribus factas. Abrahae terra promissa est, et semen in quo benedicerentur omnes gentes. Iacob prophetavit fore, ut non auferretur sceptrum de Iuda, donec venerit quae sit expectatio gentium. In hoc populus accepit terram, ut expectaret promissum Christum ex semine suo. Ideo cum admonentur profectionis ex Aegypto, hoc monentur: Ecce in hanc terram missi estis, ubi duret regnum, donec veniet Christus qui salvet vos. Sic tota lex & ceremoniae sunt in Christum directae. Promissio erat, futurum ut benedicerentur omnes gentes. Lex data est quae non benedicebat, sed maledicebat. Quare ipsa lex non est id verbum, quo benedictus sit Israël. Ergo restabat aliud verbum. Iam hoc quoque significatum est, quod quia adhuc tabernaculis utendum esset, nondum accepissent promissionem patribus factam. Sicut ad Hebraeos de sabbatismo & terra disputatur.

## 7:3
Pass from here, and go into Judea.

The third place of this chapter is, the flesh wishing to be glorified carnally, not to be crucified, as Matthew 16. Peter says: Far be it from you Lord. And the sons of Zebedee Matthew 20. although it behooves this oldness to be crucified and to die in the midst, so that we may be glorified by the glory of God, that is, by the Spirit. For it is necessary that we become like the image of Christ, Rom. 8. But they wish to be glorified without the cross: not only those who openly boast carnally before the world of the flesh of Christ, so that they may receive their reward from the world: but also those who boast before God of their own righteousnesses, unwilling to mortify themselves, and to condemn themselves, as if they were righteous: of which kind are those whom John rebukes: Do not say, we are the children of Abraham &c. that is, we are the holy Christian people &c. The Turks are not so. We read and hear the Gospel, they do not read. We are understanding of liberty, they are not. Therefore we are the children of God: although here he rejects the brothers.

Latin:

Transi hinc, & vade in Iudaeam.

Tertius locus huius capitis est, carnem velle glorificari carnaliter, non crucifigi, ut Matthaei 16. Petrus inquit: Absit à te domine. Et filii Zebedaei Matthaei 20. cum tamen oporteat hanc vetustatem crucifigi & intermori, ut gloria dei, id est spiritu glorificemur. Oportet enim nos similes fieri imaginis Christi, Rom. 8. Volunt autem glorificari citra crucem: non illi tantum, qui palàm coram mundo gloriantur carnaliter de carne Christi, ut à mundo recipiant mercedem: sed & ii, qui coram deo gloriantur de iustitiis suis, nolentes mortificare se, & damnare, quasi iusti sint: cuiusmodi sunt illi quos Ioannes increpat: Nolite dicere, filii Abrahae sumus &c. id est nos sumus populus sanctus Christianus &c. Turcae non item. Nos legimus & audimus Evangelium, isti non legunt. Nos sumus intelligentes libertatis, illi non sunt. Ergo nos sumus filii dei: cum tamen hic fratres reiiciat.

## 7:3
That your disciples also may see your works.

See with what engines they attack Christ. First, because he might relate the disciples, and their faith might be strengthened by the public glory of Christ. Second, if you trust in the truth, why do you not come forth into the light? according to that which was said above: He who does the truth, comes to the light. Here see, with what just causes Christ does not dare to boast. Because they were seeking not the glory of God, but their own, for just causes. It was the will of God that Christ should not be glorified carnally before the world. It was the will of the brothers that he should be glorified carnally. Thus not the cause, not the right, but the will of God is to be followed. As he himself testifies Matthaei 26. Do you think that I cannot ask my father, that he may present to me &c. how then would the scriptures be fulfilled?

Latin:

Ut & discipuli tui videant tua opera.

Vide quibus machinis oppugnent Christum. Primum, quod referat discipulorum, & illorum fides confortaretur publica Christi gloria. Secundo, si fidis veritati, cur non prodis in lucem? iuxta illud quod supra dictum est: Qui facit veritatem, venit ad lucem. Hic vide, quàm iustis causis non audeat Christus gloriari. Quia illi iustis causis non gloriam dei, sed suam quaerebant. Dei voluntas erat, non glorificari carnaliter Christum coram mundo. Fratrum voluntas erat, ut glorificaretur carnaliter. Ita non causa, non ius, sed voluntas dei sequenda est. Sicut ipse testatur Matthaei 26. An putas, quod non possum rogare patrem, ut exhibeat mihi &c. quomodo ergo implerentur scripturae?

## 7:5
For neither did his brothers believe &c.

Namely, there is so much no prerogative by which some are saved over others. For there is no respect of persons with God. Not kinship, not gifts, not works, but faith which justifies. He notes, however, in the brothers, the incredulity of the law, from which we may learn fear and faith. Fear, lest despising the word we be blinded just as they themselves were blinded. Faith, that we may rejoice to believe: that a people without works may be accepted. Paul treats these things in Romanos 9. and 11.

Latin:

Neque enim fratres eius credebant &c.

Scilicet adeo nulla est praerogativa, qua alii prae aliis salventur. Non enim est personarum acceptio apud deum. Non cognatio, non dona, non opera, sed fides quae iustificat. Notat autem in fratribus, legis incredulitatem, unde discamus timorem & fidem. Timorem, ne contemnentes verbum sic excaecemur, ut ipsi excaecati sunt. Fidem, ut gaudeamus credere: quod populus sine operibus acceptus sit. Tractat haec Paulus ad Romanos 9. & 11.

## 7:6
My time has not yet come.

The sons of God are not glorified, except through the cross. Therefore Christ denies that his time is present, Rom. 8. Co-heirs of Christ, if only we suffer together. And elsewhere: He who wishes to save his soul, will lose it. The sons of the world are glorified without the cross, therefore their time is always present.

Latin:

Tempus meum nondum venit.

Filii dei non glorificantur, nisi per crucem. Ideo Christus negat suum tempus adesse, Rom. 8. Cohaeredes Christi, si modo compatimur. Et aliâs: Qui volet animam suam salvare, perdet eam. Filii mundi glorificantur sine cruce, ideo tempus eorum semper adest.

## 7:7
Because his works are evil.

Here Christ condemns all the works of the world. But what world does he call? those who were according to the law about to celebrate the feast day. Is celebrating it also a work of the world? I answer. Christ here condemns all the works of the world, that is, not only the manifest outrages of the flesh, but also the wisdom and justice of the world. Therefore, he calls those who were about to go up to the feast day the world here, although according to the law. And he calls celebrating in this manner a work of the world. And this is the reason why the world holds Christ in hatred, not because he condemns what the world also condemns, but because he condemns what the wisdom of the world approves. The wisdom of the flesh thinks thus: I am keeping the feast day according to the law, therefore I am doing a good work. The church cannot stand if you take away this splendor of the bishops, these ordinations and riches, etc. Likewise, it is my right, therefore I ought to defend it. Christ condemns many things of this kind, therefore the world hates him.

Latin:

Quia opera eius mala sunt.

Hic damnat Christus omnia opera mundi. Sed quem mundum vocat? eos qui erant secundum legem acturi festum diem. An illum feriari, etiam opus mundi est? Respondeo. Christus hic omnia opera mundi damnat, hoc est non solum carnis manifesta flagitia, sed & sapientiam & iustitiam mundi. Ideo mundum vocat hic eos, qui ad festum diem ascensuri erant, quamquam secundum legem. Et opus mundi vocat huiusmodi feriari. Et haec est causa cur Christum odio habeat mundus, non quia damnat, quae & mundus damnat, sed quia damnat quae mundi sapientia probat. Sapientia carnis sic sentit: Ago festum diem iuxta legem, ergo bonum opus facio. Non potest consistere ecclesia, si tollas hunc splendorem episcoporum, has ordinationes et opes &c. Item ius meum est, ergo tueri debeo. Huiusmodi multa damnat Christus, ideo odit eum mundus.

## 7:10
Then he himself also went up to the feast day, not openly.

For the law compelled him to go up: because for him it was a calling. For it is a most true calling when we are compelled by the commandment of God. And thus you will observe the most humane conversation of Christ, when he submits himself to the law, and shows that he is held by the law. He serves the ceremonies of the law, so that he may indicate that he is held by the law of loving one's neighbor.

Latin:

Tunc & ipse ascendit ad diem festum non manifeste.

Coëgit enim eum lex ascendere: quia illi vocatio fuit. Est enim vocatio verissima, cum praecepto dei cogimur. Atque ita observabis humanissimam conversationem Christi, cum se legi subiicit, & ostendit legi se teneri. Servit ceremoniis legis, ut indicet se lege diligendi proximi teneri.

## 7:16
My doctrine is not mine.

Two things are sought here: whence the doctrine of Christ is, and how it is known. First, just as Paul proves his Gospel to be from God, in that he neither received nor learned it through man, nor from man, Galat. 1. so here, that Christ did not learn from men is an argument that the doctrine is of God, not of men. Therefore he answered: My doctrine is not mine, but his who sent me, etc. As often as he denies that his doctrine is not his own, and that he seeks not his own will, but the will of him who sent him, etc., he denies that one ought to glory in his flesh; that is, he forbids glorying in any gifts whatsoever, or trusting in works or ceremonies. Just as the hearers of the Gospel glory, not the doers, the wise, the righteous, trusting in their own righteousnesses, when all these things must be mortified: since even the flesh of Christ had to be mortified. Secondly, how the doctrine is known. The light of the flesh understands only carnal things, and believes only those things which reason perceives to be likely: therefore it can neither understand nor believe spiritual things. In this sense he says here: If any man will do his will. That is, if any man will to believe. For this is the will of the Father, to hear the Son. He shall know of the doctrine, that is, he shall both know the doctrine to be true, and shall know it to be of God. The flesh judges error to be, that all the works of men are sins: because it does not acknowledge its own impiety or blindness, distrust, and contempt of God. Again, because it justifies itself, it denies that it is justified by the righteousness of Christ alone. But where it acknowledges its sins, it at the same time acknowledges all the doctrines of men to be vain: because it feels that its conscience cannot be confirmed or raised up before God by those doctrines. And thus if it believes, then it apprehends that the doctrine of Christ is true, and not only does it apprehend this, that the doctrine is true, but also this, that the doctrine of Christ is from God: because when God judges, the conscience is raised up by the word of Christ. For the word of the Lord remains forever. Besides this, fire consumes all the remaining doctrines, and thus it happens that the Spirit of God bears witness to our spirit.

Latin:

Mea doctrina non est mea.

Duo hic quaeruntur, unde sit doctrina Christi, & quomodo cognoscatur. Primum, sicut Paulus probat suum Evangelium à deo esse, eo quod neque per hominem, neque ab homine acceperit aut didicerit, Galat. 1. ita hic, quod Christus non didicit ab hominibus, argumento est, quod doctrina dei non hominum est. Ideo respondit: Mea doctrina non est mea, sed eius qui misit me &c. Quoties negat doctrinam suam non esse suam, etse quaerere non voluntatem suam, sed eius qui se miserit &c. negat in carne sua gloriandum esse, hoc est vetat gloriari de qualibuscunque donis, confidere operibus vel ceremoniis. Sicut gloriantur auditores Evangelii non factores, sapientes, iusti, confisi suis iustitiis, cum haec omnia mortificari oporteat: quando et carnem Christi mortificari oportuit. Secundo, quomodo cognoscatur doctrina. Lumen carnis carnalia tantum intelligit, & ea tantum credit quae ratio sentit verisimilia esse: proinde non potest nec intelligere, nec credere spiritualia. In hanc sententiam hic ait: Si quis voluerit voluntatem eius facere. Id est si quis voluerit credere. Nam haec est voluntas patris, audire filium. Cognoscet de doctrina, id est et doctrinam cognoscet esse veram, & cognoscet eam ex deo esse. Caro errorem esse iudicat, omnia opera hominum peccata esse: quia impietatem suam seu caecitatem, diffidentiam, contemptum dei non agnoscit. Rursum, quia ipsa sese iustificat, negat sola Christi iustitia se iustificari. Ubi autem agnoscit peccata sua, simul agnoscit omnes doctrinas hominum esse vanas: quia sentit conscientiam suam coram deo non posse confirmari vel erigi iis doctrinis. Atque ita si credit, tum apprehendit, veram esse Christi doctrinam, neque hoc solum apprehendit, veram esse doctrinam, sed et hoc, ex deo esse doctrinam Christi: quia iudicante deo conscientia verbo Christi erigitur. Verbum enim domini manet in aeternum. Praeter hoc ignis consumit reliquas doctrinas omnes, atque ita fit ut spiritus dei testimonium perhibeat spiritui nostro.

## 7:18
He who speaks from himself.

The argument by which he proves that the doctrine is not his own: because if I were to speak from myself, I would seek my own glory: but I do not seek my own glory, because I shall be crucified, therefore the doctrine is not mine. And here you see what Paul says: You are the seal of my apostleship.

Latin:

Qui à semetipso loquitur.

Argumentum quo probat doctrinam non esse suam: quia si ex meipso loquerer, gloriam meam quaererem: sed non quaero gloriam meam, quia crucifigar, igitur non est mea doctrina. Atque hic vides, quod Paulus ait: Vos apostolatus mei sigillum.

## 7:21
I have done one work, and you all marvel.

The Jews were accusing Christ, because he violated the law:

because he was doing some work on the Sabbath. The Jews thought they were keeping the law, because they did no work on the Sabbath. For they were judging according to the letter. Therefore Christ reverses the judgment, convicting them of having violated the law because the law is spiritual: but excusing himself, because the law is fulfilled not by the letter but by the spirit. Christ excuses the violation of the Sabbath in another way in another place, Matthew 12. by a comparison of works according to this place of scripture: I desire mercy, and not sacrifice: namely, to violate the Sabbath for the sake of helping a brother. Just as he says: That the Sabbath was made for man, and not man for the Sabbath, John 5. because he is God: and therefore has the power of making the law, and of taking it away, etc. The son can do nothing of himself. Here by a most apt and most convenient reason, that the observation of the Sabbath ought not to be the letter, but the spirit. Secondly, the letter is an external observation. The spirit is to keep the Sabbath in the heart, that is, to be free from the works of nature, and to become a new creature. For the Sabbath signifies the end of the old creature in its entirety, and the beginning of the new. And this was signified in Genesis and the law, that a Sabbath would come, so that this whole creature and universe might perish. Therefore it will be necessary for us to be restored once the mortality of the flesh has been abolished. Christ began this by his death and resurrection, and he blessed the Sabbath, which had previously been blessed by the law. Furthermore, if the Sabbath is blessed by the law and the example of Christ, it follows that death is blessed. This is the observance of the spiritual sabbath, for the new creature to be mortified and refreshed. Thirdly, Christ therefore proves by an evident argument that a spiritual sabbath is required by the law, not a literal one: because by literal observance we do not even then fulfill the law, when we perform it. Therefore the law is fulfilled by a spiritual sabbath. He proves the antecedent, that on the sabbath they circumcise infants, according to the command of the law. Furthermore, to circumcise is to perform some work. The same is argued in Matthew: On the sabbath the priests slaughter sacrifices, therefore not even by performing it is the law fulfilled. Fourthly, from these he gathers in general, that the law is not fulfilled by the letter, that is, by carnal observance: and that by the justice of the law no one is justified before God, but that the law is fulfilled by faith. As Peter says in the Acts: Why do you impose a yoke, which neither we, nor our fathers were able to bear? But we believe that we are saved through grace, in the same way as they also.

Latin:

Unum opus feci, & omnes miramini.

Iudaei Christum criminabantur, quod legem violaret:

quia sabbato opus aliquod faciebat. Iudaei se legem facere putabant, quia nullum opus facerent sabbato. Iudicabant enim secundum literam. Propterea Christus invertit sententiam, coarguens illos violatae legis quod lex spiritualis sit: se vero excusans, quia lex non litera sed spiritu fiat. Sabbati violationem aliâs aliter excusat Christus, Matthaei 12. collatione operum iuxta hunc locum scripturae: Misericordiam volo, non sacrificium: scilicet sabbatum iuvandi fratris causa violare. Sicut inquit: Sabbatum propter hominem, non hominem propter sabbatum esse, Ioannis 5. quia deus sit: adeóque habeat potestatem faciendae legis, & tollendae &c. Non potest filius facere à se quicquam. Hic aptissima & convenientissima ratione, quod observatio sabbati non debeat esse litera, sed spiritus. Secundo, litera est externa observatio. Spiritus est corde sabbatizare, hoc est vacare ab operibus naturae, & fieri novam creaturam. Sabbatum enim significat finem veteris creaturae in universum, & initium novae. Atque hoc in Genesi & lege significatum est, sabbatum futurum, ut haec tota creatura & universitas intereat. Ergo necesse erit nos refici abolita mortalitate carnis. Coepit hoc Christus morte & resurrectione sua, & benedixit sabbato, quod antea lege benedictum erat. Porrò si sabbatum lege & Christi exemplo benedictum est, mortem benedictam esse consequitur. Haec sabbati spiritualis observatio est, mortificari & refici novam creaturam. Tertio, probat itaque Christus evidenti argumento, legi exigi spirituale sabbatum, non literale: quia literali observatione nec tum quidem facimus legem, cum facimus. Ergo lex spirituali sabbato fit. Antecedens probat, quod sabbato circumcidant infantes, iuxta legis mandatum. Porrò circumcidere est opus aliquod facere. Idem in Matthaeo causatur: Sabbato sacerdotes hostias mactant, ergo ne faciendo quidem legem, fit lex. Quarto, ex his colligit in universum, non fieri legem litera, hoc est observatione carnali: & iustitia legis neminem iustificari coram deo, sed fide impleri legem. Sicut in Actis Petrus inquit: Quid imponitis iugum, quod neque nos, neque patres nostri portare potuerunt? Sed per gratiam credimus salvari, quomodo & illi.

## 7:23
Why are you angry with me, because I have made a whole man &c.

Because circumcision did not pertain to the law, but was a sign of the promise made to Abraham, and it detracted from the sabbath even by this very fact, that it signified that justice is not from the works of the law, but from faith, of which it itself was a sign. He gathers two things, first that one is compelled by the law to the use of the work. Next, that the Sabbath can be violated by a work of charity: because the Lord desires mercy, not sacrifice. Thus, the end and fulfillment of all laws is, namely, faith and charity. Faith fulfills the precepts of the first table, that is, it acknowledges God, fears, trusts, sanctifies His name, and keeps the Sabbath, and bears fruit. Charity fulfills the precepts of the latter table: for neither is a work of charity valid, unless it proceeds from faith, that is, from a constant trust that what one does is acceptable to God, not because of the nature of the work, but because of His mercy, etc. Secondly, that He prefers His own work to the work of circumcision signifies that His work is greater than circumcision, namely, justification, of which circumcision is only a sign. And thus He signifies again that righteousness is not from the works of the law, but from faith, etc.

Latin:

Mihi cur indignamini, quia totum ho. &c.

Quia circumcisio non pertinebat ad legem, sed signum erat promissionis factae Abraham, & sabbato derogabat vel hoc ipso, quod significaret, non ex operibus legis, sed ex fide iustitiam esse, cuius ipsa signum erat. Duo colligit, primum lege cogi ad uscationem operis. Deinde, opere charitatis violari posse sabbatum: quia misericordiam, non sacrificium vult dominus. Ita omnium legum finis & impletio, scilicet fides et charitas. Fides implet praecepta prioris tabulae, hoc est cognoscit deum, timet, confidit, sanctificat nomem eius, & sabbatizat, & fructificat. Charitas implet praecepta posterioris tabulae: neque enim charitatis opus valet, nisi ex fide proficiscatur, hoc est ex constanti fidutia, quod deo acceptum sit id quod facit, non propter operis naturam, sed propter eius misericordiam &c. Secundo, quod suum opus praefert operi circumcisionis, significat suum opus maius esse circumcisione, scilicet iustificationem, cuius signum tantum est circumcisio. Atque ita iterum significat, non ex operibus legis iustitiam esse, sed ex fide &c.

## 7:26
Behold, He speaks openly, and nothing.

They testify concerning Christ, that they could not refute His doctrine, and yet they persecute: because they had been convicted by Christ. Thus, the doctrine of those who preach the Gospel must be irreprehensible. To Titus, chapter one. And on the contrary: Hardened persecutors, although convicted, nevertheless persecute Christ. So that we may know it is a gift of God to be captured by the Word, etc.

Latin:

Ecce palàm loquitur, & nihil.

Testificantur de Christo, quod refutari eius doctrinam non potuerunt, & tamen persequi: quia à Christo convicti fuerant. Sic oportet esse irreprehensibilem doctrinam eorum, qui praedicant Evangelium. ad Titum primo. Et econtra: Indurati persecutores quanquam convicti, persequuntur tamen Christum. Ut cognoscamus dei donum esse, verbo capi &c.

## 7:27
But we know where this man is from.

Whence the opinion arose that they would not know Christ: I do not see, except from those prophecies which predicted that Christ would come, covered in the forms of the cross, not in the form which the world approves, according to that of Isaiah: Who will believe our report? &c. For this which they bring forward: Who shall declare his generation? I think pertains to the seed, or to the posterity. And see how they prophesy against themselves, they testify that Christ will come, in such a way that he is not recognized: and when he has come in such a way, yet they do not believe him.

Latin:

Sed hunc scimus unde sit.

Unde sit orta opinio, futurum ut non norint Christum: non video, nisi ex iis prophetiis quae praedixerunt Christum venturum, formis crucis tectum, non in forma quam probet mundus, iuxta illud Esaiae: Quis credet auditui nostro? &c. Nam hoc quod adducunt: Generationem eius quis enarrabit? puto ad semen, seu ad posteritatem pertinere. Et vide quomodo contra sese prophetent, testificantur Christum venturum, ita ut non cognoscatur: & cum ita venerit, tamen non credunt ei.

## 7:28
And you know me, and you know where I am from.

I do not think it is irony, but a simple statement, by which he convicts them in this manner: The works testify that I am sent by God, and those works convince you. You know therefore that I am from God, and yet you know neither the Father, nor me: because you imagine for yourselves a God by carnal opinion, who would approve your carnal righteousnesses, who would want the flesh to be crucified by the word, who would not be about to send Christ in the form of a cross, but with carnal pomp &c.

Latin:

Et me scitis, & unde sim scitis.

Non puto ironiam esse, sed simplicem sententiam, qua coarguit eos in hunc modum: Opera testificantur quod à deo missus sum, & ea opera vos convincunt. Nostis itaque me à deo esse, & tamen neque patrem, neque me cognoscitis: quia vobis deum imaginamini carnali opinione, qui probet iustitias vestras carnales, qui verbo velit carnem crucifigi, qui non sit missurus Christum in crucis forma, sed carnali pompa &c.

## 7:30
And no one laid a hand on him.

He often consoles us concerning predestination, as Matthew 10.

Not a hair will fall from your head without the will of the Father. Thus he confirms here by his own example, that we also will not come into the hands of enemies, unless by his will: and when we come into their hands, that it is the good will of the Father.

Latin:

Et nemo misit in eum manum.

Saepe consolatur nos de praedestinatione, ut Matthaei 10.

Non cadet capillus de capite vestro sine voluntate patris. Ita hic suo exemplo confirmat, & nos non venturos in manus hostium, nisi per eius voluntatem: & cum venimus in manus eorum, bonam voluntatem patris esse.

## 7:35
Will he go into the dispersion of the Gentiles, etc.?

And here they prophesy against themselves, that the Gentiles are to be received, with Israel having been rejected, etc.

Latin:

Nunquid in dispersionem gentium &c.

Et hic contra se prophetant, gentes accipiendas esse, reiecto Israël &c.

## 7:37
On the last, however, the great day.

The eighth day of the feast was the last, which he calls in Leviticus 23. a day of assembly, or of gathering, namely because on that day they gathered, with seven days having already been spent in tabernacles: that day is called great here, undoubtedly because of its signification: because the eighth day signifies the revelation of the glory of the sons of God.

Latin:

In novissimo autem die magno.

Postremo dies festi octava erat, quem vocat Levitici 23. diem coetus, sive collectae, scilicet quod in eo die conveniebatur, exactis iam septem diebus in tabernaculis: ea dies hic magna dicitur, haud dubie propter significationem: quia octavus dies significat revelationem gloriae filiorum dei.

## 7:37
If anyone thirsts, let him come to me.

To thirst is, with sin revealed, to be confounded and mortified, as in the Psalm: As the deer longs for the fountains of waters. From which it happens that we acknowledge that all human powers and righteousnesses are mere curse: this is what the apostle says, that the Law is a pedagogue to Christ. And he himself explains in Matthew 11. Come to me all, you who labor and are burdened, etc. And from this you will understand who are capable of grace, namely the thirsty, according to that: He has filled the hungry with good things. Therefore, where there is no knowledge of sin, there is the flesh: but the flesh fights against the spirit: therefore where there is no knowledge of sin, there is also no spirit.

Latin:

Si quis sitit, veniat ad me.

Sitire est, revelato peccato confusum & mortificatum esse, ut in Psalmo: Sicut desyderat cervus ad fontes aquarum. Quo fit ut agnoscamus, merum maledictum esse omnes vires & iustitias humanas: hoc est quod apostolus inquit, Legem esse pedagogum ad Christum. Et ipse exponit Matthaei 11. Venite ad me omnes, qui laboratis & onerati estis &c. Et hinc intelliges qui sint gratiae capaces, nempe sitientes, iuxta illud: Esurientes implevit bonis. Itaque ubi non est cognitio peccati, ibi caro est: caro autem pugnat cum spiritu: ergo ubi non est cognitio peccati, ibi nec spiritus est.

## 7:38
He who believes in me, shall flow, etc.

Secondly, how therefore must one come? By faith. And thus he equates the Gentiles and the Jews, condemns those who are proud of the righteousness of works, and consoles sinners. Just as the scripture says Isaiah 44.Ezekiel thirty-sixth,forty-seventh: Rivers shall flow.

Latin:

Qui credit in me, fluent &c.

Secundo, quomodo igitur veniendum est? fide. Atque ita aequat gentes & Iudaeos, damnat superbientes iustitia operum, consolatur peccatores. Sicut dicit scriptura Esaiae 44.Ezechielis trigesimosexto,quadragesimo septimo: Fluent flumina.

## 7:39
But this he said of the spirit, which, etc.

Thirdly, he teaches what follows faith, and thus what true righteousness is, namely the reception of the Holy Spirit. Moreover, this passage is clearly taken from Isaiah: I will pour water upon the thirsty, etc.

Latin:

Hoc autem dixit de spiritu, quem &c.

Tertio, quid consequatur fidem, atque adeo quid sit vera iustitia, docet, nempe acceptio spiritussancti. Est autem hic locus planè acceptus ex Esaia: Effundam aquam super sitientem &c.

## 7:39
For the spirit was not yet given.

The Old Testament was only the law. And by the preaching of the law, nothing concerning the remission of sins or the Holy Spirit was preached. Conversely, the New Testament is the Holy Spirit, the remission of sins, and the grace of the Holy Spirit. The law is not the word through which the Holy Spirit is given. (as the Apostle says 1. Corinth. 3. We are ministers of the spirit) is. The Gospel, which because it had not yet been revealed, is said to be the law of the spirit. Because the remission of sins had not yet been preached openly: therefore neither had the spirit been given openly. And just as the Gospel was not known openly, so neither was the Holy Spirit known openly: because the Gospel had not yet been preached, and thus neither was the Holy Spirit known. Because Jesus had not yet been glorified. that is, for he could not yet be glorified before death, because it was necessary that sin heaped upon his flesh and death, be conquered through his own death.

Latin:

Nondum enim erat spiritus datus.

Vetus testamentum, lex tantum fuit. Et praedicatione legis nihil de remissione peccatorum, aut spiritusancto praedicatum est. Rursus novum testamentum est spiritussanctus, remissio peccatorum, & gratia spiritussancti. Non lex verbum, per quod datur spiritussanctus (ut ait Apostolus 1. Corinth. 3. Sumus ministri spiritus) est. Evangelium, quod quia nondum erat revelatum, dicitur lex spiritus esse. Quia nondum erat praedicata palàm remissio peccatorum: ergo neque spiritus palàm datus erat. Et sicut Evangelium non cognoscebatur palàm, ita neque cognoscebatur palàm spiritussanctus: quia Evangelium nondum erat praedicatum, adeoque neque spiritussanctus cognitus. Quia Iesus nondum erat glorificatus. id est, nondum enim potuit ante mortem glorificari, quia oportuit peccatum in carnem eius congestum & mortem, per ipsius mortem vinci.

## 7:40
This is truly the prophet. Others said: This is the Christ.

The Gospel is to some the odor of life, to others the odor of death. Which contentions are described to console those, both who preach, and who suffer persecution, lest being deterred by scandal they despair. Just as he says elsewhere: I came not to send peace, but a sword. But I am speaking of the Gospel. For the authors of contentions also are the impious teachers of ceremonies on the right and on the left. Concerning whom 2. Timoth. 2. Do not contend with words, for it is useful for nothing, except for the subversion of the hearers.

Latin:

Hic est vere propheta. Alii dicebant: Hic est Christus.

Evangelium aliis odor vitae, aliis odor mortis est. Quae contentiones describuntur ad consolandos eos, & qui praedicant, & qui patiuntur persecutionem, ne scandalo deterriti desperent. Sicut & aliâs inquit: Non veni mittere pacem, sed gladium. Sed de Evangelio loquor. Nam & contentionum autores, sunt impii doctores ceremoniarum à dextris & à sinistris. De quibus 2. Timoth. 2. Noli contendere verbis, nihil enim utile est, nisi ad subversionem audientium.

## 7:41
Does Christ come from Galilee?

The word, not the person, in the preaching of the Gospel God regards. Likewise, it teaches that the Word is not a person, nor is it made by any carnal appearance. For the word of the Lord remains forever. Therefore Paul says: But even if we have known Christ according to the flesh, yet now we know him no longer. But the flesh regards not the Word, but the person, thinking thus to itself: this is a vile, abject, condemned man; therefore he is not from God. And especially the people of the law argue thus, whose fathers were glorified even before the world. Thus they elevate the authority of Christ with this frivolous argument. He is from Galilee, therefore he is not the Christ. As if they were saying: Christ will come forth from Bethlehem with great glory in a royal manner. Secondly, human wisdom strikes against Christ here. Christ saves through death and the cross. Reason conceives, and wants a savior who does not condemn carnal things. But Christ is a refining fire, and like fuller's soap. Malachi 3. Who consumes and washes away all carnal things. All of which pertains to this, that we may learn that the cross and death are a blessing. And thus Luke 16. he says. That which is sublime among men is an abomination before God.

Latin:

Nunquid à Galilaea venit Christus?

Verbum non personam in praedicatione Evangelii respicit deus. Item verbum non persona, non aliqua carnali apparentia fieri, docet. Verbum enim domini manet in aeternum. Ideo Paulus ait: Sed etsi novimus Christum secundum carnem, nunc tamen non novimus. Sed caro non verbum, sed personam respicit, sic apudse reputans, hic est vilis, abiectus, damnatus homo: ergo non est à deo. Et praesertim sic argumentatur populus legis, cuius patres coram mundo etiam glorificati sunt. Ita isti frivolo argumento autoritatem Christi elevant. E Galilaea est, ergo non est Christus. Quasi sic dicerent: Christus ex Bethleem cum magna gloria regio more prodibit. Secundo hic in Christum impingit humana sapientia. Christus salvat per mortem & crucem. Ratio concipit, & vult salvatorem qui non damnet carnalia. Christus autem ignis conflans est, & herba fullonum. Malachiae 3. Qui consumit & eluit omnia carnalia. Quae omnia huc pertinent, ut discamus crucem & mortem benedictionem esse. Et sic Lucae 16. inquit. Quod apud homines sublime est, abominationem esse coram deo.

## 7:44
And no one laid a hand on him.

That he cannot be captured, he teaches again by his own example, that his own are in the hand of God, so that they cannot be harmed, unless the Father wills it: and when he wills, it is the good will of the Father, etc.

Latin:

Et nemo misit in eum manum.

Quod non possit capi, iterum docet exemplo sui, suos in manu dei esse, ut laedi non possint, nisi velit pater: & cum vult, bonam patris voluntatem esse &c.

## 7:49
But this crowd which does not know the law.

The reason why the princes do not believe is human wisdom, which judges of scripture carnally, when spiritual things ought to be judged spiritually. 1. Corinth. 2. And Jeremiah 23. He who has my word, shall speak my word truly. It is not enough to have the letter, unless you also judge spiritually concerning the letter. And here you see what is the cause, why many weary themselves with foolish questions: because they do not judge spiritually. This crowd, that is, we will not yield our sense to Christ: because we have the word of God.

Latin:

Sed turba haec quae non novit legem.

Causa cur non credant principes, est humana sapientia, quae de scriptura carnaliter iudicat, cum spiritualia spiritualiter iudicari debeant. 1. Corinth. 2. Et Hieremiae 23. Qui habet sermonem meum, loquetur sermonem meum vere. Non est satis habere literam, nisi & spiritualiter de litera iudices. Atque hic vides quid in causa sit, cur multi se stultis quaestionibus fatigent: quia non iudicant spiritualiter. Turba haec, id est nos sensum nostrum non cedemus Christo: quia verbum dei habemus.

## 7:52
Out of Galilee a prophet does not arise.

They are refuted by the word who allege the word, as if he were saying thus: The law forbids respect of persons, but [demands respect for] the work or the word. You, however, respect the person, not the work or the word.

Latin:

A Galilaea propheta non surgit.

Verbo redarguuntur qui verbum allegant, quasi sic dicat: Lex vetat personam respici, sed opus aut verbum. Vos autem personam respicitis, non opus aut verbum.

## 8:1 Argument
CHAPTER EIGHT.

In this place it can be asked: Why does the Lord forbid the little woman to be overwhelmed, except by those who are without sin, when nevertheless the law established that adulterers be punished? Did the Lord wish this, that there could be no ministers of the law, unless they were righteous? I answer: In this place the righteousness of the flesh is compared with the righteousness of the spirit. For because the Gospel preaches justice, the spirit convicts all men (for they are all empty of the glory of God, Roman. 3.) now the Pharisees bring this forward to be condemned according to the law, and they justify themselves, as if they could not be condemned by the law. Wherefore Christ equates them to the woman, and condemns them just as he does this woman. Therefore, first, Christ does not prohibit the ministry of the law, so that the adulteress might be punished. Second, he uses his own ministry, when he condemns both, both the adulteress and the Pharisees.

Latin:

CAPUT OCTAVUM.

Hoc loco quaeri potest: Cur dominus vetat obrui mulierculam, nisi ab iis qui sine peccato sint, cum tamen lex constituerit plecti adulteros? Anne hoc voluit dominus, non posse legis ministros esse, nisi qui iusti sint? Respondeo: In hoc loco iustitia carnis cum iustitia spiritus confertur. Quia enim Evangelium praedicat iustitiam, spiritus exequat omnes homines (sunt enim omnes vacui gloria dei, Roman. 3.) nunc pharisaei hanc adducunt damnandam secundum legem, & se iustificant, quasi lege damnari non possint. Quare Christus ipsos aequat mulieri, damnat eos iuxta atque hanc mulierem. Primum ergo non prohibet Christus ministerium legis, ut plectatur adultera. Secundo, suo ministerio utitur, cum utrosque damnat, & adulteram & pharisaeos.

## 8:6
He wrote with his finger on the ground.

When he writes on the ground, he seems to represent the stony tablets of Moses, upon which the law was written by God: and therefore upon stone, as the Apostle says: because it is only the letter, that is, as far as it concerns nature by itself it hears the law, the living affection and living nature of our hearts does not become the law.

But as to the fact that he writes twice, I do not know whether it pertains to the double tablets. Moreover, the double tablets seem to signify the double use of the law. The one is when we attempt to fulfill the law that has been approached: it makes idols, calves, when the flesh does not understand its own weakness, and yet attempts to fulfill the law, and through those works thinks itself to be justified. The other is when the law shows sin, convicts us, threatens death, so as to drive us to despair.

Latin:

Digito scribebat in terram.

Cum scribit in terram, repraesentare videtur tabulas Moysi saxeas, in quas lex à deo scripta: et ideo in saxum, sicut Apostolus ait: quia litera tantum est, hoc est, quo ad naturam per sese audit legem, non fit vivus affectus & viva natura cordium nostrorum lex.

Sed quod bis scribit, haud scio an ad duplices tabulas pertineat. Duplices autem tabulae significare videntur duplicem usum legis. Alter est, cum aditam legem conamur facere: ea facit idola, vitulos, cum caro non intelligit infirmitatem suam, & tamen legem facere conatur, & per illa opera putat se iustificari. Alter est, cum lex ostendit peccatum, coarguit nos, intentat mortem, ut adigat ad desperationem.

## 8:7
He who is without sin.

We are all sinners and void of the glory of God, as at Romans 3. etc. They were going out, that is, they were not returning, so that they might be justified by faith: because they wished to justify themselves by the works of the law.

Latin:

Qui sine peccato est.

Omnes sumus peccatores & inanes gloria dei, ut ad Romanos 3. &c. Exibant, id est non redibant, ut fide iustificarentur: quia legis operibus se iustificare volebant.

## 8:10
Has no one condemned you, woman?

That which Christ asks, whether no one has condemned her, as if he feels that he would have considered it valid if someone had condemned her: It is done because that external bodily administration of the law does not pertain to the Gospel, which is the administration of the spirit. Therefore Christ does not stone, because those to whom the administration of the law was committed did not stone. The right of the sword was instituted for the sake of protecting civil peace: and it is commanded to magistrates, that they use it: and the text adds: You shall not have pity &c. But before God, neither the just nor the unjust is for the penalty, who is punished. Therefore, first, Christ does not wish to be a minister of the letter, and of the bodily ministry of the law. Then, again, he turns to his own ministry, preaching repentance: Go, now sin no more.

But Jesus went unto the mount.

When it is said concerning the prayer of Christ, first the humiliation of Christ must be considered. For he prayed for this reason, because he was tempted. But he was humiliated for this reason, that he might be able to sympathize, Heb. 4. Then he prayed, that by his example he might confirm us also in faith, that we might know that we are heard: because he himself was heard, as he says: In that day you shall ask in my name. Concerning the stoning of adultery, Leviticus 20.

But this they said, tempting him.

Because they knew the law already before, so that there was no need to ask (but they knew that sins had been forgiven to others) therefore they wanted to test, whether he was going to do the same here, where the law so severely commands to be punished. The law fights with the Gospel. The law demands punishment for sin. The Gospel forgives freely.

Latin:

Nemo te condemnavit mulier?

Quod interrogat Christus, nemo ne damnarit, qua si sentiat se ratum habiturum si quis damnasset: Ideo fit, quia externa illa corporalis administratio legis non pertinet ad Evangelium, quod est administratio spiritus. Ideo Christus non lapidat, quia non lapidaveruntii quibus erat commissa administratio legis. Ius gladii institutum est ad tuendam civilem pacem: & praeceptum magistratibus, ut illo utantur: additque textus: Non miserearis &c. Coram deo autem nec iustus, nec iniustus ob mulctam, qui plectitur. Primum ergo non vult Christus esse minister literae, & corporalis ministerii legis. Deinde rursus convertitur ad suum ministerium, praedicans paenitentiam: Vade, iam amplius noli peccare.

Iesus autem perrexit in montem.

Cum de oratione Christi dicitur, primum extenuatio Christi consyderanda est. Nam ideo oravit, quia tentatus est. Extenuatus autem est ideo, ut posset compati, Heb. 4. Deinde oravit, ut sui exemplo & nos fide confirmet, ut sciamus nos exaudiri: quia ipse exauditus est, sicut ait: In illo die in nomine meo petetis. De lapidatione adulterii, Levitici 20.

Hoc autem dicebant, tentantes eum.

Quia sciebant legem iam ante, ut nihil opus fuerit interrogare (sciebant autem aliis remissa esse peccata) ideo volebant experiri, num idem hic facturus, ubi lex tam severe praecipit animadverti. Lex pugnat cum Evangelio. Lex exigit poenam pro peccato. Evangelium condonat gratis.

## 8:10
Neither do I condemn you.

Because I am not a minister of the corporal ministry of the law. Go, do not sin anymore. That is, do penance.

I am the light of the world.

In sum, here it is disputed what the Gospel is, and what its authority is. I am the light, that is, not a teacher, but one who illuminates and creates a new light in the hearts of believers.

Latin:

Nec ego te condemno.

Quia non sum minister corporalis ministerii legis. Vade, noli peccare amplius. Id est age paenitentiam.

Ego sum lux mundi.

In summa, hic disputatur quid sit Evangelium, & quae sit eius autoritas. Ego sum lux, id est non doctor, sed qui illuminem & creem novum lumen in cordibus credentium.

## 8:12
He who follows me.

That is, he who believes in me, does not walk in darkness:

because he who apprehends this light, shall have the light of life, that is, life-giving.

Latin:

Qui sequitur me.

Id est, qui credit in me, non ambulat in tenebris:

quia qui apprehendit hoc lumen, habebit lumen vitae, id est vivificum.

## 8:13
Your testimony is not &c.

This is, we demand the word of God, not yours.

Latin:

Testimonium tuum non est &c.

Hoc est, verbum dei postulamus non tuum.

## 8:13
My testimony is true.

Namely because I am God. And whence I come I know, namely I know that I have proceeded from the Father. And whither I go, namely to the Father. But you do not know, because you judge according to the flesh, and according to those things which human reason comprehends.

I do not judge anyone.

Just as He said above: I did not come to judge, because I came to save, not to judge.

Latin:

Verum est testimonium meum.

Scilicet quia deus sum. Et unde veniscio, scilicet scio quod à patre processerim. Et quò vado, scilicet ad patrem. Vos autem nescitis, quia secundum carnem vos iudicatis, & secundum ea quae comprehendit ratio humana.

Ego non iudico quenquam.

Sicut supra dixit: Non veni iudicare, quia veni salvare, non iudicare.

## 8:15
And if I judge.

Namely because I am about to judge, according to what is above in the fifth chapter.

Latin:

Et si iudico.

Scilicet quia sum iudicaturus, iuxta quod supra est capite quinto.

## 8:19
If you knew me, perhaps also the Father.

Because the Father is known through Christ, as above: The Son who is in the bosom of the Father, He has declared Him.

Latin:

Si sciretis me, forsitan & patrem.

Quia per Christum pater cognoscitur, ut supra: Filius qui est in sinu patris, ipse enarrabit.

## 8:21
I go, and you seek me.

Such are those who do not believe. What is the fruit of unbelief?

not to be able to find Christ.

Latin:

Ego vado, & quaeritis me.

Quales sunt qui non credunt. Quis est fructus incredulitatis?

non invenire posse Christum.

## 8:21
You seek.

Namely, through your own righteousnesses.

Latin:

Quaeritis.

Scilicet per vestras iustitias.

## 8:21
And you will die in your sin.

Namely, your righteousnesses, in which you trust, accomplish nothing to such an extent.

Latin:

Et in peccato vestro moriemini.

Scilicet adeo nihil efficiunt iustitiae vestrae, quibus vos confiditis.

## 8:21
Where I am going.

Namely, to the Father.

Latin:

Quò ego vado.

Scilicet ad patrem.

## 8:21
You cannot come.

Because you do not strive toward it by faith, but through your own righteousnesses.

Will he kill himself?

Namely, because he is flesh, just as we are. Therefore, flesh cannot arrive anywhere where we cannot, etc.

Latin:

Vos non potestis venire.

Quia non ex fide eò tenditis, sed per vestras iustitias.

Nunquid interficiet seipsum?

Scilicet quia caro est, sicut nos sumus. Caro non potest igitur aliquò pervenire, quò nos non possimus &c.

## 8:21
I am going.

Namely, through death to the Father.

Latin:

Ego vado.

Scilicet per mortem ad patrem.

## 8:23
You are from below.

That is, earthly, therefore you savor earthly things. I am from above, that is, heavenly, therefore I savor heavenly things. You are of the world, therefore you savor worldly things. Therefore because you savor nothing but carnal things, you will die in your sins.

Latin:

Vos deorsum estis.

Id est, terreni, ergo terrena sapitis. Ego desuper, id est caelestis, ergo caelestia sapio. Vos de mundo, ergo mundana sapitis. Igitur quia nihil nisi carnalia sapitis, moriemini in peccatis vestris.

## 8:24
For if you do not believe that I am.

Namely the illuminator and vivifier.

Latin:

Si enim non credideritis quia ego sum.

Scilicet illuminator & vivificator.

## 8:25
The Beginning.

Clearly that which I speak to you. Nonnus explains it thus, the very thing which I have already said to you before.

Latin:

Principium.

Planè id quod loquor vobis. Nonnus sic exponit, ipsum quod vobis iam antea dixi.

## 8:25 Common Places of This Context
COMMON PLACES OF THIS CONTEXT.

First, that which he says: I am the light of the world, Christ testifies that he is the illuminator: from which it follows that the light of nature is ignorance and blindness. He illuminates, however, by the knowledge of God, so that we may fear him, and believe.

Secondly, by no word except the word of God can the heart be vivified. And this is why those contentions about the authority of the word are proposed. Therefore doctrine is not to be accepted, unless it is known certainly to be from God.

That which Christ says, that he does not judge anyone (such as that which he said above, that he did not come to judge, but to save) is concerning his first advent and is to be understood concerning his word. For he came that he might save, having been made a sacrifice for sins, having been made a high priest who might intercede for us, having been made a king who will defend us against the gates of hell. Likewise he preached such a word, which is not law, but the remission of sin. But at his second coming he will judge, namely when there will no longer remain those whom he might save. Meanwhile, such as is the word of Christ, which will be preached even to the end of the world, such also is he himself the king. But it is a word of grace, not of wrath, therefore he himself is also a king of grace. He wishes the Father to be known through the scriptures. John 1. No one has ever seen God. And John 14. If you had known me, you would have known my Father also. Likewise: I am the way, the truth, and the life. Furthermore, to know the Father is to know his will. But the will of the Father cannot be known, except in the word: likewise it is to be known in the example of himself, in Christ, in whom we discern the mercy of the Father, that he wills, and how he wills to save. First that he wills, John 3. God so loved the world, that he gave his only begotten son for the world. Then how he wills, namely through the cross or death, Romans 6. He who has died, has been justified from sin. God cannot be known, except in the man Christ dead, and resurrected, that is, that the will of God is to justify through death, and to resurrect through faith. This is what 1 Corinthians 1. is said: Because in the wisdom of God he did not know, the world through wisdom &c. But Christ is known by his word, which is the preaching of the cross &c. as 1 Corinthians 1. That one cannot be harmed, unless God wills it. And if we are harmed, let us believe that it is the good will of God. The Pharisees seek salvation through their works, not through the cross & faith, therefore they cannot arrive at salvation: this is what he says: Where I go, you cannot come. That the flesh is ignorant of and despises God, these sentences testify: You are from below. You are of the world. You judge according to the flesh. Who Christ is, he answers: The beginning which I speak to you. Nonnus reported this sentence thus. I am that which I have already said to you before: for he had said: I am the light. Item: He who thirsts, let him come to me. Item: My flesh is truly food. But the order of the words demands that it be read thus. Entirely that which I speak with you, that is: clearly I am that very word which I speak with you: because Christ is the word of the father. John 1. And because he testifies that he is the word, he wishes to be known in the word.

Latin:

LOCI COMMUNES HUIUS CONTE-XTUS.

Primum, quod inquit: Ego sum lux mundi, testatur se Christus illuminatorem esse: è quo fit, lumen naturae ignorantiam & caecitatem esse. Illuminat autem cognitione dei, ut timeamus eum, & credamus.

Secundo, nullo verbo nisi verbo dei cor vivificari potest. Atque hoc est cur proponantur contentiones illae de autoritate verbi. Propterea non est accipienda doctrina, nisi sciatur certo ex deo esse.

Quod Christus inquit, se non iudicare quemquam (quale id quod supra dixit, se non venisse ad iudicandum, sed ad salvandum) est de primo eius adventu, & de verbo eius intelligendum. Venit enim ut salvet, factus hostia pro peccatis, factus pontifex qui interpellet pro nobis, factus rex qui nos defendet contra portas inferorum. Item verbum tale praedicavit, quod non est lex, sed remissio peccati. Secundo autem adventu iudicabit, scilicet ubi iam non restabunt quos salvet. Interim quale est verbum Christi, quod ad finem usque mundi praedicabitur, talis & ipse rex est. Verbum autem gratiae est non irae, igitur & ipse rex gratiae est. Patrem cognosci vult per scripturas. Ioannis 1. Deum nemo vidit unquam. Et Ioannis 14. Si nossetis me, & patrem meum nossetis. Item: Ego sum via, veritas, & vita. Porrò patrem nosse, est voluntatem eius nosse. Voluntas autem patris cognosci non potest, nisi in verbo: item in exemplo sui, in Christo, cognosci, in quo cernimus misericordiam patris, quod velit, & quomodo velit salvare. Primum quod velit, Ioannis 3. Sic deus dilexit mundum, ut daret unigenitum filium pro mundo. Deinde quomodo velit, scilicet per crucem seu mortem, Roman. 6. Qui mortuus est, iustificatus est à peccato. Non potest deus cognosci, nisi in homine Christo mortuo, & resuscitato, id est quod voluntas dei sit, iustificare per mortem, & resuscitare per fidem. Hoc est quod 1. Corinth. 1. dicitur: Quia in sapientia dei non cognovit, mundus deum per sapientiam &c. Cognoscitur autem Christus verbo suo, quod est praedicatio crucis &c. ut 1. ad Corinthios 1. Non posse laedi, nisi volente deo. Et si laedamur, bonam dei voluntatem esse, credamus. Pharisaei quaerunt salutem per opera sua, non per crucem & fidem, ideo non possunt ad salutem pervenire: hoc est quod inquit: Quò ego vado, vos non potestis venire. Carnem esse ignorantem & contemnentem dei, testantur istae sententiae: Vos deorsum estis. Vos de mundo estis. Vos secundum carnem iudicatis. Quis sit Christus, Respondet: Principio quod loquor vobis. Hanc sententiam sic retulit Nonnus. Hoc sum quod iam antea dixi vobis: dixerat enim: Ego sum lux. Item: Qui sitit, veniat ad me. Item: Caro meavere est cibus. Sed ordo verborum sic postulat legi. Prorsus id quod loquor vobiscum, id est: planè illud ipsum verbum sum quod loquor vobiscum: quia Christus est verbum patris. Ioannis 1. Et quia se verbum esse tessatur, vult in verbo cognosci.

## 8:26
I have many things to say and to judge concerning you.

After he explained what the Gospel is, and who he is, he adds a rebuke of the impious and those blaspheming the word of God. For because the flesh is ignorant of God, it also despises his word: and because it is judged by the word of God, therefore it hates and persecutes that. And Christ threatens all who either persecute or despise judgment, such a judgment as He already showed before in Pharaoh against the children of Israel, Psalm 105. They did not believe His word, etc. And He lifted up His hand against them. I have many things, namely, which I may accuse in you. Christ accuses when He shows sin to those who do not acknowledge sin: once sin is shown, He judges the convicted consciences. For those who persecute the word pretend a right, just as the Pharisees in the Gospel: We do not know where this man is from, etc. And those who despise live securely, thinking that which those in Isaiah 29. say: Who sees us? and who knows us? etc. For because they do not comprehend by reason the wrath of God against the flesh, therefore they do not believe the Gospel. Moreover, there are despisers, both those who openly despise, and those who do not acknowledge that the flesh sins in all pursuits and works: but they are content with the righteousness of the flesh, and they justify themselves by it: just as those who follow reason think, saying that it is a superfluous dispute to teach that all the works of all men are sins: and they think it is enough before God if you live in such a way that you cannot be reproved by the world. These are the men who are despisers, of whom it is said in Habakkuk chapter one. Those who despair also despise. And in short, all who in any matter despise the word of grace they build up in themselves. For because the Gospel is the word of grace, it demands that we expect all heavenly and earthly things from God by faith. But because the flesh does not grasp the magnitude of grace, it therefore thinks it has a just cause for why it does not believe; this is what Christ accuses. For the flesh cannot judge the sin of unbelief.

Latin:

Multa habeo de vobis loqui & iudicare.

Postea quam exposuit, quid sit Evangelium, & quis sit, subiicit increpationem impiorum & blasphemantium verbum dei. Quia enim caro ignorat deum, contemnit & verbum eius: & quia verbo dei iudicatur, ideo illud odit, & persequitur. Et minatur Christus omnibus, qui vel persequuntur, vel contemnunt iudicium, quale iudicium iam ante in Pharaone in filiis Israël corporaliter ostendit, Psalmo 105. Non crediderunt verbo eius &c. Et elevavit manum eius super eo. Multa habeo, scilicet quae accusem in vobis. Accusat Christus, cum iis, qui peccatum non agnoscunt, ostendit peccatum: ostenso peccato, convictas conscientias iudicat. Nam qui persequuntur verbum, praetendunt ius, sicut pharisaei in Evangelio: Hunc nescimus unde sit &c. Et qui contemnunt, vivunt secure, id sentientes quod illi apud Esaiam 29. Quis videt nos? & quis novit nos? &c. Quia enim ratione non comprehendunt iram dei adversus carnem, ideo non credunt Evangelio. Sunt autem contemptores, & qui palàm contemnunt, & qui non agnoscunt, carnem peccare in omnibus studiis & operibus: sed contenti sunt iustitia carnis, & illa se iustificant: quemadmodum sentiunt qui rationem se quuntur dicentes, super vacaneam disputationem esse, docere quod omnia omnium hominum opera sint peccata: sentiuntque satis esse coram deo, si sic vivas, ut à mundo reprehendi non possis. Hi sunt viri contemptores, de quibus Habacuk primo dicitur. Contemnunt & qui desperant. Et breviter, contemnunt verbum gratiae omnes, qui ulla in re aedificant in sese. Quia enim Evangelium est verbum gratiae, exigit, ut fide omnia caelestia & terrena expectemus à deo. Quia vero caro magnitudinem gratiae non capit, ideo putat sibi iustam causam esse cur non credat, hoc est quod accusat Christus. Nam caro non potest peccatum incredulitatis iudicare.

## 8:26
But he who sent me is true.

This is an objection, as if he were saying thus: You judge my judgment and my entire discourse to be a lie. Thus the flesh judges when it hears that all our things are condemned. Likewise, the flesh judges that this doctrine, that one must trust in the mercy of God alone, is vain. Therefore Christ added: He who sent me is true: that is, my judgment is the judgment of God: my discourse is the discourse of the Father. Just as he said above: The Son who is in the bosom of the Father, he has declared him to us, etc., because his judgment is true.

Latin:

Sed qui misit me verax est.

Occupatio est, quasi sic dicat: Vos meum iudicium & universum meum sermonem iudicatis esse mendacium. Sic caro iudicat, quando audit omnia nostra damnari. Item esse sola misericordia dei fidendum, hanc doctrinam iudicat vanam esse caro. Ideo Christus addidit: Qui me misit, verax est: id est meum iudicium, iudicium dei est: meus sermo, sermo patris est. Sicut supra inquit: Filius qui est in sinu patris, ipse enarravit nobis &c. quia verax est illius iudicium.

## 8:27
And they did not know.

Because it was said above, you are from below.

Latin:

Et non cognoverunt.

Quia supra dictum est, vos deorsum estis.

## 8:28
When you have lifted up the Son of Man.

He alleges the testimony of his word, namely the resurrection from the dead. Moreover, the resurrection of Christ is not only a testimony by which the enemies of the word are convicted, but it is also the power of God, by which those who believe in Christ are made alive. According to that to the Philippians 3: To know the power of his resurrection, etc. And to the Ephesians 1: According to the working of the might of his power, which he wrought in Christ Jesus, raising him from the dead. Furthermore, what he says, not in the resurrection, but: when you shall lift up, that is, when you shall crucify: he wishes this power of his to be known on the cross.

Latin:

Cum exaltaveritis filium hominis.

Allegat verbi sui testimonium, scilicet resurrectionem à mortuis. Est autem resurrectio Christi non modo testimonium, quo convincuntur hostes verbi, sed & virtus dei est, qua vivificantur qui credunt in Christum. Iuxta illud ad Philippenses 3. Ad cognoscendam virtutem resurrectionis eius &c. Et ad Ephesios 1. Secundum efficaciam potentiae virtutis eius, quam operatus est in Christo Iesu, suscitans illum à mortuis. Porrò quod ait, non in resurrectione, sed: quando exaltabitis, id est quando crucifigetis: vult hanc potentiam suam in cruce cognosci.

## 8:31
If you shall continue in my word.

What is it that he says thus: If you shall continue, you shall know: do those who do not persevere not know? I answer as the Apostle Rom. 5 says: that patience is prepared by tribulation, and proof by patience, that is, the experience of the word. Thus Christ says here, those who persevere: because he consoles those who await him by faith in temptation: certainly it happens that those who neither sustain him when they are tempted are ignorant of the consolation.

Latin:

Si vos manseritis in sermone meo.

Quid est, quod sic ait: Si manseritis, cognoscetis: an non cognoscunt qui non perseverant? Respondeo sicut Apostolus Rom. 5. inquit: Tribulatione patientiam, patientia probationem parari, id est experientiam verbi. Ita hic ait Christus, qui perseverent: quia consolatur eos, qui fide expectant eum in tentatione: certe fit ut ignari consolationis sint, qui neque sustinent eum cum tentantur.

## 8:31
You shall be my disciples indeed.

See how he attributes the work of God to himself, so that he may teach that he is God. Above he said from Isaiah that all are to be taught by God, here he says: You shall be my disciples. For he speaks not of external hearing, but of internal and spiritual hearing, which God effects in the heart, when he creates a new light.

For he distinguishes his disciples from the disciples of Moses. The disciples of Moses are those who hear only outwardly, as much as reason can, which is the hearing of the letter and mere hypocrisy. The disciples of Christ are those whose hearts are illuminated by a new light so that they may fear God. He says, Truly, that is, hearing with the hearing of the heart. You shall know, namely that God is truthful, performing what he promised.

Latin:

Vere discipuli mei eritis.

Vide quomodo opus dei sibi tribuit, ut doceat se esse deum. Supra dixit ex Esaia, omnes à deo docendos esse, hic ait: Eritis mei discipuli. Loquitur enim non de externo auditu, sed de interno auditu & spirituali, quem in corde efficit deus, cum creat novum lumen.

Distinguit enim discipulos suos à discipulis Moysi. Discipuli Moysi sunt, qui extrinsecus tantum audiunt, quantum potest ratio, qui auditus est litera & mera hypocrisis. Discipuli Christi sunt, quorum corda novo lumine illustrantur ut timeant deum. Dicit, Vere, id est audientes auditu cordis. Cognoscetis, scilicet quod deus verax est, praestans quod promisit.

## 8:32
And the truth shall set you free.

First, in what Christian liberty consists, and what it is, must be sought from this passage. Christ defines servitude twice, first in that which he says: Whoever commits sin is a slave of sin. That is, he is under sin. To be under sin is to be condemned and killed by sin: according to that: The sting of death is sin. Therefore, this is servitude, to sin and to die. Secondly, he defines servitude in that he says: The truth shall set you free: therefore, servitude is a certain lie. Moreover, this lie is that, because nature is ignorant of God, and neither believes in God nor fears God: it turns toward creatures, and trusts in the works of its own glory, its own wisdom, its own righteousness, etc., which trust is certainly a lie: because it trusts in that in which it ought not to have trusted, and despises that which is truly life: and it happens that neither do those things free from death, and the curse remains by which we are condemned to servitude. Thirdly, because it is servitude, to sin and

to die, and whatever use of clothing or foods does not free one from death: therefore there is not properly liberty in that. Fourthly, what captivity is, has been said. On the contrary, liberty is the remission of sins, that is, for sin to be pardoned, although sin may still remain in the flesh: and for the right of sin to be taken away, so that it cannot accuse, condemn, or kill. For to be a slave of sin is to be killed by sin. Those who believe by faith obtain this liberty, that through Christ sin has been blotted out, just as he says above: He who believes in him is not judged. And here: The truth shall make you free. He who believes the promise of the father will be set free. Therefore, the first thing in it is liberty, because our sins are blotted out without our merit. The right of sin and death is taken away, according to that 1. Corinth. 15. Death is swallowed up in victory. Fifthly, and because sin has been blotted out, it is necessary that righteousness should reign. And because death has been swallowed up, it is necessary that eternal life should follow. Therefore, in those in whom sin and death have been blotted out, there is now the knowledge of God, faith, fear, love of neighbor, this is what he says: The truth shall make you free: that is, you will now be truthful, the mercy of God having been known: through faith you will trust in God alone, and that faith will free you from every right of sin: because it is eternal life. The flesh cannot believe in God, but despises him. But those who are in Christ believe, and fear, etc., therefore they are free. As if a hand were suffering from some numbness, so that it cannot perform its duty, it has been a slave or captive, but if it is restored, then it will be free. This is concerning liberty Isaiah 61. To heal the brokenhearted, to preach deliverance to the captives, and recovering of sight to them that are bound &c. & often elsewhere. Sixth, because the Gospel preaches the righteousness of the spirit, therefore it wishes the use of ceremonies to be free: neither to sin if they are not done, nor to justify if they are done. But the spirit uses all external things, as faith & charity demand. They sin who do works in this, so that they may be justified through them. They sin who violate them with carnal rashness. For ceremonies are the elements of the world, which we ought to use without scandal.

Latin:

Et veritas liberabit vos.

Primum in quo sit Christiana libertas, & quid sit, ex hoc loco petendum est. Definit autem Christus servitutem bis, primo in eo quod inquit: Qui facit peccatum, servus est peccati. id est sub peccato est. Sub peccato esse, est peccato damnari & occidi: iuxta illud: Aculeus mortis est peccatum. Proinde haec est servitus peccare & mori. Secundo in eo definit servitutem, quod ait: Veritas liberabit vos: ergo servitus est mendacium quoddam. Est autem hoc mendacium, quod quia natura est ignorans dei, nec credit deo, nec timet deum: deflectit ad creaturas, & fidit operibus gloriae suae, sapientiae suae, iustitiae suae &c. quae fidutia certe mendacium est: quia fidit eo cui non debuit fidi, & contemnit id quod vere vita est: & fit ut nec illa à morte liberent, & maledictio maneat qua servitutis damnati sumus. Tertio, quia servitus est, peccare &

mori, & à morte non liberat qualiscunque usus vestium vel ciborum: ergo non est in illo proprie libertas. Quarto, quid sit captivitas, dictum est. Econtra, libertas est remissio peccatorum, hoc est condonari peccatum, quamquam reliquum sit in carne peccatum adhuc: & tolli ius peccati, ne possit accusare, damnare, occidere. Nam esse servum peccati, est à peccato occidi. Hanc libertatem consequuntur fide credentes, quod per Christum peccatum sit deletum, sicut supra inquit: Qui credit in eum, non iudicatur. Et hic: Veritas liberabit. Qui credit promissioni patris, liberabitur. Est ergo primum in eo libertas, quod sine merito nostro peccata delentur. Tollitur ius peccati & mortis, iuxta illud 1. Corinth. 15. Absorpta est mors in victoria. Quinto, Et quia peccatum deletum est, ne cesse est iustitiam regnare. Et quia absorpta est mors, necesse est vitam aeternam succedere. Proinde in iis in quibus peccatum & mors deleta sunt, iam est cognitio dei, fides, timor, dilectio proximi, hoc est quod ait: Veritas liberabit vos: id est iam veraces eritis cognita misericordia dei: per fidem confidetis solo deo, & ea fides liberabit ab omni iure peccati: quia vita aeterna est. Caro non potest deo credere, sed contemnit eum. Credunt autem, & timent &c. qui in Christo sunt, ergo liberi sunt. Ut si manus laboret stupore aliquo, ita ut suum officium facere non possit, serva seu captiva fuerit, sin autem restituatur, tunc libera erit. Haec de libertate Esaias 61. Ut mederer contritis corde, praedicarem captivis indulgentiam, clausis apertionem &c. & aliâs saepe. Sexto, quia iustitiam spiritus praedicat Evangelium, ideo usum ceremoniarum vult liberum esse: nec peccare si non fiant, nec iustificare si fiant. Sed utitur spiritus externis rebus omnibus, prout postulat fides & charitas. Peccant qui in hoc faciunt opera, ut per illa iustificentur. Peccant qui carnali temeritate violant. Sunt enim elementa mundi ceremoniae, quibus uti citra scandalum debemus.

## 8:33
We are Abraham's seed, and were never in bondage to any man &c.

That the Jews respond that they have never been in bondage, Augustine convicts them simply of a lie. I understand the Jews to be speaking of right, not of fact. For they themselves also plead a right, when they say: We are Abraham's seed, that is, no nation reigns by divine authority, or possesses land, except the Jews, which is the peculiar property of God, to whom the promise was made, that they should rule over all their enemies: Genesis 22. Therefore the Jews were free. For because the law had carnal righteousnesses, it was necessary also for a place to be given to the people for preserving carnal righteousnesses, so that the people would endure until Christ should come. But the Jews referred to this carnal liberty also the prophecies concerning Christ, Ezechielis 34. They shall no longer bear the reproaches of the nations. And they imagined a certain carnal liberty, and a carnal empire, by which they were to rule over all nations etc., although God had promised eternal life and an eternal kingdom. Hieremiae -1. The sanctuary of the Lord shall not be plucked up, and it shall not be destroyed anymore forever. Just as also the angel Lucae. 1. explains the promises: The Lord God shall give unto him the throne of David his father, and he shall reign in the house of Jacob forever, and of his kingdom there shall be no end. Furthermore, because eternal life and an eternal kingdom were promised: it was necessary that death and the cause of death, sin, be taken away: just as the first promise, the seed of Eve shall crush the head of the serpent etc. Therefore Christ was made king, liberty was prepared, that is, eternal life. Sin and death have been evacuated. This liberty is incomprehensible to the flesh, which God promised from the beginning, and performed in Christ. And this is what the Apostle Rom. 4. says, that the inheritance of the world was promised to Abraham, which is to the seed of Abraham: that is, it was promised to Christ, that he might be the heir, that is, the lord of all, and in Christ all his brothers are co-heirs. The seed of Abraham, that is, to whom the liberty was promised. We have never served anyone, that is, since we are already by right free beforehand.

Latin:

Semen Abrahae sumus, & nemini &c.

Quod Iudaei respondent, se numquam serviisse, Augustinus eos simpliciter arguit mendacii. Ego de iure, non de facto loqui Iudaeos, intelligo. Nam & ipsi ius causantur, cum inquiunt: Semen Abrahae sumus, id est nulla gens autoritate divina regnat, aut terram possidet, praeter Iudaei, quod peculium dei est, cui promissio facta est, ut dominentur omnium inimicorum suorum: Genes. 22. Ideo liberi erant Iudaei. Quia enim lex habebat carnales iustitias, oportuit & locum dari populo carnalium iustitiarum conservandarum, ut duraret populus donec veniret Christus. Sed ad hanc carnalem libertatem retulerunt & prophetias de Christo Iudaei, Ezechielis 34. Non portabunt amplius opprobria gentium. Et finxerunt carnalem quandam libertatem, & carnale imperium, quo dominaturi essent omnium gentium &c. cum tamen vitam aeternam & regnum aeternum promiserit deus. Hieremiae -1. Sanctum domini non evelletur, & non destruetur ultra in perpetuum. Sicut & angelus Lucae. 1. promissiones exponit: Dabit illi dominus deus sedem David patris eius, & regnabit in domo Iacob in aeternum, & regni eius non erit finis. Porrò quia vita aeterna & regnum aeternum promissa sunt: necesse erat mortem & mortis causam, peccatum tolli: sic ut prima promissio, Evae semen conteret caput serpentis &c. Itaque factus est Christus rex, parata libertas est, id est vita aeterna. Peccatum et mors sunt evacuata. Haec libertas est incomprehensibilis carni, quam promisit deus à principio, & praestitit in Christo. Et hoc est quod Apostolus Rom. 4. dicit, Abrahae promissam haereditatem mundi, quod est semini Abrahae: id est Christo promissum esse, ut sit haeres, id est dominus omnium, & in Christo cohaeredes omnes fratres eius. Semen Abrahae, id est cui promissa est libertas. Nemini servivimus unquam, id est cum iam antea iure liberi simus.

## 8:34
Everyone who commits sin is a slave of sin.

Christ answers, transferring the matter to slavery and liberty, concerning which the promises were properly made, as if he were saying thus: Does death not still rage against you, and do you not still sin? Therefore you are not yet free. The Father promised true liberty, by which slavery would be made void: as long as you serve sin and death, you still serve it, therefore you are not yet free. Secondly, the promised liberty confers that he who has been made free may be in the house forever: only the son is in the house forever, therefore you are not yet free. Thirdly, If only the son is in the house forever: therefore no one will be free, unless whom the son has set free.

Latin:

Omnis qui facit peccatum, servus est peccati.

Respondet Christus, transferens rem ad servitutem & libertatem, de qua proprie factae sunt promissiones, quasi sic diceret: Nónne mors adhuc saevit in vos, & adhuc peccatis? ergo nondum liberi estis. Pater promisit veram libertatem, qua servitus fieret irrita: quam diu servitis peccato & morti, eam adhuc servitis, ergo nondum liberi estis. Secundo, libertas promissa confert, ut sit in aeternum in domo is qui factus est liber: solus filius in aeternum in domo est, ergo vos nondum liberi estis. Tertio, Si solus filius in aeternum in domo est: ergo nemo liber erit, nisi quem filius liberaverit.

## 8:35
But the slave does not remain.

That is, the slave of sin and thus guilty of death. And he cannot free himself: to such an extent is free will nothing. The slave of sin is not an heir of the promises: therefore neither are you heirs of the promised liberty, because you are slaves.

Latin:

Servus autem non manet.

Id est, servus peccati adeòque reus mortis. Et se liberare non potest: adeo nihil est liberum arbitrium. Peccati servus non est haeres promissionum: ergo nec vos estis haeredes promissae libertatis, quia servi estis.

## 8:35
But the son remains forever.

Thus he simultaneously accuses all men of sin, when he asserts that only the son remains in the house of the father forever.

Latin:

Filius autem manet in aeternum.

Ita simul accusat omnes homines peccati, cum solum filium asseverat manere in domo patris in aeternum.

## 8:36
If therefore the son shall make you free.

Here he teaches how we also become free, or heirs of the promised liberty: but we shall be if the son makes us free: therefore we are not freed by our own powers or works.
