Latin:

Si ergo vos filius liberaverit.

Hic docet quomodo & nos fiamus liberi, seu haeredes promissae libertatis: erimus autem si filius nos liberet: ergo non liberamur nostris viribus aut operibus.

## 8:37
I know that you are the children of Abraham.

Namely according to the flesh. Furthermore, according to the flesh the seed is not the heir of the promise made to Abraham, but the seed according to election, that is, those who are reborn by that faith by which Abraham was justified and made father: not only of this people, but also of all nations. These are the children of God, according to that: Who were born, not of blood, but of God, John 1. Thus there is no prerogative of the flesh, of works, for any man over another, why he should be justified, but it is the gift of God justifying by faith, however you may have sinned.

Latin:

Scio quod filii Abrahae estis.

Scilicet secundum carnem. Porrò secundum carnem semen non est haeres promissionis factae Abrahae, sed semen secundum electionem, hoc est qui ea fide renati sunt, qua iustificatus est Abraham, & factus pater: non huius tantum populi, sed & omnium gentium. Hi filii dei sunt, iuxta illud: Qui non ex sanguinibus, sed ex deo nati sunt, Ioannis 1. Adeo nulla est praerogativa carnis, operum ulli homini prae alio, cur iustificetur, sed donum dei est fide iustificantis, quomodo cunque peccaveris.

## 8:37
You seek to kill me.

Namely because you are the carnal seed of Abraham, therefore you seek to kill me: according to that: And his own received him not. Psalm 17. The children of strangers have lied to me etc.

Latin:

Quaeritis me interficere.

Scilicet quia carnale semen Abrahae estis, ideo quaeritis me interficere: iuxta illud: Et sui eum non receperunt. Psalmo 17. Filii alieni mentiti sunt mihi &c.

## 8:37
Because the word etc.

That is because you neither understand, nor grasp my words, because they are of God.

Latin:

Quia sermo &c.

Id est quia nec intelligitis, nec capitis sermones meos, quia dei sunt.

## 8:38
I speak what I have seen with the Father, and you what you have seen with the Father, etc.

He uses the word for seeing. For just as the knowledge of the Father is life, so the blindness, which Satan works, is death. 2. Cor. 4. In whom the god of this world has blinded the minds of the unbelievers. The Gospel is the manifestation of the truth, so that if the heart understands distrust and faith, contempt of God and fear, it may know the wrath and mercy of God. The work of the devil is blindness, so that those things cannot be discerned. And just as the Spirit of God moves the saints to those things which are of God, so also the devil moves the hearts of the impious to his own.

Latin:

Ego quod vidi apud patrem loquor, & vos quae vidistis apud patrem &c.

Videndi verbo utitur. Sicut enim cognitio patris est vita, ita excaecatio, quam operatur satan, mors est. 2. Corinth. 4. In quibus deus huius seculi excaecavit mentes infidelium. Evangelium est manifestatio veritatis, ita ut si cor intelligat diffidentiam & fidem, contemptum dei & timorem, cognoscat iram & misericordiam dei. Opus diaboli, excaecatio est, ne illa cerni possint. Et sicut spiritus dei agit sanctos ad ea quae sunt dei, ita & diabolus agit corda impiorum ad sua.

## 8:39
Our father is Abraham.

Why do you condemn our father, since he is Abraham?

Latin:

Pater noster Abraham est.

Cur damnas patrem nostrum, cum Abraham sit?

## 8:39
If you are the children of Abraham, do the works of Abraham.

That is, believe in me, for Abraham was justified by faith. He distinguishes again the carnal seed from the elect seed. The carnal seed, the people of works, contends that the promise pertains to them, and justifies itself by works, but the people of faith occupy it. to Rom. 9.

Latin:

Si filii Abrahae estis, opera Abrahae fa-cite.

Id est credite in me, nam Abraham fide iustificatus est. Distinguit iterum carnale semen ab electo semine. Semen carnale, populus operum, contendit promissionem ad se pertinere, & iustificat se ex operibus, sed populus fidei occupat. ad Rom. 9.

## 8:40
But now you seek to kill me.

For the world hates Christ for this reason, because his Gospel is truth, but the world is a liar. The Gospel forbids trusting in creatures, in wisdom, in the works of justice, etc. Because the flesh does not hear these things, therefore the Gospel condemns it. Then here you will observe what Christian sufferings are, to suffer for the sake of the truth: and indeed the truth heard from God, that is, how certain you are that it is the word of God. Those who are not certain will not stand.

Latin:

Nunc autem quaeritis me interficere.

Nam ideo mundus odit Christum, quia Evangelium eius veritas est, mundus autem mendax. Evangelium vetat fidere creaturis, sapientiae, iustitiae operibus &c. Haec quia caro non audit, ideo damnat Evangelium. Tum hic observabis quae sint Christianae passiones, pati propter veritatem: & veritatem quidem à deo auditam, id est quam certus sis verbum dei esse. Qui non sunt certi, non consistent.

## 8:40
Abraham did not do this.

Nay, he believed in such a way that against hope he believed even in hope, when he was about to slay his son, as is held at Romanos 4.. You do the works of your father, namely, Abraham is so far from being your father, because he did not do that which you do.

Latin:

Hoc Abraham non fecit.

Immo credidit ita, ut contra spem in spem etiam crederet, cum occisurus esset filium, ut ad Romanos 4. habetur. Vos facitis opera patris vestri, scilicet adeo Abraham non pater, quia non fecit id quod vos facitis.

## 8:41
We are not born of fornication.

The flesh judges that there can be no children of Abraham except those who were born from him according to the flesh. Likewise you see here that they infer from carnal generation, namely, that they are the holy people of God, etc., because they are the seed of Abraham, to whom it had been promised: I will be their God. Freedom had been promised to the seed of Abraham, and Christ denies that those who are the seed of Abraham are free. It is necessary, therefore, that you understand that freedom which is truly freedom, eternal life: and those who are truly children of Abraham according to election to be the children of Abraham.

Latin:

Nos ex fornicatione non sumus nati.

Caro iudicat, nullos esse posse filios Abrahae praeter eos, qui ex ipso secundum carnem nati sunt. Item vides hic, quod ex carnali generatione colligant, scilicet se populum dei esse sanctum &c. quia semen Abrahae sunt, cui promissum erat: Ero deus eorum. Libertas semini Abrahae promissa erat, & Christus negat liberos esse eos, qui sint semen Abrahae. Neces se est igitur libertatem intelligas eam, quae vere est libertas, vitam aeternam: & filios Abrahae eos, qui sunt vere filii Abrahae secundum electionem.

## 8:42
If God were your father, you would surely love me.

Namely because you would have been regenerated through that Spirit, who is the love of God. And here again you see the people of the law distinguished from the people of the Gospel. The people of the law judge themselves to be the people of God, because they have heard the law: Christ condemns that people: and again He explains the promises spiritually concerning that people, who truly are the people. He says: For I proceeded from God, that is, I was born from eternity, and I came, namely into the world.

Latin:

Si deus pater vester esset, diligeretis utique me.

Scilicet quia per eum spiritum regenerati essetis, qui est amor dei. Atque hic iterum vides distingui populum legis à populo Evangelii. Populus legis iudicat se esse populum dei, quia legem audierit: Christus damnat eum populum: & rursum exponit promissiones spiritualiter de eo populo, qui vere est populus. Dicit: Ego enim ex deo processi, id est natus sum ab aeterno, & veni, scilicet in mundum.

## 8:44
You are of your father the devil, and the desires &c.

Here Christ describes the kingdom of the devil, and He proves that whoever does not believe is of their father the devil, as it is also said in 2. Corinth. 4.: The god of this world blinds the hearts of the unbelievers. And Ephe. 2.: According to the prince of the power of the air, the spirit that works in the children of disobedience. And in Coloss. 1. it is said that Christ delivers us from the power of darkness. And in baptism we testify that Satan reigns in those who do not believe, when we command him, saying: Go out, unclean spirit. And below in cap. 16. He calls Satan the prince of the world. Which is what else, than that Satan has the hearts of all in obedience to him of those who are ignorant of Christ? Next, see what works he attributes here to Satan, lying and murder. By lying he blasphemes God, by murder he kills the human race: this he does in the hearts of those who are not of Christ, so that they may be liars, that is, despisers of God, trusting in themselves. They are likewise murderers, so that they may wish themselves saved, even if all human things are lost. Such are all who are not in Christ. It is easy to discern what kind of human wisdom and justice there is, so that you may easily judge what free will can do. This is why Scripture inveighs so against the wisdom of the world. The devil promotes the fruits of these roots to the extent that God detests slaughters, lusts, and all those things about which Rom. 1. He gave them up to the desires of their hearts. Behold, this is the kingdom of Satan, in which we wretches bear fruit with such security, forgetful of that which Peter warns: Brothers, be vigilant, etc.

Latin:

Vos ex patre diabolo estis, & desyde-ria &c.

Describit hic regnum diaboli, & devincit Christus, ex patre diabolo esse, quicunque non credunt, ut & 2. Corinth. 4. dicitur: Deum huius seculi excaecare corda infidelium. Et Ephe. 2. Secundum principatum aëris spiritus qui operatur in filiis diffidentiae. Et Coloss. 1. dicitur, Christum eripere nos de potestate tenebrarum. Et in baptismo testamur regnare satanam in iis qui non credunt, cum imperamus ei, dicentes: Exi immunde spiritus. Et infra cap. 16. vocat satanam principem mundi. Quod quid aliud est, quàm satanam habere obedientia corda omnium eo rum qui sunt ignorantes Christi? Deinde vide quae hic opera satanae tribuat, mendacium & homicidium. Mendacio blasphemat deum, homicidio occidit genus humanum: hoc agit in cordibus eorum qui non sunt Christi, ut mendaces sint, id est contemptores dei, confidentes sibiipsis. Sunt item homicidae, ut se salvos velint, etiam omnia humana perdita. Tales sunt omnes, qui non sunt in Christo. Facile est cernere, cuiusmodi sint sapientia et iustitia humana, ut facile possis iudicare, quid possit liberum arbitrium. Hoc est quod scriptura sic invehitur in sapientiam mundi. Fructus harum radicum eatenus promovet diabolus, quatenus deus caedes, libidines detestatur, & ea omnia de quibus Rom. 1. Tradidit in desyderia cordis suorum. Ecce hoc regnum satanae est, cui miseri tanta securitate fructificamus, obliti eius, quod Petrus monet: Fratres, vigilate &c.

## 8:46
Which of you will convict me, etc.

The words of proving, the highest and most certain rule is Scripture, so that it is a commandment Deuteronomy 13. that a prophet should not be heard if he has taught anything against Scripture, even if he proves his speech with miracles. And he adds that by such miracles the people are tested by God. After the word, there are miracles, Mark last: The Lord confirming the sermon with the following signs. Finally, there is the life of the teacher. For Scripture requires of bishops that they be without reproach, that the adversary may be afraid, having nothing to say against us, Titus 2. And 2 Corinthians 2 it says that one must preach the Gospel, not with the adulterated word of God, but from sincerity, from God: before God (with God looking on) in Christ. Who of you. That is, because my life cannot be reproached, you cannot deny that I am from God because of my life, since Scripture is also a witness, which miracles also testify to.

Latin:

Quis ex vobis arguet me &c.

Verba probandi, summa & certissima regula scriptura est, ita ut praeceptum sit Deuteronomii 13. ut ne audiatur propheta, si quid docuerit contra scripturam, etiam si miraculis probet sermonem. Et addit, talibus miraculis à deo tentari populum. Post verbum, miracula sunt, Marci ultimo: Domino confirman te sermonem sequentibus signis. Postremo est vita docentis. Exigit enim scriptura ab episcopis, ut sint sine crimine, ut adversarius vereatur, nihil habens maledicere de nobis, ad Titum 2. Et 2. Corinth. 2. dicit Evangelizandum esse, non adulterato verbo dei, ex synceritate, ex deo: coram deo (inspectante deo) in Christo. Quis ex vobis. Id est, quia reprehendi vita mea non potest, negare non potestis propter vitam me ex deo esse, cum testis etiam scriptura est, quem testificantur etiam miracula.

## 8:47
He who is from God hears the word of God.

And this passage testifies that human powers cannot hear, that is, believe the word of God, just as he also said above: They will all be θεοδίδακτοι. Do we not say well that you have a demon? That is, for the reason that you deny that we hear the word of God, who are nevertheless the people of God.

Latin:

Qui ex deo est, verbum dei audit.

Et hic locus testatur vires humanas non posse audire, id est credere verbo dei, sicut & supra dixit: Omnes erunt θεοδίδακτοι. Nónne bene dicimus, quia daemonium habes? id est ideo quod neges nos audire verbum dei, qui tamen populus dei sumus.

## 8:48
I do not have a demon.

He refutes that insult, by which the doctrine was properly blasphemed, because they felt that the doctrine of Christ was from the devil.

Latin:

Ego daemonium non habeo.

Id convicium refellit, quo proprie doctrina blasphemabatur, quod sentirent doctrinam Christi ex diabolo esse.

## 8:49
But I honor my Father.

Fulfilling the ministry to which he sent me, preaching his glory: for when the Gospel is revealed, the glory of God is preached, Isaiah 30. although he does not share his honor with me, John 5. He has given all judgment to the Son, that all may honor the Son just as they honor the Father.

Latin:

Sed honorifico patrem meum.

Implens ministerium, ad quod ille me misit, praedicans gloriam eius: nam Evangelio revelato praedicatur gloria dei, Esaiae 30. cum tamen ille suum honorem mecum communicet, Ioannis 5. Omne iudicium dedit filio, ut omnes honorificent filium sicut honorificant patrem.

## 8:51
If anyone keeps my word.

This is properly the preaching of the Gospel, because Christ came so that, with the kingdom of sin and death destroyed, he might establish the kingdom of righteousness and eternal life. Isaiah 53. He shall see long-lived offspring. Therefore it is necessary that the word of Christ, because he reigns through this, should justify and give life. Now if the word of Christ alone justifies and gives life: therefore not our works, not our powers. Just as he says above: He who believes in me does not come into judgment. So here: He shall not see death, although he is about to lay down this corruptible and animal body, yet he shall not see judgment, which is truly death. But faith absorbs death, and all sins.

Latin:

Si quis sermonem meum servaverit.

Haec est proprie Evangelii praedicatio, quia Christus venit, ut regno peccati et mortis destructo, regnum iustitiae & vitae aeternae constituat. Esaiae 53. Videbit semen longaevum. Quare necessum est, ut verbum Christi, quia per hoc regnat, iustificet, & vivificet. Iam si solum verbum Christi iustificat & vivificat: ergo non opera nostra, non vires nostrae. Sicut supra ait: Qui credit in me, in iudicium non venit. Ita hic: Non videbit mortem, quamquam positurus sit corruptibile hoc & animale corpus, tamen non videbit iudicium, quod vere mors est. Sed fides absorbet mortem, & omnia peccata.

## 8:52
Abraham is dead.

Having heard the promise of eternal life, and indeed to be given through the word, and through the word of a man about to die, there reason is scandalized: and because it perceives nothing of eternal life, so that it might be able to understand it to be given through the word:

And because it judges that the foolish word is repugnant to eternal life, believe in Christ the mortal man, and you shall live: therefore they oppose a manifest experience, Abraham is dead. Furthermore, this is the wisdom of the cross, where life and glory lie hidden under the cross, death, and ignominy.

Latin:

Abraham mortuus est.

Audita promissione vitae aeternae, & donandae quidem per verbum, & per verbum hominis morituri, ibi vero scandalizatur ratio: & quia nihil sentit vitae aeternae, ut possit intelligere eam per verbum dari:

& quia repugnare iudicat vitae aeternae verbum stultum, crede in Christum hominem mortalem, & vives: propterea opponunt manifestam experientiam, Abraham mortuus est. Porrò haec est sapientia crucis, ubi vita & gloria latet sub cruce, morte, & ignominia.

## 8:54
If I glorify myself.

This sentiment and similar ones pertain to the infirmity of Christ and his abasement, not otherwise than that: My God, my God, why have you forsaken me? etc. For that abasement was below every grasp, or estimation of the human mind. For John did not say rashly: And the word was made flesh: And Paul: he was made sin for us. These both commend the cross and console pious hearts with the likeness of Christ. And it confirms faith, according to that in Hebrews: We have a high priest, who can sympathize. It is an immense love, which abased itself into an immense state, so that it might glorify us. And again, the immense blindness of the human heart, not seeing, nay, even despising the amplitude of grace. Secondly, If Christ says that his glory is nothing, whose flesh nevertheless was not corrupted, what is it that our flesh should boast of its own strengths and works before God? A feigned faith says that this is God, whom the scripture preaches, and in this indeed he does not err, but yet he does not know him. Faith not feigned not only professes this to be the God whom scripture preaches, but also knows him. What therefore is the difference, both have the same word, and they know by the word alone, according to that: Faith is from hearing? Feigned faith does not believe every word. True faith believes every word, therefore it also knows God. Fourthly, the impious confess that there is a God, but because they do not know him, they imagine him to be such as reason comprehends, that is, one who permits us to ourselves, so that according to our own prudence we may take care of our own affairs. And they feel as those Zephaniah 1 The Lord will not do good, he will not do evil. Therefore they are carried away to riches and to preparing glory etc. Likewise since to know God is to know that he does mercy and judgment, Jeremiah 9 you see that he judges sin, and has mercy on those who believe. From there they conceive neither his wrath, nor his mercy: therefore they neither fear, nor believe, but trust in themselves. On the contrary, the pious know God to do mercy and judgment, that is, with a faithful heart, and they truly believe that he holds sins in hatred, and that all human works are sins: and again they truly believe, because he has mercy on those who entrust themselves to him, therefore they fear. Likewise they expect from his hand all good things in life, death, in all times and circumstances. Thus he defines the knowledge of himself Psalm 77. They shall tell their children, that they may set their hope in God, and not forget the works of God, and seek out his commandments.

Latin:

Si ego glorifico meipsum.

Haec sententia et similes ad infirmitatem Christi pertinent & extenuationem, non aliter atque illud: Deus deus meus, ut quid me dereliquisti? &c. Fuit enim ea extenuatio infra omnem captum, seu aestimationem humanae mentis. Non enim temere dixit Ioannes: Et verbum caro factum est: Et Paulus: pro nobis factus est peccatum. Haec & crucem commendant & consolantur pia corda similitudine Christi. Et fidem confirmat, iuxta illud in Hebraeis: Habemus pontificem, qui possit compati. Immensus amor est, qui se in immensum extenuavit, ut nos glorificaret. Et rursum immensa caecitas humani cordis, non videntis, immo contemnentis etiam amplitudinem gratiae. Secundo, Si Christus ait suam gloriam nihil esse, cuius caro tamen non erat corrupta, quid est quod nostra caro glorietur de suis viribus & operibus coram deo? Fides ficta dicit hunc esse deum, quem scriptura praedicat, & in hoc quidem non errat, sed tamen non novit eum. Fides non ficta non modo profitetur hunc esse deum, quem scriptura praedicat, sed & novit eum. Quid igitur interest, utrique idem verbum habent, & verbo tantum cognoscunt, iuxta illud: Fides ex auditu est? Fides ficta non credit omni verbo. Vera fides omni verbo credit, propterea etiam novit deum. Quarto, Impii fatentur esse deum, sed quia non norunt, fingunt eum talem esse, qualem comprehendit ratio, hoc est qui permittat nos nobis, ut iuxta prudentiam nostram curemus nostra. Sentiuntque sicut illi Sophoniae 1. Non faciet bene dominus, non faciet male. Propterea rapiuntur ad opes ad gloriam parandam &c. Item cum nosse deum, sit scire quod faciat misericordiam & iudicium, Hieremiae 9. vides quod iudicet peccatum, & misereatur credentium. Illinec iram eius, nec misericordiam eius concipiunt: ideo nec metuunt, nec credunt, sed fidunt sibiipsis. Econtra pii norunt deum facere misericordiam & iudicium, id est fideli corde, & vere credunt quod odio habeat peccata, & peccata esse omnia opera humana: & rursum vere credunt, quia misereatur eorum qui se illi credunt, ideo timent. Item de manu eius omnia bona in vita, morte, omnibus temporibus & casibus expectant. Sic definit notitiam sui psalmo 77. Narrabunt filiis suis, ut ponant in deo spem suam, & non obliviscantur operum dei, & mandata eius exquirant.

## 8:55
You have not known him.

Namely, knowing neither his judgment nor his mercy.

Latin:

Non cognovistis eum.

Scilicet neque iudicium, neque misericordiam eius scientes.

## 8:55
And if I shall say.

Because faith works confession.

Latin:

Et si dixero.

Quia fides operatur confessionem.

## 8:56
Abraham rejoiced that he might see my day.

That is, the revelation of me: because he desired consolation from the promise concerning Christ, that is, concerning the remission of sins.

Latin:

Abraham exultavit, ut videret diem meum.

Id est revelationem mei: quia desyderavit consolationem ex promissione de Christo, id est de remissione peccatorum.

## 8:56
And he saw, and was glad.

Namely, he received consolation, therefore he is not dead.

Latin:

Et vidit, atque gavisus est.

Scilicet accepit consolationem, ergo non mortuus est.

## 9:1 Argument
CHAPTER NINE.

Because the law promises good things to the good, and threatens sad things to the evil. Reason gathers thus. This man is afflicted, therefore he is a sinner, and rejected by God. This man is fortunate, therefore he is pious: and I am dear to God, because I am fortunate God hates me, because He afflicts me. And when he sees openly that all things are favorable to the wicked, and that the better are afflicted, he condemns the judgment of God, and denies that human affairs are governed by the providence of God. In Ecclesiastes, Solomon touches upon the same arguments by reason, cap. 9. This is the most important thing among all that are done under the sun: because the same things happen to all. Whence also the hearts of the children of men are filled with malice and contempt in their life, and after these things they shall be brought down to the underworld. And the friends of Job argue: You are a sinner, therefore you are afflicted. In this opinion, and in this place, the disciples also ask who sinned, whether the blind man himself, or his parents. Because in the first commandment God threatens that He will take vengeance unto the third and fourth generation. This passage is memorable and for confirming faith, so that we may not despair in affliction, because the poor are evangelized: and so that we may not allow ourselves to be moved from our calling, when we see before our eyes the wealthy and the famous, whom weak minds desire to be like. Because of this scandal of the cross, the psalms and the prophets often offer consolation. Psalmo 4. Many say, who will show us good things? Psalmo 72. Behold, these are sinners, and abounding in the world they have obtained riches. And I said: Therefore I have justified my heart without cause. On account of this, Christ answers: Neither did this man sin, nor his parents: not that they did not have sin simply. For that is not what is being asked here.

And sin is argued below in the parents. But not for the reason that they sinned is this man blind: by the will of God the cross was imposed upon him, and so that God might be glorified through him. Here Christ caused the cause, not his sin, teaching that we should patiently sustain the will of God: and that they might thus bear it, that temptation works fruit in them, because it follows: The works of God are made manifest in those who endure: that is, in this the saints are tempted, so that from temptation they might know the will of God. Rom. 5. Affliction produces patience, patience probation, that is, the knowledge of the divine will. And to those who love God all things work together for good.

Latin:

CAPUT NONUM.

Quia lex promittit bona bonis, & minatur tristia malis. Ratio sic colligit. Hic affligitur, ergo peccator est, et à deo reiectus. Hic fortunatus est, ergo pius est: & deo charus sum, quia fortunatus sum. Deus odit me, quia affligit. Et cum videt palàm malis omnia secunda esse, meliores affligi, damnat iudicium dei, negat dei providentia res humanas regi. In Ecclesiasti Salomon eadem argumenta ratione perstringit, cap. 9. Hoc potissimum est inter omnia quae sub sole fiunt: quia eadem cunctis eveniunt. Unde & corda filiorum hominum implentur malitia & contemptu in vita sua, & post haec ad inferos deducentur. Et amici Iob argumentantur: Peccator es, ergo affligeris. In hanc sententiam & hoc loco quaerunt discipuli, quis peccaverit, ipse ne caecus, an parentes. Quia primo praecepto minatur deus, vindicaturum se in tertiam & quartam generationem. Memorabilis hic locus est & ad confirmandam fidem, ne in afflictione desperemus, quia pauperes Evangelizantur: & ne sinamus dimoveri nos à vocatione nostri, cum videmus ob oculos positos opulentos, celebres, quorum similes esse cupiunt infirmae mentes. Popter hoc scandalum crucis, saepe psalmi et prophetae consolantur. Psalmo 4. Multi dicunt, quis ostendet nobis bona? Psalmo 72. Ecce ipsi peccatores & abundantes in seculo obtinuerunt divitias. Et dixi: Ergo sine causa iustificavi cor meum. Propterea Christus respondet: Neque hic peccavit, neque parentes eius: non quod simpliciter non habuerint peccatum. Nam id hic non quaeritur.

Et peccatum infra in parentibus arguitur. Sed non ideo quia peccaverint, hic caecus est: dei voluntate illi imposita crux est, & ut glorificetur deus per ipsum. Hic causam, non ipsius peccatum causavit Christus, docens ut patienter sustineamus voluntatem dei: & quo sic ferant, operari in illis tentationem fructum, quia sequitur: Manifesta fiunt opera dei in tolerantibus: id est in hoc tentari sanctos, ut ex tentatione cognoscant voluntatem dei. Rom. 5. Afflictio patientiam, patientia probationem, id est cognitionem divinae voluntatis. Et diligentibus deum omnia cooperantur in bonum.

## 9:2
Who sinned?

Since the law punishes sinners, did these or his parents, so that he was born blind?

Latin:

Quis peccavit?

Siquidem lex punit peccatores, hi aut parentes eius, ut caecus nasceretur?

## 9:3
But that it might be manifested.

That is, for this reason he is affixed to the cross, so that the name of God might be glorified in him.

Latin:

Sed ut manifestetur.

Id est ideo cruci affigitur, ut nomen dei glorificetur in eo.

## 9:4
Night comes.

Namely, when I shall not appear, when the Gospel is not preached.

As long as I am in the world.

That is, as long as the Gospel is preached. Generally I judge the opinion to be that the day ought to be the Gospel of Christ, and the night to be the ignorance of the Gospel. For the Gospel is light, Isaiah 9. To them that dwell in the region of the shadow of death, light is risen to them. And John 1. The Word is light, etc. Night, or darkness, is the ignorance of the word of God, Luke 1. To enlighten them that sit in the shadow of death. Furthermore, what he says, that no one can work by night, what else does it signify, than that where the word of God is lacking, there we cannot perform the works of God? For by the word of the Lord they moved their camps, Numbers 9. And Psalm 106. He sent his word, and healed them. Isaiah 8. And if they speak not according to this word, it shall be because there is no morning light in them. Therefore, whatever reason can do, not being illuminated by the word of God, is condemned. The whole order of the Christian life is gathered from this history. First, temptation or affliction is a work of the divine will. Second, the fruit of temptation, that thence we may come into the knowledge of the glory of God. Third, this happens, the word working in us: because nature by itself cannot discern the glory of God in punishments. Fourth, faith is exercised, when we are led, so that we ourselves are ignorant of the counsel of the divine will, just as Joseph was led into Egypt, etc. Fifth, we who are reborn confess. Sixth, the enemies of Christ seek against his work and testimony. Seventh, of those who are Christ's, the faith is not equal. And they are weak. certain ones, whom nevertheless Christ does not despise.

Eighth, Christians are despised, like earthen vessels. Ninth, Christ alone judges concerning his word, he blinds those who see, and illuminates the blind. For he is a stone of stumbling, and a rock of scandal: Rom. 9.

Latin:

Venit nox.

Scilicet ubi non apparebo, cum non praedicatur Evangelium.

Quàm diu sum in mundo.

Id est quàm diu praedicatur Evangelium. Generali sententia iudico, diem esse oportere Evangelium Christi, Noctem esse ignorationem Evangelii. Evangelium enim lux est, Esaiae 9. Habitantibus in regione umbrae mortis, lux orta est eis. Et Ioannis 1. Verbum est lux &c. Nox, seu tenebrae, ignoratio verbi dei est, Lucae 1. Illuminare iis, qui in umbra mortis sedent. Porrò quod ait neminem posse nocte operari, quid aliud significat, quàm ubi deest verbum dei, ibi non posse nos opera dei operari? Per verbum enim domini movebant castra, Numerorum 9. Et Psalmo 106. Misit verbum suum, & sanavit eos. Esaiae 8. Quod si non dixerit iuxta verbum hoc, non erit eis matutina lux. Damnatur igitur quicquid ratio potest, non illuminata verbo dei. Totus ordo Christianae vitae ex hac historia colligitur. Primum, tentatio vel afflictio opus est divinae voluntatis. Secundo, fructus tentationis, ut inde veniamus in notitiam gloriae dei. Tertio, fit hoc, operante verbo in nobis: quia natura per sese non potest in poenis gloriam dei cernere. Quarto, exercetur fides, cum ducimur, ita ut ipsi ignoremus consilium voluntatis divinae, sicut Ioseph in Aegyptum ductus est &c. Quinto, renati confitemur. Sexto, inimici Christi requirunt adversus opus eius & testimonium. Septimo, eorum qui Christi sunt, non est pár fides. Et sunt infirmi quidam, quos tamen non aspernatur Christus.

Octavo, Christiani contemnuntur, sicut testacea vasa. Nono, Christus solus iudicat de verbo suo, excaecat videntes, & illuminat caecos. Est enim lapis offensionis, & petra scandali: Rom. 9.

## 9:24
We know that the man is a sinner.

Because they believed neither the scriptures, nor the testimony of John, nor the miracles.

Latin:

Nos scimus, quia homo peccator est.

Quia neque scripturis, neque testimonio Ioannis, neque miraculis credebant.

## 9:40
Are we also blind?

Namely, we who hold the law of God, and do it? Christ answers: If you were blind, that is, if you acknowledged your blindness, and wished to be illuminated by the light. Now because you illuminate yourselves, and follow your own reason, therefore you are blind.

Latin:

Nunquid & nos caeci sumus?

Scilicet qui tenemus legem dei, & facimus? Respondet Christus: Si caeci essetis, id est si cognosceretis caecitatem vestram, & velletis illuminari per lucem. Nunc quia ipsi vos illuminatis, & sequimini rationem vestram, ideo caeci estis.

## 10:1 Argument
CHAPTER TEN.

Augustine labors in connecting the beginning of this chapter with the end of the previous chapter, in this manner: That many are endowed with the justice of the law, such as those who had said: Are we also blind? who nevertheless because they do not believe, are not saved, because they do not enter through the door. Thus he. I, if you wish at all to connect, I think it thus coheres. The Pharisees profess that they hear and teach the word of God, and therefore are pastors: to them Christ opposes this, that he alone is the pastor, that he is the door: and that the Father can neither be taught nor known, unless one enters through Christ himself. And this is the sum of this parable (for it pertains not only to pastors, but also to the hearers of the word) he speaks of his own office, that he alone is the one who feeds, as if he were saying thus: You Pharisees feed, but with the depraved word of God: because you teach only human righteousness in the law, and you judge the righteousness of God to be carnal works, when both the law teaches the righteousness of God, and it cannot be known at all, unless I am feeding.

Latin:

CAPUT DECIMUM.

Augustinus laborat in convertendo huius capitis initio cum fine superioris capitis, in hunc modum: Multos esse iustitia legis praeditos, quales iifuerant qui dixerunt: Nun quid & nos caeci sumus? qui tamen quia non credant, non salventur, quia non ingrediantur per ostium. Sic ille. Ego, si omnino velis connectere, sic puto cohaerere. Pharisaei profitentur se audire & docere verbum dei, adeoque pastores esse: iis Christus hoc opponit, se solum esse pastorem, se esse ostium: nec doceri, nec cognosci posse patrem, nisi per se Christum intretur. Atque haec huius parabolae summa est (pertinet enim non ad pastores modo, sed & ad auditores verbi) de suo officio loquitur, quod ipse solus sit qui pascat, quasi sic dicat: Vos pharisaei pascitis, sed depravato verbo dei: quia humanam tantum iustitiam in lege docetis, & iudicatis iustitiam dei esse carnalia opera, cum & lex iustitiam dei doceat, & ea cognosci omno non possit, nisi me pascente.

## 10:8
All as many as came before me.

Namely, having entered through another door: likewise those who wish to be considered as shepherds, they are thieves. The comparison itself teaches of whom he speaks, when he says: All as many as came, namely of those who have not entered through the door, of whom he says above: He who climbs up another way, is a thief and a robber. Furthermore, not to enter through this door is to bring another word: because the door is that by which the Father is revealed: but he is revealed by the word, because not to enter through this door is to bring another word. Likewise, because he compares himself with the shepherds, he speaks of them when he says: All who came &c. who wished to be considered as shepherds, they are thieves and robbers. In two ways, first, because they felt their own doctrine was sufficient for salvation, that it was that which fed, when nevertheless there is no understanding of the word, unless Christ opens, and as it were unlocks the door. Secondly, because they also devised new traditions, which they thought contributed to the procuring of salvation. Of which kind there were also among us thieves, who taught that this work, this food, that order contributed to salvation.

Latin:

Omnes quotquot ante me venerunt.

Scilicet per aliud ostium ingressi: item qui se pro pastoribus haberi volunt, illi fures sunt. Ipsa comparatio docet, de quibus loquatur, cum inquit: Omnes quotquot venerunt, de his scilicet qui non sunt ingressi per ostium, de quibus supra dicit: Qui aliunde ascendit, fur & latro est. Porró non ingredi per hoc ostium, est afferre aliud verbum: quia ostium est quo revelatur pater: verbo autem revelatur, quia non ingredi per hoc ostium, est aliud verbum afferre. Item quia se cum pastoribus confert, de iis loquitur, cum ait: Omnes qui venerunt &c. qui se pro pastoribus haberi voluerunt, ii fures & latrones sunt. Dupliciter, primum, quia sentiebant suam doctrinam satis esse ad salutem, eam esse quae pasceret, cum tamen non sit intellectus verbi, nisi aperiente Christo, & uclut recludente ostium. Secundo, quia novas etiam traditiones comminiscebantur, quas putabant ad parandam salutem conducere. Cuiusmodi & apud nos fuerunt latrones, qui hoc opus, hunc cibum, illum ordinem docuerunt conducere ad salutem.

## 10:8
Thieves & robbers.

Because this impious doctrine does this first, that it may deceive. 2. Corinth. 11. As the serpent seduced Eve by craftiness &c. Because every human doctrine preaches works, praises human powers, promises salvation in those things in which you may not find it, strengthens the hands of the impious, saddens the heart of the just: Ezechiel 13.

Latin:

Fures & latrones.

Quia primum hoc agit impia doctrina, ut decipiat. 2. Corinth. 11. Sicut serpens Evam seduxit astutia &c. Quia omnis doctrina humana praedicat opera, laudatuires humanas, promittit salutem in iis in quibus non muenias, confortat manus impiorum, contristat cor iusti: Ezechielis 13.

## 10:8
And the sheep did not hear them.

Namely because no one snatches them from the hand of the shepherd Christ.

Latin:

Et non audierunt eos oves.

Scilicet quia nemo rapit eas de manu pastoris Christi.

## 10:9
He who does not enter through the door.

Christ himself explains the parable. The first part: He who does not enter through the door: he explains thus: I am the door, just as also to the disciples asking the way to the Father: he answered that he is the way. Thus here he calls himself the door and the doorkeeper, because he himself is the one who reveals or shows the Father to believers. Just as also above Christ is called the light: because he illuminates men, so here the door, because he reveals the Father. The antithesis will explain the matter more clearly.

The Pharisees teach and hear the law, and yet the face of Moses is veiled. They teach, learn, and do the righteousness of the world, or of men: and they judge that they have satisfied the divine law by that carnal simulation of works. They wish likewise to make satisfaction for sins through works, but they are deceived. Those, however, who acknowledge sin and believe in Christ, they make satisfaction without works; they are truly justified. To them indeed the mercy of the Father is revealed. As Paul says 2. Corinth. 3. When he shall have turned to the Lord, the veil shall be taken away. Therefore this is the meaning: I am the door, that is, I reveal the Father: and this is what he adds afterwards: I am the door, by me if any man shall enter in, that is, if I shall have opened or dispelled the darkness for anyone, he shall enter in, namely by faith: and he shall go out, namely by confession, by teaching, and by all good works. And he shall find pasture, that is, the word of life.

Latin:

Qui non ingreditur per ostium.

Parabolam Christus ipse exponit. Primam partem: Qui non ingreditur per ostium: sic exponit: Ego sum ostium, sicut & discipulis interrogantibus viam ad patrem: respondit se esse viam. Ita hic sese vocat ostium & ostiarium, quod ipse sit qui revelet seu ostendat patrem credentibus. Sicut et supra Christus lux vocatur: quia illuminat homines, ita hic ostium, quia revelat patrem. Anthithesis explicabit rem clarius.

Pharisaei legem docent & audiunt, & tamen Moy sivultus velatus est. Iustitiam mundi, seu hominum docent, discunt, & faciunt: & iudicaut se satisfecisse legi divinae illa carnali simulatione operum. Volunt item pro peccatis per opera satisfacere, sed falluntur. Qui agnoscunt autem peccatum, & credunt in Christum, illi sine operibus satisfaciunt; vere iustificantur. Illis vero reuclatur misericordia patris. Sicut ait Paulus 2. Corinth. 3. Cum conversus fuerit ad dominum, auferetur velamen. Est ergo haec sententia: Ego sum ostium, id est revelo patrem: & hoc est quod postea subiicit: Ego sum ostium, per me si quis introierit, id est si cui reclusero vel discussero tenebras, ingredietur, scilicet fide: & egredietur, scilicet confessione, docendo, & omnibus bonis operibus. Et pascus inveniet, id est verbum vitae.

## 10:10
The thief does not come, except to steal.

That is, to deceive, that the deceived may perish.

Latin:

Fur non venit, nisi ut furetur.

Id est, ut decipiat, decepti pereant.

## 10:10
I have come, that they may have life.

That is, that they may be blessed with every spiritual blessing, and be fattened. Thus far the first part of the comparison has been proposed. First: The good shepherd enters through the door. Adaptation: I myself am the door, and the shepherd. The door, because I reveal the Father. The shepherd, because by the word I justify, I give life, I feed, etc. Antithesis: He who does not enter through the door is a thief. Adaptation: They came who did not enter through the door, they therefore were thieves. Summary: Only the word of Christ feeds, gives life, nor does the word give life, unless Christ himself opens the door, and feeds.

Latin:

Ego veni, ut vitam habeant.

Id est benedicantur omni benedictione spirituali, & saginentur. Hactenus prima pars collationis proposita est. Primum: Pastor bonus ingreditur per ostium. Aptatio: Ego ipse sum ostium, & pastor. Ostium, quia revelo patrem. Pastor, quia verbo iustifico, vivifico, pasco &c. Antithesis: Qui non ingreditur per ostium, latro est. Aptatio: Venerunt qui non ingressi sunt per ostium, ii ergo latrones fuerunt. Summa: Solum verbum Christi pascit, vivificat, neque vivificat verbum, nisi ipso Christo recludente ostium, & pascente.

## 10:11
I am the good shepherd.

The second part of the parable: Just as the good shepherd buys the salvation of the sheep at the danger of his own life, so also I lay down my soul for the sheep. I said above that Christ speaks of his own proper and perpetual office, namely that he himself truly feeds and gives life. Therefore here he makes mention of his death, by which he buys life for the sheep, having been made for them sin and a curse, so that sin and the curse might be emptied. And this passage is a most ample consolation for believers, where Christ testifies that he keeps watch for the sheep, lest at any time the wolf should rush in: so that they may believe more confidently in their own conscience, both in death, and in other tribulations that the shepherd is present. Ezekiel 34. The Lord promises in the same manner that he himself will shepherd the sheep, etc. As a shepherd visits his flock. And these things indeed are said concerning the proper office of Christ who saves. And that we might know he would save, he testified by death: he left it as a true pledge of his will. Now, whoever wishes to add all ministers of the word to Christ, let him observe here that death is a sign of the apostolate. But I, in the meantime, will expound concerning the proper office of Christ. He is a hireling who seeks his own.

Latin:

Ego sum pastor bonus.

Secunda pars parabolae: Sicut bonus pastor salutem ovium periculo vitae suae emit, ita & ego animam meam pro ovibus pono. Dixi supra, Christum de suo proprio & perpetuo officio loqui, scilicet quod ipse vere pascat & vivificet. Propterea hic mentionem facit mortis suae, qua emit vitam ovibus, factus pro eis peccatum & maledictum, ut peccatum & maledictio evacuaretur. Estque hic locus amplissima consolatio credentibus, ubi Christus se testatur excubare pro ovibus, ne quando irruat lupus: ut credant confidentius conscientiae sibi, tum in morte, tum in aliis aerum nis praesentem pastorem esse. Ezechielis 34. promittit ad eundem modum dominus sese pasturum oves &c. Sicut visitat pastor gregem suum. Et haec quidem de proprio Christi officio dicuntur qui servat. Et ut sciremus servaturum, morte testatus est: tanquam verum pignus voluntatis suae reliquit. Nunc qui omnes ministros verbi vult Christo addere, ille hic observet, mortem esse signum apostolatus. Sed ego interim de proprio Christi officio exponam. Mercenarius est qui sua quaerit.

## 10:14
And I know my own, and my own know me.

The third part of the parable has a place most worthy of observation, that the shepherd knows the sheep, and the sheep in turn know him. First, that Christ knows the sheep commends his solicitude for us for the sheep: and that he calls them by name pertains to this, that he calls sheep otherwise in other ways; these are the distinctions of gifts, of vocations, of offices, which he himself distributes according to his own will. Second, that the sheep know him, this signifies that the sheep have the testimony of their own heart, and truly believe that this is truly Christ the shepherd and life-giver, and in the word of Christ are able to cry out: Abba Father, this is what Isaiah says: All θεοδίδακτοι.

And Jeremiah: They shall all know me, from the least even to the greatest. You see, however, that this part cannot be applied to the remaining ministers of the word, but pertains properly to Christ. For it follows: As the Father knows me, and I know the Father. Not as the world knows either me or the Father. But the Father knows me to be the Son, therefore He glorifies me as the Son, and shows me to others to be the Son, and I in turn glorify the Father etc. so the sheep know and glorify me the Son. Just as the Father shows me to them, so also the sheep know the Father.

Latin:

Et ego cognosco meas, & cognoscunt me meae.

Tertia pars parabolae locum habet dignissimum observatione, quod pastor oves novit, & illum vicissim oves norunt. Primum, quod Christus novit oves, commendat suam solicitudinem nobis pro ovibus: & quod vocat eas nominatim, pertinet ad hoc quod aliàs oves aliter vocat, discrimina sunt donorum, vocationum, officiorum, quae ipse pro suo arbitrio partitur. Secundo, quod oves noverunt ipsum, hoc significat oves habere testimonium cordis sui, & vere credere quod hic vere sit Christus pastor & vivificator, & in verbo Christi posse clamare: Abba pater, hoc est quod Esaias inquit: Omnes θεοδίδακτοι.

Et Hieremias: Cognoscent me omnes à minimo usque ad maximum. Vides autem hanc partem non posse vulgari in reliquos ministros verbi, sed proprie ad Christum pertinere. Nam sequitur: Sicut novit me pater, & ego agnosco patrem. Non sicut mundus novit vel me, vel patrem. Sed pater novit me esse filium, propterea glorificat me tanquam filium, & ostendit me aliis esse filium, & ego vicissim glorifico patrem &c. sic oves norunt & glorificent me filium. Quemadmodum pater ostendit me eis, sic etiam oves norunt patrem.

## 10:16
And other sheep I have.

That is, the churches of the Gentiles. He adds the fourth part, whence the sheep are, after he has said enough about feeding.

Latin:

Et alias oves habeo.

Id est gentium ecclesias. Quartam partem subiicit, unde sint oves, posteaquam de pascendo satis multa dixit.

## 10:16
Which are not of this fold.

That is, from Israel according to the flesh. For in Christ all nations had to be blessed, Genes. 12.

Latin:

Quae non sunt ex hoc ovili.

Id est ex Israël secundum carnem. Nam in Christo oportuit benedici omnes gentes, Genes. 12.

## 10:16
And them I must bring.

Because it is by Christ's power that they believe. For God gives the increase, as 1. Corinth. 3

Latin:

Et illas oportet me adducere.

Quia Christi potentia fit ut credant. Deus enim dat incrementum, ut 1. Corinth. 3

## 10:16
And they shall hear my voice.

Namely, although through other ministers, yet it is Christ's voice that renews and illuminates those who hear.

Latin:

Et vocem meam audient.

Scilicet quanquam per alios ministros, tamen Christi vox est quae innovat & illuminat audientes.

## 10:16
And there shall be one fold and one shepherd.

The Church gathered from Jews and Gentiles through mercy. One shepherd is Christ, who feeds and renews the hearts of all believers. Because He spoke a little before about the bringing in of the Gentiles, it is necessary that this sentence be understood: There shall be one fold and one shepherd, concerning the Church, already gathered from the Gentiles and Jews. The sentence began to be fulfilled when the Gospel was first preached to the Gentiles: and it must needs be fulfilled in that manner, just as it began, until the end of the world. I say these things, lest anyone imagine that there still remains some new state of the Church, into which the Gentiles might gather, and assemble by an external rite. Otherwise, if the prophecy has not yet begun to be fulfilled, Christ has not been the shepherd until now. And because Christ's pasture and kingdom is not a visible or corporal kingdom, but a kingdom of faith, that is, illuminating and renewing hearts by the knowledge of God, which is faith, which knowledge or faith, because it arises from the promise or word concerning Christ. Therefore, the kingdom of faith is properly called the kingdom of Christ, which lasts from the time the Gospel was promulgated until the saints have been glorified. For then the kingdom shall be delivered to the Father, that is, it will no longer be a kingdom of faith, of knowing the Father through the word of Christ, but it will be a kingdom of glory when we shall know the Father just as we have been known. First, Christ says that he is the door and the doorkeeper, who reveals the Father: therefore reason does not reveal the Father. Item, that he is the shepherd, who feeds, and does not kill.

Secondly, that by his death he redeemed life for the sheep, and by his death testifies that he is solicitous for the sheep. Now if he himself redeemed us by his death, therefore works do not redeem, but we are justified by faith. Thirdly, that he knows the sheep, and in turn is known thus, just as the Father reveals him. Fourthly, that the sheep are the Gentiles and the Jews.

Latin:

Et fiet unum ovile & unus pastor.

Ecclesia ex Iudaeis & gentibus per misericordiam collecta. Unus pastor Christus, qui corda omnium credentium pascit & renovat. Quia de adducendis gentibus paulo ante locutus est, necesse est intelligi hanc sententiam: Erit unum ovile & unus pastor, de ecclesia, iam collecta ex gentibus & Iudaeis. Coepit impleri sententia, quum primum est Evangelium in gentes praedicatum: implerique ad eum modum necesse est, sicut coepit, ad finem usque mundi. Haec dico, ne quis imaginetur adhuc superesse novum quendam ecclesiae statum, in quem gentes coëant, & externo ritu conveniant. Alioqui si non dum coepta est impleri prophetia, non fuit hactenus pastor Christus. Et quia Christi pascua, & regnum non est visibile vel corporale regnum, sed regnum fidei, hoc est illuminans & renovans corda cognitione dei, quae fides est, quae cognitio seu fides, quia è promissione seu verbo de Christo oritur. Igitur regnum fidei proprie regnum Christi dicitur, quod à promulgato Evangelio durat, donec glorificati fuerint sancti. Tum enim tradetur regnum patri, id est iam non erit regnum fidei, cognoscendi patris per verbum Christi, sed erit regnum gloriae, cum patrem cognoscemus quemadmodum cogniti sumus. Primo, Christus ait se esse ostium & ostiarium, qui revelet patrem: ergo ratio non revelet patrem. Item se esse pastorem, qui pascat, non occidat.

Secundo, se morte sua vitam ovibus redemisse, & morte testari, quod solicitus sit pro ovibus. Nunc si ipse morte sua nos redemit, ergo non redimunt opera, sed ex fide iustificamur. Tertio, quod cognoscat oves, & vicissim sic cognoscatur, sicut pater eum revelat. Quarto, Oves esse gentes & Iudaeos.

## 10:17
Therefore the Father loves me.

Namely, for this reason he made me a shepherd, because I lay down my soul, that is, because I shall die, for that reason he glorifies. According to that Psalm 109 He shall drink of the torrent in the way, therefore he shall lift up his head. Item Psalm 68 It shall please God more than a young calf. And Isaiah 53 If he shall lay down his soul for sin, he shall see a long-lived seed, that is, he shall reign forever, and the will of the Lord shall be directed in his hand. Moreover, he describes in this place his kingdom, what sort it is to be, namely such, that he is to suffer, and because he is obedient to the Father, it will come to pass that he will glorify him and make him king, and that he will glorify him when he shall have risen. For the Jews were hoping that the Messiah would reign carnally and in the manner of the world. Wherefore, just as above he rejects the carnal sheep of Israel, so here too he teaches that the kingdom is to be spiritual.

Latin:

Propterea me diligit pater.

Scilicet ideo me pastorem fecit, quia pono animam meam, id est quia moriar, ideo glorificat. Iuxta illud Psalmi 109. De torrente in via bibet, propterea exaltabit caput. Item Psalmo 68. Placebit deo super vitulum novellum. Et Esaiae 53. Si posuerit pro peccato animam suam, videbit semen longaevum, id est in aeternum regnabit, & voluntas domini in manu eius dirigetur. Describit autem hoc loco regnum suum, quale id futurum sit, tale scilicet, se passurum esse, & quia sit patri obediens, futurum ut eum glorificet & regem faciat, esseque glorificaturum cum resurrexerit. Nam Iudaei sperabant, carnaliter & mundi more regnaturum Messiam. Quare sicut supra reiicit oves Israël carnales, ita hic quoque docet regnum spirituale futurum esse.

## 10:17
And I will take it up again.

That is, I will rise again. First, you will observe here that just as Christ was glorified through the cross, so it is necessary that we also become like the image of the son of God, Rom. 8. Death and all afflictions are instruments by which glory is prepared for the sons of God. Second, he who in temptation feels that he obeys the will of God by suffering, his heart will rest and will keep the sabbath: just as Christ says that he is to suffer, not because the fury of the world compels this, but because it pleases the Father so. As Job said: As it pleased the Lord, so it is done: Thus says Christ, that he is to suffer because it seems so to the loving Father. Which sense and affection is truly the faith of one committing himself to the mercy and good will of the Father as Psalm 15. I foresaw the Lord always in my sight &c. And 25. For thy mercy is before my eyes, and I have been well pleased in thy truth &c. In these words: I will lay down, I will take up, the arguments of the divinity of Christ are brought forth. But besides this, see how he teaches us in temptations to look not at external force, but at the will of God: as if he were saying thus: Even if the gates of hell invade, nevertheless by the divine will I will lay down, I will receive my soul. However they may rage the gates of hell, let us feel thus: Let the world persecute, yet we shall be afflicted only so far as the divine will allows, not so far as the world wishes.

Latin:

Et iterum sumam eam.

Id est resurgam. Primum hic observabis, quod sicut per crucem Christus est glorificatus, ita oportet & nos similes fieri imaginis filii dei, Rom. 8. Mors & omnes afflictiones, sunt instrumenta quibus paratur gloria filiis dei. Secundo, Qui in tentatione sentit se voluntate dei parere patiendo, illius cor quiescet, et sabbatizabit: sicuti Christus dicit se passurum esse, non quia hoc mundi furor cogat, sed quod sic patri placeat. Sicut Iob dicebat: Sicut domino placuit, ita factum est: Sic inquit Christus, ideo se passurum esse, quia ita videatur amanti patri. Qui sensus & affectus vere fides est committentis se misericordiae & bonae voluntati patris ut Psalmo 15. Providebam dominum in conspectu meo semper &c. Et 25. Quoniam misericordia tua ante oculos meos est, & complacui in veritate tua &c. In iis verbis: Ego ponam, ego sumam, prodita sunt argumenta divinitatis Christi. Sed praeter hoc vide, quomodo doceat nos in tentationibus spectare non externam vim, sed voluntatem dei: quasi sic dicat: Esto invadant portae inferorum, tamen divina voluntate ponam, recipiam animam. Utut furiant portae inferorum, sic nos sentiamus: Esto persequatur mundus, eatenus tamen affligemur, quatenus divina voluntas sinet, non quatenus vult mundus.

## 10:18
This commandment I have received from my father.

That is, this precept, although the power is in my hands, yet it is from the father. When the Jews had heard this, a dissension was made again, that is, upon the preaching of the Gospel being heard, concerning the fact that Christ alone is the shepherd, the only one who reveals the father.

Latin:

Hoc mandatum accepi à patre meo.

Id est hoc praeceptum, quamquam penes me sit potestas, tamen à patre est. Hoc cum audierant Iudaei, dissentio iterum facta est, id est audita praedicatione Evangelii, de eo quod solus Christus pastor sit, solusis qui revelet patrem.

## 10:19
And the were taking place.

ἐγκαίνια can be called new things. ἐγκαινίζειν signifies to renew, it was the month of November.

Latin:

Facta sunt autem ἐγκαίνια.

ἐγκαίνια novalia dici possunt. ἐγκαινίζειν innovare significat, erat mensis Novembris.

## 10:20
Many were saying, he has a demon.

This is what the Apostle says, that the Gospel is a scandal to the Jews, that is, to the holy ones holding forth the word of God. For they say that the preaching of the word of God is from the devil. But to the Gentiles the Gospel is foolishness. For what is more foolish to reason than that human ordinances, the works of all men, should be condemned, that public tranquility should be disturbed? &c. There are also those who say: These words are not of one having a demon, because the sheep recognize the word, & bear witness concerning the word.

Latin:

Multi dicebant, daemonium habet.

Hoc est quod Apostolus dicit, Iudaeis scandalo esse Evangelium, id est sanctis prae se ferentibus verbum dei. Nam ii praedicationem verbi dei ex diabolo esse dicunt. Gentibus autem stultitia est Evangelium. Quid est enim stultius rationi, quàm ordinationes humanas, omnium hominum opera damnari, tranquillitatem publicam turbare? &c. Sunt item qui dicunt: Haec verba non sunt daemonium habentis, quia oves agnoscunt verbum, & testificantur de verbo.

## 10:25
I speak to you, and you do not believe.

Because I have not come as such, as you desired: I do not establish this bodily kingdom of yours, I condemn your impiety, I condemn your works and your righteousnesses, although they may seem to be done according to the law.

Latin:

Loquor vobis, & non creditis.

Quia non veni talis, qualem vos desyderabatis: non stabilio hoc corporale regnum vestrum, condemno impietatem vestram, condemno opera & iustitias vestras, quanquam illae videantur secundum legem fieri.

## 10:25
The works that I do, they bear witness.

Namely that I am Christ, that you are not of the sheep, but of Satan. For no one comes to me, unless the Father has drawn him.

Latin:

Opera quae ego facio, testimonium perhibent.

Scilicet quod sum Christus, quod vos non estis ex ovibus, sed ex satana. Nemo enim ad me venit, nisi pater traxerit eum.

## 10:28
I give them eternal life.

And thus the glory of God, wisdom, righteousness, etc.

Latin:

Vitam aeternam do eis.

Adeoque gloriam dei, sapientiam, iustitiam &c.

## 10:30
I and the Father are one.

Therefore neither will my sheep be able to be snatched from my hands: because the Father's power is my power, and mine is the Father's: Micheae 5. He shall stand and feed in the strength of the Lord, in the majesty of the name of the Lord his God.

Latin:

Ego & pater unum sumus.

Ergo neque de meis manibus extorqueri poterunt oves meae: quia patris potentia mea potentia est, & mea patris: Micheae 5. Stabit et pascet in fortitudine domini, in sublimitate nominis domini dei sui.

## 10:34
Because I said you are gods.

Christ does not deny in this place, as it could have seemed to the Pharisees, that he is God by nature, but by a figure of speech he confounds the unbelieving Pharisees so that they might be blinded. And first indeed he clearly explained in that place that he is God by nature, when he says: Whom the Father has sanctified and sent into the world. Where he distinguished himself from the hearers of the word of God, and argued from the lesser: If the other hearers of the word are gods, how much more is he God who has been sanctified by the Father, that he might save, and sent that he might make the hearers of the word participants of the glory of God, according to the testimony of the prophets? Esaiae 9. The mighty God, the Father of the world to come, the Prince of peace. And 42. I have given my spirit upon him &c. that he may open the eyes of the blind. Item 61. The spirit of the Lord is upon me, that I might place strength for those who mourn. And Hieremiae 23. He shall execute judgment and justice &c. and this is the name which they shall call him, our just God. Then just as scripture proclaims that all things are subject to man on account of Christ: Item just as it says that man was created to the image and likeness: So certainly the appellation of God belongs to man on account of Christ, through whom we become like God, renewed by the light of his spirit, the wisdom, and the justice of God: as the Apostle 2. Corinth. 3. says: We are transformed from glory to glory as by the spirit of the Lord.

Latin:

Quia ego dixi dii estis.

Non negat Christus hoc loco, ut videri potuit pharisaeis, se natura deum esse, sed figura orationis incredulos pharisaeos confundit ut excaecentur. Et primum quidem in eo clare explicavit, se natura deum esse, cum inquit: Quem pater sanctificavit, & misit in mundum. Ubi se discrevit ab auditoribus verbi dei, argumentatusque est ex minore: Si dii sunt caeteri auditores verbi, quanto magis deus est is qui sanctificatus est à patre, ut salvet, & missus ut auditores verbi faceret participes gloriae dei, iuxta testimonium prophetarum? Esaiae 9. Deus fortis pater futuri seculi princeps pacis. Et 42. Dedi spiritum meum super eum &c. ut aperiat oculos caecorum. Item 61. Spiritus domini super me, ut ponerem fortitudinem lugentibus. Et Hieremiae 23. Faciet iudicium & iustitiam &c. & hoc est nomen quod vocabunt eum, deus iustus noster. Deinde sicut scriptura praedicat, omnia esse subiecta homini propter Christum: Item sicut dicit, creatum hominem ad imaginem & similitudinem: Ita certe appellatio dei competit homini propter Christum, per quem similes dei fimus, renovati spiritus eius luce, sapientia, & iustitia dei: ut Apostolus 2. Corinth. 3. inquit: Transformamur à claritate in claritatem tanquam à domini spiritu.

## 11:1 Argument
CHAPTER ELEVENTH.

In the history of the resurrected Lazarus, we do not seek an allegory, so that by the death of Lazarus the death of the soul is represented, and other things of this kind. But the deed itself must be considered, and first indeed. Because by death we are punished with all the evils of the world, according to that Genesis 2. In whatever day you shall eat from it, you shall die the death. And Genesis 3. In labors you shall eat from it etc. It is necessary that we understand these evils, but we shall not understand them except in Christ. Secondly, Christ is promised, who might free us from all these evils, and give eternal life: therefore concerning death itself he now pronounces in a new way, saying, that death is not death, that sickness is not sickness. And so that he might testify to this, he does not only teach by his own resurrection that death has been emptied, but also by others being resurrected. He shows the same in other human calamities, as for instance concerning poverty, Matthew 16. concerning the forgotten loaves etc. Thirdly, when he says: This sickness is not unto death, but so that the son of God might be glorified through it. The Law feels that these are punishments, Christ on the contrary pronounces them to be instruments, by which God might be glorified. For he wishes to be glorified in our infirmity against the strong things of the world. 1. Corinthians 1. The foolish things of the world, he has chosen God, that he might confound the wise, has chosen the weak things of this world, etc. You see therefore that the cross is an instrument of the glory of God. And therefore, let each one bear the cross imposed upon him according to his calling, in such a way as if it were an instrument by which God is to be glorified, and let him serve his calling: and let his eyes see straight, that is, let him proceed straight, and not decline to those things which are seen by the flesh. Fourthly, that will not happen unless Christ goes before, as a pillar of cloud and fire, that is, unless you have been raised up and animated by the Word Christ, so that you may feel that you are an instrument of the glory of God, you will not be able to endure, because he says here: I am the resurrection and the life: he who believes in me, even if he has died, shall live. Here reason, here human works accomplish nothing.

Fifthly, the flesh seeks what are good works, not understanding that these afflictions imposed upon us by God are truly good works, when they are borne with faith. Therefore it feigns various forms of life, having neglected its calling, etc., now it undertakes this, now that. For thus says Isaiah: We have all gone astray. Sixthly, the sum of sums is transacted by this word, by which life is promised. I am the resurrection and the life, etc., is added to the testimony of the resurrected Lazarus, so that just as you see Lazarus to have been resurrected, so you may not doubt that by the power of the same word you also are to be resurrected and saved. Moreover, the affections of Christ are also to be considered, so that from thence you may know his will. First, he himself went of his own accord into Judea, not yet asked by the little women. Second, when he arrived at the little women, they, as if the matter were hopeless, asked for nothing. Third, he is moved by a bitter and vehement affection of a certain indignation against the kingdom of death, and by pity for us, so that he weeps. Fourth, he again challenges the unbelief of Martha, Matthew 9. when he was raising the daughter of the synagogue ruler, he was laughed at, even by the flute players, that is, by the Pharisees and scribes. Fifth, he himself teaches why these things are done, namely so that it might be believed, and thus so that the unbelievers might be confounded, and the believers might be confirmed.

Latin:

CAPUT UNDECI-MUM.

IN historia resuscitati Lazari, non requiremus allegoriam, ut Lazari morte repraesentetur animae mors, & hoc genus alia. Sed factum ipsum consyderandum est, & primum quidem. Quia morte omnibus malis mundi mulctamur, iuxta illud Genesis 2. In quacunque die comederitis ex eo, morte moriemini. Et Genesis 3. In laboribus comedes ex ea &c. Oportet intelligamus haec mala, non intelligemus autem nisi in Christo. Secundo, Christus promissus est, qui liberet nos ab iis malis omnibus, & det vitam aeternam: propterea de morte ipsa iam novo modo pronunciat, dicens, mortem non esse mortem, morbum non esse morbum. Et ut hoc testetur, non ipse modo sua resurrectione docet mortem esse evacuatam, sed etiam aliis resuscitandis. Idem ostendit in aliis calamitatibus humanis, utpote de inopia, Matthaei 16. de oblitis panibus &c. Tertio, cum ait: Haec infirmitas non est ad mortem, sed ut glorificetur filius dei per ipsam. Lex sic sentit esse poenas, Christus econtra instrumenta esse pronunciat, quibus glorificetur deus. Vult enim glorificari in nostra infirmitate adversus fortia mundi. 1. Corinth. 1. Quae stulta sunt mundi, elegit deus, ut confundat sapientes, & infirma elegit huius mundi &c. Vides igitur crucem esse instrumentum gloriae dei. Proindeque cuique crux pro sua vocatione imposita est, eam sic ferat, tanquam instrumentum quo sit glorificandus deus, & serviat suae vocationi: & oculi eius recta videant, id est rectà pergat, nec declinet ad ea, quae carne videntur. Quarto, id non fiet nisi praecedente Christo, ceu columna nubis & ignis, id est nisi verbo Christo fueris erectus & animatus, ut sentias te gloriae dei instrumentum esse, non poteris durare, quia hic dicit: Ego sum resurrectio & vita: qui credit in me, etiam si mortuus fuerit vivet. Nihil hic ratio, nihil opera humana efficiunt.

Quinto, caro quaerit quae nam sint bona opera, non intelligens has afflictiones divinitus nobis impositas, vere bona opera esse, quum fide feruntur. Propterea fingit varias formas vitae, neglecta vocatione &c. iam hoc, iam illud orditur. Sic enim inquit Esaias: Omnes erravimus. Sexto, summa summarum hoc agitur verbo, quo promissa est vita. Ego sum resurrectio & vita &c. est additum testimonio resuscitati Lazari, ut sicut vides resuscitatum Lazarum esse, ita nihil dubites quin eiusdem verbi potentia tu quoque sis resuscitandus & servandus. Sunt autem & affectus Christi consyderandi, ut inde voluntatem eius cognoscas. Primum ipse ultro abiit in Iudaeam, nondum rogatus à mulierculis. Secundo cum ad mulierculas pervenit, iam illae ceu desperata re, nihil petunt. Tertio commovetur acerba & vehementi affectus indignatione quadam adversus regnum mortis, & commiseratione nostri, ita ut lachrymetur. Quarto iterum interpellat incredulitatem Marthae, Matthaei. 9. cum archisynagogi filiam suscitaret, ridebatur, etiam à tibicinibus, id est à pharisaeis & scribis. Quinto ipse docet, cur haec fiant, scilicet ut crederetur, adeoque ut confunderentur increduli, & confirmarentur credentes.

## 11:1
Now there was a certain man who was sick.

In affliction, either the love of God is felt, or there is doubt concerning the will of God toward us. The love of God is felt when, raised up by faith, we feel that we are being tested by the good will of God, and we hope that it will come to pass that the Father will end the affliction according to his mercy, according to Psalm 90. Because he has hoped in me, I will deliver him &c. And for those who feel in this way, the temptation is not a temptation. Nor indeed can the good will be known except in Christ, that is, unless the heart is confirmed by the word of the grace of Christ &c. On the other hand, those who doubt concerning the will of God toward them, they do not bear the temptation by faith, and they feel just as those did, Ezekiel 3.

The Lord does not see us, and has abandoned the earth, where the Lord rebukes those who are in despair.

Latin:

Erat autem quidam languens.

In afflictione aut sentitur amor dei, aut de voluntate dei erga nos dubitatur. Sentitur amor dei, cum fide erecti, sentimus bona voluntate dei nos tentari, speramusque fore ut pro misericordia sua afflictionem finiat pater, iuxta Psalmum 90. Quoniam in me speravit, liberabo cum &c. Et iis qui sic sentiunt, tentatio non est tentatio. Neque vero cognosci bona voluntas potest nisi in Christo, hoc est nisi corde per verbum gratiae Christi confirmato &c. Econtra qui de voluntate dei erga se dubitant, ii non ferunt fide tentationem, sentiuntque perinde atque illi, Ezechielis 3.

Non videt nos dominus, & reliquit terram, ubi dominus arguit desperabundos.

## 11:3
Lord, behold, he whom you love is ill.

This seems to have been the affection of weak women in this place. Lord, because you love, therefore save. For faith feels thus: because the Father loves, it will come to pass that He will not desert.

Latin:

Domine, ecce quem amas infirmatur.

Muliercularum hoc loco affectus hic fuisse videtur. Domine quia amas, ideo serva. Sic enim sentit fides: quia amat pater, futurum ut non deserat.

## 11:4
This illness is not unto death.

This is a new voice, a new preaching, according to that which He Himself says: The poor are evangelized. The Gospel is such a preaching, which teaches that afflictions, poverty, etc., are instruments of the glory of God, not a curse. The Law teaches that there are punishments and curses, death and all sad things, etc. Christ, with the law removed, changes punishments into instruments of salvation, and by death overcomes death: because God wishes to be glorified by our weakness against the strong things of the world. 1. Corinth. 1. Therefore He says, that the Son of God may be glorified through him, that is, that the faith of believers may be confirmed, and the unbelievers may be confounded.

Latin:

Infirmitas haec non est ad mortem.

Haec nova vox est, nova praedicatio, iuxta id quod ipse dicit: Pauperes Evangelizantur. Evangelium est eiusmodi praedicatio, quae docet afflictiones, paupertatem &c. esse instrumenta gloriae dei, non maledictionem. Lex docet esse poenas & maledictiones, mortem & omnia tristia &c. Christus sublata lege, mutat poenas in instrumenta salutis, & morte vincit mortem: quia vult deus infirmitate nostra adversus fortia mundi glorificari. 1. Corinth. 1. Ergo dicit, ut glorificetur filius dei per eum, id est ut confirmetur fides credentium, & confundantur increduli.

## 11:6
Then indeed He remained in the same place for two days.

But see how Christ acts with the afflicted; He hears the sick one, and does not hasten. Then He begins the journey First, when he understands that Lazarus is dead, this is what Paul Rom. 12. commands, to pray instantly, that is, even if we do not immediately obtain that which we seek, let us not despair, but let us continue to pray and hope, etc. For he wishes our faith to be exercised and strengthened. He wishes his gifts, which we seek, to be understood. He wishes his mercy to be acknowledged. Thus Habakkuk 2. It is still a vision for a time, and it will appear at length, in the end it will not lie. But Christ even delays for a while until Lazarus dies, so that he may show that there is hope for the saints even in the midst of death: which was also exhibited in Abel Genesis 4..

Latin:

Tunc quidem mansit in eodem loco duobus diebus.

Sed vide quomodo agat Christus cum afflicto, audit aegrotantem, nec festinat. Deinde iter ingreditur primum ubi intelligit extinctum esse Lazarum, hoc est quod Paulus Rom. 12. iubet, instanter orare, id est etiam si non consequamur subito id quod quaerimus, non desperemus, sed pergamus orare & sperare &c. Vult enim exerceri & corroborare fidem nostram. Vult intelligi dona sua quae petimus. Vult agnosci suam misericordiam. Sic Habacuk 2. Adhuc visus procul, & apparebit tandem, in finem non mentietur. Christus vero etiam differt tantisper dum emoriatur Lazarus, ut ostendat & in media morte sanctis spem esse: quod & in Abel Genesis 4. exhibitum est.

## 11:9
Are there not twelve hours in the day?

The day is not perpetual, that is, the time of the ministry of God is not perpetual.

Latin:

Nónne duodecim sunt horae diei?

Non est perpetuus dies, id est non est perpetuum tempus ministerii dei.

## 11:9
If anyone walks in the day, he does not stumble.

That is, while this time of my ministry lasts, it is not permitted for me to be idle. And serving my calling, I shall not offend, I shall not stumble, that is, whatever shall happen, it will happen by the good will of God, whom I serve. Here Christ warns that one must serve one's calling, just as also Rom. 12. and 1. Corinth. 12.. Secondly, he consoles those who suffer persecution while they serve their calling, as also 1. Peter 1. If it is necessary to be sorrowful for a little while. You have an example of apostolic weakness, such as there are many passim in the Gospel: by which our hearts ought to be confirmed, that we may believe that he tolerates sinners and the foolish. Likewise that, just as he himself was an Apostle, we too may learn to tolerate his infirmity in faith and morals, according to that Rom. 15. Let every one of us please his neighbor for his good to edification etc.

Latin:

Si quis ambulat in die, non offendit.

Id est dum hoc tempus ministerii mei durat, non licet mihi vacare. Et serviens meae vocationi, non offendam, non impingam, id est quicquid acciderit, bona voluntate dei accidet, cui servio. Hic Christus monet serviendum esse vocationi, sicut & Rom. 12. & 1. Corinth. 12. Secundo consolatur eos, qui patiuntur persecutionem, dum vocationi suae serviunt, ut & 1. Petri 1. Paulisper si opus est contristari. Exemplum habes infirmitatis apostolicae, qualia sunt multa passim in Evangelio: quibus corda nostra confirmari oportet, ut credamus eum tolerare peccatores & insipientes. Item ut sicut ipse Apostolus fuit, discamus & nos suam infirmitatem in fide & moribus tolerare, iuxta illud Rom. 15. Unusquisque nostrum placeat proximo in bonum ad aedificationem &c.

## 11:15
That you may believe.

That the faith of all who believe and perceive may be confirmed, even in the midst of death, that the Lord is present.

Latin:

Ut credatis.

Ut confirmetur fides omnium credentium & cernentium etiam in media morte dominum praesto esse.

## 11:16
Let us also go that we may die.

Just as Peter's boldness and fall: so also his boldness is exhibited in this place, and his incredulity below, that we may learn that it is not of our own strength to believe. Thus also 2. Corinth. 4. That the sublimity of God may be not of us.

Latin:

Eamus & nos ut moriamur.

Sicut Petri audacia & lapsus: ita & huius audacia hoc loco, & incredulitas infra exhibetur, ut discamus non esse nostrarum virium credere. Sic & 2. Corinth. 4. Ut sit sublimitas dei non ex nobis.

## 11:19
Many of the Jews.

That is, who all testify concerning the deed.

Latin:

Multi ex Iudaeis.

Id est qui omnes testificantur de facto.

## 11:20
Martha meets him, but Mary was sitting at home.

As in the Gospel of Luke, Martha and Mary are compared: So here too, representing work and life, she comes to meet him. Mary sits at home, nor does she go out to meet him, before she was called.

Latin:

Martha occurrit illi, Maria autem do-mi sedebat.

Sicut in Evangelio Lucae, conferuntur Martha & Maria: Ita hic quoque repraesentans operam & vitam venit obviam. Maria domi sedet, nec exit obviam, priusquam evocaretur.

## 11:21
Lord, if you had been here, my brother, etc.

Regarding what Paul says in Rom. 10.: How shall they call on him in whom they have not believed? And Matthew 21.: All things whatsoever you shall ask, believing, you shall receive. Which passages teach both what prayer is, and in what manner one ought to pray. First, faith, that is, the presumption of the Father's mercy, begins prayer, by which faith it comes to pass that you believe you are heard and will obtain whatever can be granted through His glory. Therefore Martha, certain that Christ loved Lazarus, in confidence of His love, says: Lord, if you had been here, my brother would not have died: nor indeed does she stop at this, but she adds: I know that whatsoever you will ask: as if she were saying: Although he is dead, nevertheless I do not doubt that you can raise him, nor likewise do I doubt that you wish to, for you love him.

Latin:

Domine si fuisses hic, frater meus &c.

Quod ad Rom. 10. ait Paulus: Quomodo invocabunt, in quem non crediderunt? Et Matthaei 21. Omnia quaecunque petieritis, credentes accipietis. Qui loci docent, & quid oratio sit, & quo modo sit orandum. Primum fides, id est praesumptio misericordiae patris inchoat orationem, qua fide fit, ut credas te exaudiri & impetraturum quicquid per illius gloriam concedi potest. Ideo Martha certa quod Christus dilexerit Lazarum, fiducia eius amoris, inquit: Domine, si fuisses hic, frater meus non fuisset mortuus: neque vero in hoc subsistit, sed subiicit. Scio quicquid petieris: quasi sic diceret: Quanquam mortuus sit, tamen non dubito quin excitare possis, neque item dubito quin velis, diligis enim.

## 11:24
Your brother shall rise again. I know that he shall rise again in the last day.
