Martha does not yet perceive that she has been heard, she has not yet grasped this word: Your brother shall rise again: but she struggles, now expecting nothing beyond the common lot of others, as unbelief is accustomed to do. Ahaz, Isaiah 7., when the Lord promises him help, does not listen, denying that he will tempt the Lord, but continues to use human help: because he expected nothing beyond the common lot. On the contrary David, about to engage with Goliath, remembered what had happened to him before beyond the common lot, wherefore he trusts now also that the Lord will be present against nature.

Latin:

Resurget frater tuus. Scio quia resur-get in novissimo die.

Nondum sentit Martha se exauditam esse, nondum concepit verbum hoc: Resurget frater tuus: sed laborat, iam nihil ultra communem sortem aliorum expectans, sicut solet infidelitas. Achas, Esaiae 7. cum promittit ei dominus auxilium, non audit, negans se tentaturum dominum, sed uti pergit humano auxilio: quia nihil ultra sortem communem expectabat. E contra David congressurus cum Goliad, commeminit quid acciderit ei antea praeter communem sortem, quare nunc etiam confidat, contra naturam affore dominum.

## 11:25
I am the resurrection and the life.

That is, wherever, whenever I am present, life and resurrection reign. Therefore, do not look only to that time, rather turn your eyes hither to me, as you consider common events and the accidents of creatures. Do you not see? Look at me who am the resurrection etc., according to that Osee 13. I will be thy death, O death etc.

Latin:

Ego sum resurrectio & vita.

Id est ubicunque, quandocunque ego praesto sum vita, resurrectio regnat. Propterea ne ad illud tantum tempus respicias, huc potius refer oculos ad me, quoad eventus communes, quoad casus creaturarum consyderes. Non vides? me vide qui resurrectio sum &c. iuxta illud Osee 13. Ero mors tua ô mors &c.

## 11:25
He that believeth in me, even if he be dead.

How, however, he becomes resurrection and life to us, he indicates in these words: He that believeth in me, that is, by faith I become resurrection and life: do not seek signs, works etc. You have the Word: Thy brother shall rise again, believe in him, and you shall live.

Latin:

Qui credit in me, etiam si mortuus.

Quomodo autem resurrectio & vita nobis fiat, iis verbis indicat: Qui credit in me, id est fide fio resurrectio, & vita: noli quaerere signa, opera &c. Verbum habes: Resurget frater tuus, illi crede, & vives.

## 11:27
I have believed that thou art the son of the living God.

It is a Hebraism: I have believed, for I believe, that thou art Christ the son of the living God, and thus who also empties death, the heir of all things, and reigning forever etc. Do not circumscribe the time thus: I know beforehand that he will rise again.

Latin:

Ego credidi, quod tu es filius dei vivi.

Hebraismus est: Ego credidi, pro credo, quod tu es Christus filius dei vivi, atque adeo qui & mortem evacuas, haeres omnium, & regnans in aeternum &c. Ne circumscribe tempus sic: ante scio quia resuret.

## 11:28
She went and called Mary.

That it is described that Mary alone is called, follows witnesses not summoned: in this it happens, that he signifies that all men bear witness concerning the works of God: but that the fruit of the works of God and consolation reach those who are called. Thus he calls the afflicted saints to consolation, so that meanwhile the world does not know what will happen, and thinks that the saints are rather going to weep, when it sees them suffering the most sorrowful things. Just as also the children of Israel were led out of Egypt: from Zephaniah 3. Christians are called a poor and needy people. And yet in the same place it is written: There shall be none to make them afraid, that is, you shall not fear evil etc. Those who follow Mary become witnesses of the glory of God.

Latin:

Abiit & vocavit Mariam.

Quod describitur Mariam tantum vocari, sequi testes non accersitos: in hoc fit, ut significet de operibus dei omnes homines testari: fructum autem operum dei & consolationem pervenire ad vocatos. Sic sanctos afflictos vocat ad consolationem, ut interim nesciat mundus quid fiet, putetque sanctos potius ad plorandum ire, cum videt eos tristissima quaeque pati. Sicut & filii Israël ex Aegypto educebantur: è Sophoniae 3. Christiani populus pauper et egenus vocantur. Et tamen eodem loco scriptum est: Non erit qui terreatur, id est non timebis malum &c. Qui Mariam sequuntur, testes fiunt gloriae dei.

## 11:33
He groaned in the spirit, and troubled himself.

Groaning is a certain indignation, by which Christ is moved against the kingdom of death, wishing to empty out sin and death, so that he may show that he hates the kingdom of death, and does not wish that the sinner should perish, but rather that he should be converted and live: Ezekiel 18. These affections argue and bear witness to the charity of Christ toward us, and he reminds us that we should safely and cheerfully believe in him. Just as he says to the Hebrews: That we may approach with confidence. To me that groaning and the tears seem to proceed from the same cause, that he pitied the human race: which the Jews feel the tears, to have expressed the love with which he loved Lazarus: certainly they bear witness concerning the charity of Christ.

Latin:

Infremuit spiritu, & turbavit.

Fremitus indignatio quaedam est, qua commovetur Christus adversus regnum mortis, volens peccatum & mortem evacuare, ut ostendat se odisse regnum mortis, nec velle ut pereat peccator, sed potius ut convertatur & vivat: Ezechielis 18. Hi affectus arguunt & testantur charitatem Christi erga nos, & commonet ut tuto, alacriter nos credamus ei. Sicut ille ad Hebraeos inquit: Ut cum fiducia accedamus. Mihi fremitus ille & lachrymae ab eadem causa proficisci videntur, quod miseraretur genus humanum: quod Iudaei sentiunt lachrymas, expressisse amorem quo Lazarum dilexerit: certe testificantur de charitate Christi.

## 11:37
Could not this man who opened.

In this manner are divine works accustomed to be blasphemed. And consciences hear voices of this kind, such as those are in Psalm 21. He hoped in the Lord. Item: He saved others, now let him save himself.

Latin:

Non poterat hic qui aperuit.

Ad hunc modum solent opera divina blasphemari. Et huiusmodi voces audiunt conscientiae, quales illae sunt in Psalmo 21. Speravit in domino. Item: Alios salvos fecit, nunc salvet seipsum.

## 11:40
Did I not say to you that if you believe.

Because you have heard me, that is to say, for I have already testified that it would come to pass that Lazarus would rise again.

Latin:

Nónne dixi tibi quoniam si credideris.

Quia audisti me, scilicet, iam enim testificatussum fore, ut resurgat Lazarus.

## 11:42
But I knew that you always hear me.

Although Christ refers all things to the Father, John 5. I do not seek my own will, but the will of him who sent me. And John 7. Then he says here that he gives thanks, so that he may show, when he attributes the work to the Father, that it is likewise the Father's will, word, and the power of the word: which word he himself preaches, so that we may know that the Father burns with the same charity which the Son exhibited, so that we may not doubt that we are justified, although we lack works, when we believe in the Son. Since the Father approves the preaching of the Son. Understand the cave as death, the stone as sin. Lazarus bound by his face and hands and feet signifies the veiled face of Moses. As long as we are in these, we cannot see into the end of the law, that is Christ, as the Apostle says.

Latin:

Ego autem sciebam, quod semper me audis.

Quanquam Christus omnia referat ad patrem, Ioannis 5. Non quaero voluntatem meam, sed voluntatem eius qui misit me. Et Ioannis 7. Tum hic inquit, se gratias agere, ut ostendat, cum patri opus tribuit, patris item voluntatem, verbum, & verbi potentiam esse: quod verbum ipse praedicat, ut sciamus flagrare patrem eadem charitate, quam exhibuit filius, ne dubitemus iustificari nos, quanquam careamus operibus, cum credimus filio. Siquidem filii praedicationem pater approbat. Speluncam mortem intellige, lapidem peccatum. Ligatus Lazarus vultu ac manibus pedibusque, significat Moysi faciem velatam Quàm diu in iis sumus, non possumus videre in finem legis, id est Christum, ut Apostolus ait.

## 11:47
The Pharisees and the chief priests therefore gathered together.

See the impiety of the princes; first, they confess that the things which were being done by Christ were being carried out by divine power. Secondly, through envy, they allege that a kingdom is being sought, and they pretend a public necessity, namely, that if Christ invades the kingdom, the Romans will bring war. Aaron, when asked why he had made the calf, answered that he had complied with the people demanding it, namely, so that they would not be dispersed. Thus it is accustomed to happen in public affairs, where the matter is administered without faith, one sins out of fear, and protections are sought according to the flesh. Thus the princes of the Jews also wished to appear to think: if Christ continues, perhaps he will invade the kingdom; if he has invaded the kingdom, it will be all over for us.

Latin:

Collegerunt ergo pharisaei & pontifices.

Vide impietatem principum, primum fatentur divinitus geri quae fiebant à Christo. Secundo per in vidiam causantur regnum affectari, & praetendunt publicam necessitatem, scilicet si Christus regnum in vadit, Romanos bellum allaturos esse. Aaron interrogatus cur vitulum fecisset, respondit, se populo obsecutum esse id postulanti, scilicet ne dispergeretur. Sic in rebus publicis fieri solet, ubi sine fide administratur res, metu delinquitur, quaeruntur praesidia secundum carnem. Sic volebant & principes Iudaeorum videri sentire, si pergat Christus, fortasse regnum invadet: si regnum invaserit, actum erit de nobis.

## 11:49
But one of them, Caiaphas.

That Caiaphas prophesied because he was high priest: do high priests, therefore, although impious, prophesy piously? I answer, nay, on the contrary, the example is proposed for this reason, that it might appear that the magistrates are blinded. For in this prophecy God used the impious high priest just as He used Pharaoh, only to blind him and destroy him. Therefore it was necessary for the high priest to prophesy, so that it might be signified that the magistrates are vessels of wrath. Nor did he understand the prophecy of Caiaphas, but he felt that Christ had to be removed from the midst, so that there would be no material for a Roman war from that. Nor did he believe that Christ was the savior, but he prophesied just as the donkey of Balaam, he himself not knowing what he was prophesying: and yet by this voice God bore witness to the saints, that Christ was to be killed for the salvation of the people. Such also was that prophecy Matthaei 27. His blood be upon us, and upon our children etc.

Latin:

Unus autem ex ipsis Caiphas.

Quod Caiphas prophetavit, quia pontifex erat: ergóne pontifices, quanquam impii, pie prophetant? Respondeo, immo econtra, ideo propositum est exemplum, ut appareret excaecari magistratus. Nam in hac prophetia sic est usus impio pontifice deus, sicut est usus Pharaone tantum ad excaecandum eum & perdendum. Ideo oportuit pontificem vaticinari, ut significaretur magistratus esse vasa irae. Neque intelligebat prophetiam Caiphas, sed sentiebat Christum è medio tollendum esse, ne qua foret inde materies belli Romani. Neque credebat Christum salvatorem esse, sed vaticinatur sicut asina Balaam, ipse nesciens quid vaticinaretur: & tamen hac voce deus testimonium perhibuit sanctis, quod pro salute populi esset interemptus Christus. Talis & illa prophetia erat Matthaei 27. Sanguis eius super nos, & super filios nostros &c.

## 11:55
And the passover of the Jews was near.

By what rite the passover of the old testament was accustomed to be celebrated, you may read Exodi 12. There is no doubt, however, that by that passover Christ was signified, according to that 1. Corinth. 5. Christ our passover is sacrificed. Then because it is a corporal passover, it is necessary that it be a type of the eternal passover. For Israel was being led out of Egypt, so that he might be blessed with the eternal blessing, which was promised to the fathers. Meanwhile, because these blessings were corporal, by which no one was freed from sin and from death, it is necessary that they were signs of spiritual and eternal blessings. Just as Paul also says Colossen. 2. Let no man judge you in meat or in drink etc. which are a shadow of things to come: but the body is of Christ. Because the law does not bless with eternal blessings, therefore it delivers both the righteousnesses of the flesh, and the signs of spiritual blessings. Secondly, the departure from Egypt through the Red Sea, without doubt signifies the liberty into which we have been called through the Gospel, which liberty we enter through the Red Sea, that is, through death. For we are planted together with Christ through death, for it is necessary that that old Adam be plainly mortified. 1. Corinth. 10. Paul writes: that our fathers were all baptized into Moses in the cloud and in the sea. Into Moses, that is, the word of Moses, when he ordered them to stand still to see the great works of God. That they were entering through the sea, that is, they were being mortified; that the cloud went before, they were being vivified. Thirdly, the Passover was a sign to the Jews by which they might be admonished how they were led out of Egypt, as appears Exodus 12. & 13. Furthermore, because it was a monument of a past and corporal thing, it certainly figured that a better Passover was to follow. Therefore Jeremiah 16. it is written: Behold the days shall come, and it shall no more be said: The Lord liveth, who brought forth the children of Israel. Therefore that corporal Passover [signified] another departure &c. Does Jeremiah therefore fight with Moses, who says, with an everlasting worship in your generations? I answer, because he adds in your generations, he signifies that as long as that carnal Israel lasts, as long as that generation is the people of God, this Passover ought to be celebrated, as if he were saying: Therefore in your generations let this rite endure perpetually, because you are carnal Israel. To me you are now a people, and with you, carnal Israel, I have done this, so that I might testify that I am mindful of the promise made to the fathers: therefore, as long as you endure, let this passover be celebrated. For after Christ appeared, that carnal generation and that carnal Israel ceased to be the people of God.

The Jews were accustomed to begin the year from the spring.

Because I can establish nothing certain concerning the numbers, I omit why he assigns the first month, why the tenth day. Although, because he wishes the time of the passover to be the beginning of months, it sufficiently appears that the beginning of the future age and the time of revealing the Gospel is the passover. Secondly, because he commands the neighboring Israelites to be summoned, if one is not enough for eating the lamb, it signifies that Christ is to be eaten in common with the neighbors, that is, the Gentiles. Thirdly, the lamb without blemish is Christ, who, since he did not commit sin, was made sin for us. For it was necessary for us to have a high priest who was innocent, for whom it would not be necessary to offer sacrifices for his own sins: Hebre. 7. For this also is an argument of the future high priest, that although the law commands the high priest to intercede for the people, it is nevertheless necessary for the intercessor himself to intercede for himself: by which it was signified that he is not truly one of these high priests, who themselves also have need of someone who may intercede for them. Fourth, a male, to designate both the kingdom and the power of the word of Christ, Psalm 21. For the kingdom is the Lord's, etc. Fifth, a yearling, because Christ is tender and weak, as Isaiah 53. calls him, a man knowing infirmity.

Sixth, that which is read among us: According to which rite you shall take also a kid: the Hebrews have: From the lambs and the goats you shall take, that is, you shall take from the flock. It is, however, an idiom, but it signifies that Christ was taken from the flock, that is, from the number of the brethren, and so also that he is a brother: and not only that, but made like unto us in all things, and tempted according to the likeness without sin: so that the hearts of the believers might be increased in confidence, by committing themselves to a brother: and we may know that he sympathizes, because he himself also has suffered.

The immolation of the paschal lamb.

Seventh, the whole multitude, that is, the whole Israel from the Gentiles and the Jews. Eighth, him, if he speaks as if of a single lamb, the whole multitude should immolate together, when nevertheless in almost every house single lambs were slaughtered. But he preferred to speak in this way, so that he might indicate that by those many, the one Christ is represented. Ninth, he shall immolate, namely through the confession of faith, through the preaching of the Gospel, as Psalm 49. Immolate to God a sacrifice of praise. Tenth, at evening. Evening in the scriptures almost always signifies the final time, in which the Gospel, hidden until then, was revealed, as Genesis 3. When they heard the voice of the Lord God walking in paradise in the evening. Eleventh, and they shall take of his blood. He commands hyssop to be dipped in blood, with which the doorposts and the threshold are then to be sprinkled. That the Gospel is signified by hyssop, Psalm 50. also testifies: You shall sprinkle me, O Lord, with hyssop. You shall give joy to my hearing. For by the Gospel the blood of Christ is sprinkled upon the hearts of the elect. To be sprinkled with the blood of Christ, indeed, is to be sanctified, as 1 Peter 1. Unto sanctification of the spirit, unto obedience and sprinkling of the blood of Jesus Christ. Twelfth, And they shall place etc. The doorposts and the lintel signify the entire building, that is, the whole church, which must be sprinkled with the blood of Christ. Thirteenth, And they shall eat the flesh. He calls this whole time, during which the kingdom of faith lasts, night, until the revelation of the sons of God, when the kingdom of glory will be begun. In that night they shall eat the lamb. That is, they shall be fed by the Gospel. For thus Christ wishes to be eaten, as He Himself says John 6. The flesh profits nothing, it is the spirit that gives life. With raw herbs. Furthermore, the Gospel should be neither raw, nor boiled with water. The Gospel is said to be raw to those who are themselves raw, that is, not mortified. And boiled with water to those by whom it is corrupted by a carnal sense, by human traditions, they hear, so that the Gospel has accomplished nothing in afflicted consciences, which were healing themselves through satisfactions and the righteousness of works, by which the Gospel was no longer the Gospel. Fourteenth, but the meats are roasted, when pure hearts are mortified, and consumed by fire, that is, by the judgment of God, they are refreshed.

Fifteenth, that the Gospel was signified by the eating of meats, pertains to this, that it is certain that by the power of the Gospel a resurrection is brought about, and a new flesh is prepared. To conceive the Lamb Christ, that is, the Word by faith, and having conceived it, to be reborn: for because the meats were eaten, the resurrection was signified: but because this does not happen entirely at once, but is perfected little by little, until the flesh dies: therefore in the meantime the reborn have need of manifold consolation, as Paul says: Let the word of God abound among you. Therefore, by the lettuces or rather bitter herbs the law is figured, which little by little mortifies the remnants of the old man in the flesh, and little by little more evidently reveals unbelief, foolishness, and hypocrisy. As Jeremiah says 21. After you showed me, I struck my thigh &c. By the unleavened breads the consolation of the Gospel was signified, by which the knowledge of God, faith, wisdom, fear, and all the fruits of the Spirit increase, and thus Paul says: Beholding the glory of the Lord, we are transformed into the same image. The head with the feet, and the entrails are for those who imagine Christ carnally. Those who believe altogether hypocritically, to them the lamb is eaten without a head, that is, in them faith is not the work of the power of God. And therefore they are ignorant of the head of Christ, that is, the power of God in Christ. Likewise, those who eat without the entrails, they simulate faith without the passions of Christ. Those who eat without the feet, they do not confess Christ; therefore, it is necessary that the whole lamb be eaten. You shall gird your loins. Girded loins is explained at Ephes. 6. Having girded your loins in truth. For it is signified that the sense of the flesh ought to be pressed by the word of God, so that it may remain in the simplicity of the faith of the spirit, lest one sew something onto the Gospel, etc. Likewise, that sandals signify the Gospel, Paul teaches in the same place: Feet shod in the preparation of preaching the Gospel. Holding a staff in the hands: the battles of the word. And you shall eat in haste: haste signifies fervor, the ardor of the spirit, etc.

Latin:

Proximum autem erat pascha Iudaeorum.

Quo ritu pascha veteris testamenti celebrari solitum sit, leges Exodi 12. Non dubium est autem, illo paschate significatum esse Christum, iuxta illud 1. Corinth. 5. Pascha nostrum immolatus est Christus. Deinde quia corporale pascha est, necesse est esse typum paschatos aeterni. Educebatur enim Israëlex Aegypto, ut benediceretur aeterna benedictione, quae erat promissa patribus. Interim hae benedictiones quia corporales fuerunt, quibus nemo à peccato & à morte liberabatur, necesse est signa fuisse benedictionum spiritualium & aeternarun. Sicut & Paulus inquit Colossen. 2. Nemo vos iudicet in cibo aut in potu &c. quae sunt umbra futurorum: corpus autem Christi. Quod quia lex non benedicit aeternis benedictionibus, ideo & iustitias carnis tradit, & signa benedictionum spiritualium. Secundo, profectus ex Aegypto per mare rubrum, haud dubie significat libertatem, in quam vocati sumus per Evangelium, quam libertatem per mare rubrum, hoc est per mortem ingredimur. Nam Christo complantamur per mortem, oportet enim planè mortificari veterem illum Adam. 1. Corinth. 10. scribit Paulus: patres nostros omnes in Moysen baptizatos esse in nube & mari. In Moysen, id est verbum Moysi, cum ille consistere iuberet visuros magnalia dei. Quod per mare ingrediebantur, id est mortificabantur, quod nubes praecedebat, vivificabantur. Tertio, pascha Iudaeis fuit signum quo admonerentur quomodo ex Aegypto educti sint, ut apparet Exodi 12. & 13. Porrò quia fuit praeteritae rei & corporalis monimentum, certe figurabat, melius pascha secuturum esse. Propterea Hieremiae 16. scriptum est: Ecce dies venient, & non dicetur ultra: Vivit dominus, qui eduxit filios Israël. Ergo illud corporale pascha alius egressus &c. Pugnat igitur Hieremias cum Moyse, qui dicit, cultu sempiterno in generationibus vestris? Respondeo, quia addit in generationibus vestris, significat quod quàm diu carnalis ille Israël durat, quàm diu illa generatio populus dei est, celebrari hoc pascha debeat, quasi diceret: Ideo in vestris generationibus perpetuo duret hic ritus, quia vos carnalis Israël. Mihi iam populus estis, & vobiscum carnali Israël hoc egi, ut testificarer me memorem esse promissionis factae ad patres: ergo quoad duratis, celebretur hoc pascha. Nam posteaquam apparuit Christus, iam illa carnalis generatio, & ille carnalis Israël desiit esse populus dei.

Iudaei soliti sunt annum ordiri à vere.

Quia de numeris nihil certi constituere possum, omitto cur primum mensem, cur decimum diem assignet. Quamquam quod principium esse mensium vult paschatos tempus, satis apparet, initium futuri seculi & revelandi Evangelii tempus paschatos esse. Secundo, quod iubet vicinos Israëlitas accersi, si una non sit agno edendo satis, significat Christum communiter cum vicinis, id est gentibus edendum esse. Tertio, agnus absque macula Christus est, qui cum non fecerit peccatum, factus est pro nobis peccatum. Oportuit enim nobis pontificem esse innocentem, cui non esset necesse pro suis peccatis hostias offerre: Hebre. 7. Est enim & hoc argumentum futuri pontificis, quod cumlex praecipiat pontifici interpellare pro populo, oporteat tamen ipsum interpellatorem pro sese interpellare: quo significatum est, non esse vere pontifices hos, qui & ipsi habeant opus aliquo, qui pro ipsis interpellet. Quarto, masculus, ad designandum & Christi regnum & potentiam verbi, Psalmo 21. Quoniam domini est regnum &c. Quinto, anniculus, quia tener sit & imbecillis Christus, ut appellat eum Esaias 53. Virum scientem infirmitatem.

Sexto, quod apud nos legitur: Iuxta quem ritum tolletis & hoedum: Hebraei habent: Ex agnis & capris tolletis, id est de grege accipietis. Est autem idiotismus, significat autem Christum acceptum esse ex grege, id est ex numero fratrum, adeoque & fratrem esse: nec id modo, sed per omnia similem factum nobis, & tentatum secundum similitudinem sine peccato: ut & fiducia augerentur corda credentium, commissura se fratri: & sciamus illum compati, quia & ipse passus sit.

Immolatio agni paschalis.

Septimo, universa multitudo, id est totus Israël ex gentibus & Iudaeis. Octavo, eum, si loquitur quasi unicum agnum, universa multitudo simul immolet, cum tamen in singulis ferè aedibus singuli agni mactarentur. Sed ita maluit loqui, ut indicaret multis illis unicum Christum repraesentari. Nono, immolabit, scilicet per confessionem fidei, per praedicationem Evangelii, ut Psalmo 49. Immola deo sacrificium laudis. Decimo, ad vesperam. Vespera ferè in scripturis significat tempus novissimum, quo Evangelium eatenus absconditum revelatum est, ut Genesis 3. Cum audissent vocem domini dei deambulantis in paradiso vesperi. Undecimo, & sument de sanguine eius. Isopum iubet imbui sanguine, quo deinde aspergantur postes & limen. Isopo Evangelium significari, vel Psalmus 50. testatur: Asperges me domine isopo. Auditu meo dabis gaudium. Evangelio vero sanguis Christi aspergitur cordibus electorum. Aspergi vero sanguine Christi, est sanctificari, ut 1. Petri 1. In sanctificationem spiritus, in obedientiam & aspersionem sanguinis Iesu Christi. Duodecimo, Et ponent &c. Postes & superliminare, universum aedificium significat, id est totam ecclesiam, quam oportet aspersam esse sanguine Christi. Tredecimo, Et edent carnes. Noctem vocat omne hoc tempus, quo durat regnum fidei, usque ad revelationem filiorum dei, cum regnum gloriae inchoabitur. Ea nocte edent agnum. id est Evangelio pascentur. Sic enim edi vult Christus, ut ipse ait Ioannis 6. Caro non prodest quicquam, spiritus est qui vivificat. Crudis herbis. Porró Evangelium neque crudum sit, neque coctum aqua. Crudum Evangelium dicituriis, qui ipsi crudi sunt, id est non mortificati. Et coctum aqua iis, quia vitiatum sensu carnali, traditionibus humanis, audiunt, ut nihil effecerit Evangelium in afflictis conscientiis, qui medicabantur sibi per satisfactiones & iustitias operum, quibus iam Evangelium non erat Evangelium. Decimoquarto, sed assaeigni carnes, sunt, quando pura corda mortificata, & igni, hoc est iudicio dei consumpta, reficiuntur.

Decimo quinto, quod carnibus edendis significatum est Evangelium, eó pertinet, quod Evangelii potentia resurrectionem fieri, & novam carnem parari, certum est. Agnum Christum, id est fide verbum concipere, eoque concepto renasci: quod enim carnes edebantur, significata est resurrectio: sed quia hic non statim tota fit, sed subinde perficitur, donec intereat caro: ideo interim renatis opus est multiplici consolatione, sicut Paulus inquit: Abundet verbum dei inter vos. Proinde per lactucas vel potius acidas herbas lex figuratur, quae subinde mortificat reliquias vetustatis in carne, subinde evidentius revelat infidelitatem, insipientiam, & hypocrisin. Sicut Hieremias inquit 21. Postquam ostendisti mihi, percussi femur meum &c. Perazymos panes significata est Evangelii consolatio, qua augescit cognitio dei, fides, sapientia, timor, & omnes fructus spiritus, & sic ait Paulus: Speculando gloriam domini, in eandem imaginem transformamur. Caput cum pedibus, & intestinis, nis, qui carnaliter imaginantur Christum. Qui omnino simulanter credunt, illis agnus sine capite editur, hoc est in his fides non est opus potentiae dei. Ideóque ignorant caput Christi, hoc est potentiam dei in Christo. Item qui sine intestinis edunt, ii sine passionibus Christi simulant fidem. Qui sine pedibus edunt, ii non confitentur Christum, propterea totum agnum edi oportet. Renes vestros accingetis. Renes accincti, exponitur ad Ephes. 6. Succincti lumbos in veritate. Significatur enim, sensum carnis premi debere verbo dei, ut maneat in simplicitate fidei spiritus, ne assuat Evangelio aliquid &c. Item calciamenta significare Evangelium, eodem loco docet Paulus: Calciati pedes in praeparationem praedicandi Evangelii. Tenentes baculum in manibus: pugnas verbi. Et edetis festinanter: festinantiam fervorem significat, ardorem spiritus &c.

## 12:1 Argument
CHAPTER TWELVE.

The scope of this history is that faith is regarded by God, not the work. By faith all works, of whatever kind and of whatever quantity they are, are accepted. For in reality a vile work is this profusion of ointment. But faith caused the work to be of such great value as she herself was. Thus, on the contrary, however great the works are, if faith is lacking, they are cursed, according to that Luke 13 We have eaten in your presence &c. he will say to you: I do not know you. Whatever you do, your heart feels thus: I do not know whether God wishes me well, or on the contrary, perhaps he himself does not regard me. These hypocrites console themselves with works, and seek to enter, and do not find the way. Others through despair rush into all kinds of sins. The saints, having conceived the Gospel and the promise of salvation, are lifted up and trust in mercy, and feel that they are regarded by God: and they in turn rejoice in their heart to do what God commands, to live, to die. From this it is easy to understand what faith is, so that you may not imagine any faith to be sterile or fictitious. Mary represents the church, the Nard the word: the feet of Christ, by which the word is announced: Judas the preachers of the law, who condemn the works of faith.

Latin:

CAPUT DUODE-CIMUM.

Scopus huius historiae est, à deo respici fidem non opus. Fide omnia opera, qualiacunque, & quantulacunque sunt, accepta esse. Nam re ipsa vile opus est haec ungenti profusio. Sed fides faciebat, ut tanti fieret opus, quanta ipsa erat. Sic econtra, quantacunque opera sunt, si desit fides, maledicta sunt, iuxta illud Lucae 13. Manducavimus coram te &c. dicet vobis: Nescio vos. Quicquid agis, sic sentit cor tuum: Nescio utrum mihi bene velit deus, an contrà, fortasse ipse tenon respicit. Hi hypocritae consolantur se operibus, & quaerunt ingredi, & non inveniunt viam. Alii per de sperationem ruunt in omnia genera peccatorum. Sancti concepto Evangelio & promissione salutis, eriguntur & confidunt misericordiae, & sentiunt se respici à deo: et ipsi vicissim gaudent corde facere quae praecipit deus, vivere, mori. Ex hoc facile est intelligere quid sit fides, ne quam fidem possis sterilem aut fictitiam imaginari. Maria repraesentat ecclesiam, Nardus verbum: Pedes Christi, quibus nunciatur verbum: Iudas praedicatores legis, qui damnant opera fidei.

## 12:7
Let her alone, that against the day of my burying?

For against the day of my burying I shall be anointed for burial: that is, let her alone so that she may show me the testimony of her faith now that I am about to die, that she believes that I am Christ, whether I live or whether I die, although the world may persecute me.

Latin:

Sinite illam, ut in diem sepulturae?

Nam in die sepulturae inungar ad sepeliendum: id est sinite ut exhibeat mihi testimonium fidei suae iam morituro, quod credat Christum esse me, sive vivam, sive moriar, quanquam persequatur mundus.

## 12:8
The poor you will always have.

By these words the faith of Mary is amplified, the cross is not to contribute something to the poor. The cross is to contribute to the person of Christ, because the princes of the Jews had already condemned him, and had destined him for death. Furthermore, faith is declared more evidently in the cross than in works, however specious they may be outside of the cross; this is what Christ approves of the duty of Mary, saying: You will always have the poor with you, that is, I wish for the account of the poor to be had, but I also wish for this, that in the cross you may testify concerning your faith. And the work of Mary, because it testifies concerning me, I approve in the cross: because it is a work of such great faith. Therefore from these things you understand, first, that neither afflictions, nor works of charity, or of ceremonies are regarded by Christ without faith. According to that Psal. 17. They cried out, and there was none to save them: but that faith is regarded which equates all works and passions. Secondly, that afflictions are preferred to works, because in afflictions faith exercises itself more evidently. Therefore, those who allege this passage for ceremonies, before which the poor are neglected, let them consider that the cross and afflictions are here praised by Christ: indeed, faith exercising itself in the cross.

Latin:

Pauperes semper habebitis.

His verbis amplificatur fides Mariae, crux non est, in pauperes aliquid conferre. Crux est in personam Christi conferre, quod hunc iam damnassent principes Iudaeorum, destinassentque morti. Porrò fides evidentius declaratur in cruce quàm in operibus, quantuncunque speciosis extra crucem, hoc est quod Christus probat officium Mariae, inquiens: Pauperes semper habebitis vobiscum, id est volo pauperum rationem haberi, sed et hoc volo ut in cruce testificemini de fide vestra. Et Mariae opus, quia testificatur de me, in cruce probo: quia tantae fidei opus est. Itaque ex iis intelligis, primum neque afflictiones, neque opera charitatis, vel ceremoniarum respici à Christo sine fide. Iuxta illud Psal. 17. Clamaverunt, nec erat qui salvos faceret: sed fidem respici quae aequat omnia opera & passiones. Secundo praeferri afflictiones operibus, quod in afflictionibus fides se evidentius exerceat. Ergo qui hunc locum pro ceremoniis allegant, prae quibus ne gliguntur pauperes, ii consyderent, crucem & afflictiones hic à Christo laudari: immo fidem exercentem se in cruce.

## 12:12
On the next day, however, a great crowd.

They bear witness to Christ, that he is the son of David, and the king promised in the scriptures, those who proclaim him with acclamations. Wherefore it also behooved him in to be carried into the city publicly. For thus it had also been predicted through the prophet Zechariah ch. 9. That they were crying out ὡς ἀννᾶ, that is, save. The remaining Evangelists add, Hosanna to the son of David, that is, bring salvation to the son of David, Save the son of David. Furthermore, what is it that we pray for salvation for him, when salvation should rather be sought by us from him? I answer, that it is prayed in this psalm, that Christ may reign, that things may proceed happily for him, that he himself may be glorified. The Gospel preaches, not our glory, but Christ's: not our kingdom, but Christ's. And the salvation of Christ is, for the word of the power of God to reign in the hearts of the saints. Therefore it is sung, that things may proceed prosperously for Christ, that he himself may reign, namely that consciences may acknowledge that outside the kingdom of Christ, all our works are sins. Hosanna, that is, save. Blessed: and thus blessing, or thus with the whole kingdom. In the name of the Lord, not in the power of a creature, but in the power of God.

King of Israel: promised in the scriptures, in Micah: Do not fear, because this kingdom is a kingdom of grace. Daughter of Zion, you Zion, the whole kingdom of the faithful. He comes to you, namely as the consoler of consciences. The disciples did not know these things, that it was necessary for him to be carried into the city according to the scriptures: they remembered, having received the Holy Spirit.

Latin:

In crastinum autem turba multa.

Testimonium perhibent Christo, quod sit filius David, & rex promissus in scripturis, hi qui acclamationibus praedicant eum. Quare & oportuit eum in urbem vehi publice. Sic enim & praedictum erat per prophetam Zachariam cap. 9. Quod clamabant ὡς ἀννᾶ, hoc est sanctifica. Addunt reliqui Evangelistae, hos anna filio David, id est salutem fac filio David, Salva filium David. Porrò quid est, quod nos illi salutem oramus, cum ab ipso potius nobis salus petenda sit? Respondeo, orari hoc psalmo, ut Christus regnet, ut ipsi feliciter procedat res, ut ipse glorificetur. Evangelium praedicat, non nostram gloriam, sed Christi: non nostrum regnum, sed Christi. Et Christi salus est, regnare verbum potentia dei in cordibus sanctorum. Proinde canitur, ut Christo res prospere procedat, ut ipse regnet, scilicet quod conscientiae agnoscant extra regnum Christi, omnia opera nostra peccata esse. Hos anna, id est sanctifica. Benedictus: atque adeo benedicens, vel sic cum toto regno. In nomine domini, non in potentia creaturae, sed in potentia dei.

Rex Israël: promissus in scripturis, in Michea: Noli timere, quia hoc regnum, regnum gratiae est. Filia Sion, tu Sion totum regnum fidelium. Venit tibi, scilicet tanquam consolator conscientiarum. Haec non cognoverunt discipuli, quod oporteret cum secundum scripturas vehi in urbem: recordati sunt, accepto spiritusancto.

## 12:19
The Pharisees therefore said among themselves.

The Pharisees persecute, because the preaching of Christ cannot but be a scandal. For the Gospel teaches that human righteousnesses are the simulation of the foolish, and the ignorance of God: and that the confidence which they place in works is vain. According to that Isaiah 28. We have placed our hope in a lie. Since the Pharisaical reason does not perceive these things, it happens that it condemns the Gospel.

Latin:

Pharisaei ergo dixerunt ad se.

Pharisaei persequuntur, quia Christi praedicatio non potest non esse scandalo. Docet enim Evangelium, iustitias humanas esse simulationem insipientium, & ignorationem dei: & fiduciam quam faciunt opera, vanam esse. Iuxta illud Esaiae 28. Posuimus mendacium spem nostram. Haec cum ratio Pharisaica non cernat, fit ut damnet Evangelium.

## 12:20
There were however certain Gentiles among them.

The Pharisees persecute, the Gentiles receive the Gospel. And because Christ is nothing but a minister of the circumcision, therefore the apostles bring the Gentiles to Christ. For through the apostles the Gospel was to be preached among the Gentiles, who until now were a people without God, to be hereafter, not only a people of God, but a son of God, Hosea 1. Christ therefore begins to preach concerning his own glorification, and teaches that it will come to pass that he will not be glorified before he has been diminished, and that there is another glory, namely an internal kingdom, and that there is another way to that glory, namely through the cross, and that he is not to be glorified by that concourse of the multitude, that is, by external glory or a bodily kingdom etc.

Latin:

Erant autem quidam gentiles exiis.

Pharisaei persequuntur, gentes excipiunt Evangelium. Et quia Christus non est nisi circumcisionis minister, ideo apostoli ad Christum gentes adducunt. Nam per apostolos erat Evangelium praedicandum in gentes, quae hactenus fuerunt populus sine deo, futurus post hac, non modo populus dei, sed filius dei, Osee 1. Incipit itaque Christus praedicare de glorificatione sui, docetque fore ut non glorificetur priusquam extenuatus fuerit, neque glorificari se illo concursu multitudinis, hoc est externa gloria seu corporali regno &c. esse aliam gloriam, scilicet regnum internum, esse & aliam viam ad eam gloriam, scilicet per crucem.

## 12:23
The hour has come that he should be glorified.

Being ignorant of the faith, they think that the glory is mine, this an external kingdom. But another kingdom presses, namely the spiritual, by which I shall truly be glorified, but not except through the cross.

Latin:

Venit hora ut clarificetur.

Ignorantes fidei, putant gloriam esse meam, hoc regnum externum. Atqui instat aliud regnum, scilicet spirituale, quo vere glorificabor, sed non nisi per crucem.

## 12:24
Unless a grain of wheat falling into.

This is an excellent commendation of the cross: that it is death through which we may bear fruit. As it is said in Romans 6: those planted together with the death of Christ bear fruit. Reason and law feel that death is a punishment. And, as the Apostle says: Death is the wages of sin. For those who have been inserted into the death of Christ, that is, for those who believe, death is no longer a punishment, but a certain transplantation, if you transfer a plant from sterile soil into more fertile soil. Secondly, furthermore, because the glory of God is eternal, by which the whole body of Christ is glorified, it is necessary for this oldness to be mortified. And therefore nothing pertains to the kingdom of Christ for those who pursue external glory, liberty, etc.

Latin:

Nisi granum frumenti cadens in.

Egregia est haec crucis commendatio: Mortem esse per quam fructum faciamus. Sicut ad Romanos 6. dicitur: Complantatos morti Christi fructificare. Ratio & lexsentiunt, mortem esse poenam. Et, sicut Apostolus ait: Mors est stipendium peccati. Qui inserti sunt morti Christi, id est iis qui credunt, mors iam non est poena, sed transplantatio quaedam, si è sterili solo transferas plantam in feracius. Secundo, porrò, quia gloria dei aeterna est, qua glorificatur totum Christi corpus, necesse est hanc vetustatem mortificari. Ideoque nihil ad Christi regnum pertinet, qui externam gloriam, libertatem &c. sectantur.

## 12:25
He who loves his soul shall lose it.

By the name of soul and life in this world are comprehended all the desires of the flesh, so that the meaning is that it is necessary to hate the life of the flesh (that is, all things which the flesh seeks, all things in which the life of this flesh consists), glory, pleasure, wealth, etc. As it is also said in Matthaei 16.: He who wishes to follow me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross. Let him deny (he says) himself, concerning his whole nature, life, speaking of the energy of the flesh. Secondly: But how will this come to pass? By the works and powers of free will? But free will cannot hate, deny, or resign from the heart life, pleasures, and all the desires of the flesh, as this sentence clearly testifies, that free will can do nothing. But hearts are purified by faith, says Peter in the Acts, that is, where God has given a taste of mercy, the heart willingly resigns and denies all the desires of the flesh.

Latin:

Qui amat animam suam, perdet eam.

Nomine animae & vitae in hoc mundo comprehenduntur omnia desyderia carnis, ut sit sententia, odisse oportere vitam carnis (id est omnia quae quaerit caro, omnia in quibus est huius carnis vita) gloriam, voluptatem, opes &c. Ut & Matthaei 16. dicitur: Qui vult me sequi, abneget semetipsum, & tollat crucem suam. Abneget (inquit) seipsum, de tota natura, vita, energia carnis locutus. Secundo: Qui fiet autem hoc? operibus'ne & viribus liberi arbitrii? At liberum arbitrium non potest odisse, abnegare, resignare ex corde vitam, voluptates, & omnia desyderia carnis, ut haec sententia planè testificatur, nihil pose liberum arbitrium. Purificantur autem corda fide, inquit Petrus in Actis, id est ubi deus gustum dederit misericordiae, iancor ultro resignat, & abnegat oia desyderia carnis.

## 12:26
If any man serves me.

He who serves in whatever his calling may be, let him follow me, by enduring the cross and death. Thus at last the cross will be endured, if it is believed that just as Christ has been glorified, so also are they to be glorified who believe in him.

Latin:

Si quis mihi ministrat.

Qui ministrat quoncunque in sua vocatione, me se quatur, tolerando crucem & mortem. Ita demum tolerabitur crux, si credetur, quod sicut Christus glorificatus est, ita sint & ii glorificandi qui credunt ei.

## 12:27
My soul is troubled, Father, glorify.

What Christ was saying a little before, that death is the way to glorification, he now approves by his own example. For he is tempted, and not only that, but he is also helped by the Father, so that we may know that it is necessary for us to be tempted, just as he himself was tempted. And when we are tempted, it will come to pass that the Father will console us, not otherwise than he consoled the Son. For the Gospel is a certain promise of help and salvation, and Christ is a certain pledge of help and salvation. What kind of temptation it was can be gathered from these words:

Father, sanctify, etc., unto this hour, namely, that I may suffer.

Latin:

Anima mea turbata est, pater sanctifica.

Quod paulo ante dicebat Christus, mortem esse viam ad glorificationem, id modo exemplo suo approbat. Tentatur enim neque id modo, sed & à patre iuvatur, ut sciamus, oportere nos tentari, quemadmodum & ipse tentatus est. Et cum tentamur, fore ut consoletur pater, non aliter atque filium consolatus est. Est enim Evangelium certa promissio auxilii & salutis, & Christus certum pignus auxilii & salutis. Cuiusmodi fuerit tentatio, ex iis verbis colligi potest:

Pater sanctifica &c. in hanc horam, scilicet ut patiar.

## 12:27
Not for my sake, but for yours.

That you may believe, in the same way you also are to be comforted and helped, just as I have been helped.

Latin:

Non propter me, sed propter vos.

Ut credatis, ad eundem modum vos quoque consolandos & iuvandos esse, sicut ego adiutus sum.

## 12:31
Now is the judgment of this world.

Here Christ describes his kingdom, when he had previously taught that that carnal kingdom was not his kingdom. The preaching of the Gospel judges the world, Rom. 1. The wrath of God is revealed against all ungodliness and unrighteousness. And Ioannis 16. the Holy Spirit will convict the world of judgment, because the prince of this world is judged: therefore the world is condemned of sin. I call sins, however, not only those which the world condemns, such as murders, adulteries, but also those which the world cannot condemn because of blindness, such as ignorance, contempt of God, unbelief, all of which the Gospel reveals and condemns. Furthermore, by the preaching of the Gospel the world is already judged, which judgment the consciences of those who believe the Gospel feel. But on the last day this judgment will be made manifest, so that those who have believed may be glorified.

Latin:

Nunc iudicium est huius mundi.

Hic Christus describit regnum suum, cum antea docuisset carnale illud regnum non esse suum regnum. Evangelii praedicatio iudicat mundum, Rom. 1. Revelatur ira dei super omnem impietatem & iniustitiam. Et Ioannis 16. Spiritussanctus arguet mundum de iudicio, quia princeps huius mundi iudicatur: damnatur itaque mundus peccati. Voco autem peccata, non ea modo quae mundus damnat, ut sunt caedes, adulteria, sed & ea quae damnare propter caecitatem mundus non potest, ut sunt ignoratio, contemptus dei, incredulitas, quae omnia revelat & damnat Evangelium. Porrò praedicatione Evangelii iam iudicatur mundus, quod iudicium conscientiae eorum sentiunt, qui Evangelio credunt. Novissimo vero die palàm fiet hoc iudicium, ut glorificentur ii qui crediderunt.

## 12:31
The prince of this world will be cast out.

First note, concerning the prince (that which we said above) that is, to be held captive by Satan, and to be driven to all kinds of vices, the hearts of all those in whom the spirit of Christ is not. And Satan shall now be cast out, namely through the Gospel, which is the power of God. Satan shall be cast out, so that he can do nothing against those who believe the Gospel. Therefore Christ says this: Hitherto the word has not been preached effectively for destroying the gates of hell. Now from this time the word shall be preached, by whose power Satan shall be subdued: and that shall be the kingdom of Christ, to cast out Satan by his word. Therefore Christ reigns in him from whose heart Satan has been cast out: and the heart has been renewed by the knowledge of God, by faith, by the fear of God, by hatred of the desires of the flesh, etc. He shall be cast out, that is, the word shall be published by which those who were previously his captives may be freed from the kingdom of Satan, by which the sons of God shall be begotten.

Latin:

Princeps huius mundi eiicietur foras.

Primum nota, à principe (id quod supra diximus) id est à satana teneri captiva, et impelli ad omnia genera vitiorum, corda eorum omnium, in quibus non est spiritus Christi. Et satan iam eiicietur foras, scilicet per Evangelium, quod est potentia dei. Satan eiicietur, ut nihil possit adversum eos qui credunt Evangelio. Hoc ergo dicit Christus: Hactenus non est praedicatum verbum efficax ad destruendas portas inferorum. Iam nunc praedicabitur verbum, cuius potentia subigetur satan: atque id erit Christi regnum, eii cere satanam verbo suo. Ergo in eo regnat Christus, è cuius corde satan eiectus est: & innovatum est cor cognitione dei, fide, metu dei, odio desyderiorum carnis &c. Eiicietur foras, id est evulgabitur verbum quo à regno satanae liberentur, qui antea eius captivi erant, quo gignentur filii dei.

## 12:32
If I be lifted up.

That is, crucified, just as also above John 3. As Moses lifted up the serpent in the desert, etc. I will draw all things to myself, that is, both Jews and Gentiles. To myself is a Grecism; the Latins say simply to me.

Latin:

Ego si exaltatus fuero.

Id est crucifixus, sicut & supra Ioannis 3. Sicut Moyses exaltavit serpentem in deserto &c. Omnia traham ad me, id est & Iudaeos, & gentes. Ad meipsum Graecismus est, Latini dicunt simpliciter ad me.

## 12:33
And this he said, signifying by what death.

Christ discourses in this whole sermon about his kingdom:

From that point on, from the place: The hour has come, that the son of man should be glorified, etc. He explains what kind his future kingdom is. And that is most clearly explained by this epiphonema. Two kingdoms are proposed, the kingdom of Satan, and the kingdom of Christ. The kingdom of Christ are those in whom the world has been judged, and from whom Satan has been cast out, that is, those in whom the impiety of the human heart has begun to be revealed, in whom the old Adam has begun to be mortified, and into whom the spirit of Christ has been poured, which inflames them with the knowledge of God, the fear of God, and the confidence of the soul to suffer and do all things which God demands. You see, the kingdom of Christ is not in any external appearance, ceremonies, or work: but in the heart, where this judgment is felt. The kingdom of Satan are those in whom the flesh has not begun to be judged, to whom the impiety of the heart is not revealed: they are void of the fear of God, of recognition, of faith: and within themselves they despise God, and trust in creatures, some in their own prudence, others in their own holiness, others in wealth, others in other things of this kind. They are all men whom the Holy Spirit has not renewed; of them the Apostle writes 2. Corinth. 4. that the god of this world, that is, Satan, holds their hearts captive, lest the Gospel should shine in them. Furthermore, because Satan holds the hearts of the impious captive, he drives some to one crime and others to another. And this is that excellent freedom of the human will, a servitude, by which it obeys Satan, who drags it up and down: because those who are void of the spirit of God, it is necessary that they be subjected to the will of Satan. As is to be discerned in Saul, Judas, and many others. But how we may be freed from the kingdom of Satan, Christ submits: When I shall be exalted from the earth, I will draw all things to myself, that is, the death of Christ will destroy the kingdom of sin and death: Colossians 1 Who has delivered us from the power of darkness, and has translated us into the kingdom of the son of his love, etc. And then, making peace through the blood of his cross, etc. We are therefore freed from the kingdom of Satan through the death of Christ, believing the Gospel promising the remission of sins, obtained by the death of Christ. Now also the death of Christ mortifies those who are translated into his kingdom.

Latin:

Hoc autem dicebat, significans qua morte.

Christus hac tota concione de regno suo disserit:

Inde usque ab eo loco: Venit hora, ut clarificetur filius hominis &c. Exponit quale sit regnum suum futurum. Idque hoc epiphonemate clarissime exponitur. Duo regna proponuntur, regnum satanae, & regnum Christi. Regnum Christi sunt, in quibus iudicatus est mundus, & è quibus satan eiectus est, id est quibus revelari coepit impietas cordis humani, in quibus mortificari coepit vetus Adam, & in quos effusus est spiritus Christi, qui eos cognitione dei, timore dei, fiducia animae inflammat ad patienda & facienda omnia quae exigit deus. Vides, non in externa aliqua specie, ceremoniis, opere esse regnum Christi: sed in corde, ubi hoc iudicium sentitur. Regnum satanae sunt, in quibus non coepit iudicari caro, quibus non revelatur impietas cordis: ii vacant timore dei, agnitione, fide: & apud se contemnunt deum, & fidunt creaturis, alii sua prudentia, alii sua sanctitate, alii opibus, alii aliis huiusmodi. Sunt omnes homines, quos non renovavit spiritussanctus, de iis scribit Apostolus 2. Corinth. 4. Deum huius seculi, id est satanam, habere captiva eorum corda, ne illucescat in eis Evangelium. Porrò quia captiva habet impiorum corda satan, alios ad alia scelera impellit. Atque haec est illa egregia voluntatis humanae libertas, servitus, qua satanae sursum ac deorsum rapienti paret: quia qui spiritu dei vacant, eos necesse est subigi arbitrio satanae. Sicut in Saule, Iuda, & aliis multis est cernere. Quomodo autem liberemur à regno satanae, subiicit Christus: Ego cum exaltatus fuero à terra, omnia mecum traham, id est Christi mors destruet regnum peccati & mortis: ad Colossenses 1. Qui eripuit nos de potestate tenebrarum, & transtulit in regnum filii dilectionis suae &c. Et deinde, pacificans per sanguinem crucis &c. Liberamur ergo à regno satanae per Christi mortem, credentes Evangelio promittenti remissionem peccatorum, impetrata morte Christi. Iam & mors Christi mortificat eos qui in regnum eius transferuntur.

## 12:34
We hear from the law, that Christ.

Isaiah 9 Upon the throne of David, and upon his kingdom he shall sit, to establish it and strengthen it in judgment and justice, that is, he himself shall judge and justify from thence even unto eternity. And Daniel 7 A kingdom which shall not be corrupted. These things they cite. Observe the contradiction, the Jews think the kingdom of Christ will be a worldly and civil kingdom: and yet they confess that Christ is to remain, and to reign perpetually. Thus their own speech convicts the impious. For how shall a worldly kingdom be eternal? etc. This indeed is the rock upon which the impious Jews strike. All scripture teaches, the kingdom The internal administration of Christ, that is, that hearts are ruled by the virtue, power, and spirit of Christ. And as the prophets say: In his days shall righteousness and peace arise. For Christ implants in the hearts of believers wisdom, righteousness, peace, etc. But the ungodly understand the kingdom of Christ to be a certain honest civil administration, and the righteousness of works, where those are righteous who work well. Thus not only did the Jews of old think, that is, the teachers of the righteousness of the flesh. But the kingdom of Christ is to justify by the spirit, and to save believers, with no regard for either good or evil works. This also is to be observed, that just as Christ, although he dies, yet reigns forever: So those who are in Christ, although they die, yet live. This is what the prophets say: that they shall dwell securely, that is, although those who are Christ's are afflicted, yet they are secure that they shall not perish.

Latin:

Nos audimus ex lege, quia Christus.

Esaiae 9. Super solium David, & super regnum eius sedebit, ut confirmet illud & corroboret in iudicio & iustitia, hoc est ipse iudicabit et iustificabit inde usque in sempiternum. Et Danielis 7. Regnum quod non corrumpetur. Haec citant illi. Observa pugnantiam, Iudaei sentiunt Christi regnum fore mundanum, & civile regnum: & tamen fatentur Christum mansurum esse, & regnaturum perpetuo. Ita suus impios sermo redarguit. Quomodo enim mundanum regnum fuerit aeternum? &c. Haec vero rupes est in quem impingunt Iudaei impii. Tota scriptura docet, regnum Christi administrationem internam, hoc est corda regi virtute, potentia, spiritu Christi. Et sicut prophetae dicunt: In diebus eius orietur iustitia & pax. Inserit enim Christus in corda credentium, sapientiam, iustitiam, pacem &c. Impii vero intelligunt regnum Christi esse, honestam quandam civilem administrationem, & iustitias operum, ubi iusti sint, qui bene operantur. Sic non modo sentiebant olim Iudaei, hoc est doctores iustitiae carnis. At regnum Christi est iustificare spiritu, & salvare credentes, nullo vel bonorum, vel malorum operum respectu. Est & hoc observandum, quod sicut Christus quanquam moriatur, tamen regnat in aeternum: Ita qui in Christo sunt, quanquam moriantur, tamen vivunt. Hoc est quod prophetae dicunt: secure habitaturos, id est quanquam affligantur qui Christi sunt, tamen securi sunt se non perituros esse.

## 12:34
Who is this son of man?

Since scripture calls him the son of God, Psalm 2. Thou art my son, this day have I begotten thee. There is another question in which the ungodly are no less offended than in the former. The Jews thought that Christ would be a powerful prince before the world: but Christ lowered himself below the lot of the most abject men, tempted in all things, etc., that he might testify that he sympathizes with us who suffer.

Latin:

Quis est iste filius hominis?

Cum scriptura vocet eum filium dei, Psalmo 2. Filius meus es tu, ego hodie genui te. Altera quaestio est in qua non minus offenduntur impii, quàm in superiore. Christum sentiebant Iudaei fore potentem principem coram mundo: at Christus extenuavit se infra abiectissimorum hominum sortem, tentatus per omnia &c. ut testaretur se compati nobis patientibus.

## 12:35
Yet a little while, the light is in you.

That is, for a short time yet the word will be preached to you. The light, that is the word which illuminates. The office of the word is to illuminate: Psalm 118. Your word is a lamp to my feet, and a light to my paths, that is, the heart having conceived the word of God, knows the will of God, tastes the same things, wills the same things which God wills: it tends to the same place, whither the word directs: It trusts in no creature, it fears nothing of the assault of any creature. Then He warns that they should walk in the light, lest darkness occupy them, namely, the word being despised, and the Son of God being trampled upon, then it happens that you are delivered into a reprobate sense.

Latin:

Adhuc modicum, lumen in vobis est.

Id est adhuc brevi tempore praedicabitur vobis verbum. Lumen, id est verbum quod illuminat. Officium verbi, est illuminare: Psalmo 118. Lucerna pedibus meis verbum tuum, & lumen semitis meis, id est cor verbo dei concepto, cognoscit voluntatem dei, sapit eadem, vult eadem quae deus: tendit eôdem, quorsum dirigit verbum: Non confidit ullae creaturae, nihil metuit ullius creaturae impetum. Deinde monet ut in luce ambulent, ne tenebrae occupent eos, scilicet contempto verbo, & conculcato filio dei, tum fit ut in sensum reprobum trademini.

## 12:36
These things Jesus spoke.

It was predicted that it would be that few would believe, and likewise that there would be those who would persecute the Gospel, so that those who believe might not be scandalized by the paucity, the vileness, and other forms of the cross. For only the remnants will be converted, Isaiah 10. that is, the vilest part of the people. To the greatest and to all the wisest, the Gospel is foolishness, this is what Christ says elsewhere: Blessed is he who shall not have been scandalized in me. And Psalm 26. Do not emulate the malignant. And Luke 12. Do not fear, little flock. For Satan does this, that he may deter from the Gospel by various scandals.

Latin:

Haec locutus est Iesus.

Praedictum est fore, ut pauci credant, fore item qui persequantur Evangelium, ne scandalizentur qui credunt, paucitate, vilitate, & aliis formis crucis. Namreliquiae tantum convertentur, Esaiae 10. id est vilissima populi pars. Maximis & sapientissimis quibusque Evangelium est stultitia, hoc est quod Christus aliâs inquit: Beatus qui non fuerit in me scandalizatus. Et Psalmo 26. Noli aemulari in malignantibus. Et Lucae 12. Nolite timere pusillus grex. Nam hoc agit satan, ut variis scandalis deterreat ab Evangelio.

## 12:38
Lord, who has believed our report?

Isaiah 53, chapter describes both the death and the glory of Christ: but because the prophet sees that the Gospel will be the word of the cross, and that Christ will appear in forms other than those which the flesh approves, he therefore exclaims: And the arm of the Lord, etc. That is, because the Gospel preaches the righteousness of faith, not of works, it condemns all human works, it crucifies the flesh; who, then, is going to believe? Very few, certainly. For God has chosen the foolish and weak things of the world. And the arm, that is, the power of the Lord, to whom has it been revealed? For the divine power, judgment, and mercy cannot be known unless the Father has revealed them. Therefore, this sentence: Who has believed, etc., comforts the saints against scandals, if many wise men, holy men, etc., oppose Christ. It teaches also that the power or arm of God is not believed, nor known, except by those who are θεοδίδακτοι.

Latin:

Domine quis credidit auditui nostro?

Describit Esaias 53. capite, & mortem, & gloriam Christi: quia vero videt propheta Evangelium fore verbum crucis, Christum appariturum aliis formis, quàm quas caro probet, ideo exclamat: Et brachium domini &c. id est quia Evangelium praedicat iustitiam fidei, non operum, condemnat omnia humana opera, crucifigit carnem, quis tandem crediturus est? paucissimi certe. Nam stulta & infirma mundi elegit deus. Et brachium, id est potentia domini, cui revelata est? Neque enim cognosci potest divina potentia, iudicium, & misericordia, nisi revelarit pater. Itaque haec sententia: Quis credidit &c. consolatur sanctos adversus scandala, si adversentur Christo multi sapientes, sancti &c. Docet item non credi, nec cognosci potentiam seu brachium dei, nisi ab iis qui θεοδίδακτοι sint.

## 12:41
When he saw his glory.

The glory of God is that by which we trust, not in ourselves, but we trust in God: when he has recognized in his heart the distinction between the glory of God and the glory of men.

Latin:

Quando vidit gloriam eius.

Gloria dei est qua fidimus, non nobis, sed fidimus deo: quando agnovit corde discrimen gloriae dei, & gloriae hominum.

## 12:42
He who believes in me, does not believe in me.

He begins a new sermon, and one properly evangelical. First, from where the Gospel has proceeded: for There is frequent dispute about this passage in this whole history, and it will be disputed in all ages concerning the authority of the Gospel, for the reason that the law seems to fight with the Gospel, so that it cannot seem that both are the word of God, this is what they say at other times: We are disciples of Moses. Item, we know from where Moses was. It is a new doctrine, to be justified by faith with no regard for good or evil works. Therefore, consciences must be strengthened, lest they waver, lest they despair, when works and the uncleanness of the human heart are judged by God.

Latin:

Qui credit in me, non credit in me.

Orditur novam concionem proprieque Evangelicam. Primum, unde sit profectum Evangelium: nam de eo loco frequenter disputatur in hac tota historia, & disputabitur omnibus seculis de autoritate Evangelii, eo quod lex cum Evangelio pugnare videatur, ut videri non possit utrunque esse verbum dei, hoc est quod aliâs dicunt: Nos Moysi discipuli sumus. Item unde fuerit Moyses, scimus. Nova doctrina est, fide iustificari nullo bonorum aut malorum operum respectu. Proinde confirmandae sunt conscientiae, ne vacillent, ne desperent, cum à deo iudicantur opera & immunditia humani cordis.

## 12:45
And he who sees me etc.

That is, those who receive Christ carnally, that is, those who hear him as a teacher, imitating the works of Christ, these do not feel that the word of Christ is power, but a human ordinance. They truly receive Christ or the faith of Christ who feel that the word is the power of God, in whom the word is effective, judges, and gives life. Those who see Christ in this way, they also know the Father.

Latin:

Et qui videt me &c.

Id est, qui carnaliter accipiunt Christum, hoc est qui audiunt tanquam doctorem, opera Christi imitantes, hi non sentiunt esse potentiam verbum Christi, sed humanam ordinationem. Vere accipiunt Christum seu fidem Christi, qui sentiunt verbum esse potentiam dei, in quibus verbum est efficax, iudicat & vivificatur. Qui sic vident Christum, illi norunt & patrem.

## 12:46
I have come as a light into the world.

Light is that by which we know God, just as Jeremiah says, that he is a God doing mercy, judgment, and justice. And from this, fear and faith arise in the heart. Secondly, Christ says that he has come as a light himself into the world: so that he who believes in this, may not remain in darkness: therefore, in those whom Christ does not illuminate, it is necessary that there be darkness. Thirdly, there is darkness, which is the ignorance of both the judgment and the mercy of God. Just as we see the impious imagine God, neither as a severe judge, nor as one caring so much for us that he is present, or hears us, when there is need of his help. Thus those felt Micah 2 He will not drop upon us, confusion will not overtake us. And Ahaz, Isaiah 7 when he was tempted, because he lacked light, he could not trust in God, who was promising victory against the Syrians and Samaritans. And hearts empty of faith in temptation feel this way, why is it that you implore divine aid? God does not care for you, or look upon you. Behold, this blindness, this ignorance of the divine will toward us, he calls darkness, and free will or reason cannot remedy this darkness. Fourthly, Christ truly illuminates: that is, he who has heard Christ promising the remission of sins, and justifying him, he at the same time conceives light, by which he may know the magnitude of the divine goodness. Fifthly, from these you will gather what the Gospel is: for it is to preach that Christ is the light: but we are participants of this light not by works, but by faith. Therefore, Christian righteousness is faith, namely to believe that Christ is the one who blots out sins, and illuminates our darkness.

Latin:

Ego lux in mundum veni.

Lux est qua deum noscimus, sicut Hieremias inquit, quod sit deus faciens misericordiam, iudicium, & iustitiam. Atque hinc timor & fides exoriuntur in corde. Secundo, Christus inquit se lucem venis se in mundum: ut qui in hoc credit, non maneat in tenebris: ergo quos non illuminat Christus, in iis neces se est tenebras esse. Tertio, Sunt autem tenebrae, ignoratio & iudicii & misericordiae dei. Sicut videmus imaginare deum impios, neque severum adeo iudicem, neque tantopere curantem nos, ut adsit, exaudiat, cum eius auxilio opus fuerit. Sic sentiebant illi Micheae 2. Non stillabit super nos, non comprehendet nos confusio. Et Achas, Esaiae 7. cum tentaretur, quia luce carebat, deo fidere non potuit, promittenti victoriam adversus Syros & Samaritas. Et corda vacua fide in tentatione sic sentiunt, quid est quod implores divinam opem? Te non curat, aut respicit deus. Ecce hanc caecitatem, hanc ignorationem voluntatis divinae erga nos, tenebras vocat, atque iis tenebris non potest mederi liberum arbitrium aut ratio. Quarto, Christus vere illuminat: id est qui audierit Christo promittenti remissionem peccatorum, & iustificanti eum, is simul concipit lucem, qua magnitudinem bonitatis divinae cognoscat. Quinto ex iis colliges, quid sit Evangelium: est enim praedicare, Christum esse lucem: eius autem lucis participes non operibus, sed fide. Est ergo iustitia Christiana fides, scilicet credere Christum esse, qui deleat peccata, & illuminet tenebras nostras.

## 12:48
The word which I have spoken, that will judge him in the last day.

What the office of Christ is, must be known from the word which he preaches. He preaches, however, the remission of sins: he does not establish any law, but presents himself, promising the remission of sins to all believers, that is, to those receiving it. The consciences of men feel that they would indeed obtain life if they had fulfilled the law of God: but Christ says: Behold, I am here, I promise life: for this very reason, because you have not performed the law, I demand nothing, but I provide life to those who receive it. Through Moses the law was given, through Christ grace and truth came to be. Secondly, why does Christ deny that he came as a judge, and yet previously said, now is the judgment of the world? I answer: Christ preaches the word by which he judges the whole world, that is, that the glory of the flesh is as grass. But he does not immediately reveal this judgment to the hearts of the impious and unbelievers, until he appears in majesty: nor does he grant them to hold it for the just judgment of God, but for injustice. Therefore he denies that he judges him who does not receive the word: for to him it is not yet revealed. This judgment is, however, revealed to believers, who trust in Christ, and acknowledge that all glory of the flesh is sin. Thirdly, how does he say, by the word are unbelievers to be judged by their own? Is not the Gospel the remission of sin, which frees from judgment? This is that in which all the hope of the Christian mind is situated: that we are judged by the Gospel, not by the law, that is, that there is a regard for faith and unbelief, not for works.

Latin:

Sermo quem locutus sum, ille iudica-bit eum in novissimo.

Quod sit Christi officium, ex verbo oportet congnosci quod praedicat. Praedicat autem remissionem peccatorum: non condit legem aliquam, sed se exhibet, promittens remissionem peccatorum omnibus credentibus, hoc est accipientibus. Conscientiae hominum sentiunt se consequuturos quidem vitam, si legem dei implessent: at Christus inquit: Ecce hic adsum, vitam promitto: hanc ipsam ob causam, quia tu legem non praestitisti, nihil exigo, sed vitam praesto accipientibus. Per Moysen lex data, per Christum gratia & veritas facta est. Secundo, cur negat se Christus venisse ut iudicem, & tamen antea dixit, nunc esse iudicium mundi? Respondeo: Christus praedicat verbum quo iudicat universum mundum, id est gloriam carnis esse ceu foenum. Sed hoc iudicium non statim revelat impiorum & incredulorum cordibus, donec appareat in maiestate: neque dat eis ut habeant pro iusto iudicio dei, sed pro iniustitia. Ideo negat se iudicare eum qui non accipit verbum: nam illi nondum revelatur. Revelatur autem hoc iudicium credentibus, qui fidunt Christo, agnoscunt peccatum esse omnem gloriam carnis. Tertio, quomodo ait, sermone suo iudicandos esse incredulos? Nónne Evangelium est remissio peccati, quae à iudicio liberat? hoc est in quo spes omnis Christianae mentis sita est: quod Evangelio non lege iudicamur, id est quod fidei & incredulitatis, non operum respectus est.

## 13:1 Argument
CHAPTER THIRTEENTH.

He prefaces the history of the passion with a description of the washing of the feet. For this was as it were a certain type of the passion. Christ casts himself below all his own, and not for any cause of his own, but that he might wash their feet. For thus in the passion he was made the last of all, a worm and no man, the reproach of men and the outcast of the people, that he might make satisfaction for our sin: by which we might be certain that, although dying, yet with sin taken away through Christ, we would live. Furthermore, because Christ abolishes sin by death, and being resurrected begins life: the death of Christ effects in the saints a death, by which sin may be blotted out: the life of Christ works eternal life in the saints. And this is the true contemplation of the passion of Christ, to be mortified together with him, to be vivified together with him. For it is necessary that we be made like to the image of the son of god &c.

Latin:

CAPUT DECIMUM TERTIUM.

Praemittit historiae passionis, descriptionem lotionis pedum. Fuit enim haec passionis velut typus quidam. Deiicit se Christus infra suos omnes, neque id sua aliqua causa, sed ut illorum pedes lavet. Sic enim in passione factus est omnium novissimus, vermis & non homo, opprobrium hominum & abiectio plebis, ut pro nostro peccato satisfaciat: quo certi essemus, quanquam morientes, tamen peccato per Christum sublato, nos victuros esse. Porrò quia Christus peccatum morte abolet, & resuscitatus vitam inchoat: mors Christi efficit in sanctis mortem, qua peccatum deleatur: vita Christi in sanctis vitam aeternam operatur. Atque haec est vera contemplatio passionis Christi, simul cum ipso mortificari, simul cum ipso vivificari. Oportet enim similes fiamus imaginis filii dei &c.

## 13:1
When he had loved his own.

Two things are proposed in this place, which are to be observed in Christ who was about to undergo death. First, the cause is why he underwent death, namely love. For he says: When he had loved his own &c. he loved them unto the end, that is, he endured until he suffered: and he testified to his love by death. According to that 15. below: No one has greater love than that he lay down his life &c. This very presumption of the love of Christ towards us is faith, and the argument by which he consoles the saints. Paul to Romans 5. He commends his love &c. And 1. Corinthians 1. God is faithful, by whom you were called &c.

Latin:

Cum dilexisset suos.

Duo proponuntur hoc loco, quae in Christo mortem adituro spectanda sunt. Primum, causa est cur mortem adierit charitas. Ait enim: Cum dilexisset suos &c. in finem dilexit eos, id est perduravit donec pateretur: & morte dilectionem testatus est. Iuxta illud 15. infra: Maiorem charitatem habet nemo, quàm ut vitam ponat &c. Haec ipsa praesumptio amoris Christi erga nos, fides est, & argumentum quo consolatur sanctos. Paulus ad Romanos 5. Commendat charitatem &c. Et 1. Corinth. 1. Fidelis deus, per quem vocati &c.

## 13:3
That the father had given all things into his.

Thus far concerning the cause of death, now concerning the second, which the Evangelist says Christ knew of his death: and he adds afterwards, that he also knew that the father had given all things into his hands, and that he had come forth from God, and that he was about to go to God &c. First, these things pertain to the whole history of the events done there, namely that Christ was compelled: because he was the messenger of the father, and all things had been delivered to him by the father, to establish the testament promised in the scriptures. Moreover, that testament is the remission of sins, to which he added a sign, the communication of his body and blood, this is what is clearly set forth: Knowing that the Father had given all things into his hand, that is, knowing that the Father had subjected all things, and thus sin and death, to him. Therefore he established a testament of abolishing sin, of abolishing death: that we might know this to be ratified, he himself goes before, and by his own resurrection has testified that death is subjected to him. According to that 1. Corinth. 15. Death is swallowed up in victory: he therefore increases the confidence of pious hearts, when he indicates that all things have been delivered to Christ into his hands by the Father: that God has established his testament in Christ: that which he had promised, that the victory of sin and death is to be sought only in Christ. Secondly, by his own example he teaches that God undertakes nothing from us, unless our consciences are certain that our ministry is pleasing to God. For whatever is not done from faith, is sin. You will find those who teach the word of God, who ordain something: Here it is not enough that they do not teach depraved things, that they do not ordain impious things, but at the same time this must be provided, that they know their ministry is approved by God, that is, that God wills that these things be carried out through them. For thus Christ, he undergoes death for this reason, because he knows that he has come forth from the Father, that is, that he has been sent for this purpose. But consciences are made certain through calling.

Latin:

Quod omnia sibi pater in.

Hactenus de mortis causa, nunc de secundo, quod ait Evangelista praescisse Christum mortem: & addit postea, scisse etiam quod omnia dederit ei pater in manus, & à deo exierit, & quod ad deum esset abiturus &c. Primum, haec ad totam historiam rerum ibi gestarum pertinent, scilicet coactum esse Christum: quia patris nuncius erat, & omnia ei à patre tradita erant, condere testamentum promissum in scripturis. Est autem id testamentum remissio peccatorum, cui signum addidit, corporis & sanguinis sui communicationem, hoc est quod diserte appositum est: Sciens quod omnia dedisset in manum pater, id est sciens quod omnia, adeoque peccatum & mortem, subiecisset ei pater. Ideo testamentum abolendi peccati, abolendae mortis condidit: quod ut ratum esse sciremus, ipse praeit, & sua resurrectione testatus est mortem sibi subiectam esse. Iuxta illud 1. Corinth. 15. Absorpta est mors in victoria: auget ergo fidutiam piorum cordium, cum indicat, Christo omnia in manus esse tradita à patre: Deum in Christo condidisse testamentum suum: quod promiserat, victoriam peccati & mortis non nisi in Christo quaerendam esse. Secundo, exemplo suo docet, nihil suscipere deum à nobis, nisi conscientiae certae sint, deo placere ministerium nostrum. Quicquid enim non fit ex fide, peccatum est. Invenies qui verbum dei docent, qui ordinant aliquid: Hic non est satis, quod non docent prava, quod non ordinant impia, sed simul hoc est praestandum, ut sciant, suum ministerium deo probari, hoc est, velle deum ut per ipsos haec gerantur. Sic enim Christus, ideo mortem obit, quia scit se à patre exisse, id est in hoc missum esse. Redduntur autem conscientiae certae per vocationem.

## 13:6
Lord, do you wash my feet?

The contention of Christ and Peter indicates what the washing of the feet signified. For when Christ says: Unless I wash you, you will have no part with me: Item: He who has been washed, does not need except to wash his feet: certainly he speaks of the fruit of the passion. For the sprinkling of the blood of Christ is that by which we are sanctified. Moreover, in this contention the purpose is the scandal of the cross, which Peter denies he will commit, that he should be washed by the Lord.
