The flesh feels that it is a deed unworthy of divine majesty, that Christ casts himself down so far, that he performs the most vile office. Reason cannot imagine that the Lord of glory is he who has cast himself down so far, that he has been made a curse by God. Moses weakened the authority of Dathan and Abiram, in that he predicted they would die by a new kind of death (and yet Christ was affected by a most contumelious death) by which example he elevates the authority of the word of Dathan. Therefore reason feels that reverence and glory are owed to Christ. Secondly, Peter ought not to have turned away from this humiliation of Christ, but himself to be mortified together with him, to acknowledge the will of the Father, to give thanks for such great mercy, that he gave his son for the sins of the world: and the rest which a penitent heart feels, and one truly acknowledging Christ. For this is the acknowledgment of Christ, to feel that Christ has been humbled below us, that he stands by below us, so that we may discern how he does not act as Lord, but as a minister, who makes us of more value than himself. Thirdly, you see that carnal worship is an ignorance of the divine will. It is argued For Peter says: What I do, you do not know, etc. Fourth, in this way the flesh is always offended by the vileness of Christ, it persecutes him, because it cannot acknowledge him for Christ. And our hearts (because it is necessary to be mortified together with Christ) cannot feel that he is the savior, who condemns and mortifies all our things, justice, wisdom, honor. Just as the children of Israel could not believe, when led out into the desert, they labored under the scarcity of all things, that they had been called out by the Lord from Egypt, but they judged that they had been led out by the human counsel of Moses.

Latin:

Domine tu mihi lavas pedes?

Contentio Christi & Petri indicat quid significarit lotio pedum. Nam cum Christus ait: Nisi lavero te, non habebis partem mecum: Item: Qui lotus est, non indiget nisi ut pedes lavet: certe de fructu passionis loquitur. Nam aspersio sanguinis Christi, est qua sanctificamur. Est autem in hac contentione propositum scandalum crucis, quod neget Petrus se commis surum, ut à domino lavetur. Sentit caro, indignum esse facinus maiestate divina, quod se deiicit Christus eò usque, ut vilissimum officium faciat. Non potest ratio imaginari, dominum gloriae eum esse, qui se eò usque deiecerit, ut maledictum sit à deo factus. Moyses labefecit autoritatem Dathan & Abiron, eo quod morituros novo mortis genere praedixit (& Christus tamen contumeliosissima morte affectus est) quo exemplo Dathan verbi autoritatem elevet. Sentit ergo ratio, deberi Christo reverentiam & gloriam. Secundo, debuit Petrus non aversari hanc Christi humiliationem, sed ipse unà mortificari, agnoscere voluntatem patris, agere gratias pro tanta misericordia, quod filium dederit pro peccatis mundi: & reliqua quae sentit coruere paenitens, & Christum vere agnoscens. Haec est enim agnitio Christi, sentire quod infra nos humiliatus sit Christus, quod infra nos assistat, ut cernamus quàm non agat dominum, sed ministrum, qui nos pluris se facit. Tertio, vides carnalem cultum esse ignorationem voluntatis divinae. Arguitur enim Petrus: Quod facio, tu nescis &c. Quarto, ad hunc modum semper offenditur caro vilitate Christi, persequitur eum, quod pro Christo agnoscere non possit. Et corda nostra (quia unà cum Christo mortificari oportet) non possunt sentire, eum esse salvatorem, qui damnet & mortificet omnia nostra, iustitiam, sapientiam, honorem. Sicut nec filii Israël credere poterant, cum educti in desertum, laborarent omnium rerum penuria, quod à domino evocati essent ex Aegypto, sed humano Moysi consilio, iudicabant se eductos esse.

## 13:7
What I do, you do not know.

Here Peter is argued of ignorance, lest anyone think that the reverence proceeding from the estimation of the flesh was the work of the Holy Spirit, which certainly was in Peter. Yet he was not yet sufficiently confirmed against the scandal of the cross, which Luke also indicates, when Christ was predicting the passion, he adds: But they understood none of these things.

Latin:

Quod ego facio, nescis.

Hic arguitur ignorantiae Petrus, ne quis putet reverentiam à carnis aestimatione profectam, spiritus sancti opus fuisse, qui certe erat in Petro. Nondum tamen contra scandalum crucis satis confirmatus erat, id quod indicat & Lucas, cum Christus praediceret passionem, subiicit: Ipsi vero horum nihil intellexerunt.

## 13:7
But you will know afterwards.

Namely when you shall have seen both my death and my resurrection. For the experience of temptation will teach that it is necessary for you to be cast down, and to be glorified through the cross.

Latin:

Scies autem postea.

Scilicet ubi videris & mortem, & resurrectionem meam. Experientia enim tentationis docebit, oportere vos deiici, & per crucem glorificari.

## 13:8
You shall never wash my feet.

An example of stubbornness, and of the opinions of the flesh, and of righteousness. For before we are enlightened, we cannot but approve the flower or glory of the flesh. Known is the stubbornness of Thomas, John 20. and of David, 1 Chron. 21. and of Josiah, 2 Chron. 25. and Prov. 14. There is a way which seems right to a man &c. And these examples warn us that we should believe the word of God against the sense of the flesh: likewise that we should sustain the works of God against the judgment of the flesh.

Latin:

Non lavabis mihi pedes in aeternum.

Exemplum pertinaciae, & opinionum carnis, & iustitiae. Nam priusquam illuminemur, non possumus non probare florem seu gloriam carnis. Nota est pertinacia Thomae, Ioannis 20. & Davidis, 1. Paralip. 21. & Iosiae, 2. Paralip. 25. & Proverb. 14. Est via quae videtur homini iusta &c. Monentque haec exempla, ut verbo dei contra sensum carnis credamus: item opera dei sustineamus contra carnis iudicium.

## 13:8
If I do not wash you, you will not have.

Here Christ shows what the fruit of the washing is, namely that this washing represents the sprinkling of blood, by which Christ sanctifies the faithful, as if he were saying thus: In that you have this honor for me, that you do not permit yourself to be washed, you justify and sanctify yourself. For the flesh justifies itself by such worship. But come now, you err, for you will not be justified by such worship, unless I wash you: unless I myself, having been put to death, sanctify you sprinkled with my blood, you will not be justified.

Latin:

Si non lavero te, non habebis.

Hic Christus ostendit quis sit lotionis fructus, scilicet quod haec lotio repraesentet aspersionem sanguinis, qua sanctificat Christus fideles, quasi sic diceret: Quod hoc mihi honoris habes, ne permittas te lavari, iustificas & sanctificas te. Caro enim iustificat se tali cultu. Age vero erras, neque enim tali cultu iustificaberis, nisi lavem te: nisi ipse mortificatus, sanguine te meo aspersum sanctificem, non iustificaberis.

## 13:9
Lord, not my feet only.

Peter lets go of his stubbornness. For thus faith is accustomed to present us wholly to the divine will, so that it may deal with us however it finally pleases. We do not even wish to discern the counsel of God; he himself renews the outcome, he himself the way for by his own will. Such is that Davidic saying: If you say to me I am ready, you do not please. And here it is to discern, whatever of faith, which, when the Lord is tempting, so resigns itself to Him, that it trusts. And this is the worship and justice which God approves, not that carnal reverence.

Latin:

Domine non tantum pedes.

Remittit Petrus pertinaciam. Sic enim solet fides exhiberi nos voluntati divinae totos, ut nobiscum agat illa utcunque tandem libet. Nos cernere consilium dei ne volumus quidem, ipse exitum, ipse viam innovat pro sua voluntate. Quale illud est Davidicum: Si dixeris mihi praesto sum, non places. Atque hic est cernere, quaevis fidei, quae tentante domino sic se resignat illi, ut confidat. Estque hic cultus & iustitia, quam probat deus, non illa carnalis reverentia.

## 13:10
He who is washed, does not need.

It seems it can be understood most simply thus: you are all washed except for the feet, that is, the saints are washed, they have begun to be sanctified, yet sin has not yet been emptied out: but it is emptied out when Christ washes, that is, when He mortifies the flesh, and sprinkles us with blood, etc. Moreover, there are remnants of sin in the feet, that is, in the mind, which acts and carries us men. For that the mind is signified by the feet is clear from Ephesians: Having your feet shod with the preparation of the gospel of peace. Furthermore, what He says, he who is washed, openly declares that He is speaking of spiritual washing. Secondly, see the power of grace; He had already denied that they were clean, as those whose feet had not yet been washed: and in Peter He had already rebuked sin: afterwards He adds: You are clean. So that He may teach that although there is sin in the flesh, yet it is absorbed and forgiven by grace to faith. Just as Romans 8 teaches: There is now no condemnation to those who are in Christ. In sum, this washing represents both the humiliation of Christ, and the fruit which returns to us from it, of whom He has been made a minister is. Let the troubled conscience therefore contemplate Christ expressed in this type, in which He exhibited Himself to us, not as Lord, not as Judge, but as a minister. And He cast Himself below us, so that we might perceive that our salvation is a care to Him. Secondly, the fruit of the washing is the sprinkling of the blood by which we are sanctified. Moreover, the washing is admitted by faith. When we believe that He has been made a minister to us, that He washes us, then certainly we are washed, just as Peter also began to be washed when he began to believe.

Latin:

Qui lotus est, non indiget.

Simplicissime videtur sic intelligi posse, totus lotus es praeter pedes, id est sancti loti sunt, sanctificari coeperunt, nondum tamen evacuatum est peccatum: evacuatur autem cum lavat Christus, hoc est cum mortificat carnem, & aspergit nos sanguine &c. Sunt autem reliquiae peccati in pedibus, id est in mente, quae agit et fert nos homines. Significare enim mentem pedibus ex Ephe. liquet: Calciati pedes Evangelio pacis. Porrò quod ait, qui lotus est, palàm declarat se loqui de spirituali lotione. Secundo, vide vim gratiae, iam negarat eos mundos esse, ut quorum pedes nondum loti essent: & in Petro peccatum iam reprehenderat: postea subiicit: Vos mundi estis. ut doceat quodquanquam sit in carne peccatum, tamen gratia absorberi & condonari fidei. Sicut Roma. 8. docet: Nihil nunc damnationis est in iis, qui sunt in Christo. In summa, repraesentat haec lotio et deiectionem Christi, & fructum qui inde ad nos redit, quorum minister factus est. Contempletur igitur conscientia confusa Christum in hoc typo expressum, in quo se exhibuit nobis, non dominum, non iudicem, sed ministrum. Abiecitque se infra nos, ut curae sibi esse salutem nostram cerneremus. Secundo, fructus lotionis est aspersio sanguinis qua sanctificamur. Admittitur autem lotio fide. Cum credimus quod nobis sit minister factus, quod nos lavet, tum certe abluimur, sicut & Petrus lavare coepit, ubi credere coepit.

## 13:12
After therefore He washed.

After He exhibited the image of His abasement, and indicated the fruit of the abasement, namely that through the cross He Himself is glorified and glorifies: now He adapts the same to us, so that He may commend the cross to us. For because it is necessary that sin be washed away by death, it is necessary that the Christian life be a life of the cross: this therefore He commends by His own example, so that we may perceive that death is that by which we may be glorified.

Latin:

Postquam ergo lavit.

Posteaquam exhibuit imaginem deiectionis suae, & fructum deiectionis indicavit, scilicet quod per crucem glorificetur ipse & glorificet: nunc eandem ac commodat ad nos, ut commendet nobis crucem. Nam quia morte peccatum ablui oportet, necesse est, vitam Christianam esse crucis vitam: hanc igitur commendat exemplo suo, ut mortem sentiamus esse quo glorificemur.

## 13:16
Amen amen I say to you, etc.

Here Judas is admonished for the third time, and not simply:

For he ought to have been moved, if not by words, certainly by the miserable spectacle of such great perturbation in Christ. But the more Christ languishes, the more firmly he is hardened. And it happens in all the enemies of Christ, that the weaker Christ becomes, the less they pity, etc. as Psalm 68 Upon the pain of my wounds.

Latin:

Amen amen dico vobis &c.

Hic tertio admonetur Iudas, neque simpliciter:

debuit enim moveri, si non verbis, certe miserabili spectaculo tantae perturbationis in Christo. Sed quo Christus magis languescit, eo firmius ille induratur. Acciditque in omnibus inimicis Christi, ut quo fit infirmior Christus, eo minus miserescant &c. ut Psalmo 68. Super dolorem vulnerum meorum.

## 13:18
He who eats bread with me, shall lift up.

He resigns Psalm. 40. his glory to Christ, and complains of those who, offended in the forms of the cross, in Christ and his body, despise and persecute him.

For because the Gospel is foolishness to the flesh, the world cannot but hate the Evangelists. The Gospel teaches that one must not trust in the arm of flesh, that human wisdom is blindness, that human holiness is contempt of God. Those who feel these things within themselves, and do not strive for the arduous things which the world admires, are mocked by the world, and much more so those who teach these things. Here Christ consoles the pious with his own example in the psalm, so that they may endure on the cross, lest they themselves also, being scandalized, fall away, for it will be that those who sustain the cross shall be glorified. Just as he says in the Psalm: You have set me up in your sight forever. But who those are of whom Christ complains, this place indicates, which has been cited: He who eats bread with me, that is, those who boast of the Gospel, these are a scandal to the Gospel, and to them in turn the Gospel is a scandal.

He who receives whomever I send.

It follows aptly, he who receives whom I send, that is, because my ministers will be endowed with the same forms, the world will attempt to trample these as well: therefore I now confirm the pious, lest they be offended by the insignificance of the ministers.

This sentence has been twisted toward human traditions, but it pertains only to those who announce the word of God. For they alone are sent by Christ, this is what he says, whom I send. And therefore he also says in Matthaei 10.: He who receives a prophet in the name of a prophet, etc. For those alone are prophets who bring the word of God. But that those who do not bring the word of God are not sent, is clear from that John 10 He who does not enter by the door, etc. And from Jeremiah 25 I did not send them, etc. I did not speak to them, etc. Secondly, it is therefore evident that this sentence is to be understood concerning the word of God alone. Now you will also observe here, Christ testifies in this place, that the word announced through the apostles is that very thing by which hearts are justified: and that no other bodily voice is to be expected from heaven, nor signs, etc., so that hearts may certainly feel that the remission of sins has occurred, that they are justified, if they believe the voice of the minister. Christ is troubled, because the life, the glory of the word, the salvation of Judah, and of the whole people is endangered: as in Psalm 68 Confusion has covered my face. And he is troubled so that Christ may divest himself of all forms of God, his wisdom, and his strength, and resign them to the Father: as in Isaiah 53 Because his soul has labored, he shall see and be satisfied. And Psalm 58 I will keep my strength for you. And by this example it is to be discerned how faith abdicates all our own things, and expects consolation from the Father alone.

Latin:

Qui manducat mecum panem, levabit.

Resignat Psalm. 40. gloriam suam Christo, & queritur de iis qui offensi in formis crucis, in Christo & eius corpore, contemnunt et persequuntur ipsum.

Quia enim Evangelium carni stultitia est, non potest non odisse mundus Evangelistas. Docet Evangelium, non esse fidendum brachio carnis, humanam sapientiam esse caecitatem, humanam sanctitatem esse contemptum dei. Haec qui sentiunt apud se, & non contendunt ad ardua quae mundus miratur, ridentur à mundo, multo magis qui haec docent. Hic Christus exemplo suo consolatur pios in psalmo, ut perdurent in cruce, ne scandalizati ipsi quoque desciscant, fore ut glorificentur qui sustinent crucem. Sicut in Psalmo inquit: Erexisti me in conspectu tuo in aeternum. Sed qui sint de quibus querit Christus, indicat hic locus, qui citatus est: Qui manducat mecum panem, id est qui gloriantur de Evangelio, isti scandalo sunt Evangelio, & eis vicissim scandalo est Evangelium.

Qui recipit quencunque misero.

Apte sequitur, qui accipit quem misero, id est quia iisdem formis praediti erunt ministri mei, conabitur hos quoque mundus conculcare: ideo iam nunc confirmo pios, ne tenuitate ministrorum offendantur.

Haec sententia detorta est ad traditiones humanas, pertinet autem ad eos tantum, qui verbum dei nunciant. Nam ii soli sunt missi à Christo, hoc est quod dicit, quem misero. Ideoque & Matthaei 10. ait: Qui recipit prophetam in nomine prophetae &c. Sunt enim prophetae solum ii, qui verbum dei afferunt. Quod vero non sint missi, qui verbum dei non afferunt, liquet ex eo Ioannis 10. Qui non intrat per ostium &c. Et ex. 25. Hieremiae: Non mittebam eos &c. Non loquebar ad eos &c. Secundo, constat ergo, hanc sententiam de solo verbo dei intelligendam esse. Iam & hic observabis, Christus testificatur hoc loco, verbum per apostolos annunciatum, illud ipsum esse quo iustificentur corda: nec aliam ultra vocem corporalem è caelo spectandam esse, nec signa &c. ut certo corda sentiant, contigisse remissionem peccatorum, iustificari se, si credant voce ministri. Perturbatus est Christus, quia vita, gloria verbi, salus Iudae, & totius populi periclitatur: sicut Psalmo 68. Operuit confusio faciem meam. Perturbaturque ut exuat se Christus omnibus dei formis, sapientia, & fortitudine sua, resignetque eas patri: ut Esaiae 53. Pro eo quod laboravit anima eius, videbit & saturabitur. Et Psalmo 58. fortitudinem meam à te custodiam. Atque hoc exemplo est cernere, quomodo abdicet nostra omnia fides, & expectet consolationem à solo patre.

## 13:21
And when He had said these things, He was troubled in spirit.

The beginning of the passion is this perturbation and confusion of Christ, which assailed Him perceiving the sin of Judas the traitor. And the temptation is overcome, by resigning all those things which were impending to the divine will. Just as also testifies. below: Now is the son of man glorified. He resigns, however, first his life, then the greed of Judas who was to be saved: and not of him only, but also of the people whom he represented here, and of the pseudo-church which he figured here. Thirdly, the grief conceived from the magnitude of the scandal, that from the disciples, and one who had not been the lowest, he should fall away: then the weak were alienated, about to think ill of Christ, if indeed those had departed, by whom he had been most familiarly known. He laments these things in Psalm. 40. which is cited here. They refer to this Judas, who are a scandal to the Gospel, and abuse the name of Christ to satisfy their own greeds. How all these things struck the mind of Christ, no one could conceive in speech. Let us discern the example of one resigning all these things to the divine will, which affection of Christ the psalms treat most copiously. Hence it can be understood, why Judas is so sedulously warned: You are not all clean. And that: I do not say of you all. And below: One of you shall betray me &c. admonishing Judas, desiring to save him, and that the scandal be diminished.

Latin:

Et cum haec dixisset, turbatus est spiritu.

Initium passionis est haec perturbatio & confusio Christi, quae incessit cernentem Iudae proditoris peccatum. Et vincitur tentatio, resignanda ea omnia quae imminebant, divinae voluntati. Sicut & testatur infra: Nunc clarificatus est filius hominis. Resignat autem primum vitam suam, deinde servandi Iudae cupiditatem: nec huius tantum, sed & populi quem hic repraesentabat, & pseudoecclesiae quam hic figurabat. Tertio, dolorem è scandali magnitudine conceptum, quod ex discipulis & qui non fuerat infimus, desciscat: tum imbecilles alienabantur male sensuri de Christo, siquidem ii discessissent, quibus familiarissime cognitus fuerat. Haec deplorat Psalm. 40. qui hic citatur. Hunc Iudam referunt, qui Evangelio scandalo sunt, & nomine Christi abutuntur ad explendas suas cupiditates. Haec omnia quomodo perculerint animum Christo, nemo oratione concipi queat. Nos exemplum cernamus resignantis haec omnia divinae voluntati, quem affectum Christi psalmi multo copiosissime tractant. Hinc intelligi potest, cur tam sedulo moneatur Iudas: Non estis mundi omnes. Et id: Non de omnibus vobis dico. Et infra: Unus ex vobis tradet me &c. admonens Iudam, cupiens illum servare, & minui scandalum.

## 13:27
What you do, do quickly.

Does Christ command that Judas should proceed? I answer: It is the judgment of God, by which he blinds the hardened, as Psalm 68 Pour out upon them your wrath, and let the fury of your anger overtake them, etc. And it is a curse rather than a command, as if he were saying thus: Now I have warned you so many times in vain: therefore go that you may perish. As Isaiah 6 Hear hearing, and do not understand. And Jeremiah 25 Drink, and be drunken, and vomit, and fall. And it is an example proposed to blasphemers and despisers of the Gospel, who, when they hear the judgment of God being preached, do not repent nevertheless. Secondly, at the same time he here resigns all those things of which we spoke before, the glory of the word, and the salvation of Judas: because Judas was about to betray him, he scandalizes the word: therefore he says: What you do, do quickly: as Luke 12 he says: How I am constrained until it be accomplished? For Christ burns that the preaching of the Gospel be hastened: likewise that the time of this people be shortened. But no one knew this, because the sin of Judas had not yet been brought forth, in case by chance he might repent.

Latin:

Quod facis, fac citius.

Iubet'ne Christus, ut pergat Iudas? Respondeo: Iudicium dei est, quo excaecat induratos, sicut Psalm. 68. Effunde super eos iram tuam, & furor irae tuae comprehendat eos &c. Estque maledictio potius quàm mandatum, quasi sic diceret: Iam toties frustra moneo te: ergo vade ut pereas. ut Esaiae 6. Audite audientes, & nolite intelligere. Et Hieremiae 25. Bibite, & inebriamini, & vomite, et cadite. Esque blasphemis & contemptoribus Evangelii exemplum propositum, qui cum audiunt iudicium dei praedicari, non resipiscunt tamen. Secundo, simul hic resignat ea omnia, de quibus ante diximus, gloriam verbi, & salutem Iudae: quia Iudas eum proditurus erat, scandalizat verbum: ergo dicit: Quod facis, fac citius: sicut Lucae 12. inquit: Quomodo angor dum perficiatur? Ardet enim Christus, maturari praedicationem Evangelii: item abbreviari tempus huius populi. Hoc autem nemo scivit, quia nondum proditum fuit peccatum Iudae, si forte resipisceret.

## 13:30
When therefore he received the morsel.

After Judas departed, he began to preach both the passion and the glorification. And this is the sum of this passage. Since it must be suffered, that I may glorify the father, that I may be obedient to the father, that by my death I may satisfy for the sins of the world: therefore do not be scandalized by my death. For by this also the father is glorified, and he in turn will glorify me, and will declare that I have taken away the sins of others, and that by my death sin, and by my resurrection death, has been emptied, etc.

Latin:

Cum ergo ille accepit buccellam.

Posteaquam Iudas abiit, praedicare coepit & passionem, & glorificationem. Estque haec huius loci summa. Siquidem patiendum est, ut patrem clarificem, ut patri sim obediens, ut mea morte pro peccatis mundi satisfaciam: ideo nolite scandalizari mea morte. Nam & hac pater glorificatur, & ipse vicissim glorificabit me, declarabitque, tulisse me aliena peccata, & mea morte peccatum, mea resurrectione mortem evacuatam esse &c.

## 13:31
If God is glorified in him.

God is glorified when those things which are owed to him are attributed to him. Obedience of the son was owed to the father, likewise wisdom, fortitude, etc. were owed, so that the son might not glorify himself, claiming glory by his own wisdom or power, but might wait to be glorified by the father. In that manner the psalms sing: Bring glory to God. And Isaiah 6. Sanctify the Lord of hosts, etc. To the Romans 4. Paul says: that Abraham gave glory to God, that is, because God had promised Abraham that he would give him seed, etc. he believed, and by that faith he doubted nothing to resign the son to God:

When he was commanded to offer, he yielded from his own wisdom which judged that, with his son killed and his wife sterile, no other offspring would occur. Thus he prays & Daniel cap. 9. To you O Lord is justice, but to us confusion of face. And this is the glory which is owed to God, to acknowledge that he is just, strong, wise, merciful &c. Thus Christ, because he had been sent to suffer, obeys the Father: he resigns his life and his glory to the Father, awaiting consolation from him, as he says in the Psalm: You are my glory, and the one lifting up my head &c. How do the words stand, when at the beginning he says: Now he is glorified, and afterwards, God will glorify him? I answer that what he says: Now he is glorified &c. is to be understood concerning the glory which he has before the Father, in that, having been made obedient, he resigned glory to him. Thus therefore he says: Now the Son of man is glorified, that is, now having been emptied he resigned himself to the Father: which emptying before the Father is the glory of the Son, although that glory has not yet been revealed in the Son. He is glorified therefore before God, in that the emptying is the glory of the Son before God. And God is glorified in him, that is, the Son glorifies the Father through this, that he resigns all things to him. And he makes it testified that he trusts in the Father's wisdom and fortitude, not his own: and before the world he declares that the Father's power is glorified.

Latin:

Si deus clarificatus est in eo.

Deus clarificatur, quando tribuuntur ei quae debentur. Debebatur patri obedientia filii, debebatur item sapientia, fortitudo &c. ne ipse se filius glorificaret, vindicans sua sapientia aut potentia gloriam, sed expectaret glorificari à patre. Ad eum modum canunt psalmi: Afferte gloriam deo. Et Esaiae 6. Dominum exercituun sanctificate &c. Ad Romanos 4. ait Paulus: Abraham gloriam dedisse deo, id est quia deus promiserat Abrahae, se daturum esse semen &c. credebat, eaque fide nihil dubitabat resignare deo filium:

cum offerre iuberetur, cessit de sua sapientia quae iudicabat, occiso filio, uxore sterili non obventuram aliam prolem. Sic orat & Daniel cap. 9. Tibi domine iustitia, nobis autem confusio faciei. Atque haec est gloria quae deo debetur, agnoscere quod iustus sit, fortis, sapiens, misericors &c. Sic Christus quia missus erat ut pateretur, obedit patri: vitam, gloriam suam resignat patri, expectans ab illo consolationem, sicut in Psalmo dicit: Tu es gloria mea, & exaltans caput meum &c. Quomodo constant verba, cum principio dicat: Nunc clarificatus est, & postea, deus clarificabit eum? Respondeo, quod ait: Nunc clarificatus est &c. accipiendum est de gloria, quam habet coram patre, in eo quod obediens factus, resignavit ei gloriam. Sic ergo dicit: Nunc clarificatus est filius hominis, id est nunc exinanitus resignavit se patri: quae exinanitio coram patre, gloria filii est, quamquam ea gloria nondum sit revelata in filio. Clarificatus est igitur coram deo, eo quod exinanitio sit gloria filii coram deo. Et deus clarificatus est in eo, hoc est filius glorificat patrem per hoc, quod resignat ei omnia. Et testatum facit quod fidat patris sapientia & fortitudine, non sua: & coram mundo declarat, quod patris potentia glorificetur.

## 13:31
And God will glorify him.

As if he were saying, the Father will reveal his glory, according to that 1 Kings 2 Those who honor me, I glorify. And Heb. 5 He was heard for his reverence: that is, because he gave glory to the Father, therefore he was heard, he was glorified.

Latin:

Et deus clarificabit eum.

Quasi dicat, pater revelabit eius gloriam, iuxta illud 1. Reg. 2. Honorificantes me, glorifico. Et Heb. 5. Exauditus est pro sua reverentia: id est quia dedit patri gloriam, ideo exauditus, glorificatus est.

## 13:33
Little children, yet a little while I am with you.

As if he were saying, I am now preaching the passion to you, so that you may believe it is not fortuitous, as if I were rejected by God, but that it must be suffered by the will of the Father, and that it will come to pass that I shall be glorified by this passion. Furthermore, where I shall have gone, I will begin a spiritual kingdom, where you also will be mortified, just as I am mortified.

Latin:

Filioli adhuc paulisper vobiscum sum.

Quasi diceret, iam praedico vobis passionem, ut credatis non esse fortuitam, tanquam à deo reiectus sim, sed voluntate patris patiendum esse, foreque ut hac passione glorificer. Praeterea ubi abiero, regnum spirituale incipiam, ubi vos quoque mortificabimini, sicut ego sum mortificatus.

## 13:33
You will seek me.

You will desire to be glorified also, just as I said to the Jews: Where I am, you cannot come. Where I go, you cannot come: that is, you are neither able to bear the cross yet, nor will you be glorified immediately. For it is necessary that you wait, until you are clothed with power from on high, and until the sons of God are revealed.

Latin:

Quaeretis me.

Desyderabitis vos quoque glorificari, sicut dixi Iudaeis: Ubi ego sum, vos non potestis venire. Quò ego vado, vos non potestis venire: id est neque crucem ferre adhuc potestis, neque mox glorificabimini. Oportet enim ut expectetis, donec induamini virtute exalto, & dum revelentur filii dei.

## 13:33
So also I say to you.

That is, the Jews will never arrive, they will never be glorified, you will be glorified. But they had not yet received the fullness, or the tithes of the Holy Spirit, therefore they could not bear the cross.

Latin:

Ita & vobis dico.

Id est Iudaei nunquam pervenient, nunquam glorificabuntur, vos glorificabimini. Sed nondum acceperant plenitudinem, seu decimas spiritussancti, ideo non potuerunt ferre crucem.

## 13:34
A new commandment I give unto you.

That the Gospel is not a law, it is sufficiently evident: for it is the remission of sins, according to that Luke last: Preach the remission of sins. What then, regarding what he says here, A new commandment? I answer: After Christ has justified believers, then he teaches what the use of the law is. Those who use the law outside of Christ, use it in such a way that they justify themselves through the law, which is impious. For it is impossible to be justified by the law, Rom. 8. And there is righteousness through faith only in Christ, which those who have obtained through Christ, to them Christ now shows the use of the law, namely that the law must be used, not for this that it may justify us, but as a testimony of justification. For it is necessary that the law of God be fulfilled, as Matthew 5. and this is what he says: By this shall all men know, that you are my disciples. He does not say, by this you shall be disciples: because it is necessary that they have already been made disciples through faith. Charity is a testimony of faith etc. It is therefore a new commandment, because this use of the law was first revealed through the Gospel, that the law does not justify, but is a testimony of justification. Secondly, it is a new commandment, because it commands from the heart, nothing concerning external rites or ceremonies:

as if he were saying: The Law bound love to certain ceremonies: I simply command that you become all things to all men, to the Gentiles as Gentiles: to the Jews as Jews, I demand nothing beyond charity, as Rom. 13. This is the sum of the law, Love your neighbor as yourself. Thirdly, the Pharisaical reason understands the law concerning charity only, concerning external duties: not concerning the love of the heart, and as far as the law is letter only, that is, as far as it is understood only by the flesh. The old word is of the old man etc. But it becomes a new word when it is preached with a renewed heart: because now it is understood differently, it is done differently than it was understood or done before the renewal or illumination of the heart. The Law is always letter, until it is illuminated by the Holy Spirit.

Latin:

Mandatum novum do vobis.

Evangelium non esse legem, satis constat: est enim remissio peccatorum, iuxta illud Lucae ultimo: Praedicate remissionem peccatorum. Quid igitur, quod hic ait, Mandatum novum? Respondeo: Postea quàm iustificavit credentes Christus, tum docet qui sit legis usus. Qui utuntur lege extra Christum, sic utuntur, ut se iustificent per legem, id quod impium est. Nam impossibile est lege iustificari, Rom. 8. Estque iustitia per fidem tantum in Christum, quam qui sunt per Christum consecuti, illis iam Christus ostendit usum legis, scilicet lege utendum esse, non in hoc ut nos iustificet, sed tanquam testimonio iustificationis. Oportet enim legem dei fieri, ut Matthaei 5. atque hoc est quod inquit: In hoc cognoscent omnes, quod discipuli meisitis. Non dicit, per hoc eritis discipuli: quia oportet iam ante factos esse discipulos per fidem. Charitas testimonium est fidei &c. Est itaque novum mandatum, quia hic usus legis primum revelatus est per Evangelium, quod lex non iustificet, sed sit testimonium iustificationis. Secundo, novum mandatum est, quia praecipit de corde, nihil de externis ritibus aut ceremoniis:

quasi sic diceret: Lex alligabat dilectionem ad certas ceremonias: ego simpliciter praecipio, ut fiatis omnia omnibus, gentibus gentes: Iudaeis Iudaei, praeter charitatem nihil exigo, ut Rom. 13. Haec est summa legis, Dilige proximum tuum sicut teipsum. Tertio pharisaica ratio intelligit legem de charitate tantum, de externis officiis: non de dilectione cordis, & quoad lex litera tantum est, hoc est quoad à carne tantum intelligitur. Vetus verbum est veteris hominis &c. Fit autem novum verbum, cum innovato corde praedicatur: quia iam aliter intelligitur, aliter fit quàm intelligebatur aut fiebat ante innovationem aut illuminationem cordis. Lex semper litera, donec illuminetur per spiritum sanctum.

## 13:34
That you love one another.

With love unfeigned, and in such a way as I have humbled myself beneath you. And in this all shall know: that is, this shall be the testimony of your justification.

Latin:

Ut diligatis invicem.

Charitate non simulata, & ita ut ego humiliavi me infra vos. Et in hoc cognoscent omnes: id est hoc erit testimonium iustificationis vestrae.

## 13:36
Whither I go, thou canst not follow me now.

A most grave example of confirming faith, the apostles proposed themselves, just as Paul also says, that he was converted, so that he might be an example to those who were about to believe essence. It is argued that in the apostles there was at one time diffidence, at another ignorance, at another something else. Thus it is to discern both the companionship of Christ, tolerating our sins, justifying although we are sinners. It is also to see how through sins he instructs the saints. A little above, the stubborn ignorance of Peter is proposed, when he held honor for Christ in a certain carnal manner. Here also the presumption of Peter is described: Because truly nature is adjudged to death on account of sin (On whatever day you shall eat, you shall die the death) it cannot but succumb to death: therefore no powers of free will, no carnal intentions can tolerate death. But it is the glory of God, which vivifies the hearts of the dying with his spirit, so that now death may not be death. Just as Psalm 21 says: Their hearts shall live for ever and ever. That is, although they die, yet life in the heart absorbs the force of death. And it is the work of God vivifying those who believe. Thus Psalm 43 says: Their arm shall not save them, but thy right hand and thy arm, and the light of thy countenance, because thou wast pleased in them &c. And 1 Corinthians 15 Thanks be to God, who hath given us the victory through our Lord Jesus Christ. And faith is proved by such temptations, where now no creatures bring aid: just as through the whole life, in poverty and infamy, where counsel has failed reason.

Latin:

Quò ego vado, non potes me modò sequi.

Gravissimum exemplum confirmandae fidei, apostoli seipsos proposuerunt, sicut & Paulus inquit, se conversum esse, ut esset exemplum iis, qui credituri essent. Arguitur in apostolis aliâs diffidentia, aliâs ignorantia, aliâs alia. Sic est cernere & comitatem Christi, tolerantis peccata nostra, iustificantis quanquam peccatores. Est & videre, quomodo per peccata erudiat sanctos. Paulò superius proponitur pertinax Petri ignorantia, quum carnali quodam more Christo honorem habebat. Hic & praesumptio Petri describitur: Quia vero natura adiudicata morti est propter peccatum (Quacunque die comederis, morte morieris) non potest non succumbere morti: ideo nullae vires liberi arbitrii, nullae carnales intentiones possunt mortem tolerare. Sed gloria dei est, quae morientium corda vivificat spiritu suo, ut iam mors non sit mors. Sicut Psalm. 21. dicit: Vivent corda eorum in seculum seculi. id est quanquam moriantur, tamen vita in corde absorbet vim mortis. Estque id opus dei vivificantis credentes. Sic Psalm. 43. dicit: Brachium eorum non salvabit eos, sed dextera tua & brachium tuum, & illuminatio vultus tui, quoniam complacuisti in eis &c. Et 1. Corinth. 15. Deo gratia, qui dedit nobis victoriam per dominum nostrum Iesum Christum. Probaturque fides huiusmodi tentationibus, ubi iam nullae creaturae opem ferunt: sicut per omnem vitam, inopia & infamia, ubi defuerit rationi consilium.

## 13:37
Why can I not follow you now?

For that he had not sufficiently understood what Christ had predicted concerning the passion, appears from Luke. Why not? Namely, since I have clearly determined even to die together with you, and I feel this way entirely. Peter wonders why he cannot follow: because reason perceives neither the power of death nor its own weakness, Rom. 7. the Apostle says: I once lived without the law, that is, I did not perceive the judgment of the law. Just as neither that Pharisee Lucae 18. perceived his own sin, when he said that he was not like the others.

Latin:

Quare non possum te sequi modo?

Nam non satis intellexisse, quae praedixerat Christus de passione, apparet ex Luca. Quare non? scilicet cum ego planè constituerim, etiam unà mortem obire, atque ita prorsus sentiam. Miratur Petrus, cur sequi non possit: quia ratio nec vim mortis, nec suam imbecillitatem cernit, Rom. 7. inquit Apostolus: Ego aliquando sine lege vixi, hoc est non sensi iudicium legis. Sicut nec pharisaeus ille Lucae 18. cernebat peccatum suum, cum diceret, se non esse sicut caeteros.

## 13:38
Will you lay down your soul for me?

Would you overcome death? And that for me, who myself came into this for the purpose that I might overcome.

Latin:

Animam tuam pro me pones?

Tu'ne mortem vinceres? idque pro me, qui ipse in hoc veni ut vincam.

## 13:38
Amen amen I say to you, the cock shall not crow, until you deny me thrice.

This is the outcome of the glory of the flesh. Peter had determined within himself that he would perform the duty of faith, would follow constantly, etc. And Peter feels or perceives nothing else, such is the glory of the flesh. But because it was carnal presumption, not faith, therefore it did not endure. Because all glory of the flesh is as the glory of hay. But if the glory of pious intentions ends in this way, then what is to be the outcome of the glory of carnal men, of those who trust in the things of the world? etc.

Latin:

Amen amen dico tibi, non cantabit gal-lus, donec ter me neges.

Hic est exitus gloriae carnis. Petrus constituerat apud se, se officium fidei facturum, constanter secuturum &c. Neque aliud sentit aut cernit Petrus, ea gloria carnis est. Sed quia carnalis praesumptio non fides erat, ideo non duravit. Quia omnis gloria carnis, ceu gloria foeni. Quod si sic exit gloria piorum intentionum, tum carnalium quis est futurus exitus gloriae, eorum qui rebus mundi fidunt? &c.

## 14:1 Argument
ON THE FOURTEENTH CHAPTER OF JOHN, COLLECTANEA.

At the beginning of this chapter, in very many places, Christ again indicates for what purpose He has come, what His office is: which is properly the Evangelical preaching: namely, that He is the one who prepares mansions, who returning will receive us to Himself, that He is the way, the truth, and the life. Finally, concluding, He explains those former things more clearly, when He says: No one comes to the Father, except through Me. To the same point belongs what follows, that the Father is known if He Himself is known, and the Father is seen if He Himself is seen. You have both the scope of this passage and the head to which the remaining history must be referred, so that we may learn that we are not justified by our own powers, not saved by our own merits, but that we are sanctified by the Spirit of Christ, saved by the grace of Christ: and that this is the way to salvation, which Christ goes before, namely death: and that it is necessary for us to be made similar to that image. Concerning the passion, enough has been said before. Here now Christ consoles the apostles, saying: Let not your heart be troubled, namely when you see Me being crucified, when for you also the gates of hell they will threaten death, then let not your heart be troubled.

Latin:

IN DECIMUM QUAR-TUM CAPUT IO-ANNIS, COL-LECTA-NEA.

Initio huius capitis plerisque locis Christus iterum indicat, ad quid venerit, quod sit officium suum: quae proprie Evangelica praedicatio est: scilicet se esse qui mansiones paret, qui rediens nos recepturos sit ad se, se esse viam, veritatem, & vitam. Postremo finiens, explicat superiora illa clarius, cum inquit: Nemo venit ad patrem, nisi per me. Eôdem pertinet, quod sequitur, patrem cognosci si ipse cognoscatur, & patrem videri si ipse videatur. Habes huius loci & scopum, & caput, ad quod reliqua historia referenda est, ut discamus non nostris viribus iustificari nos, non nostris meritis salvari, sed Christi spiritu nos sanctificari, Christi gratia nos salvari: esseque hanc viam ad salutem, quam praeit Christus, scilicet mortem: eíque imagini oportere nos similes fieri. De passione ante satis multa. Hic nunc apostolos consolatur Christus, inquiens: Ne turbetur cor vestrum, scilicet ubi me videritis crucifigi, ubi vobis quoque portae inferorum mortem minabuntur, tum non conturbetur cor vestrum.

## 14:1
You believe in God, and believe in me.

Because Christ is crucified, the flesh denies that he is the author of salvation. Likewise, because the saints are afflicted, the flesh denies that these are a care to God. Against this scandal Christ confirms the disciples, saying: You believe in God, and believe in me. That is, if you believe that God is about to glorify you, that God loves you: believe that he glorifies in this way, just as you see me glorified: and believe that this death of mine is the way to glory, both mine and that of my whole body, that it makes satisfaction for the sins of the world, etc. You see therefore that Christ wishes hearts to be confirmed by faith, not by any external signs.

Latin:

Creditis in deum, & in me credite.

Quia Christus crucifigitur, negat eum caro salutis autorem esse. Item quia affliguntur sancti, negat caro hos deo curae esse. Adversus hoc scandalum confirmat Christus discipulos, inquiens: Creditis in deum, & in me credite. id est si creditis quod deus sit vos glorificaturus, quod diligat vos deus: credite quod hoc modo glorificet, quemadmodum me videtis glorificatum: & credite quod haec mea mors via sit ad gloriam, & meam & universi corporis mei, quod satisfaciat pro peccatis mundi &c. Vides igitur velle Christum, fide confirmari corda, non externis ullis signis.

## 14:2
If it were not so, I would have told you, because I go to prepare a place for you.

Insofar as the mansions are not yet prepared, there is indeed a place, but it is not yet completed: it will be completed, however, when Christ, having emptied the kingdom of death through his death, shall have gone to reign, appeasing the Father, and sanctifying the believers. In my judgment this is the simplest meaning: There are mansions, namely where the Father may glorify, but these mansions are not yet completed: because the kingdom of death it has not yet been emptied. Then he teaches how they are absolved, when he says: Therefore I said that I would go away, so that I may absolve. And because I foretold this, you ought to believe that the place of glorifying is prepared. And this is the sum of the sentence. There are the predestined whom the father is about to glorify: but they cannot be glorified, except through Christ, unless Christ abolishes sin and death. You see that everything returns to this, that it is Christ who prepares the mansions, that we cannot be glorified except through Christ, so that the father has loved us in Christ.

Latin:

Si quo minus, dixissem vobis, quia va-do parare vobis locum.

Quatenus nondum sunt paratae mansiones, locus quidem est, sed nondum absolutus est: absolvetur autem, cum Christus, evacuato regno mortis per suam mortem, ierit regnaturus, placans patrem, & sanctificans credentes. Meo iudicio simplicissima haec est sententia: Sunt mansiones, scilicet ubi pater glorificet, sed hae mansiones nondum absolutae sunt: quia regnum mortis nondum est evacuatum. Deinde docet, quomodo absolvantur, cum dicit: Ideo dixi me abiturum, ut absolvam. Et quia hoc praedixi, credere debetis paratum esse locum glorificandi. Estque haec sententiae summa. Sunt praedestinati quos pater glorificaturus est: sed ii non possunt glorificari, nisi per Christum, nisi Christus aboleat peccatum & mortem. Vides eò redire omnia, Christum esse qui paret mansiones, non posse nos nisi per Christum glorificari, ut nos in Christo dilexerit pater.

## 14:2
And if I shall go to prepare a place for you.

These things seemed to conflict above: In my father's house there are many mansions. And: if not, I would have told you, because I go &c. So also here they seem to conflict, because Christ says that they know both whither he goes, and what is the way of arriving at the father. However, in this part of the text, the head of the disputation must be observed, that we are not justified by human strengths, not by human merits, but through Christ.

Latin:

Et si abiero ad parandum vobis locum.

Pugnare videbantur haec supra: In domo patris mei mansiones multae sunt. &: si quo minus, dixissem vobis, quia vado &c. Ita & hic pugnare videntur, quod Christus ait, scire eos & quorsum abeat, & quae sit via perveniendi ad patrem. Est autem in hac parte textus observandum caput disputationis, non humanis viribus, non humanis meritis iustificari nos, sed per Christum.

## 14:4
And whither I go you know, and the way you know.

Because you believe in me, you have performed miracles in my name, you have seen also the father testifying concerning me, therefore you know also that the father is about to glorify me: furthermore you also know me, Although that faith of yours may not yet have been declared, or proven by temptation.

Latin:

Ego quò vadam scitis, & viam scitis.

Quia in me creditis, meo nomine fecistis miracula, vidistis & patrem de me testificare, ideo scitis & glorificaturum me esse patrem: praeterea & me nostis, quanquam ea vestra fides nondum declarata sit, seu probata tentatione.

## 14:5
Lord, we do not know where you are going.

Although there was faith in the apostles, nevertheless they did not yet grasp that it was necessary for Christ to be crucified: and that through death Christ was about to inaugurate a kingdom, and that a spiritual one. A little before, confirming the apostles, he promises that it will come to pass that they are glorified: here he adds, that they know by what way they are to be glorified, if indeed he himself is departing to receive the kingdom etc. Therefore he says that they know whither he is going, and what the way is: And yet Thomas denies that the apostles know. How do these things agree? I answer: There was faith in the apostles, as appears John 6. You have the words of eternal life, and we believe and have known etc. And from the words of Christ, in the previous chapter: You are clean. And by this faith they knew Christ, who is the way to the Father. They knew also the Father because they had seen the miracles, by which he bore witness to the Son: these had nourished faith in the hearts of the apostles: and this is the knowledge of the Son and of the Father. But although faith was present, nevertheless they did not yet know what was to be done. Just as Abraham, although he had received a son by faith, nevertheless did not yet know that the son was to be offered, nor did faith yet declare itself in resigning the son. So also in the apostles: although faith was present, nevertheless the proof of faith was absent in them. For it is the cross that proves, Rom. 5. Proof produces patience &c. And Proverbs 17. As gold is proved by fire, so the Lord proves hearts. But this is to be crucified, that faith may be proved: and with faith proved, the flesh may be mortified, and the spirit may grow in the knowledge of Christ. This is what Paul says to the Romans 10.: That you may prove what is the will of God &c.

Latin:

Domine nescimus quò vadis.

Quanquam esset in apostolis fides, tamen nondum capiebant, oportere crucifigi Christum: & quod per mortem regnum Christus auspicaturus esset, idque spirituale. Paulò ante confirmans apostolos, promittit fore ut glorificentur: hic subiungit, quon, qua via sint glorificandi, siquidem ipse abeat regnum accepturus &c. Ideo ait scire, quorsum eat, & quae via sit: Et tamen Thomas negat apostolos scire. Qui conveniunt haec? Respondeo: Erat in apostolis fides, ut apparet Ioannis 6. Verba vitae aeternae habes, & nos credimus & cognovimus &c. Et ex verbis Christi, capite superiori: Vos mundi estis. Atque hac fide norant Christum, qui via est ad patrem. Norant & patrem quod vidissent miracula, quibus ille filio testimonium perhibuit: haec fidem in apostolorum cordibus aluerant: estque haec filii & patris cognitio. Sed quanquam adesset fides, nondum tamen norant quid faciendum esset. Sicut Abraham, quanquam fide filium accepisset, tamen nondum norat filium offerendum esse, neque dum se fides in resignando filio declarabat. Sic & in apostolis: quamquam adesset fides, tamen probatio fidei aberat illis. Crux enim est quae probat, Rom. 5. Probatio patientiam &c. Et Proverbiorum 17. Sicut igne probatur aurum, sic probat corda dominus. Hoc est autem crucifigi, ut probetur fides: & probata fide, mortificetur caro, grandescatque spiritus cognitione Christi. Hoc est quod ad Romanos 10. Paulus ait: Ut probetis quae sit voluntas dei &c.

## 14:6
I am the way, the truth, and the life.

I, although I am thus mortified &c. am the way, namely, because I carry believers, as Isaiah 40. He shall lift them in his bosom, and he himself shall carry those that are with young. And the truth, because I am light. And the life, because I give life, according to that: He who believes in me shall not die forever. This is what we have already warned before, that the Gospel is properly preached here. For when they deny that they know where they must go, Christ refreshes them with the word, saying, if they wish both to know glory, and to know the way, and to enter the way, that is into the glory of God: let them not seek the powers, light, or justice of creatures, but Christ: let them cast themselves into him, who, grasped by faith, is the way, the truth, and the life, however much he may suffer, or those who believe in him. You know now, he says, what must be done, so that you may be able to enter the dwelling through the true way.

Latin:

Ego sum via, veritas, & vita.

Ego, quanquam sic mortificer &c. sum via, scilicet, quia gero credentes, ut Esaiae 40. Sinu suo levabit, & fetas ipse portabit. Et veritas, quia lux. Et vita, quia vivifico, iuxta illud: Qui credit in me non morietur in aeternum. Hoc est, quod iam antea monuimus, praedicari hic proprie Evangelium. Nam cum illi se negant scire quò sit eundum, Christus verbo reficit eos, inquiens, si velint & gloriam nosse, & viam scire, & ingredi viam, id est in gloriam dei: requirant non creaturae vires, lucem aut iustitiam, sed Christum: in hunc se coniiciant, qui comprehensus per fidem, via, veritas, & vita est, utut patiatur vel ipse, vel qui credunt in eum. Scitis iam, inquit, quid faciendum sit, ut possitis ingredi habitationem per viam veram.

## 14:6
No one comes to the Father, except through me.

This is the sentence: No one can by his own powers, or by his own merits, arrive at the Father. The Law terrifies consciences, so that they do not arrive at the Father. The Word of Christ, justifying us by the righteousness of Christ, leads us to the Father. From that follows that which is set forth concerning knowing the Father. For because no one arrives at the Father, except through the Son: so also no one knows the Father, except through the Son Christ. For first, the will of the Father is ignored, namely that He wills to save: unless we discern it in Christ. Then, neither is it believed by the Father, because reason by itself does not perceive that it receives anything from God. A confused conscience flees the Father, it does not trust in Him: but having been justified by the Word of Christ, they no longer despise the Father, nor do they flee like the Israelites: but they stand before Him within the cloud, like Moses: and they are illuminated by divine light, so that they may know His power and mercy, trust in the Father, perceive that they receive all things from His hands: and expect from thence all things corporal and spiritual. This is what the Apostle says 1. Corinth. 1. When the world had not known God through wisdom in the wisdom of God, it pleased Him by the foolishness of preaching to save those who believe. Reason inquires after God by its own powers: likewise by its own merits, yet it fails to attain it. For first, because reason cannot arrive at the knowledge of God, it altogether denies God.

Then because uncleanness is revealed in works, therefore he despairs. But justified by the foolishness of preaching, we come into the knowledge of God the Father, when believing in the word of Christ, we experience the power of the Father in afflictions, whatever those may be. The same is signified in that, because when the people could not hear the voice of God, they demand that another speak for them. Where also Christ is promised, who may intercede for us before the Father, who may be the propitiation. Therefore, in whom there is faith in Christ, they know the power and mercy of the Father, etc.

Latin:

Nemo venit ad patrem, nisi per me.

Sententia haec est: Nemo potest suis viribus, aut suis meritis pervenire ad patrem. Lex absterret conscientias, ne perveniant ad patrem. Verbum Christi iustificans nos Christi iustitia, perducit ad patrem. Ex eo sequitur id, quod de cognoscendo patre subiectum est. Nam quia nemo ad patrem pervenit, nisi per filium: sic & nemo cognoscit patrem, nisi per filium Christum. Primum enim voluntas patris ignoratur, scilicet quod velit salvare: nisi eam in Christo cernamus. Deinde neque creditur patri, quia ratio per sese non sentit se aliquid à deo accipere. Conscientia confusa fugit patrem, non credit se ei: iustificata autem verbo Christi, iam non contemnunt patrem, nec fugiunt sicut Israëlitae: sed consistunt coram ipso intra nubem, sicut Moyses: & illuminantur luce divina, ut noscant potentiam & misericordiam, fidant patri, sentiant se omnia ex illius manibus accipere: expectent inde omnia corporalia & spiritualia. Hoc est quod Apostolus ait 1. Corinth. 1. Cum non cognovisset mundus per sapientiam deum in sapientia, visum est stultitia praedicationis salvos facere credentes. Ratio inquirit deum suis viribus: item suis meritis, necasse quitur tamen. Primum enim, quia ratio non potest in cognitionem dei pervenire, prorsus negat deum.

Deinde quia in operibus revelatur immundities, ideo desperat. Stultitia vero praedicationis iustificati, venimus in cognitionem dei patris, Cum credentes verbo Christi, potentiam patris experimur in afflictionibus, quales quales illae sint. Idem significatum est in eo, quod cum populus non posset audire vocem dei, postulat ut pro se alius loquatur. Ubi & promittitur Christus, qui pro nobis interpellet apud patrem, qui sit propitiatorium. Itaque in quibus est fides in Christum, illi norunt patris potentiam & misericordiam &c.

## 14:7
If you had known me, you would have known my Father also, etc.

Namely, by the foolishness of the preaching about Christ, the Father will be known, where faith will be tested and will increase. And lest these things seem to conflict: If you had known me, you would have known my Father also. And what follows: Have you seen him? I answer as above, they know, and they do not know: because faith was present, but the testing of faith was absent: Christ answers the two questions of the apostles, which is the way, and whither he goes, teaching that he is the way, teaching also that he goes to the Father: and so goes to the Father, that those who are to come to him cannot except through Christ. When the question arises, what is the glory of the Father: He answers, that the Father is known if Christ is known. That is, while the word of Christ is conceived, the glory of the Father who consoles is felt, gives life, and sanctifies, whose consolation and sanctification is eternal life.

Latin:

Si cognovissetis me, & patrem &c.

Scilicet stultitia praedicationis de Christo cognoscetur pater, ubi fides probabitur & augescet. Et ne haec pugnare videntur: Si cognovissetis me, & patrem noscetis. & quod sequitur: Vidistis eum? Respondeo sicut supra, norunt, & non norunt: quia fides aderat, abfuit autem fidei probatio: Respondet autem Christus duabus quaestionibus apostolorum, quae via sit, & quorsum eat, docens se esse viam, docens item se ire ad patrem: atque ita ire ad patrem, ut qui ad illum venturi sint, non queant nisi per Christum. Cum oritur quaestio, quae sit gloria patris: Respondet, patrem cognosci si Christus cognoscatur. id est, dum verbum Christi concipitur, sentitur gloria patris consolantis, vivificantis, sanctificantis, quae consolatio & sanctificatio vita aeterna est.

## 14:8
Lord, show us the Father, etc.

Namely, so that consciences may be made more certain, that is, if you show us a sign, then we will believe. Philip is not yet content with the word, but proceeds to seek the Father in other ways beyond the word. Because reason cannot believe, therefore it desires to be made more certain by other ways and signs. Therefore, by this disputation it is shown that the conscience is certified by faith, that one must not cling to anything other than the word, and that other signs are not to be expected, etc. Philip feels that he would be more firm if he could see the Father, when he says: And it is enough for us. On the contrary, Christ taxes his unbelief when he says: I have been with you for so long a time, that is, I am teaching, and you have not yet known me, that is, you do not yet know that the Father wishes to be known through me, that my word is the power of the Father: in which alone the Father wishes to be known, and in no other way. For he says Matthaei 17. Hear him. And Ioannis 1. No one has ever seen God: the only-begotten Son who is in the bosom of the Father, he has declared him.

Latin:

Domine ostende nobis patrem &c.

Scilicet ut certiores reddantur conscientiae, id est si nobis exhibueris signum, tum credemus. Nondum verbo contentus est Philippus, sed pergit praeter verbum aliis modis inquirere patrem. Quia credere ratio non potest, ideo aliis modis & signis reddi certior cupit. Igitur hac disputatione ostenditur, certificari fide conscientiam, non aliter in verbo haerendum esse, non expectanda alia signa &c. Philippus sentit se firmiorem fore, si patrem cernat, cum inquit: Et sufficit nobis. Contrà, Christus incredulitatem taxat, cum ait: Tanto tempore vobiscum sum, id est doceo, & nondum novistis me, id est nondum scitis velle patrem per me cognosci, meum verbum esse potentiam patris: in quo solo velit pater cognosci, non aliter. Ait enim Matthaei 17. Ipsum audite. Et Ioannis 1. Deum nemo vidit unquam: unigenitus filius qui est in sinu patris, ipse enarravit.

## 14:9
Philip, he who sees me, sees also the Father.

He repeats the previous discourse: He who sees me, sees also the Father, that is, since the Father wishes to be made known through me, do not seek another way of knowing, but believe my word, that I am he who presents you to the Father. Through me believe that you will come to the Father, then you will know the Father, you will experience by faith the power and mercy of the Father, you will feel him comforting and vivifying. The Father wishes to be believed by my word, and to save those who believe. The flesh is crucified again, when the world also persecutes the word. And God permits that there should be rage against the saints. It is crucified likewise, when in whatever afflictions one must stand firm in faith, and consolation is not to be expected from elsewhere. Just as also by faith alone the saints console themselves, are killed, die, as in Psalmo 15. I foresaw the Lord always in my sight, for this reason my heart has rejoiced etc.

Latin:

Philippe qui videt me, videt & patrem.

Repetit superiorem concionem: Qui me videt, videt & patrem, id est cum pater innotescere velit per me, noli quaerere aliam viam cognoscendi, sed meo verbo crede, quod ego is sim, qui sistam vos patri. Per me crede te venturum ad patrem, tum cognosces patrem, experieris fide potentiam & misericordiam patris, senties consolantem & vivificantem. Vult pater credi verbo meo, & credentes salvare. Iterum crucifigitur caro, cum & mundus verbum persequitur. Et permittit deus, ut in sanctos saeviatur. Crucifigitur item, cum in quibuscunque afflictionibus consistendum est fide, nec expectanda aliunde consolatio. Quemadmodum & sola fide se consolantur sancti, occiduntur, moriuntur, ut Psalmo 15. Providebam dominum in conspectu meo semper, propter hoc laetatum est cor meum &c.

## 14:9
And how do you say, show us etc.

Namely, since there is no other way of knowing the Father, besides that which I have indicated: you do not believe etc. when I have taught that I am the life and the way. Do you not believe that I am in the Father: that the same divinity is both the Father's and mine, and that the Father wishes to be known through me? Why is the Father known when the Son is known? I answer, because the Son is in the Father, and the Father is in the Son: because the word by which the Son is known is the power of both the Son and the Father: therefore when the Son is known by his word, it is necessary that the Father also be known by the same.

Latin:

Et quomodo tu dicis, ostende nobis &c.

Scilicet, cum non sit alia via cognoscendi patrem, praeter eam quam indicavi: non credis &c. cum docuerim me esse vitam & viam. Non credis quod in patre sim: quod eadem divinitas sit & patris & mea, quodque velit per me cognosci pater? Quare cognito filio, cognoscitur pater? Respondeo, quia filius in patre est, & pater in filio: quia verbum quo cognoscitur filius, est potentia & filii & patris: ergo cum cognoscitur filius suo verbo, necesse est & eodem patrem cognosci.

## 14:10
The words that I speak to you, etc.

In which you know me, and so in the same you know the Father. He adds certain things concerning the word, so that he may teach what kind of knowledge it is, by which he himself is also known: it is, however, faith, by which we apprehend Christ in the word. Therefore he says thus: By the word you shall know me. Furthermore, that word, because it is not a human power, therefore in it you shall also know the Father: and because by my word you shall know me to be life, you shall certainly know that I am in the Father.

Latin:

Verba quae ego loquor vobis &c.

In quibus cognoscitis me, adeoque in iisdem patrem cognoscitis. Subiicit de verbo quaedam, ut doceat qualis nam sit cognitio, qua & ipse cognoscitur: est autem fides, qua in verbo Christum apprehendimus. Proinde sic ait: Verbo me cognoscetis. Porrò id verbum, quia non est humana potentia, ideo in eo & patrem cognoscetis: & quia meo verbo cognoscetis me vitam esse, cognoscetis certe me in patre esse.

## 14:12
Believe because of the works themselves.

Which testify concerning the word. Finally, he alleges the testimony of the word: If you do not believe the word, yet believe the signs by which the Father has testified concerning me. For signs are testimonies of the word. In sum, Christ does this, that he may confirm consciences against the scandal of the cross. He did not want consciences to know, but to believe: nor to enter these darknesses, so that they might commit themselves to God, but always to hesitate: What if God does not regard us? Now also the saints are left as if cast away, so that the world may rage against them, etc. Here, however, he desires to know the will of God toward them, and Christ consoles those so affected, lest they seek another consolation than in him and in his death: that he who is life, has thus received the kingdom, and thus likewise gives life, although he is dead.

Latin:

Propter opera ipsa credite.

Quae de verbo testificantur. Postremo allegat verbi testimonium: Si verbo non creditis, at signis quibus testificatus est pater de me, credite. Sunt enim signa verbi testimonia. In summa, hoc agit Christus, ut confirmet conscientias adversus scandalum crucis. Non scire conscientias, sed credere voluit: nec ingredi has tenebras, ut se committant deo, sed semper haesitent: Quid si non respiciat nos deus? Iam & reliquuntur sancti velut abiecti, ut in eos saeviat mundus &c. Hic vero scire voluntatem dei erga se cupit, sic affectos consolatur Christus, ne quaerant aliam consolationem quàm in eo & in sua morte: se qui vita sit, sic accepisse regnum, sic item vivificare, quanquam mortuus.

## 14:12
Amen amen I say to you, he who believes &c.

Do not doubt that through me you are to have knowledge, that my word is the power of God, that by my word you are to be saved, although I am now to be crucified.

Latin:

Amen amen dico vobis, qui credit &c.

Ne diffidatis quin per me sitis cognituri, quin meum verbum sit potentia dei, quin meo verbo sitis servandi, quanquam crucifigar modò.

## 14:12
Because I go to the Father.

That is, I shall begin my kingdom, where I may fulfill all things: because I am in the Father. For therefore are all things subjected to Christ, because he is the Son. And he conceives the epilogue of the nearest disputation and of that former consolation. For a little before he taught that the Father is known through Christ himself, for this reason, because he himself is in the Father. And therefore by the same word by which the Father himself shows himself, which he may confirm, namely that his word is the power of the Father, he added: He who believes in me, the works which I do, he does. That is: If indeed by faith in me you are to do the same works, you shall know that my word is the power of God. But why does he say: Because I go to the Father? I answer, for the reason that because Christ is in the Father, he himself does the works of the Father, but not therefore do we also effect the same: but therefore we effect the same works, because Christ who is in the Father, is now in us. For to go to the Father is to fulfill all things. to the Ephesians 1. & 4. And as Paul explains the psalm. Just as to give gifts to men, to illuminate and to sanctify. For it is the kingdom of Christ, by which he reigns on earth in the hearts of believers.

And that is to sit upon the throne of David his father. As far as consolation pertains, just as he began to confirm the apostles above, that they should believe in him although he was dying, so here he promises that it will come to pass that they shall be glorified: because he himself is going to the father.

And whatever you shall ask.

Namely, to be certifiers of my kingdom.

Latin:

Quia ego vado ad patrem.

Id est regnum auspicabor, ubi impleam omnia: quia in patre sum. Nam ideo Christo subiiciuntur omnia, quia filius est. Et proximae disputationis & superioris illius consolationis epilogum concipit. Nam paulò ante docuit, patrem cognosci per se Christum, id ideo, quia ipse sit in patre. Ideoque eodem verbo quo ipse pater, ostendit se, quod ut confirmet, scilicet verbum suum esse patris potentiam, adiecit: Qui credit in me, opera quae ego facio, facit. id est: Siquidem fide in me eadem opera facturi estis, potentiam dei meum verbum esse cognoscetis. Cur autem ait: Quia ad patrem vado? Respondeo, ideo quia in patre Christus est, opera patris ipse facit, non autem ideo & nos eadem efficimus: sed ideo nos eadem opera efficimus, quia Christus qui in patre est, iam in nobis est. Nam ire ad patrem, est implere omnia. ad Ephesios 1. & 4. Et sicut exponit Paulus psalm. Sicuti dare dona hominibus, illuminare et sanctificare. Est enim regnum Christi, quo in terra regnat in cordibus credentium.

Idque est sedere super solium David patris. Quod ad consolationem attinet, sicut supra confirmare coepit apostolos, ut in se credant quanquam morientem, ita hic promittit fore, ut glorificentur: quia ipse ad patrem abeat.

Et quicquid petieritis.

Scilicet certificaturi de regno meo.

## 14:13
In my name.

What he adds, in my name, excludes our merits: because we are heard on account of Christ, just as we are also loved in Christ, as it is said in 1. Peter 2.: Offering spiritual sacrifices, acceptable to God through Jesus Christ. Just as also in the law the people did not offer by themselves, did not enter into the holy of holies by themselves, but the high priest offered: So also we ask and offer through the high priest Christ, etc. Nor should you estimate the amplitude of grace anywhere more closely than in the priesthood of Christ. Do you see, moreover, that the glorification of Christ embraces both kingdom and priesthood? etc.

Latin:

In nomine meo.

Quod addit, in nomine meo, excludit nostra merita: quia propter Christum exaudimur, sicut & in Christo diligimur, ut 1. Petri 2. dicitur: Offerentes hostias spirituales, acceptas deo per Iesum Christum. Sicut & in lege non per sese offerebat populus, non per sese ingrediebatur in sancta sanctorum, sed offerebat pontifex: Ita & nos per pontificem Christum rogamus & offerimus &c. Neque amplitudinem gratiae usquam propius aestimes, quàm in sacerdotio Christi. Vides autem glorificationem Christi, & regnum & sacerdotium complecti? &c.

## 14:13
That the father may be glorified through the son.

Namely when it is acknowledged that he gave the son so that he might save us. The father is glorified when glory is attributed to him, not to us: that is, when we acknowledge that we are not saved by our own merits, not by our own wisdom, not by our own justice, but we trust in His mercy: since He gave His Son for our sins, etc. And the Gospel touches upon this glory, according to the Psalm: The heavens declare the glory of God. Moreover, this is the sum of this epilogue: He commands us to believe for this reason, because He is going away to reign, and He predicts that He will bear witness to His kingdom, and will reign in such a way: that He will accomplish through us works as great as those He Himself accomplished: likewise that He will hear us whenever we have asked. And with these promises the heart must be strengthened against the gates of hell. Christ reigns by the Word, therefore it is necessary that we experience the power of the Word. Since the kingdom of Christ is the power of God.

If you love me, keep my commandments, etc.

The sum of the sentence is this: When Christ departs to begin His reign, He predicts what kind of kingdom it will be. For the apostles thought, as appears in the Acts, that Christ was going to establish a carnal Israelite kingdom. Therefore He says that He is going to reign through the Holy Spirit, by vivifying the faithful with eternal life. For as often as the prophets prophesy about the kingdom of Christ, they describe an eternal kingdom. Isaiah 23. Your eyes will see Christ, an opulent dwelling, a tabernacle, which shall in no wise it will be able to be transferred, because our Lord is magnificent there alone. Do you see that the prophet has described an eternal kingdom, which cannot be any kingdom, unless the kingdom of sin and death has been overturned?

Latin:

Ut glorificetur pater per filium.

Scilicet cum agnoscitur, quod filium dederit ut salvaret nos. Glorificatur pater, quando ei gloria tribuitur, non nobis: hoc est quando agnoscimus nos non nostris meritis, non nostra sapientia, non nostra iustitia salvari, sed misericordiae eius fidimus: siquidem dedit filium pro peccatis nostris &c. Atque hanc gloriam tangit Evangelium, iuxta Psalm. Caeli enarrant gloriam dei. Est autem huius epilogi haec summa: Iubet ideo credere, quia ipse regnaturus abeat, praedicitque se regno suo testimonium per hibiturum esse, atque ita regnaturum: effecturum per nos tanta opera, quanta ipse effecit: item exauditurum se esse nos quandocunque rogaverimus. Et iis quidem promissionibus est firmandum cor contra inferorum portas. Verbo regnat Christus, necesse est igitur experiamur vim verbi. Siquidem regnum Christi, potentia dei est.

Si diligitis me, praecepta mea &c.

Sententiae summa haec est: Cum abit Christus regnum auspicaturus, praedicit quale regnum futurum sit. Putabant enim apostoli, sicut in actis apparet, Christum instituturum esse regnum Israëliticum carnale. Ideo dicit, quod regnaturus sit per spiritum sanctum, vivificandis vita aeterna fidelibus. Quoties enim de regno Christi vaticinantur prophetae, regnum aeternum describunt. Esaiae 23. Oculi tui videbunt Christum, habitationem opulentam, tabernaculum, quod nequaquàm transferri poterit, quia solum ibi magnificus est dominus noster. Vides prophetam aeternum regnum descripsisse, quod esse non potest ullum, nisi regno peccati & mortis everso?

## 14:15
If you keep the commandments &c.

What is it that he says: If you keep the commandments? &c. are the commandments kept without the Holy Spirit? I answer, the text itself explains the matter. First, he calls the Holy Spirit the Spirit of truth: therefore, whatever the Holy Spirit does not effect is a lie. And so, the apostles could not keep the commandments of Christ without the Holy Spirit. Indeed, they lie who lack the Spirit of God. Secondly, below Christ says: You know him. Therefore, the Holy Spirit was in them. But just as faith had not yet been proven by temptations, so neither was the Holy Spirit yet known to the apostles. For faith and the energy of the Spirit are known in temptation. Therefore, in sum, he says this: If you shall have endured believing in me, the Holy Spirit will be revealed to you. And you will understand that not only concerning that Pentecost, but concerning the revelation of the Holy Spirit, during this perpetual Pentecost until the very end of the world.

Latin:

Si servaveritis mandata &c.

Quid est quod inquit: Si servaveritis mandata? &c. an sine spiritusancto servantur mandata? Respondeo, Textus ipse rem explicat. Primum vocat spiritunsanctum, spiritum veritatis: ergo quicquid non efficit spiritussanctus, mendacium est. Itaque mandata Christi servare non poterant apostoli sine spiritusancto. Siquidem mentiuntur, qui carent spiritu dei. Secundo infra ait Christus: Vos cognoscitis eum. Fuit ergo spiritussanctus in eis. Sed sicut fides nondum probata fuerat tentationibus, ita nec dum spiritussanctus cognitus apostolis. Nam fides & energia spiritus in tentatione cognoscuntur. Proinde in summa hoc ait: Si perduraveritis credentes in me, revelabitur vobis spiritussanctus. Idque intelligetis non modò de illa Pentecoste, sed de revelatione spiritussancti, durante in finem usque mundi perpetua hac Pentecoste.

## 14:16
And he will give another paraclete.

He promises that the Holy Spirit will be in the church, who may console, who may be a pledge and earnest of grace. For thus the Apostle calls him 2. Corinth. 1. Because where Christ reigns, there it is necessary for this testament of the Holy Spirit to be, who consoles, animates, and confirms the saints. This testimony is the confidence by which a terrified conscience conceives joy, having heard the word of the remission of sins. And, as the Apostle says, it cries Abba Father. Just as therefore Christ says that he will be present to the church until the consummation of the age: For here Christ reigns, he will always be present, so that those who are saints may have the spirit testifying concerning the kingdom of Christ etc. He calls him the spirit of truth, because he makes them truthful. For because the flesh does not discern its own spirit, therefore it justifies its own works, its own intentions, and pretends that it believes, when it does not believe. It pretends the fear of God, when nevertheless it despises it. Nature pretends that it seeks the glory of God, when nevertheless it seeks its own. And so all human righteousnesses are mere lies. But where the Holy Spirit has shone into hearts, he reveals that hypocrisy, and brings it about that there is fear and faith in the heart.

Latin:

Et alium paracletum dabit.

Promittit fore in ecclesia spiritum sanctum, qui consoletur, qui sit pignus & arrabo gratiae. Sic enim vocat Apostolus 2. Corinth. 1. Quia ubi regnat Christus, ibi necesse est hoc testamentum spiritussancti esse, qui consolatur, animat, & confirmat sanctos. Est hoc testimonium fiducia, qua conscientia territa concipit gaudium, audito verbo remissionis peccatorum. Et, ut inquit Apostolus, clamat Abba pater. Sicut itaque Christus ait se affore ecclesiae usque ad consummationem seculi: Nam hic regnat Christus, semper aderit, ut qui sunt sancti, habeant spiritum testificantem de regno Christi &c. Vocat spiritum veritatis, quia veraces facit. Caro enim quia non cernit spiritum suum, ideo sua opera, suas intentiones iustificat, & simulat se credere, cum non credat. Simulat metum dei, cum tamen contemnat. Simulat natura gloriam dei se quaerere, cum tamen suam quaerat. Itaque sunt omnes humanae iustitiae mera mendacia. At spiritussanctus ubi illuxerit cordibus, eam hypocrisin revelat, & efficit timorem & fidem in corde.

## 14:17
Whom the world cannot receive.

I have here a passage most worthy of observation against the impious doctrine concerning free will. To know the Holy Spirit is to know his works. Now the works of the Holy Spirit are to believe God, to fear God, to love God, according to Paul in Galatians. Furthermore, these things, just as nature, if it conceives and perceives what it is to believe God, to fear God: certainly it knows the Holy Spirit, etc., because it does not see him, etc.

Latin:
