Quem mundus non potest accipere.

Habeo hic locum dignissimum observatu adversus impiam doctrinam de libero arbitrio. Spiritum sanctum nosse, est opera eius nosse. Sunt autem opera spiritussancti, credere deo, deum timere, diligere deum, apud Paulum Galat. Porrò haec sicut & natura, si concipit ac sentit, quid sit credere deo, timere deum: certe novit spiritumsanctum &c. quia non videt eum &c.

## 14:17
But you see me.

Now you know me to be life, so that although I may be crucified, I nevertheless live.

Latin:

Vos autem videtis me.

Iam nunc cognoscitis me vitam esse, ut quanquam crucifigar, vivam tamen.

## 14:18
I will not leave you orphans.

He explains the promises concerning his kingdom more broadly, so that he may raise up weak consciences, and impress upon them that he is present, lest they despair anywhere. For because the Father is not known in the work of creation, the Son was given, through whom the will of the Father might be known: who makes us more certain that the Father clearly wills this, that we be saved, and wills to bring aid, provided we call upon him. This is the sum of the Gospel. Now where such confidence is not in the heart, it is necessary that all worship of God, and all works, be hypocrisy.

I will not leave you orphans, that is, otherwise destitute of all protection by all creatures. Thus also the Father himself calls him Psalm 77 the father of orphans, and the avenger of widows.

Latin:

Non relinquam vos orphanos.

Promissiones de regno suo latius exponit, ut erigat imbecilles conscientias, & inculcet eis se praesentem adesse, necubi desperent. Nam quia non cognoscitur pater in opere creationis, filius datus est, per quem voluntas patris cognoscatur: qui certiores nos facit, planè hoc velle patrem, ut salvemur, velle opem ferre, modò se invocemus. Haec Evangelii summa est. Iam ubi non est in corde huiusmodi fiducia, necesse est hypocrisin esse omnem cultum dei, & omnia opera.

Non relinquam vos orphanos, scilicet alioqui destitutos omni praesidio ab omnibus creaturis. Sic & pater ipse vocat eum Psalmo 77. patrem orphanorum, & vindicem viduarum.

## 14:19
Because I live.

Namely, although life may be hidden by death, this is what the Apostle says: Your treasures are hidden.

Latin:

Quia ego vivo.

Scilicet quanquam vita abscondatur morte, hoc est quod Apostolus ait: Thesauri vestri absconditi.

## 14:19
And you shall live.

Because by the same spirit both Christ and those who are in Christ live.

In that day.

This sentence is referred to the final judgment. These are arguments to me that these things pertain to the resurrection: both because he consoles those left on earth, by which words he indicates that it will come to pass that from the resurrection they will recognize the power of God in Christ: and because afterwards he interprets that to manifest himself is that which is to live, and to make a dwelling among those who keep the commandments. In that day, namely, I shall so reign that I will testify to you concerning my kingdom, so that you may know that I am the power of God. I shall testify, however, when I will lift up, console, and vivify those who are tempted.

Latin:

Et vos vivetis.

Quia eodem spiritu vivunt & Christus & qui sunt in Christo.

In die illa.

Haec sententia relata est ad extremum iudicium. Mihi argumento sunt, quod ad resurrectionem pertineant haec: & quia consolatur relictos in terra, quibus verbis indicat fore, ut ex resurrectione agnoscant dei potentiam in Christo: & quia postea interpretatur, manifestare se, id esse quod vivere, & mansionem facere apud eos qui servent mandata. In die illa, scilicet sic regnabo, ut testificaturus sim vobis de regno meo, ut cognoscatis dei potentiam me esse. Testificabor autem, cum tentatos sublevabo, consolabor, & vivificabo.

## 14:21
He who has my commandments.

Namely by true faith, but because the flesh does not have them, although it may pretend to have them, the letter is the word of God to the flesh, and not the spirit.

Latin:

Qui habet mandata mea.

Scilicet vera fide, sed quia caro non habet, quanquàm simulet se habere, litera est verbum dei carni, & non spiritus.

## 14:21
He who loves me, will be loved by my Father.

Because in Christ all are loved. And I will manifest myself to him, that is, from time to time more clearly, while faith is being proven.

Latin:

Qui diligit me, diligetur à patre meo.

Quia in Christo omnes diliguntur. Et manifestabo me ei, id est subinde clarius, dum probatur fides.

## 14:23
If anyone loves me, my word &c.

This is, I am thus about to show myself by the word: If anyone loves me, the word &c. As if he were saying: Renounce those carnal imaginations about the kingdom. But at least you must think about the word, which is the truest knowledge of the father, glorification, & kingdom &c. The Son given to us, so that through him an approach to the kingdom might be open to us, and our sins might be forgiven.

Latin:

Si quis diligit me, sermonem meum &c.

Hoc est, sic ostensurus sum me verbo: Si quis diligit me, sermonem &c. Quasi diceret: Abdica illas carnales imaginationes de regno. Sed de verbo saltem tibi cogitandum, quod est verissima patris cognitio, glorificatio, & regnum &c. Filius nobis datus, ut per ipsum nobis pateret aditus ad regnum, & condonarentur nobis peccata.

## 14:25
These things I have spoken to you.

That word indeed I have announced, because for this I came that I might announce the word. But you will not understand that word, unless the Holy Spirit teaches: and he also will be given, when I shall have been glorified. For for this I came: for because my word is the power of God, therefore it is not heard carnally.

Latin:

Haec locutus sum vobis.

Id verbum quidem ego annunciavi, quia in hoc veni ut annunciarem verbum. Sed id verbum non intelligetis, nisi docente spiritusancto: atque is quoque dabitur, cum glorificatus fuero. Nam in hoc veni: quia enim verbum meum potentia est dei, ideo non auditur carnaliter.

## 14:25
Remaining with you.

Meanwhile, that I am with you, that is, this is the word which you have now heard & received, nor is there any reason for you to expect anything further, except the Holy Spirit, who will declare this very word to you. For it is the virtue & power of God.

Latin:

Apud vos manens.

Interim quod vobiscum sum, id est hoc est verbum quod iam audistis & accepistis, nec est quod quicquam expectetis ultra, nisi spiritumsanctum, qui hoc ipsum verbum vobis declarabit. Nam virtus & potentia dei est.

## 14:26
Whom the Father will send for my sake.

That is, in my name. Just as he says above: I will ask the Father, and he will give the Spirit: so understand here that the Spirit is given for the sake of Christ, because we are loved in Christ. We receive the Holy Spirit through him, and for his sake, who makes us sons by adoption, just as he himself is Son by nature. For in this we receive the Holy Spirit, that we may become sons. Furthermore, we are made sons for the sake of Christ, and therefore we also receive the Spirit for the sake of Christ.

Latin:

Quem mittet pater propter me.

Id est in nomine meo. Sicut supra dicit: Rogabo patrem, & dabit spiritum: ita hic intellige spiritum dari propter Christum, quia in Christo diligimur. Accipimus per ipsum, & propter ipsum spiritumsanctum, qui nos adoptione filios facit, sicut ipse natura filius est. Nam in hoc accipimus spiritumsanctum, ut filii fiamus. Porrò propter Christum filii efficimur, proinde & propter Christum spiritum accipimus.

## 14:26
He will teach you all things.

Namely, spiritually. He will remind you of all things, namely those which I have said to you, and not others. First, in the New Testament no new doctrine is handed down: because it is not a ministry of the letter, but it is simply the reception of the Holy Spirit. Second, therefore it cannot be understood that the Holy Spirit has established any new doctrine which has not been set forth in the Scriptures. Third, and for that reason Christ says Matthew last: Teach what I have commanded you. Furthermore, if any other doctrine were necessary, mention of it ought also to have been made. Fourth, what then is the office of the Holy Spirit? I answer: To receive the Holy Spirit is to have the law of God inscribed upon the hearts, Jeremiah 31. This is what Christ says here: He will teach you all things, namely by that which to the ears of the flesh the word of God cannot be heard. He who regenerates you will teach you: because spiritual things are grasped spiritually, as 1. Corinth. 2.

Latin:

Ille docebit vos omnia.

Scilicet spiritualiter. Admonebit omnia, scilicet quae ego dixi vobis, & non alia. Primo novo testamento nulla nova doctrina traditur: quia non est ministerium literae, sed est simpliciter acceptio spiritussancti. Secundo, ergo non potest intelligi, quod spiritussanctus novam aliquam doctrinam sanxerit, quae non sit prodita scripturis. Tertio, ideóque Christus Matthaei ultimo dicit: Docete quae ego praecepi vobis. Porrò si necessaria esset aliqua alia doctrina, oportuit & eius fieri mentionem. Quarto, quod igitur est officium spiritussancti? Respondeo: Accipere spiritumsanctum, est inscribi cordibus legem dei, Hieremiae 31. Hoc est quod hic inquit Christus: Ille vos docebit omnia, scilicet eo quod auribus carnis verbum dei non potest audiri. Ille regenerans vos docebit: quia spiritualia spiritualiter capiuntur, ut 1. Corinth. 2.

## 14:27
Peace I leave with you, my peace I give unto you &c.

That is, the life & joy of the Holy Spirit. This is also the sum of the Gospel. The Law preaches wrath, because it reveals and judges sin. And that judgment is death, as appears 1. Corinth. 15. The Gospel preaches peace, that is, life & joy. But that life, that joy, as long as the oldness of the flesh lasts, is covered by the cross. This is what he says, my peace, that is, the life by which I live. The joy which my spirit produces, but in the midst of afflictions.

Latin:

Pacem relinquo vobis, pacem meam do vobis &c.

Id est vitam & gaudium spiritussancti. Est & haec Evangelii summa. Lex iram praedicat, quia peccatum revelat & iudicat. Idque iudicium mors est, ut apparet 1. Corinth. 15. Evangelium pacem praedicat, id est vitam & gaudium. Sed ea vita, id gaudium, quo ad durat vetustas carnis, tectum est cruce. Hoc est quod ait, meam pacem, id est vitam qua ego vivo. Gaudium, quod spiritus meus parit, sed in mediis afflictionibus.

## 14:27
Let not your heart be troubled, neither let it be afraid.

Therefore do not be troubled, because I said: I go, & I come to you: because I go away to receive a kingdom: for because Christ reigns, he is superior to all creatures of life & death, so that no creature can harm the body of Christ, Rom. 8.

Latin:

Non turbetur cor vestrum, neque for.

Ideo non turbemini, quia ego dixi: Vado, & venio ad vos: quia ego abeo regnum accepturus: quia enim Christus regnat, superior est omnibus creaturis vitae & mortis, ut nocere nulla creatura possit corpori Christi, Rom. 8.

## 14:28
If you loved me, you would surely rejoice.

Just as above Christ argues with Peter, because he did not want to admit him to wash his feet, so here he condemns carnal worship.

This is carnal worship, when the flesh holds honor for Christ with carnal duties and thoughts, imagining him to be holy, etc., and therefore that nothing is less fitting for him than to be crucified. Likewise, the flesh desires to hear that Christ sanctifies without mortification, etc., when nevertheless Christ cannot be known apart from mortification. The apostles felt this way; they grieved that an unworthy thing was happening to Christ, they grieved that they were being torn away from Christ, when they ought to have felt this way: to rejoice that through death the Father was declaring his glory, that Christ was inaugurating his kingdom, whom they would then truly know when he reigned. The flesh desires the custom of Christ, justification without mortification. The Spirit feels that Christ cannot be known as long as he is known carnally, but is known when we feel him inwardly consoling and vivifying, which does not happen unless we are mortified. Therefore the Spirit rejoices that Christ goes to the Father, that he reigns, because then at last it feels his power. This is what he says below: It is expedient for you that I go. Do you see, moreover, how sweetly he commends the cross to us, when he testifies that he is therefore departing and leaving us, so that he may truly console us, so that he may truly declare himself to us. Do you see, likewise, how nothing carnal worship is?

Latin:

Si diligeretis me, gauderetis utique.

Sicut supra Petrum Christus arguit, quod nollet admittere loturum, ita hic cultum carnalem damnat.

Carnalis cultus hic est, cum caro habet honorem Christo carnalibus officiis & cogitationibus, imaginatur eum esse sanctum &c. propterea nihil minus convenire ei, ut crucifigatur. Item cupit caro audire, Christum sanctificare sine mortificatione &c. cum tamen citra mortificationem Christus cognosci non possit. Sic sentiebant apostoli, dolebant rem indignam accidere Christo, dolebant se divelli à Christo: cum sic sentire debuissent: gaudere quod per mortem declararet pater gloriam suam, auspicaretur regnum Christus, quem tum vere cognoscerent cum regnaret. Caro consuetudinem Christi, iustificationem sine mortificatione desyderat. Spiritus sentit, non posse cognosci Christum, quoad carnaliter cognoscitur, cognosci autem cum sentimus eum intus consolantem & vivificantem, quod non fit nisi mortificemur. Propterea gaudet spiritus, Christum abire ad patrem, regnare: quia tum demum sentit eius potentiam. hoc est quod infra ait: Expedit vobis ut ego vadam. Vides autem quàm suaviter commendet nobis crucem, cum se ideo abire ac deserere nos testatur, ut vere consoletur, ut vere declaret se nobis. Vides item, quàm nihil sit carnalis cultus?

## 14:28
Because he is greater than I.

Namely, although I may die, yet he is life.

A wonderful consequence indeed: If you loved me, you would surely rejoice that I go to the Father. Why would you rejoice? Because the Father is greater than I. That is: you would rejoice for this reason, because the Father is about to glorify me, whom I now glorify, having been made obedient to him even unto death. You would rejoice in this, that the Father is about to glorify me, to reveal me to you through the Holy Spirit, then at last you will know me. Meanwhile, while you know me carnally, you do not yet know me. For what he says, the Father is greater than I, does not allow the sentiment to be referred to nature, but to condition, that is, although you see me being crucified and diminished, the Father nevertheless, who will glorify, is greater.

Latin:

Quia maior me est.

Scilicet quamquàm ego moriar, tamen ille vita est.

Mira vero consequentia: Si diligeretis me, gauderetis utique quod ad patrem vado. Quare gauderetis? quia pater maior me est. id est: ideo gauderetis, quia pater glorificaturus est me, quem ego iam glorifico, factus illi obediens usque ad mortem. Hoc gauderetis, quod pater sit glorificaturus me, declaraturus me vobis per spiritum sanctum, tum demum cognoscetis me. Interim dum carnaliter nostis, nondum nostis. Quod enim ait, pater maior me est, non sinit sententiam ad naturam referri, sed conditionem, id est quanquam me videatis crucifigi & extenuari, pater tamen qui glorificabit, is maior est.

## 14:29
And now I have told you.

Because it is necessary for you to be armed with the word.

Latin:

Et nunc dixi vobis.

Quia oportet vos verbo armatos esse.

## 14:29
That when it has come to pass.

Namely, when with me departing the temptation rushes in, as was said above: You believe in God, believe also in me.

Latin:

Ut cum factum fuerit.

Scilicet cum me abeunte ingruit tentatio, ut supra dictum est: Creditis in deum, & in me credite.

## 14:30
For the prince of this world comes.

That is, the hour of the passion is pressing. This is the prerogative of Christ, and of his whole body. The flesh is condemned by death, and is captive to the gates of hell. But Christ comes, that he may destroy the kingdom of Satan, as he himself said above: Now is the judgment of the world, now the prince of this world shall be cast out, that is: The Prince of the world attacks me, but he does not conquer. For Christ is he through whom he is conquered.

And he has nothing.

But it is asked, if Satan does not conquer, why does he kill Christ? Christ answers that he dies for this reason, that the will of the Father might be satisfied. It is not by the power of Satan that Christ dies, but by the will of the Father, who willed that sin be blotted out by the death of Christ. Therefore it is not in the power of the world or of Satan to kill any member of Christ, but in the will of the Father, who through our weakness wishes to make his own power testified. 1. Corinth. 1. But I suffer for this reason, that the world may know that I love the Father.

Latin:

Venit enim princeps mundi huius.

Id est instat hora passionis. Haec est praerogativa Christi, & totius corporis sui. Caro est damnata morte, & captiva est portarum infernarum. At Christus venit, ut destruat regnum satanae, sicut ipse supra ait: Nunc iudicium est mundi, nunc princeps mundi huius eiicietur foras, hoc est: Princeps mundi oppugnat me, sed non vincit. Nam Christus is est per quem vincitur.

Et non habet quicquam.

At quaeritur, si non vincit satan, cur occidit Christum? Respondet Christus, se ideo mori, ut satis fiat voluntati patris. Non est satanae potentia, quod Christus moritur, sed voluntas patris, qui peccatum morte Christi deleri voluit. Ideo non est mundi, aut satanae potentia occidere membrum aliquod Christi, sed voluntas patris, qui per infirmitatem nostram suam potentiam vult testatam facere. 1. Corinth. 1. Sed ideo patior, ut cognoscat mundus quod diligo patrem.

## 15:1 Argument
CHAPTER FIFTEENTH.

Latin:

CAPUT DECIMUM-QUINTUM.

## 15:1
I am the true vine.

Above, in a long discourse, he comforted and strengthened weak consciences: here he does the same through a parable. He compares himself, however, to a vine, and the Father to a husbandman, just as Isaiah ch. 5. also makes him a vinedresser.

Those who teach the righteousnesses of free will, they imagine that Christ is the life, in this way: Reason indeed works well by itself, but those good works are accepted by God if faith is added beyond them. Just as Jerome also writes to the Galatians: Abraham indeed worked well, even before he believed, but those works were afterwards accepted by God when he believed. But Christ calls himself the vine, and the true one, that is, the efficacious one, that is, one which from itself begets branches, animates, vegetates, and makes them fruitful. This discourse consoles the saints, that they may trust in the power of Christ, not their own, that they may know Christ to be powerful and efficacious in them, from whom as branches they have been born and vivified. As when he teaches that he is the head, which the Apostle explains, that he fills all things, and compacts the body, to Ephesians 4. Which the doctors of human righteousness think such a head to be, as are the leaders of men: not that it effects anything in others, but is something more sublime than others. But Christ is the head in such a way that he animates his whole body, just as a natural head distills life into the whole body. You see therefore in this whole sermon, the powers of free will are condemned, and furthermore weak consciences are confirmed, which when the power of the gates of hell terrifies, Christ wishes them to trust in his power, so that through our infirmity the power of God may be glorified etc. The true vine, that is, the efficacious one, not an imaginary one which effects nothing etc.

Latin:

Ego sum vitis vera.

Supra longa oratione consolatus est, & confirmavit imbecilles conscientias: hic idem agit per parabolam. Comparat autem se viti, patrem agricolae, sicut & Esaias cap. 5. vinitorem ex eo facit.

Qui docent liberi arbitrii iustitias, illi imaginantur Christum vitam esse, hoc modo: Rationem quidem per sese bene operari, sed ea bona opera accepta esse deo, si praeter ipsa fides addatur. Sicut & Hieronymus scribit ad Galatas: Bene quidem operatum esse Abraham, etiam priusquam crederet, sed ea opera postea fuisse accepta deo cum crederet. Sed Christus se vitem vocat, & veram, id est efficacem, hoc est quae ex se gignat palmites, animet, vegetet, ac fecundet. Hic sermo consolatur sanctos, ut fidant potentiae Christi, non suae, ut sciant potentem & efficacem in se Christum esse, ex quo ceu palmites nati & vivificati sunt. Ut cum docet se esse caput, quod exponit Apostolus, implere eum omnia, & coaugmentare corpus, ad Ephesios 4. Quod doctores humanae iustitiae arbitrantur huiusmodi caput esse, ut sunt hominum praefecti: non quod aliquid efficiat in aliis, sed sit aliquid sublimius aliis. At Christus sic caput est, ut animet totum corpus suum, sicut naturale caput vitam distillat in totum corpus. Vides ergo hac tota concione, liberi arbitrii vires damnari, & praeterea confirmari imbecilles conscientias, quas cum terret potentia portarum infernarum, fidere vult Christus suae potentiae, quod per nostram infirmitatem dei potentia glorificetur &c. Vera vitis, id est efficax, non imaginaria quae nihil efficit &c.

## 15:1
And my Father is the husbandman.

He who planted this vine, and preserved it, because the counsel of the Lord abideth for ever, Psalm 32: as if he were to say thus: Do not despair, since the Father has planted this word, namely: He who shall believe, shall be saved. Trust in this word, because no creatures shall prevail. The Father who loves me, will certainly preserve the whole shrub.

Latin:

Et pater meus agricola est.

Qui plantavit hanc vitem, & servavit, quia consilium domini in aeternum manet, Psalmo 32: quasi sic dicat: Ne diffidite, siquidem pater plantavit hoc verbum, scilicet: Qui crediderit, salvus erit. Fidite hoc verbo, quia nullae creaturae praevalebunt. Pater qui cum diligit me, certe servabit totum fruticem.

## 15:2
Every branch in me that beareth not fruit.

Namely, he who seems to be a branch in me, that is through me, this is he whom I do not animate and make fruitful, although he may be endowed with the righteousness of the flesh: that is, he who trusts in his own works and powers, and does not trust in Christ.

Latin:

Omnem palmitem in me non ferentem fructum.

Scilicet qui videtur esse palmes in me, id est per me, hoc est quem ego non animo & fecundo, quanquàm iustitia carnis praeditus sit: id est qui fidit suis operibus & viribus, & non fidit Christo.

## 15:2
And every one that beareth fruit.

Truly the branch which is made fruitful by the vine, he will purge, that is, he will continually mortify, until it receives the tithes of the spirit. For this is the Christian life, to be renewed in the spirit of the mind etc. Ephes. 4.

Latin:

Et omnem qui fert fructum.

Vere palmitem qui fecundatur à vite, purgabit, id est subinde mortificabit, donec accipiat decimas spiritus. Nam haec est vita Christiana, renovari spiritu mentis &c. Ephes. 4.

## 15:3
Now you are clean.

Although the oldness of the flesh may remain, yet you are clean because of the word, which you have believed and which I have spoken to you. Christ commends grace, and here he calls them clean, whom a little before he had said ought to be cleansed. For those who believe, although there are remnants of sin in them, yet because they believe, they are righteous, and the remaining sin is forgiven them, just as he says to the Romans: There is now no condemnation for those who are in Christ. Secondly, he adds whence they are clean, on account of the word: which word? This is the sum of the preaching of Christ, which he himself indicated above, where he says: I am the way, the truth, and the life. Likewise: He who sees me, sees also the Father: that is, that he who believes is justified, that he is received into grace through Christ. Therefore, the word of which he speaks is the whole Gospel.

Latin:

Iam vos mundi estis.

Quanquàm reliqua sit vetustas carnis, tamen mundi estis propter sermonem, cui credidistis & quem ego locutus sum vobis. Gratiam commendat Christus, & hic cum mundos vocat, quos paulò ante dixerat purgari oportere. Nam qui credunt, quanquàm reliquiae peccati sint, in iis reliquiae tamen quia credunt, iustae sunt, & condonatur eis reliquum peccatum, sicut ad Romanos inquit: Nulla nunc condemnatio iis qui in Christo sunt. Secundo subiicit unde mundi sint, propter sermonem: quem sermonem? Summa praedicationis Christi est haec, quam supra indicavit ipse, ubi dicit: Ego sum via, veritas, & vita. Item: Qui videt me, videt & patrem: id est iustificari eum qui credit se per Christum recipi in gratiam. Est itaque sermo de quo loquitur totum Evangelium.

## 15:3
On account of the word etc.

The word does not cleanse except the one receiving it, otherwise it would have cleansed the Pharisees too, and Christ himself says above: He who has my commandments. And in the Acts: Purifying their hearts by faith: wherefore it is necessary that you understand here on account of the word believed, that is, that the apostles are clean for this reason, because they believe the word of Christ. It is memorable what Augustine argues in this place concerning the sacraments, why Christ here says on account of my word, when nevertheless the sacraments cleanse. He answers: That they are not cleansed by the sacrament, except through the word, and he teaches rightly that the sacraments are efficacious through faith. And he explains himself the sentence commonly tossed about in the schools: The word comes to the element, and it becomes a sacrament. These are the words of Augustine. Why does he not say, you are clean because of the baptism by which you have been washed, but says because of the word which I have spoken to you? unless because even in the water, the word cleanses. Take away the word, and what is water, but water? The word comes to the element, and it becomes a sacrament, even itself as a visible word. For he had certainly said this also, when he washed the feet of the disciples: He who is washed, does not need except to wash his feet, but is clean all over. Whence [comes] this such great power of water, that it touches the body and washes the heart, unless by the word acting? not because it is spoken, but because it is believed. These are the words of Augustine. Then he says: Which I have spoken to you, because it is the word of Christ that justifies, human traditions do not justify: because they cannot render me certain of the power, mercy, [and] divine justice. As Isaiah says cap. 29. In vain they worship me with the commandments of men &c. You will observe the antithesis. If we are clean because of the speech of Christ: therefore not because of works &c.

Latin:

Propter sermonem &c.

Sermo non mundat nisi accipientem, alioqui & pharisaeos mundasset, & ipse Christus supra inquit: Qui habet mandata mea. & in actis: Fide purificans corda: quare necesse est, ut hic intelligas propter sermonem creditum, id est mundos esse apostolos propterea, quia sermoni Christi credant. Memorabile est, quod hoc loco disputat de sacramentis Augustinus, cur hic Christus dicat propter sermonem meum, cum tamen sacramenta purificent. Respondet: Nec sacramento purificari, nisi per sermonem, docetque probe sacramenta per fidem efficacia esse. Et exponit ipse sententiam vulgo iactatam in scholis: Accedit verbum ad elementum, & fit sacramentum. Verba Augustini haec sunt. Quare non ait, mundi estis propter baptismum quo loti estis, sed ait propter verbum, quod locutus sum vobis? nisi quia & in aqua, verbum mundat. Detrahe verbum, & quid est aqua, nisi aqua? Accedit verbum ad elementum, & fit sacramentum, etiam ipsum tanquam visibile verbum. Nam & hoc utique dixerat, quando pedes discipulis lavit: Qui lotus est, non indiget nisi ut pedes lavet, sed est mundus totus. Unde ista tanta virtus aquae, ut corpus tangat & cor abluat, nisi faciente verbo? non quia dicitur, sed quia creditur. Haec Augustinus. Deinde dicit: Quem ego locutus sum vobis, quia verbum Christi est quod iustificat, humanae traditiones non iustificant: quia non possunt me reddere certum de potentia, misericordia, iustitia divina. Sicut Esaias dicit cap. 29. Frustra colunt me mandatis hominum &c. Observabis antithesin. Si propter sermonem Christi mundi sumus: ergo non propter opera &c.

## 15:4
Abide in me, and I in you.

Namely by faith, because by faith we are made one spirit of Christ &c. If now you shall have abided in me, and I shall abide in you, that is, I will be effective and powerful in you, even if the gates of hell terrify.

Latin:

Manete in me, & ego in vobis.

Scilicet fide, quia fide efficimur unus spiritus Christi &c. Si nunc in me manseritis, & ego manebo in vobis, id est efficax, & potens ero in vobis, etiam si terreant portae inferorum.

## 15:4
As the branch cannot bear &c.

So Christ, because He fills all things, transfuses the spirit into the saints, animates them, vivifies them: just as the vine transfuses natural sap into the branches &c.

Latin:

Sicut palmes non potest ferre &c.

Ita Christus, quia implet omnia, transfundit spiritum in sanctos, animat eos, vivificat eos: sicut palmes transfundit naturalem succum in ramos &c.

## 15:4
If you shall have remained in me.

In this place Christ teaches both faith and works. Those things which follow immediately pertain to confirming faith: If you shall have remained in me, and my words shall have remained in you &c. For it is a promise, by which consciences are rendered certain that they are heard, so that our prayers may be as it were a certain experience of faith, or a proof. In the same manner He promised above cap. 14. Whatever you shall ask in my name, I will do this &c. And Matth. 21. All things whatsoever you shall ask in prayer believing, you shall receive. And the heart is to be lifted up by such promises, so that he may certainly feel that he is heard, that there is for him in all temptations a certain refuge. To this pertain those things which have been said concerning the priesthood of Christ.

Latin:

Si manseritis in me.

Hoc loco tum fidem, tum opera docet Christus. Ad fidem confirmandam pertinent quae proxime sequuntur: Si manseritis in me, & verba mea in vobis manserint &c. Est enim promissio, qua conscientiae certae redduntur, quod exaudiantur, ut sint preces nostrae velut experientia quaedam fidei, seu probatio. Ad eundem modum supra promisit cap. 14. Quicquid petieritis in nomine meo, hoc faciam &c. Et Matth. 21. Omnia quae petieritis in oratione credentes, accipietis. Estque talibus promissionibus cor erigendum, ut certo sentiat se exaudiri, esse sibi in omnibus tentationibus certum perfugium. Huc pertinent quae de pontificatu Christi dicta sunt.

## 15:5
Without me you can do nothing.

That is, just as branches wither when cut from the vine, so all things that are not in Christ die, even though they may seem to be branches: even though they may have the righteousness of the flesh, they die nevertheless: because they do not have the vivid sap of the vine &c. Wherefore He adds: If anyone shall not have remained in me, that is, even if he be endowed with all human righteousness, he shall be cast out, that is, he shall wither.

Latin:

Sine me nihil potestis facere.

Id est, sicut palmites arescunt à vite recisi, ita moriuntur omnia quae non sunt in Christo, quamquàm videantur esse palmites: quanquàm habeant iustitiam carnis, moriuntur tamen: quia non habent vividum succum vitis &c. Quare subiicit: Si quis in me non manserit, id est etiam si praeditus sit omni iustitia humana, foras mittetur, id est arescet.

## 15:7
And my words in you.

What it is to remain in him, he explains when he says: If my words shall have remained in you: so that he may teach that to remain in Christ is nothing else, except to believe his word, and just as he himself speaks, to have his word, in the same way as cap. 6. he explains himself: He who comes to me, shall not hunger: and, he who believes in me, shall not thirst forever. For this is to remain in Christ, for he teaches that consciences cannot be certified except by the word.

Latin:

Et verba mea in vobis.

Quid sit manere in ipso, exponit cum inquit: Verba mea si manserint in vobis: ut doceat quod manere in Christo, nihil aliud sit, nisi verbo eius credere, & sicut ipse loquitur, verbum eius habere, quemadmodum cap. 6. se exponit: Qui venit ad me, non esuriet: &, qui credit in me, non sitiet in aeternum. Nam hoc est manere in Christo, docet enim conscientias non posse nisi verbo certificari.

## 15:8
In this is my father glorified.

That is, in sanctifying you, and in hearing my word. It is a Hebraism, is glorified, for is being glorified. And the meaning is: The glory of the father becomes known in you, when you bear fruit abundantly. Moreover, this sentence embraces two things: first, that the fruit which we make is the glory of the father, that is, a work of the power of the father, not of our own strengths. And he said that also a little before, when he says that the father the husbandman prunes the branch: that is, that it is the work of the father to sanctify, not of human strengths: just as also in general, the Gospel proclaims the kingdom, the glory, the power of God, that is, it proclaims this, that we are justified, we are sanctified by the power of God, not by human strengths. Secondly, it teaches that the Father becomes known when we bear fruit, that is, the Father shows himself, shows mercy and power, when he sanctifies, and brings it about that those who are sanctified fear him, and believe in him, and no longer trust in any human things, and affairs, and strengths.

Latin:

In hoc clarificatus est pater meus.

Id est in sanctificandis vobis, & in audiendo verbo meo. Hebraismus est glorificatus est, pro glorificatur. Estque sententia: Gloria patris innotescit vobis, cum vos ubertim fructificatis. Complectitur autem haec sententia duo: primum, quod fructus quem facimus sit gloria patris, id est opus potentiae patris, non nostrarum virium. Idque & paulò ante dixit, cum ait patrem agricolam purgare palmitem: id est patris opus esse sanctificare, non humanarum virium: sicut & in universum, Evangelium praedicat regnum, gloriam, potentiam dei, id est hoc praedicat, quod iustificemur, sanctificemur potentia dei, non humanis viribus. Secundo docet, quod pater innotescat cum fructum ferimus, id est pater ostendit se, ostendit misericordiam & potentiam, cum sanctificat, efficitque ut qui sanctificantur timeant eum, & credant ei, nec fidant iam ullis humanis, & rebus, & viribus.

## 15:8
And you may be made my disciples.

Finally, he returns again to the word. You may be made my disciples, as if he were saying thus: When the Father is glorified, then you are my disciples, that is, when you believe in my word. For they are Christ's disciples who believe in his word, John 8. If you abide in my word, you are truly my disciples. And this is the meaning, the Father is glorified, that is, he becomes known by my word. You will know the Father when you have believed in my word: because the Father's mercy is felt when we believe that we are justified through Christ, and it comes about that we are able to fear and to trust. And thus glory is rendered to the Father.

Latin:

Et efficiamini mei discipuli.

Redit postremo iterum ad verbum. Efficiamini mei discipuli, quasi sic dicat: Cum clarificatus est pater, tunc mei discipuli estis, id est cum meo verbo creditis. Nam Christi discipuli sunt, qui eius verbo credunt, Ioannis 8. Si manseritis in sermone meo, vere discipuli mei estis. Estque haec sententia, pater glorificatur, id est innotescit meo verbo. Patrem noscetis ubi verbo meo credideritis: quia patris misericordia sentitur, cum credimus nos per Christum iustificari, & fit ut timere ac fidere possimus. Atque ita gloria redditur patri.

## 15:9
As the Father has loved me, so I also etc.

This is the reason for all things which have been promised above, and the torch by which he inflames us to the doing of the law: therefore he added this as a kind of epilogue to the promises, and the head to the sermon on works which follows. And it is the final and highest consolation against all temptations, to trust that the Father loves us. Since He valued us so highly, that He cast Christ below us, so that Christ became a minister: namely, He who washed our feet. Therefore He says thus: As the Father has loved me, so I love you. In order to render the conscience certain that we are loved: He argues thus, that it is most certain that the Father loves the Son, as He who showed it by glorifying Him, first through other miracles after the resurrection. Therefore Christ also wishes it to be most certain to our consciences that He Himself loves us, as if He were saying thus: You see that it is most certain that I am loved by the Father: so feel that it is certain that you are loved by me, even if your glorification is deferred. Christ therefore does this, that He may certify consciences. The conclusion also pertains to the same: Abide in my love, that is, be certain that I love you: because if you shall have felt this for certain, you will also keep my commandments etc. this is, the very observation of my commandments will be a testimony that you abide in my love etc. as above.

Latin:

Sicut dilexit me pater, sic ego &c.

Haec ratio omnium quae supra promissa sunt, & fax qua inflammat nos ad faciendam legem: ideo promissionibus hunc velut epilogum adiecit, & caput sermoni de operibus qui se quitur. Estque postrema & summa adversus omnes tentationes consolatio, fidere quod diligat nos pater. Siquidem tanti fecit nos, ut infra nos Christum abiiceret, ut factus sit Christus minister: nempe qui lavit pedes nostros. Ideo sic ait: Sicut dilexit me pater, ita ego diligo vos. Ut certam reddat conscientiam diligi nos: sic argumentatur, certissimum esse, quod filium pater diligat, ut qui id ostenderit glorificato eo, primum per alia muracula post resurrectionem. Ideo certissimum quoque vult conscientiis nostris esse Christus, quod ipse nos diligat, quasi sic dicat: Videtis certissimum esse, quod à patre diligar: ita sentite certum esse, quod à me diligamini, etiam si differatur glorificatio vestra. Agit itaque hoc Christus, ut certificet conscientias. Eôdem pertinet & conclusio: Manete in dilectione mea, id est certi estote quod diligam vos: quod si pro certo senseritis, & praecepta mea servabitis &c. hoc est ipsa observatio praeceptorum meorum, erit testimonium quod maneatis in dilectione mea &c. ut supra.

## 15:10
If you shall have kept my commandments.

To be made law is nothing else, except to be humbled even to the extreme: because the law is the word of judgment by which all our things are argued. Thus Christ made the law, having been attenuated even unto the last, made obedient to the Father even unto the death of the cross. He requires therefore that we also do the law in like manner, that is, that we be emptied, just as he also was emptied, which law the flesh neither understands nor does, but faith understands and does. He consoles therefore the saints, as if he were saying thus: Feel that I love you, even as if I empty myself, just as it is certain that I am loved by the father, although he may seem to have abandoned me.

Latin:

Si praecepta mea servaveritis.

Legem sieri nihil aliud est, nisi ad extremum usque humiliari: quia lex est verbum iudicii quo arguuntur omnia nostra. Sic Christus legem fecit, extenuatus est ad novissimum usquod, factus obediens patri usque ad mortem crucis. Exigit itaque ut similiter faciamus & nos legem, hoc est extenuemur, sicut & ipse extenuatus est, quam legem caro nec intelligit, nec facit, fides autem intelligit & facit. Consolatur itaque sanctos, quasi sic dicat: Sentite quod diligam vos, tam & si extenuem, sicut certum est me à patre diligi, quanquam reliquisse videatur.

## 15:11
That my joy &c.

Christ's kingdom is to reign in hearts, wherefore he himself also rejoices at the same time, that is, he reigns and is glorified: and our joy is perfected, that is, we are vivified and confirmed &c.

Latin:

Ut gaudium meum &c.

Christi regnum est, in cordibus regnare, quare simul gaudet & ipse, hoc est regnat & glorificatur: & nostrum gaudium perficitur, id est nos vivificamur & confirmamur &c.

## 15:12
This is my commandment, that you love.

The whole law delivers altogether two things, how we may serve God, and how we may converse with men. Therefore Christ has taught thus far, that the father is not known, nor worshipped, except by faith in Christ. This is almost the sum of the previous sermon: No one comes to the father, except through me &c. And thus Christ completed the first part of the law, when he taught how we may be justified before God. The other part of the law teaches how one must converse with men. Therefore also this commandment concerning charity is subjected. Those who do not understand the doctrine of faith will wonder here why he did not also command concerning loving God: but he completed that part of the law concerning the worship of God above, when he taught that it is the worship of the Father to believe in Christ.

Because he says my commandment, he opposes it to the law of Moses, which was not understood before, the commandment concerning charity. Therefore, when it is understood in a new way, it also becomes a new word. Because the flesh, just as it understands the commandment concerning worshipping God as concerning external works, not concerning trust and fear: so also it does not understand the commandment concerning charity as concerning the affection of the heart. Christ, however, explains the commandment concerning charity: As I have loved you: when he places charity in this, that we humble and cast ourselves below all, and resign to all things, honor, and life. And thus you see that extreme attenuation of ourselves is commanded by the law, just as Christ also says: Whoever wishes to follow me, let him deny himself. Whence it sufficiently appears that the law cannot be fulfilled by free will. For nature seeks the things that are its own. You see likewise how the vindication of things, of reputation, of life is forbidden. As he says in Deuteronomy: Vengeance is mine. And so, just as he takes away glory from God who justifies himself, who arrogates wisdom to himself: so he abrogates the glory of God who arrogates vengeance to himself. And from this fountain are born those laws, Matthaei 5.

Latin:

Hoc est praeceptum meum, ut diligatis.

Duo omnino tota lex tradit, quomodo deo serviamus, & quomodo cum hominibus conversemur. Proinde Christus hactenus docuit, patrem non cognosci, non coli, nisi fide in Christum. Haec ferè concionis superioris summa est: Nemo venit ad patrem, nisi per me &c. Atque ita Christus primam partem legis absolvit, quando docuit, quomodo coram deo iustificemur. Altera pars legis docet, quomodo cum hominibus conversandum sit. Ideo & hoc praeceptum de charitate subiicitur. Mirabuntur hic, qui fidei doctrinam non intelligunt, cur non & de deo diligendo praeceperit: sed eam partem legis de cultu dei supra absolvit, cum docuit, cultum patris esse, credere in Christum.

Quod dicit praeceptum meum, opponit legi Moysi, quod ante non est intellectum, praeceptum de charitate. Proinde cum novo modo intelligitur, fit & novum verbum. Quia caro sicut praeceptum de colendo deo intelligit de externis operibus, non de fiducia & timore: sic & praeceptum de charitate non intelligit de affectu cordis. Exponit autem Christus praeceptum de charitate: Sicut dilexi vos: cum charitatem in eo ponat, ut humiliemus & abiiciamus nos infra omnes, & resignemus omnibus rem, honorem, & vitam. Atque ita vides praecipi lege extremam extenuationem nostri, sicut & Christus ait: Qui vult me sequi, abneget seipsum. Unde satis apparet, legem non posse fieri libero arbitrio. Natura enim quaerit quae sua sunt. Vides item quomodo sit interdicta, rerum, famae, vitae vindicta. Sicut inquit in Deuteronomio: Mihi vindicta. Itaque sicut deo adimit gloriam, qui ipse se iustificat, qui sibi sapientiam arrogat: ita dei gloriae abrogat, qui vindictam sibi arrogat. Et ex hoc fontenascuntur leges illae, Matthaei 5.

## 15:13
Greater love than this no one has.

Namely, just as I will that you also love one another. When He says soul, He already embraces everything. For friends, that is, for those whom you love, whether they love you or not.

Latin:

Maiorem hac dilectionem nemo habet.

Scilicet quemadmodum volo, ut & vos diligatis mutuò. Cum dicit animam, iam omnia complectit. Proamicis, id est iis quos diligis, sive ii te diligant, sive non.

## 15:13
As I have loved you.

At the same time, Christ here commends to us the magnitude and power of His love, and testifies that He sanctions the same law for us and for Himself, that just as He wills that we love one another, so He Himself also loves us, and seals that love with His death as if with a seal.

Latin:

Sicut dilexi vos.

Simul hic nobis commendat Christus magnitudinem, & vim amoris sui, & testatur eandem legem sancire nobis et sibi, quod sicut vult ut nos diligamus mutuò: ita nos ipse etiam diligit, eumque amorem morte sua velut sigillo consignat.

## 15:15
I will no longer call you servants.

Christ adds the testimonies of His love toward us. First, that He has made us not servants, but friends. Second, that He Himself has chosen us freely. The Apostle Roman. 8. distinguishes sons from servants in this manner, that those are sons in whom is the spirit of adoption: servants are those in whom the spirit is not: that sons are heirs of eternal life, servants are cast out. Just as also Isaac alone obtained the inheritance of the father, while Ishmael was sent away elsewhere: So here Christ distinguishes servants from friends, because servants do not know the will of the master, friends do know it. Those who are of Christ know the will of God toward them: because to be in Christ is nothing else than to feel with firm faith that the Father has had mercy on us, and has received us into grace, and will protect and guard us whatever may happen. Therefore they cheerfully bear the cross because they know that they are looked upon by the Father, and that this is the good will of the Father to be glorified in this way. But servants do not know the will of the master, that is, they do not have the word of faith in their heart. Therefore they do not know that God is propitious to them. Let the servant be offered without faith, feeling thus within himself, I do not know whether I am accepted by God or not, perhaps He does not look upon me. And this infidelity, because it is in the heart of the servant, causes him not to endure the cross: indeed when he is tempted, he feels that God is angry with him, he feels that he is rejected. You have an example in the Israelites murmuring against Moses. And what Christ says here, that servants do not know the will of the master, appears beautifully in the words of the Israelite multitude, Exodi 17. Is the Lord among us, or not? And Deuteronom. 29. The Lord has not given you a heart to understand and eyes to see &c. Thus also the people of the law are ignorant of the will of God toward them.

Latin:

Iam non dicam vos servos.

Subiicit Christus testimonia amoris sui erga nos. Primum, quod nos non servos, sed amicos fecerit. Secundo, quod ipse elegerit nos gratis. Apostolus Roman. 8. discernit filios à servis in hunc modum, filios esse, in quibus spiritus adoptionis est: servos in quibus spiritus non est: filios haeredes esse vitae aeternae, servos eiici. Sicut & solus Isaac haereditatem patris consecutus est, Ismaël aliò ablegatus: Ita hic Christus discernit servos ab amicis, quod servi nesciant voluntatem domini, amici sciant. Qui Christi sunt, sciunt voluntatem dei erga se: quia nihil aliud est esse in Christo, nisi firma fide sentire, quod pater sit nostri misertus, & receperit nos in gratiam, tueaturiam & custodiat quicquid acciderit. Ideo alacres ferunt crucem quod sciant se à patre respici, & hanc esse patris bonam voluntatem sic glorificari. Servi vero nesciunt voluntatem domini, id est non habent in corde verbum fidei. Ideo nesciunt sibi deum esse propitium. Offeratur sine fide servus, sic apud se sentiens, nescio utrum deo sim acceptus, nec'ne, fortasse me non respicit. Atque haec infidelitas, quia est in corde servi, facit ut non toleret crucem: immo cum tentatur, sentit sibi irasci deum, sentit se reiici. Habes exemplum in Israëlitis murmurantibus adversum Moysen. Et quod hic ait Christus, servos nescire voluntatem domini, pulchre apparet in verbis Israëliticae multitudinis, Exodi 17. Est ne dominus in nobis, an non? Et Deuteronom. 29. Non dedit dominus cor intelligens et oculos videntes &c. Sic etiam populus legis ignorat voluntatem dei erga se.

## 15:15
All things that I have heard from my Father, I have made known to you.

That is, I have preached to you the Gospel of peace and life, by which you may be comforted against the scandal of the cross, and against the gates of hell &c. Thus therefore Christ argues, that I love you, the testimony is the word, by which I have made you friends, by which word against the scandal you are armed, since either I am suffering, or you are about to suffer.

Latin:

Quaecunque audiv à patre, nota feci vobis.

Id est praedicavi vobis Evangelium pacis & vitae, quo consolemini adversus scandalum crucis, & adversus portas inferorum &c. Sic ergo argumentatur Christus, quod diligam vos, testimonio verbum est, quo vos amicos feci, quo verbo adversus scandalum armemini, cum vel ego patiar, vel vos passuri estis.

## 15:16
You have not chosen me, but I have chosen you.

There are those who refer this to the election of the apostles, by which they were called to office. I do not see what prevents referring it to the election of all the saints: because a little later he says: Whatever you shall ask the father: which it is sufficiently evident pertains to all the saints. Therefore, the first thing to be observed is that he chose freely without our merits. Thus, therefore, he argues: That I love you, it appears from this, because I chose you freely. Just as the Apostle to Romans 5. argues: If he had mercy on us to justify, certainly he will also have mercy to save.

Latin:

Non vos me elegistis, sed ego vos.

Sunt qui ad apostolorum electionem referunt, qua illi ad officium vocati sunt. Ego non video quid vetet referre ad omnium sanctorum electionem: quia paulò post dicit: Quodcunque petieritis patrem: quod ad omnes sanctos pertinere, satis constat. Primum ergo observandum est, quod gratis elegit sine meritis nostris. Sic ergo argumentatur: Quod diligam vos, inde apparet, quia gratis elegi. Sicut Apostolus ad Romanos 5. argumentatur: Si misertus est nostri ut iustificaret, certe miserescet etiam ut salvet.

## 15:16
That whatever you shall ask the father in my name, he may give to you.

This is what the Gospel accomplishes in sum, that it renders them certain of the will of the father, so that we may be able to ask, and that it may be certain that it is not asked in vain. Therefore, we do not plead our merits, but we ask on account of the son, whom the father testified he loved when he glorified him. And so we cannot ask in vain, when we ask in the name of the son.

Latin:

Ut quodcunque petieritis patrem in no-mine meo, det vobis.

Hoc in summa efficit Evangelium, ut certos reddat de voluntate patris, ut petere possimus, & ut certum sit non frustra peti. Proinde non causamur merita nostra, sed propter filium petimus, quem testatus est pater se diligere, cum glorificavit. Itaque non possumus frustra petere, cum nomine filii petimus.

## 15:17
This I command you, that you love one another.

Finally, he repeats the law, as if he were saying thus. Because I love you, because I have testified so manifoldly concerning love my own, etc. I desire that you in turn should love one another. For thus the Gospel commands, by showing the example of Christ: because it is necessary that the people of the Gospel be spontaneous, as the Psalm says 109.

Latin:

Hoc mando vobis, ut diligatis invicem.

Postremo repetit legem, quasi sic dicat. Quia diligo vos, quia tam multipliciter testificatus sum de amore meo &c. volo ut vos vicissim diligatis inter vos. Sic enim praecipit Evangelium, ostentando exemplum Christi: quia oportet Evangelii populum spontaneum esse, sicut Psalm. dicit 109.

## 15:18
If the world hates you.

Above, Christ comforted his own against all kinds of temptations: and especially against that weakness by which consciences desire to be certified concerning justification. For those things pertain to this which he discusses with Philip: He who sees me, sees also the Father, etc., when he, namely, desires to discern some sign of the divine will, by which the conscience might be rendered more certain. Christ recalls [them] to the word, so that he may raise up the conscience and confirm it by faith in the word: otherwise no sign would be of benefit. To which sentiment it was said elsewhere: No other sign shall be given to this generation, except the sign of Jonah: that is, faith in Christ, dying and rising again, will be of benefit, but to see signs will profit nothing. And these things indeed thus far. Now he comforts against external persecution. But he comforts by his own example, namely, so that he may confirm the faith of the saints, which is done when they perceive that just as Christ has been glorified, so they themselves are also to be glorified, etc. And because the Gospel preaches the glory of God, therefore it does not permit that we should avenge ourselves: according to that: Vengeance is mine. And the Apostle says, that the Gospel is carried about in earthen vessels, that is, that Christians ought to suffer, and to expect glorification only from God. You see, however, that the whole Gospel is prepared for the present struggle. If the world hates you. namely, you who condemn human hypocrisy. Know that it has hated me before you. namely, so that, strengthened by my example, you may believe that it is done by the good will of God. If you had been of the world. if you had approved of human wisdom and justice, the world would love what is its own. But because you are not of the world. because you approve of the wisdom and justice of the spirit. The servant is not greater than the lord. because it behooves us to become similar to the image of God, as the Apostle says. If they have persecuted me &c. namely, if Satan dared against me, whom he saw glorified by God with such great miracles. Because they do not know him who sent me. that is, they think themselves wise and just, nor do they perceive the hypocrisy of the flesh. Therefore, when this hypocrisy is condemned, it is necessary for them to persecute the word.

For my name's sake. that is, thinking that they are offering service to God: because they perceive that word to be such that it is opposed to the glory of God, and thus they seem to themselves to be defending that word.

Latin:

Si mundus vos odit.

Supra consolatus est Christus suos adversus omnia genera tentationum: & in primis adversus eam imbecillitatem, qua conscientiae de iustificatione cupiunt certificari. Nam eò pertinent, quae cum Philippo agit: Qui videt me, videt & patrem &c. cum ille scilicet divinae voluntatis signum aliquod cernere cupit, quo certior reddatur conscientia. Christus ad verbum revocat, ut conscientiam erigat, & confirmet fide verbi: caeterum nullum signum profore. In quam sententiam dictum est aliâs: Non dabitur isti generationi aliud signum, praeter signum Ionae: id est fides in Christum, morientem & resurgentem profutura est, caeterum signa videre nihil proderit. Et haec quidem hactenus. Nunc consolatur adversus externam persecutionem. Consolatur autem suo exemplo, scilicet ut fidem sanctorum confirmet, id quod fit cum sentiunt, quod sicut Christus glorificatus est, ita sint & ipsi glorificandi &c. Et quia Evangelium praedicat dei gloriam, ideo non permittit ut ipsi nos vindicemus: iuxta illud: Mihi vindicta. Et Apostolus inquit, Evangelium vasis fictilibus circumferri, hoc est Christianos oportere pati, & glorificationem solum à deo expectare. Vides autem Evangelium totum ad praesentem pugnam comparatam esse. Si mundus vos odit. scilicet vos damnantes hypocrisin humanam. Scitote, quia me priorem vobis odio habuit. scilicet ut consirmati meo exemplo credatis bona voluntate dei fieri. Si de mundo fuissetis. si probassetis sapientiam & iustitiam humanam, mundus quod suum est, diligeret. Quia vero de mundo non estis. quia probatis sapientiam & iustitiam spiritus. Servus non est maior domino. quia oportet nos similes fieri imaginis dei, ut inquodt Apostolus. Si me persecuti sunt &c. scilicet si ausus fuit satan contra me, quem vidit tantis miraculis glorificari à deo. Quia nesciunt eum qui misit me. id est ipsi se putant sapientes & iustos, nec hypocrisin carnis sentiunt. Proinde cum damnatur haec hypocrisis, necesse est eos psequi verbum.

Propter nomen meum. id est putantes se obsequium praestare deo: quia sentiunt id verbum tale esse, quod adversaretur gloriae dei, & sic videntur sibi tueri illud verbum.

## 15:22
If I had not come and spoken.

It is sufficiently evident that Christ reproaches the unbelievers his coming, preaching, and miracles, by all of which he invited them to believe. Just as Luke 13 he reproaches them with his benefits. How often would I have gathered you, and you would not? And Isaiah 5 What is it that I ought to have done more to my vineyard, and have not done? And Ezekiel 16 I have given you as the bud of the field multiplied, etc. So also here he reproaches his benefits, as if he were saying: Therefore the Gospel was revealed, that it might be made manifest, that faith is so required without regard for works, that unbelief condemns, even though one may have all the righteousnesses of the world. And if he had not revealed this, they would indeed be sinners, but yet the conscience would not judge unbelief, would not feel it to be a sin: because it would lack the word according to which the conscience judges. Now since the conscience feels that unbelief is a sin, why does it not believe, why does it distrust, why does it despair, why do they seek their own righteousnesses rather than his word? Therefore Christ does not say that they would not have been guilty of sin if he had not come, but that they would not have had this sin, namely of unbelief. Secondly, neither does he argue this, that unbelief would not have been a sin if he had not come: but that they would not have had that sin, that is, their consciences would not have been able to judge and feel that it was a sin. Therefore he explains sin in this way, just as scripture for the most part uses it for sin, insofar as it is known, judged, and

it is felt, just as Paul also speaks: The sting of death is sin. Therefore, as if Christ were calling upon the very consciences of the unbelievers, as if he were saying thus, since their consciences have been convicted by my word that unbelief is sin, why therefore do they not believe? And it is an enthymeme: If I had not come, they would not have sin: that is, their consciences would not have been convicted so that they might feel that unbelief is sin: because they would not have had the word, which the conscience follows in judging. But now their own consciences are judged: they acknowledge that unbelief is sin, etc. Just as also above chapter 12. The word which I have spoken, that will judge, etc. But all these things pertain to this, that we may understand how holily faith is required: for if unbelief is sin, not only will the Father save those who believe in him, but he also commands that we believe.

Latin:

Si non venissem & locutus fuissem.

Satis constat, Christum exprobrare incredulis adventum suum, praedicationem, & miracula, quibus omnibus invitarit eos ad credendum. Quemadmodum Lucae 13. exprobrat eis sua beneficia. Quoties volui vos congregare, & noluistis? Et Esaiae 5. Quid est quod debui ultra facere vineae meae, & non feci? Et Ezechielis 16. Multiplicatam quasi germen agri dedi te &c. Ita & hic exprobrat sua benesicia, quasi se dicat: Ideo revelatum est Evangelium, ut palàm fieret, adeò fidem exigi citra operum respectum, ut incredulitas damnet, etiam tametsi omnes mundi iustitias habeat. Atque hoc si non revelasset, essent quidem peccatores, sed tamen conscientia non iudicaret incredulitatem, non sentiret esse peccatum: quia verbo careret secundum quod conscientia iudicat. Nunc cum conscientia sentiat incredulitatem esse peccatum, cur non credit, cur diffidit, cur desperat, cur suas iustitias requirunt potius quàm suum verbum? Itaque non dicit Christus non fuisse peccati reos, si non venisset, sed eos hoc peccatum, scilicet incredulitatis non habituros fuisse. Secundo, neque hoc agit, incredulitatem non fuisse peccatum, si non venisset: sed eos id peccatum habituros fuisse, id est conscientias non potuisse iudicare, & sentire id esse peccatum. Proinde sic exponit peccatum, quemadmodum scriptura plerunque usurpat pro peccato, quatenus cognoscitur, iudicatur, &

sentitur, sicut & Paulus loquitur: Aculeus mortis peccatum est. Proinde velut ipsas conscientias incredulorum appellet Christus, quasi sic dicat, cum conscientiae meo verbo convictae sint, peccatum esse incredulitatem, cur igitur non credunt? Estque enthimema: Si non venissem, peccatum: non haberent: id est non fuissent convictae eorum conscientiae, ut sentirent incredulitatem esse peccatum: quia verbum non habuissent, quod in iudicando conscientia sequitur. Nunc autem ipsorum conscientiae iudicantur: agnoscunt incredulitatem esse peccatum &c. Sicut & supra capite 12. Sermo quem locutus sum, ille iudicabit &c. Haec autem omnia eò pertinent, ut intelligamus quàm sancte fides exigatur: nam si peccatum est incredulitas, non modò servabit pater, qui sibi credunt, sed praecipit etiam ut credamus.

## 15:22
They would not have sin.

That is, they would not have felt that unbelief is sin.

Latin:

Peccatum non haberent.

Id est non sensissent incredulitatem esse peccatum.

## 15:22
But now they have no excuse.

The consciences have been convicted that unbelief is sin. He who hates me, hates my father also, that is, just as those who persecute me confess their hatred of me: so they confess that they do not believe. And the father hates mine, that is, although they persecute the Gospel, they wish to appear to be defending the glory of God. If I had not done works, that is, if I had not testified to my doctrine by miracles. But now both they have seen, that is, they are convicted, and yet they do not believe.

Latin:

Nunc autem excusationem non habent.

Conscientiae convictae sunt, quod incredulitas sit peccatum. Qui me odit, & patrem meum odit, id est sicut ii fatentur odium mei, qui me persequuntur: ita fatentur se non credere. Et patrem meum odit, id est quanquam persequuntur Evangelium, videri volunt se tueri gloriam dei. Si opera non fecissem, id est si non testificatus fuissem meam doctrinam miraculis. Nunc autem & viderunt, id est convicti sunt, & tamen non credunt.

## 15:24
And they hate me, and my father.

Although they wish to appear to be defending the glory of the father, when they oppose the Gospel, they bring forward their own righteousnesses, they defend their own wisdom, not the glory of God. Because Christ alone proclaims the glory of the father, and teaches that our righteousnesses are sin, for the reason that the heart is void of trust toward God, and of the fear of God. Because human hypocrisy does not discern this, therefore it condemns the Gospel.

The word which is in their law, namely testifying against them.

Latin:

Et oderunt me, & patrem.

Quanquàm velint videri tueri gloriam patris, cum adversantur Evangelio, ferunt suas iustitias, suam sapientiam tuentur, non gloriam dei. Quia Christus solus praedicat gloriam patris, docetque nostras iustitias peccatum esse, propterea quod cor vacet fiducia erga deum, & metu dei. Quod quia non cernit hypocrisis humana, ergo damnat Evangelium.

Sermo qui in lege eorum, scilicet testificante contra eos.

## 15:25
They have held me in hatred without a cause.

Without a cause is to be referred to the miracles, so that you may understand it thus. Since he testified concerning the word of Christ with so many miracles, then they hardened themselves in such a way that they cannot but persecute: although they have neither the word, nor divine authority for why they persecute.

Latin:

Odio me habuerunt gratis.

Gratis referendum est ad miracula, ut sic intelligas. Cum tot miraculis testificatus sit de verbo Christi, tum sic indurverunt, ut non possint non persequi: quam quàm non habeant, neque verbum, neque autoritatem divinam cur persequantur.

## 15:26
But when the paraclete shall have come.

Namely when I, through the resurrection, shall have been glorified I will be, whom I will send. I, namely, already glorified. The Spirit of truth, that is, who makes men truthful. He will bear witness, namely in the hearts of individuals, which is the earnest and pledge of grace, as the Apostle says.

Latin:

Cum autem venerit paracletus.

Scilicet ubi ego per resurrectionem glorificatus fuero, quem ego mittam. Ego, scilicet iam glorificatus. Spiritum veritatis, id est qui veraces facit. Ille testimonium perhibebit. scilicet in singulorum cordibus, quod arrabo & pignus gratiae, ut Apostolus inquit.

## 15:26
And you bear witness, etc.

You have been with me from the beginning, that is, you have already heard the doctrine long ago, so that you may be able to bear witness concerning my doctrine. And this is a notable passage for the authority of the apostles against those who say that the apostles were also men, and thus attempting to establish their own human traditions.

Latin:

Et vos testimonium &c.

Ab initio mecum estis, id est iam olim audistis doctrinam, ut possitis exhibere testimonium de mea doctrina. Estque hic insignis locus pro autoritate apostolorum contra eos, qui apostolos etiam homines fuisse dicunt, et sic suas traditiones humanas statuere conantes.

## 16:1 Argument
CHAPTER SIXTEEN.

Christ adds an epilogue to the preceding discourse, teaching that we should arm ourselves with his word against the scandal of the cross. For the conscience cannot be strengthened by the protection of any creature. As Isaiah testifies: The glory of the flesh is as the glory of hay. But the word of God is life, and therefore it gives life to consciences. To this point pertain those things which are said in this place: These things I have spoken to you, that you may not be scandalized. Likewise. When the hour shall come, you may remember that I told you. Thus Christ promises neither signs nor a new word, but commands them to remember his word. which has already been brought forth.

Latin:

CAPUT DECIMUM-SEXTUM.

Epilogum addit Christus superiori concioni, docens, ut sermone suo nos armemus adversus scandalum crucis. Nec enim potest conscientia creaturae ullius praesidio confirmari. Sicut testatur Esaias: Gloria carnis, quasi gloria foeni. At verbum dei vita est, ideoque vivificat conscientias. Huc pertinent ea quae hoc loco dicuntur: Haec locutus sum vobis, ne scandalizemini. Item. Cum venerit hora, memores sitis quia dixerim vobis. Adeò nec signa, nec novum verbum promittit Christus, sed iubet eius verbi recordari quod iam proditum est.

## 16:1
These things I have spoken to you.

Namely concerning death, concerning your persecutions: likewise that I, having undoubtedly received the kingdom, will be present with you. So that you may not be scandalized. That is, being confirmed by this word, you may endure on the cross, and against persecutions.

They will make you without synagogues, that is, they will proscribe you, and will excommunicate you, etc.

Latin:

Haec locutus sum vobis.

Scilicet de morte, de persecutionibus vestris: item quod ego haud dubie accepto regno adfuturus sum vobis. Ut non scandalizemini. id est hoc verbo confirmati duretis in cruce, & adversus persecutiones.

Absque synagogis facient vos, id est proscribent vos, & excommunicabunt &c.

## 16:2
But the hour comes, that everyone who kills.

This is the highest scandal, that by the judgment of the whole world Christians are held to be both foolish and impious. Just as wisdom and justice are the highest glory, so this is the supreme degree of ignominy, to disparage wisdom and justice. The Pharisees think thus: God commanded the Law, therefore the works of the Law justify. But Christ denies that the Law is either fulfilled or that one is justified, which, because they do not understand, they condemn, and they seem to themselves to be defending the Law of God. The Gentiles approve of customs and received opinions, against which if anyone should hand down anything, they judge this person to be insane. You have an example Amos 7. The whole land could not bear the words, etc. They think they are offering service. Namely because the Pharisees judge themselves to be defending the Law of God. For they do not see what the power of the Law is.

An example is in the Acts, where they said that Stephen was preaching against the law, against the temple, against the people. And these things they will do to you, because they have not known the father, nor me. That is, they do not understand the righteousness of faith: they do not know that the righteousness of God is truth, not hypocrisy. That you may remember, that I. Namely, that you may confirm yourselves with the word, and endure in the cross.

Latin:

Sed venit hora, ut omnis qui interficit.

Hoc scandalum summum est, quod totius mundi iudicio habentur Christiani tum pro fatuis, tum pro impiis. Sicut summa gloria est sapientia & iustitia, ita ignominiae gradus hic supremus est, derogare sapientiam & iustitiam. Pharisaei sic sentiunt: Legem deus praecepit, ergo legis opera iustificant. Christus vero negat tum fieri legem, tum iustificari, quod cum non intelligunt, damnant, videnturque sibi legem dei tueri. Gentes probant mores & receptas opiniones, contra quas si quid tradat quispiam, hunc insanire iudicant. Habes exemplum Amos 7. Non poterat universa terra sustinere sermones &c. Arbitrantur se obsequium praestare. scilicet quia legem dei tueri se iudicant pharisaei. Non enim vident quae sit vis legis.

Exemplum est in Actis, ubi Stephanum contra legem, contra templum, contra populum praedicare dicebant. Et haec facient vobis, quia non noverunt patrem, neque me. id est non intelligunt iustitiam fidei: nesciunt, iustitiam dei veritatem esse, non hypocrisim. Ut reminiscamini, quod ego. scilicet ut confirmetis vos verbo, & duretis in cruce.

## 16:5
But these things I did not say to you from the beginning.

Because you were not yet suffering, as he says elsewhere: The children of the bridegroom cannot mourn, as long as the bridegroom is with them.

Latin:

Haec autem vobis ab initio non dixi.

Quia nondum patiebamini, sicut aliâs inquit: Non possunt lugere filii sponsi, donec cum ipsis sponsus est.

## 16:5
And now I go to him who sent me.

Christ wishes his own to feel most certainly that the father will glorify his word, even though those who preach the word are condemned and killed by the judgment of the world. Therefore he repeats so often that he is going to the father, that is, although he is departing, is being killed now, yet this is the way by which it behooves him to be glorified, and that through death he is about to inaugurate his kingdom. And therefore he adds: None of you asks me. That is, I myself am already preaching of my own accord, so that it may be most certain to you that I am going to the father. Just as also above: Where I go you know, and the way you know, etc. For the disciples had the word of Christ, they believed him to be the savior, and therefore that it would come to pass that he would be glorified: but they were ignorant of the manner, and their faith had not yet been tested by temptation.

Therefore, although they had the word, they were not yet confirmed. In this manner he says here: None of you asks. As if he were to say, I have indeed foretold it to you, and you know the father, although your faith has not yet been tested. For he also censures their unbelief with those words which he added below.

Latin:

Et nunc vado ad eum qui misit.

Christus vult, suos certissime sentire patrem glorificaturum verbum suum, tametsi mundi iudicio damnentur, occidantur, qui praedicant verbum. Ideo toties repetit, se ad patrem ire, id est quanquam abeat, occidatur modò, tamen hanc esse viam qua glorificari eum oporteat, & per mortem se regnum auspicaturum esse. Ideoque subiicit: Nemo ex vobis interrogat me. id est ipse iam ultro praedico, ut certissimum sit vobis, me ire ad patrem. Sicut & supra: Quò ego vado scitis, & viam scitis &c. Habebant enim discipuli verbum Christi, credebant eum esse salvatorem, itaque futurum esse ut glorificaretur: caeterum modum ignorabant, & fides eorum nondum probata erat tentatione.

Itaque quanquàm verbum habebant, nondum tamen confirmati erant. Ad eum modum hic dicit: Nemo ex vobis interrogat. quasi dicat, vobis quidem praedixi, & patrem nostis, quanquàm fides vestra nondum sit probata. Nam & incredulitatem eorum taxat iis verbis quae subiecit.

## 16:6
Sorrow shall fill your heart.

The word of God is life, as John 1. Therefore it pacifies and gladdens hearts. But before it vivifies, it mortifies the sense of the flesh, as here. The apostles were trusting in the carnal presence of Christ: now when Christ predicts that he is going to the father, he snatches away from the flesh of the apostles that in which they were trusting: and he indicates in what other thing they ought to trust, namely Christ the king and priest with the father. Why has it been said often above: Whatever you shall ask the father in my name, he will give to you. You see that the power of the word of God is the same, God promises the land to the Jews who have gone out from Egypt. There he first excludes them from that land which they had held, and casts them into so many dangers. Thus in justification he commands that we believe in Christ, by which word he at the same time mortifies all our things, and commands that we expect help, glory, etc., from Christ alone. Sorrow shall fill your heart, namely when carnal trust shall be mortified by my word. But I tell you the truth.

namely that which gives life, although in the beginning it mortifies carnal confidence. It is expedient for you that I go. Namely, that I may begin the kingdom. The Paraclete will not come. That is, this kingdom will not be revealed.

Latin:

Tristitia implebit cor vestrum.

Verbum dei vita est, ut Ioannis 1. Ideo pacificat & exhilarat corda. Sed priusquam vivificet, mortificat sensum carnis, sicut hic. Apostoli confidebant in carnali praesentia Christi: nunc cum praedicit Christus se abiturum esse ad patrem, eripit carni apostolorum id in quo fidebant: & indicat in quo alio fidere debeant, scilicet Christo rege & sacerdote apud patrem. Cur saepe supra dictum est: Quicquid petieritis patrem in nomine meo, dabit vobis. Eandem vides esse verbi dei vim, deus promittit Iudaeis ex Aegypto egressis terram. Ibi primum excludit eos ea terra quam tenuerant, & in tot discrimina coniicit. Sic in iustificatione praecipit, ut Christo credamus, quo verbo simul mortificat omnia nostra, & praecipit ut expectemus à solo Christo opem, gloriam &c. Tristitia implebit cor vestrum, scilicet cum meo verbo mortificabitur carnalis fiducia. Sed ego veritatem dico vobis.

scilicet quae vivificat, quanquam principio mortificet carnalem fiduciam. Expedit vobis, ut ego usdam. scilicet ut inchoëm regnum. Paracletus non veniet. id est non revelabitur hoc regnum.

## 16:7
It is expedient that I go.

Nature wishes to be glorified without the cross: but the glory of the spirit and of eternal life cannot be attained, unless the flesh and this animal life are extinguished. Therefore also Christ died, that he might be glorified and blessed with eternal blessings: as it is written in Psalmo 20.. This is what he says here, it is expedient for you, because otherwise he could not begin the spiritual kingdom. And to this sentiment he adds: If I do not go, the paraclete will not come, that is, it will not be revealed. Furthermore, for the paraclete to be revealed is for the wisdom and justice of God to be revealed. Nor is the kingdom of Christ anything other than the revelation of the wisdom and justice of God. The Apostles were expecting a corporal kingdom. But Christ testifies that for this reason he must depart, so that it may be made plain that his kingdom is not a corporal kingdom: but a spiritual one, by which believers may feel that they are justified and live, even when they die. And so that this kingdom might be begun, it was necessary to depart. Psalmo 67. You have ascended on high &c. Therefore Christ departs, so that he may inaugurate the spiritual kingdom. But what the spiritual kingdom is, or what kind it is, he describes here:

whence you will easily estimate what the church is.

Latin:

Expedit ut ego vadam.

Natura vult glorificari sine cruce: at gloria spiritus & vitae aeternae non potest attingi, nisi extincta carne & hac vita animali. Ideo & Christus mortuus est, ut glorificaretur & benedicatur benedictionibus aeternis: sicut Psalmo 20. scriptum est. Hoc est quod hic ait, expedit vobis, quia aliâs inchoare regnum spirituale non potuit. Et in hanc sententiam subiicit: Si non abiero, paracletus non veniet, id est non revelabitur. Porrò paracletum revelari, est revelari sapientiam & iustitiam dei. Neque aliud est regnum Christi, nisi revelatio sapientiae & iustitiae dei. Apostoli expectabant corporale regnum. At Christus testatur, ideo se oportere abire, ut palàm fiat, non esse corporale regnum suum regnum: sed spirituale, quo sentiant credentes, iustificari et vivere se, etiam cum moriuntur. Atque ut id regnum inchoaretur abeundum erat. Psalmo 67. Ascendisti in altum &c. Ideo abit Christus, ut regnum spirituale auspicetur. Spirituale autem regnum quid sit, aut quale sit, hic describit:

unde quid sit ecclesia, facilè aestimabis.

## 16:7
But if I go away, I will send him to you.

The Holy Spirit is sent so that he may convict, whence first of all you will learn that the flesh itself cannot convict itself: otherwise why would the Holy Spirit be sent to convict the flesh? For reason does not discern unbelief, the contempt of God which is known to the flesh: according to that Jeremiah 6 They knew not how to blush. And of Proverbs: Because the foolish are right in his own eyes. This is why Scripture calls reason darkness, at Ephesians 4 Having the intellect darkened by darkness. 2 Corinthians 3 A veil is placed over their heart. Hence it appears what the light of nature, what reason, what free will can do.

Latin:

Si autem abiero, mittam eum ad vos.

Spiritussanctus mittitur ut arguat, unde primum omnium disces, carnem ipsam sese non posse arguere: alioqui cur mitteretur spiritussanctus ad arguendam carnem? Non enim cernit ratio incredulitatem, contemptum dei qui cognascitur carni: iuxta illud Hieremiae 6. Erubescere nescierunt. Et Proverbiorum: Quia stulti recta in oculis eius. Hoc est cur tenebras vocet scriptura rationem, ad Ephesios 4. Tenebris obscuratum habentes intellectum. 2. Corinth. 3. Velamen positum est super cor eorum. Hinc apparet quid lumen naturae, quid ratio, quid liberum arbitrium possit.

## 16:8
And when he has come, he will convict.
