He will convict, just as he said above, that now is the judgment of the world, and the prince of the world is cast out: So here he says that the flesh is convicted, that is, that the flesh is condemned and killed, just as he also says at Genesis 6 My spirit shall not remain in man, because he is flesh. For it is the work of the Holy Spirit to reveal unbelief, the ignorance of God in the flesh, and to condemn, and to cause that we acknowledge ourselves to be guilty of eternal death and curse. Just as at Romans 1 Now the wrath of God is revealed upon all impiety etc. The Holy Spirit in the hearts of those who receive it, it reveals impiety, and likewise judges and condemns impiety in the unbelieving by the word.

Latin:

Et cum venerit ille, arguet.

Arguet, sicut supra dixit, nunc iudicium esse mundi, & principem mundi foras eiici: Ita hic dicit, argui carnem, id est damnari & occidi carnem, sicut & Genesis 6. dicit: Non permanebit spiritus meus in homine, quia caro est. Est enim opus spiritussancti, revelare incredulitatem, ignorationem dei in carne, & damnare, & efficere ut agnoscamus nos reos esse aeternae mortis, & maledictionis. Sicut ad Romanos 1. Nunc revelatur ira dei super omnem impietatem &c. Spiritussanctus in eorum cordibus qui accipiunt, revelat impietatem, iudicat item & condemnat impietatem in incredulis verbo.

## 16:8
He will convict the world.

That he says the world is convicted signifies that the preaching of the Gospel pertains to all nations.

Latin:

Arguet mundum.

Quod ait mundum argui, significat Evangelii praedicationem ad omnes gentes pertinere.

## 16:9
Of sin indeed, because they did not believe.

Just as for the Gospel faith alone is righteousness: namely that: If you have committed all the sins of all people, yet you believe that the Father has mercy on you on account of Christ, you are saved, as above: He who believes in him is not judged. For this is the glory of the Gospel, that sinners are saved. So on the contrary, unbelief alone is sin, just as he also said above: He who does not believe is already judged: because he does not believe in the only-begotten Son of God. And John 12 The word which I have spoken, that will judge. You see the breadth of mercy. Furthermore, the flesh judges thus, that good works are righteousness, and evil works are unrighteousness. Therefore the Holy Spirit convicts, that is, reveals to the heart that our righteousnesses are unclean: therefore those who do not believe in Christ are unrighteous, this is what he says concerning sin: because they did not believe in me, that is, the flesh thought good works were righteousness: but the Spirit convinces that righteousness is faith in me. which when they are empty, therefore they have sin.

Latin:

De peccato quidem, quia non crediderunt.

Sicut Evangelio sola fides iustitia est: ut scilicet: Si omnia omnium peccata feceris, tamen credis misereri tui patrem propter Christum, salvus sis, sicut supra: Qui credit in eum, non iudicatur. Nam haec est Evangelii gloria, salvari peccatores. Ita econtra sola incredulitas peccatum est, sicut & supra dixit: Qui non credit, iam iudicatus est: quia non credit in unigenitum filium dei. Et Ioannis 12. Sermo quem locutus sum, ille iudicabit. Vides amplitudinem misericor diae. Porrò caro sic iudicat, iustitiam esse bona opera, iniustitiam mala opera. Itaque spiritussanctus arguit, id est revelat cordi nostras iustitias immundas esse: propterea iniustos esse, qui non credunt in Christum, hoc est quod ait de peccato: quia non crediderunt in me, id est, Caro bona opera putabat esse iustitias: at spiritus convincit iustitiam esse fidem in me, qua cum vacent, ergo peccatum habent.

## 16:10
Of righteousness indeed, because I go to the father.

That the world is reproved of righteousness, is to reveal its righteousness, so that it may appear to be uncleanness, according to that: Every man is a liar. Because indeed Christ reigns, therefore the uncleanness of human righteousness is revealed, and a new righteousness is given. For this is the office of Christ to condemn human righteousnesses, and to give eternal righteousness, which is life eternal itself. As it is said Malachi 3.: Who shall stand to see him? for he is like a refining fire, and like fuller's herb. Therefore it seems it can be understood most simply thus. The Holy Spirit will reprove the world of righteousness, that is, he will reveal the impurity of the world's righteousness: Because I go, that is, I have begun to reign. For Christ to go to the father, is clearly nothing else than to begin his reign. Furthermore, for Christ to reign, is to condemn the flesh and to give the righteousness of God, to fill all things, as he writes to the Ephesians. And now you shall not see me. that is, my kingdom will not be in a carnal and visible presence.

Latin:

De iustitia vero, quia vado ad patrem.

Mundum argui de iustitia, est iustitiam eius revelare, ut appareat immunditiam esse, iuxta illud: Omnis homo mendax. Quia vero Christus regnat, ideo revelatur iustitiae humanae immundities, & nova iustitia donatur. Hoc est enim Christi officium damnare iustitias humanas, & donare iustitiam aeternam, quae est ipsa vita aeterna. Sicut Malachiae 3. dicitur: Quis stabit ad videndum eum? ipse enim quasi ignis conflans, & quasi herba fullonum. Proinde videtur simplicissime sic intelligi posse. Spiritussanctus arguet mundum de iustitia, id est revelabit iustitiae mundi impuritatem: Quia ego vado, id est regnare coepi. Nam Christum ire ad patrem, planè aliud non est quàm auspicari regnum. Porrò regnare Christum, est damnare carnem & donare iustitiam dei, implere omnia, sicut ad Ephesios scribit. Et iam non videbitis me. id est regnum meum non erit in carnali & visibili praesentia.

## 16:11
Of judgment however, because the prince of this world etc.

Judgment is chastisement or reprobation, as above It has been said: Now is the judgment of the world, etc. Furthermore, when it has been revealed that unbelief is sin, and that righteousness is that new righteousness which the Gospel preaches, not that which the world conceives, then it follows that the Spirit discerns the flesh, the kingdom of Satan, and the kingdom of God. It condemns and hates all the works and counsels of Satan's kingdom, just as it says in Rom. 8.: If by the Spirit you mortify the works of the flesh, etc. Thus, therefore, it says: The Spirit will convict the world of judgment, that is, it will condemn the world, because it does not judge Satan, but approves him. It is the kingdom of Satan to seek glory, wealth, and pleasures: these the world does not disapprove. But the Holy Spirit judges otherwise, and feels that the kingdom of Satan is the desires of the flesh, and therefore it hates and mortifies them: this is what it says: He will convict the world of judgment. But why? Because the prince of this world has already been judged, that is, the gates of hell have already been destroyed, the word has been published and preached, against which Satan can do nothing. Death has been conquered and sin has been conquered: just as the Apostle argues in Romans 6.: Sin shall not have dominion over you. Therefore, the kingdom of Christ is not the use of ceremonies, not merely an external polity: but it is a revelation, by which unbelief is made manifest to the heart: it is made manifest that righteousness is not the righteousness of the flesh, but that which Christ effects, filling and vivifying us. Since where the heart has been renewed, then they are judged, that is, they are condemned. all the works, the counsels of the kingdom of darkness, which Satan carries on in those in whom the spirit of God is not.

Latin:

De iudicio autem, quia princeps mun-di huius &c.

Iudicium est castigatio seu reprobatio, sicut supra dictum est: Nunc iudicium est mundi &c. Porrò ubi revelatum est, incredulitatem esse peccatum: iustitiam esse novam illam quam praedicat Evangelium, non eam quam concipit mundus, tum sequitur ut spiritus discernat carnem, regnum satanae, & regnum dei. Damnat & odit omnia opera & consilia satanae regni, sicut Rom. 8. ait: Si spiritu opera carnis mortificaveritis &c. Sic ergo ait: Spiritus arguet mundum de iudicio, id est damnabit mundum, quod non iudicet satanam, sed probet. Satanae regnum est, gloriam, opes, voluptates quaerere: haec mundus non improbat. At spiritussanctus aliter iudicat, sentit regnum satanae esse cupiditates carnis, ideoque odit et mortificat: hoc est quod ait: Arguet mundum de iudicio. Sed quare? quia princeps mundi iam iudicatus est, id est portae inferorum sunt iam destructae, verbum aeditum est & praedicatum, adversus quod nihil potest satan. Victa mors est & victum peccatum: sicut ad Romanos 6. argumentatur Apostolus: Peccatum vobis non dominabitur. Est ergo regnum Christi, non usus ceremoniarum, non externa tantum politia: sed est revelatio, qua cordi palàm fit incredulitas: palàm fit, iustitiam esse, non carnis iustitiam, sed eam quam efficit Christus, implens & vivificans nos. Quoniam ubi renovatum fuerit cor, tum iudicantur, hoc est damnantur omnia opera, consilia regni tenebrarum, quod gerit satan in iis, in quibus non est spiritus dei.

## 16:12
I have yet many things to say unto you, but ye cannot &c.

Christ came into this world, about to announce the remission of sins, which is what the Gospel is. Therefore, through Christ we have received the word, which alone is sufficient for salvation, John 1: The only begotten son who is in the bosom of the father, he has declared it. And John 15. All things that I have heard from my father, I have made known unto you. And Matthew last: Teaching them to observe all things whatsoever I have commanded you. And Isaiah 61. He has sent me to announce. And to the Hebrews 1. In these last days by his son. Nor is there any new word which the holy spirit brings: but it is that same word which was preached by Christ, as John 15. When the paraclete shall come, he will bear witness of me. And 14. He will remind you of all things which I have said unto you. For when he says, which I have said, he certainly signifies that it will be the same word. And briefly, there is one Gospel promised in the holy scriptures, which was preached through Christ to the Jews, and scattered among all nations by the apostles having received the holy spirit. Because without the holy spirit the word of Christ is not understood. Therefore, when this passage: I have yet many things to say unto you To say, they object: Beware of believing that any new word has been handed down which pertains to salvation, beyond the Gospel preached by Christ. But this also pertains to the matter: The Apostles still thought that the kingdom of Christ would be a corporal kingdom, as appears in Acts 1.; they did not know that the Gospel was to be spread among the nations. Briefly, they did not yet understand the cross and the glory of the cross, even though the word had been published. Therefore, Christ is content to have said a few things. He says that he omits those things which they cannot bear, that is, which they do not yet understand. Whence it is sufficiently clear that he is speaking about spiritual doctrine. Truly, the impious use this sentence to confirm the papacy and ecclesiastical ordinances, all of which are carnal and external, and which can even be understood by the impious. See the insanity of men, who have thought that the apostles could have understood the doctrine of faith, which is repugnant to every sense of nature. They could not understand the ordinance of days and garments. This passage teaches that the word is not understood without the Holy Spirit. For he says fewer things for this reason, because they themselves did not understand. But he adds: When the spirit of truth shall have come, etc., that is, the Holy Spirit, he will cause you to know clearly this word which I have preached. Just as also the Apostle 2 Corinthians 3.: We, beholding the glory of the Lord with open face, are transformed into the same image from glory to glory. as from the spirit of the Lord. To the same point pertains what he says here: He will glorify me, that is, he will make me known. I still have many things to say to you. Namely, concerning the cross and its glory, concerning the Gospel being spread among the nations, concerning the spiritual kingdom. He will teach you all truth. That is, he will cause you to understand the word. For he will not speak from himself. That is, a different word than the word of the Father. But whatever he hears, that is, the word of the Father. And he will announce to you the things that are to come. That is, concerning the cross and its glory, concerning the Gospel being spread among the nations.

He will glorify me, that is, he will make me known among all nations. Because he will receive from what is mine. That is, he will receive the word of the Father. All things whatsoever the Father has, are mine. That is, the power of the Father and of me is the same.

Latin:

Adhuc multa habeo vobis dicere, sed non &c.

Christus venit in hunc mundum, annunciaturus remissionem peccatorum, id quod Evangelium est. Itaque per Christum verbum accepimus, quod unum ad salutem satis est, Ioannis primo: Unigenitus filius qui est in sinu patris, ipse enarravit. Et Ioannis 15. Omnia quae audivi à patre meo, nota feci vobis. Et Matthaei ultimo: Docentes servare omnia quencunque mandavi vobis. Et Esaiae 61. Ad annunciandum misit me. Et ad Hebraeos 1. Novissime in filio. Nec est aliquod novum verbum, quod spiritussanctus afferat: sed est idem illud verbum, quod à Christo praedicatum est, sicut Ioannis 15. Cum venerit paracletus, ille testimonium perhibebit de me. Et 14. Ille admonebit vos omnium quae dixi vobis. Nam cum ait, quae dixi, significat certe idem verbum fore. Et breviter unum est Evangelium promissum in scripturis sanctis, quod est per Christum praedicatum Iudaeis, & per apostolos accepto spiritusancto sparsum in omnes gentes. Quia sine spiritusancto non intelligitur verbum Christi. Proinde cum hunc locum: Adhuc multa habeo vobis dicere, opponunt illi: Cave credas novum aliquod verbum traditum esse, quod ad salutem pertineat, praeter Evangelium à Christo praedicatum. Sedhuc pertinet: Apostoli adhuc putabant regnum Christi corporale regnum fore, sicut apparet Actuum 1. nesciebant Evangelium spargendum esse in gentes. Breviter crucem & crucis gloriam nondum intelligebant, tametsi verbum aeditum esset. Ideo Christus contentus est pauca dixisse. Ea dicit se omittere quae non possint portare, id est quae nondum intelligant. Unde satis clarum est eum de spirituali doctrina loqui. Verum hac sententia utuntur impii ad confirmandum papatum, & ordinationes ecclesiasticas, quae omnia carnalia & externa sunt, & quae etiam ab impiis intelligi possunt. Vide insaniam hominum, qui putarint apostolos intelligere potuisse doctrinam fidei, repugnantem omni sensui naturae. Non potuerunt intelligere ordinationem dierum ac vestium. Docet hic locus, verbum non intelligi sine spiritusancto. Nam ideo pauciora dicit, quod ipsi non intellexerint. At adiicit: Cum venerit spiritus veritatis &c. id est spiritussanctus, efficiet, ut hoc verbum quod praedicavi, clare cognoscatis. Sicut & Apostolus 2. Corinth. 3. Nos revelata facie gloriam domini speculantes, in eandem imaginem transformamur à claritate in claritatem, tanquam à domini spiritu. Eôdem pertinet, quod hic ait: Ille meclarificabit, id est notum faciet. Adhuc multa habeo vobis dicere. scilicet de cruce & eius gloria, de Evangelio spargendo in gentes, de regno spirituali. Docebit vos omnem veritatem. id est efficiet ut intelligatis verbum. Non enim loquetur à semetipso. id est aliud verbum quàm patris verbum. Sed quaecunque audiet, id est verbum patris. Et annunciabit vobis quae ventura sunt. id est de cruce & eius gloria, de Evangelio spargendo in gentes.

Ille me clarificabit, id est notum faciet in omnibus gentibus. Quia de meo accipiet. id est verbum patris accipiet. Omnia quaecunque habet pater, mea sunt. id est eadem est potentia patris & mei.

## 16:16
A little while and you will not see me.

And here an example of apostolic ignorance, or rather unbelief, is proposed. For the Lord had predicted quite a lot concerning his passion and resurrection, which they as yet neither understood nor believed. A little while and you will not see me. That is, for a little while you will not see me, namely at the time of the passion, when I shall die. And again a little while and you will see me. That is, a little later you will see me again, namely when I shall have risen. Because I go to the Father. That is, through death I shall inaugurate the spiritual kingdom.

Latin:

Modicum & iam non videbitis me.

Et hic proponitur exemplum ignorantiae apostolicae, immo incredulitatis. Praedixerat enim dominus de passione & resurrectione satis multa, quae nondum nec intelligebant, nec credebant. Modicum & iam non videbitis me. id est aliquandiu non videbitis me, scilicet tempore passionis, cum moriar. Et iterum modicum & videbitis me. id est paulò post iterum videbitis me, scilicet ubi resurrexero. Quia vado ad patrem. id est per mortem auspicabor regnum spirituale.

## 16:19
You are inquiring about this among yourselves.

The solicitude of Christ for us is described in the fact that He meets those who are hesitating. And it pertains to this, that we may feel that the afflicted are regarded: nay, even that He anticipates those who pray: just as He promises Isaiah 65. It shall be that before they call, I will hear them &c.

Latin:

De hoc quaeritis inter vos.

Solicitudo Christi pro nobis describitur in eo quod haesitantibus occurrit. Et eó pertinet, ut sentiamus respici afflictos: immo etiam antevortere precantes: sicut pollicetur Esaiae 65. Erit antequam clament, ego exaudiam eos &c.

## 16:20
Amen I say to you, that you shall lament, but the world &c.

You see here also afflictions, faith, and charity being preached. Furthermore, He exaggerates the afflictions when He says: The world shall rejoice, by which it is signified how the world mocks the Gospel when it sees Christ languishing: just as it is written Psalm 34. They rejoiced against me. And Psalm 21. All who see me, have mocked me &c. Furthermore, He teaches from the example which He subjoined concerning a woman in labor from where consolation is to be expected. You shall lament and weep, namely when I shall suffer.

Latin:

Amen dico vobis, quia plorabitis, mun-dus autem &c.

Vides & hic afflictiones, fidem, & charitatem praedicari. Porrò exaggerat afflictiones, cum ait: Mundus gaudebit, quo significatum est, quomodo subsannet mundus Evangelium, ubi viderit languere Christum: sicut scriptum est Psalmo 34. Adversum me laetatisunt. Et Psalmo 21. Omnes videntes me, deriserunt me &c. Porrò unde sit expectanda consolatio, docet exemplo quod subiecit de parturienti. Plorabitis & flebitis, scilicet cum patiar ego.

## 16:21
A woman when she is in labor, has sorrow.

First, those in labor can be relieved by no human aid; such is also the affliction of the church, when the world rages, when it persecutes, which no creatures console, according to that Psalm 66. My father and my mother have forsaken me, but the Lord has taken me up &c.

The Apostle therefore teaches that in such affliction they should hope for consolation plainly from no one but God. Christ was being killed, the doctrine was being rejected: because it seemed to be opposed to the word of God. Since he thus consoles the disciples, certainly the glorification of Christ by God was to be expected: the afflictions of the church are perpetually similar. Secondly, he teaches that the beginning of life is that affliction, just as the beginning of life is the pain of a woman in labor. For just as death is the punishment of sin, so also is that labor of those giving birth, as Genesis 3. And in this, life is foreshadowed in death, namely that through death one passes to life. He wishes therefore for the disciples to trust, and to perceive that the cross is imposed for the mortifying of sin, through which faith is exercised, and life is restored. And your joy no one will take from you. Namely, with faith confirmed through the resurrection, against which the gates of hell will not prevail.

And in that day you will not ask &c.

That is, when I have risen, you will openly see that I have been glorified: you will likewise see how I have been glorified. Now indeed you do not understand that it is necessary for me to be crucified, and to be glorified through death, but a little later you will discover it by the thing itself. What Jeremiah says chapter 30. In the latter days you will understand these things. Christ teaches the same thing here, that it will come to pass that the resurrection itself will testify that it was necessary for Christ to suffer: therefore, lest they be scandalized meanwhile by the cross, the doctrine and authority must be confirmed by the resurrection. And that in this temptation, as happens in others, the good will of God is declared by the outcome, this is what Scripture everywhere commands, to wait and to endure. Isaiah 8. I will wait for the Lord, who hides his face from Jacob. And Isaiah 25. Behold our God, we will wait for him, and he will save us, etc. Thus Abraham is preserved when about to sacrifice his son, and by waiting and enduring he recognized the good will of God.

Latin:

Mulier cum parturit, tristitiam habet.

Primum parturientes nulla ope humana levari possunt, talis est & ecclesiae afflictio, cum furit mundus, cum persequitur, quam nullae creaturae consolantur, iuxta illud Psal. 66. Pater meus & mater mea de reliquerunt me, dominus autem assumpsit me &c.

Docet itaque Apostolus, ut in tali afflictione planè non sperent consolationem, nisi à deo. Christus occidebatur, doctrina reprobabatur: quia videbatur verbo dei adversari. Quum sic consoletur discipulos, certe fuit expectanda glorificatio Christi à deo: similes sunt ecclesiae afflictiones perpetuò. Secundo docet vitae initium esse eam afflictionem, sicut vitae initium est dolor parturientis. Nam perinde atque mors peccati poena est, ita & ille parturientium labor, ut Genesis 3. Estque in hoc adumbrata vita in morte, scilicet quod per mortem ad vitam transeatur. Vult ergo confidere discipulos, & sentire crucem mortificando peccato impositam esse, per quam fides exercitetur, & vita restituatur. Et gaudium vestrum nemo tollet à vobis. scilicet confirmata fide per resurrectionem adversus quam non praevalebunt portae inferorum.

Et in illo die non interrogabitis &c.

Id est ubi resurrexero, palàm videbitis me glorificatum esse: videbitis item quomodo sim glorificatus. Nunc quidem non intelligitis, oportere me crucifigi, & per mortem glorificari, at paulò post re ipsa comperietis. Quod Hieremias ait capite. 30. Innovissimo dierum intelligetis ea. Idem hic docet Christus, fore ut ipsa resurrectio testetur, oportuisse Christum pati: proinde ne scandalizentur interea cruce, doctrinam & autoritatem resurrectione confirmandam esse. Et quod in hac tentatione, idem in aliis accidit, eventu declaratur bona voluntas dei, hoc est quod passim iubet scriptura expectare & sustinere. Esaiae 8. Expectabo dominum, qui abscondit faciem suam à Iacob. Et Esaiae 25. Ecce deus noster, expectabimus eum, & salvabit nos &c. Sic servatur Abraham immolaturus filium, & expectando, sustinendoque cognovit bonam voluntatem dei.

## 16:23
Amen I say to you, if you ask the Father for anything.

See how often he repeats the same promise concerning his priesthood, so that he may render consciences most certain that those praying are heard. Furthermore, what he adds, In my name, demands faith from those praying.

Latin:

Amen dico vobis, si quid petieritis pa-trem.

Vide quoties eandem promissionem repetat de sacerdotio suo, ut reddat conscientias quàm certissimas exaudiri precantes. Porrò quod addit, In nomine meo. fidem exigit à precantibus.

## 16:23
And I do not say to you, that I will ask the Father for you.

What? Does Christ not intercede for the saints, when Above he says: I will ask the Father? I answer: Christ here makes his brothers and co-heirs equal to himself: because Christ's inheritance is the kingdom, and it is also the priesthood. The saints are both kings and priests, brothers and co-heirs. For he calls them brothers Psalm 21. I will declare thy name unto my brethren &c. Therefore this pertains to this, that we may know for certain that the promise by which he promises that the Father will hear those who pray pertains to us. And Christ wishes that he not only be asked, that is, that the promise of the priesthood does not pertain to him alone, but through him to all his brothers. Furthermore, he added that condition through him, when he says: Because you have loved me and have believed. That is, because you have become my brothers, for we are joined to Christ by faith.

And I do not say to you, that I will ask the Father for you. That is, that the promise of the priesthood pertains to me alone. Because you have loved me. Therefore he loves you on account of me, not on account of your merits. And you have believed, that I came out from God. That is, you have believed that I was sent to save you. Furthermore, to believe that Christ was so sent, is without doubt to perceive that it is the will of the Father to remit sins on account of Christ.

Now we know that thou knowest all things, and needest not that any man should ask thee &c. That you anticipated us, when we wished to ask &c.

Latin:

Et non dico vobis, quod ego rogabo patrem de vobis.

Quid? an non interpellat Christus pro sanctis, cum supra dicat: Ego rogabo patrem? Respondeo: Christus hic aequat sibi fratres suos & cohaeredes: quia Christi haereditas est regnum, est et sacerdotium. Sunt & reges, & sacerdotes, sancti, fratres & cohaeredes. Vocat enim fratres Psalmo 21. Narrabo nomen tuum fratribus &c. Proinde haec eò pertinet, ut sciamus certo, ad nos pertinere promissionem qua pollicetur, patrem exauditurum esse precantes. Vultque Christus, se non solum rogari, id est promissionem sacerdotii non ad se solum pertinere, sed peripsum ad omnes fratres suos. Porrò eam conditionem per ipsum adiecit, cum inquit: Quia me amastis & credidistis. id est quia mei fratres facti estis, nam fide coniungimur Christo.

Et non dico vobis, quod ego rogabo patrem de vobis. id est quod ad me solum pertineat promissio sacerdotii. Quia vos me amastis. Itaque amat vos propter me, non propter merita vestra. Et credidistis, quia à deo exivi. id est credidistis quod missussum ad salvandos vos. Porrò credere Christum ita missum esse, est haud dubie sentire, voluntatem patris esse remittere peccata propter Christum.

Nunc scimus quod scis omnia, & non opus est tibi &c. Quod antevorteris nos, cum vellemus interrogare &c.

## 16:24
Until now you have not asked for anything in etc.

Because Christ was not yet glorified, the apostles did not yet understand what kind of kingdom or priesthood his would be, they were still ignorant of the Gospel: and so they were not yet certain that they were heard on account of Christ, because they thought the kingdom was external and corporal. For they did not know that Christ was sent for this very purpose, that he might certify consciences concerning the will of the Father, nor that there was any other kingdom or office of Christ: therefore he says: Hitherto you have asked nothing in my name: because, to wit, it was not yet certain through my resurrection that you would be heard for my sake, nor had the Gospel yet been promulgated: now the resurrection will testify concerning my word, concerning my authority, concerning my office, etc. The Holy Spirit will be given, who will reveal the Gospel, whence it will be certain that you are to be heard for my sake. Hitherto you have asked nothing in my name. That is, the Gospel not yet being revealed, it is not certain that one praying is heard for the sake of Christ. Ask, and you shall receive, to wit, with confidence, so that you may hesitate in nothing.

Latin:

Usque modò non petistis quicquam in &c.

Quia Christus nondum erat glorificatus, nondum intelligebant apostoli quale regnum, aut sacerdotiuncius esset futurum, Evangelium adhuc ignorabant: adeoque nondum certi erant, propter Christum se exaudiri, quod putarent regnum externum, & corporale fore: nec sciebant in hoc ipsum missum esse Christum, ut certificaret conscientias de voluntate patris, nec aliud esse Christi vel regnum, vel officium: ideo ait: Hactenus nihil petistis in nomine meo: quia scilicet nondum certum fuit per resurrectionem meam, quod propter me exaudiremini, nec dum Evangelium promulgatum erat: nunc resurrectio testabitur de verbo meo, de autoritate, de officio &c. dabitur spiritussanctus, qui Evangelium revelet, unde certum erit, quod propter me exaudiendi sitis. Usque modò non petistis quicquam in nomine meo. id est nondum revelato Evangelio certum esse, propter Christum exaudiri precantem. Petite, & accipietis, scilicet fiducia, ut nihil haesitetis.

## 16:24
That your joy may be full.

Full joy is peace, which faith, that is, a conscience certified concerning the mercy of God, produces. Thus therefore he says: Hitherto you have not been certain, but now you will be certain through my resurrection. Therefore consciences will be secure and peaceful.

Latin:

Ut gaudium vestrum sit plenum.

Plenum gaudium, pax est, quam fides, hoc est certificata de misericordia dei conscientia parit. Sic ergo ait: Hactenus nondum certifuistis, nunc autem certi eritis per meam resurrectionem. Ideo securae & pacatae erunt conscientiae.

## 16:25
These things I have spoken to you in proverbs.

That is, hitherto my word is obscure to you, until the Holy Spirit is given, who will cause you to understand the word.

For the mystery of the Gospel is hidden from the world.

Latin:

Haec in proverbiis locutus sum vobis.

Id est hactenus vobis obscurum est verbum meum, dum detur spiritussanctus, qui efficiet ut verbum intelligatis.

Est enim Evangelii mysterium absconditum à mundo.

## 16:25
The hour comes when no longer in proverbs.

Namely, when I shall have given the Holy Spirit, there I shall speak my word openly among you. Because the Holy Spirit renews hearts, and moves them so that they may conceive the word of Christ. Thus also above he joined the impulse of the Holy Spirit with the word: Whatsoever he shall hear he shall speak &c. For the Gospel is the ministry of the spirit, when hearts conceived with new light through the Holy Spirit believe the word of Christ so that they may trust in God, and magnify him: and cast themselves securely upon him, when they have made a taste of his goodness. And this is truly to know the Father, just as it is also written in Jeremiah 31.: All shall know me &c. and a man shall not teach his neighbor. That is, God shall be known if the Holy Spirit teaches within. Thus also here he says: The hour comes when I shall speak openly to you, that is, when the Holy Spirit having been given you shall know the Father. In that day you shall ask. The Gospel now having been known, you shall ask in my name, namely when, the Gospel having been known, you shall have learned what my office is.

Latin:

Venit hora cum iam non in proverbiis.

Scilicet ubi spiritumsanctum dedero, ibi meum verbum palàm eloquar inter vos. Quia spiritussanctus renovat corda, & movet ut concipiant verbum Christi. Ita & supra coniunxit impulsum spiritus sancti cum verbo: Quaecunque audiet loquetur &c. Est enim Evangelium ministerium spiritus, cum corda nova luce concepta per spiritumsanctum credunt verbo Christi ut fidant deo, & magnifaciant eum: reiiciantque se in eum secure, ubi bonitatis eius gustum fecerunt. Atque hoc est vere nosse patrem, quomodo & Hieremiae 31. scriptum est: Omnes noscent me &c. & non docebit vir proximum suum. id est cognoscetur deus si intus doceat spiritussanctus. Ita & hic ait: Venit hora cum palàm loquar vobis, id est cum donato spiritusancto agnoscetis patrem. In illo die petetis. cognito iam Evangelio in nomine meo petetis, scilicet cum cognito Evangelio didiceritis quod sit officium meum.

## 16:32
Now you believe, behold the hour comes.

That is, do not trust in this strength of yours, etc.

And I am not alone, because the Father is with me, that is to say, although it will seem that he has left me. That in me you may have peace, that is to say, against the scandal of the cross.

Latin:

Modò creditis, ecce venit hora.

Id est nolite fidere isto vestro robore &c.

Et non sum solus, quia pater mecum est, scilicet quanquam videbitur me reliquisse. Ut in me pacem habeatis, scilicet contra scandalum crucis.

## 17:1 Argument
CHAPTER SEVENTEENTH.

Latin:

CAPUT DECIMUM-SEPTIMUM.

## 17:1
Father, the hour has come.

That is to say, when the world will condemn me. Hitherto you have glorified me with many miracles, so that the judgment of the world might be refuted. Now because I shall seem to be left by you, and I shall be crucified, I ask you to glorify me again, so that in turn your glory may be made manifest: because the Father cannot be glorified, except by this word of the Son. Glorify your Son, that is, raise me up now that I am dying, so that your Son may glorify you, that is, so that I may receive the kingdom.

Latin:

Pater venit hora.

Scilicet cum mundus damnabit me. Hactenus multis miraculis ita glorificasti me, ut mundi iudicium redargueretur. Nunc quia videbor relictus esse à te, & crucifigar, quaeso rursus clarifica me, ut vicissim tua gloria palàm fiat: quia non potest pater glorificari, nisi hoc filii verbo. Clarifica filium tuum, id est iam morientem resuscita, ut filius tuus clarificet te, id est ut regnum accipiam.

## 17:1
That your Son may glorify you.

He uses two arguments; the first is, that your Son may glorify you, that is, that the glory of God may shine forth. And therefore this argument is pleaded, so that it may confirm the consciences of the saints against scandal, that is to say, so that they may not they doubt that God will glorify his name, even though he now dissembles, tolerates the blasphemers, etc. The second argument is that he should glorify it for this reason, lest we perish, so greatly does Christ thirst for our salvation. Now this also indicates how he is to be glorified: namely, in such a way that he may receive the kingdom, and may vivify all whom the Father has given to him.

As you have given him power over all flesh. That is, you have subjected all things under his feet, and have made him heir.

That he may give eternal life to all whom you have given to them. That is, that he may vivify the hearts of his saints.

Latin:

Ut filius tuus clarificet te.

Duobus argumentis utitur, prius est, ut filius tuus clarificet te, id est ut inclarescat gloria dei. Atque ideo causatur hoc argumentum, ut confirmet conscientias sanctorum adversus scandalum, scilicet ut ne dubitent quin deus glorificaturus sit nomen suum, tametsi nunc dissimulet, toleret blasphemantes &c. Alterum argumentum est, ut ideo glorificet, ne nos pereamus, tantopere sitit salutem nostram Christus. Iam & hoc indicat quomodo sit glorificandus: nempe sic ut accipiat regnum, vivificetque omnes quos sibi dedit pater.

Sicut dedisti ei potestatem omnis carnis. id est omnia subiecisti sub pedibus eius, & fecisti haeredem.

Ut det vitam aeternam omnibus quos dedisti eis. id est ut vivificet corda suorum sanctorum.

## 17:2
And this is eternal life.

What justice is, what eternal life is, this passage of the Gospel explains most clearly. Moreover, it is to know God through Christ: for to know is to believe, according to that saying of Isaiah: His knowledge shall save many. Therefore, it is eternal life itself to believe that God has had mercy on us on account of Christ, and to trust in God. Reason feels that all things turn out by chance, and that God has no business with us, therefore it is ignorant of God, and rashly judges that both good and evil things happen. A bad conscience, and one feeling the judgment of God, considers God only as a cruel judge: and when it begins to heal itself, it attempts to appease God with works: yet it never pacifies the conscience, it always hates, it always flees from God, it does not trust in him, nothing from he does not expect from him, but trusts in his own works, in his own prudence, and seeks peace from them. Therefore, neither does such a heart know God, since it does not acknowledge his mercy. But where the heart has conceived the word of Christ, by which sins are forgiven, and in the word of Christ perceives his mercy, there it acknowledges God, expects all things from him, trusts in him, fears him alone, rejoices in all his works, and permits itself to his will in all things. Such knowledge is faith itself, and true worship of God, etc.

Latin:

Haec est autem vita aeterna.

Quid iustitia, quid vita aeterna sit, hic Evangelii locus planissime exponit. Est autem cognoscere deum per Christum: Cognoscere vero credere est, iuxta illud Esaiae: Cognitio eius salvabit multos. Est itaque ipsa vita aeterna credere, quod deus nostri misertus sit propter Christum, & fidere deo. Ratio sentit, res omnes fortuito verti, neque quicquam negotii deo nobiscum esse, ideo ignorat deum, & temere tam bona quàm mala iudicat accidere. Mala conscientia & sentiens iudicium dei, deum pro saevo iudice tantum habet: & cum incipit sibi mederi, placare deum conatur operibus: neque tamen unquam pacificat conscientiam, semper odit, semper fugit deum, non fidit ei, nihil ab eo expectat, sed fidit suis operibus, suae prudentiae, & ab iis quaerit pacem. Itaque neque tale cor deum novit, siquidem misericordiam eius non agnoscit. At ubicor conceperit verbum Christi, quo peccata remittuntur, & in verbo Christi misericordiam eius sentiscit, ibi agnoscit deum, expectat ab eo omnia, fidit ei, timet eum solum, gaudet in omnibus eius operibus, & permittit se eius voluntati per omnia. Talis cognitio ipsa fides est, & verus dei cultus &c.

## 17:3
That they may know you, the only true God.

Hilarius against the Arians interprets this sentence thus: that they may know you and Jesus Christ to be the only true God. To me, he seems to have spoken simply to this effect: that they may know you to be the only true God, namely you who are Father, Son, and Holy Spirit. And that they may know Jesus Christ, that is, that they may know why he came, why he suffered, why he rose again. For thus is Christ known, by the knowledge of whom the mercy of God is perceived. To the same end must be referred what he says above: No one comes to the Father, except through me, etc.

Latin:

Ut cognoscant te deum solum verum.

Hilarius contra Arianos sic interpretatur hanc sententiam, ut cognoscant, te & Iesum Christum esse solum verum deum. Mihi videtur simpliciter locutus esse in hanc sententiam, ut cognoscant te solum esse verum deum, te scilicet qui pater es, filius, & spiritussanctus. Et ut cognoscant Iesum Christum, id est cognoscant quid venerit, cur passus sit, cur resurrexerit. Sic enim cognoscitur Christus, quo cognito misericordia dei sentitur. Eôdem referenda sunt quae supra dicit: Nemo venit ad patrem, nisi per me &c.

## 17:4
I have glorified you upon the earth.

I have glorified you, that is, I have made your glory manifest by preaching the Gospel, by which the glory of the Father is revealed, as Esaiae 40. The glory of the Lord shall be revealed, etc.

Because by the Gospel the righteousness and wisdom of the flesh is condemned, and it is made plain that the righteousness of the flesh is a simulation, that carnal worship of God is a mere lie, and that the wisdom of the flesh is ignorance of God. Furthermore, there is given by the Gospel a word in which the righteousness of God is truly known, by which we are truly justified. It likewise glorifies the Father, because it attributes all glory to Him, not usurping for itself wisdom or strength by which it might vindicate itself: but it simply resigned itself and all things to the Father, awaiting glorification, not from its own gifts, but from the will of the Father, who said: Vengeance is mine. I have finished the work which you gave me to do. By this He signifies both that the word is from God, and that Christ suffers by the command of God. And now glorify me, you. That is, resurrect me.

Latin:

Ego te clarificavi super terram.

Ego te clarificavi, id est palàm feci gloriam tuam, praedicando Evangelium, quo revelatur gloria patris, sicut Esaiae 40. Revelabitur gloria domini &c.

quia Evangelio damnatur iustitia & sapientia carnis, & palàm fit, iustitiam carnis esse simulationem, cultum dei carnalem merum esse mendacium, sapientiam carnis esse ignorationem dei. Praeterea donatur Evangelio verbum in quo vere cognoscitur iustitia dei, quo vere iustificamur. Glorificat item patrem, quod ei gloriam omnem tribuit, non usurpans sibi sapientiam, aut fortitudinem qua vindicaret se: sed simpliciter resignavit se & omnia patri, expectans glorificationem, non à suis donis, sed à voluntate patris, qui dixit: Mihi vindicata. Opus consummavi quod dedisti mihi ut faciam. per hoc significat & verbum à deo esse, et mandato dei pati Christum. Et nunc clarifica tu me. id est resuscita me.

## 17:5
The glory which I had before the world was made.

Christ wishes to be glorified with the Father, or before the Father, that is, with that glory which is valid before the Father: and this is wisdom, righteousness, eternal life. Before the world Christ is crucified and condemned: but before the Father He is glorified, so that the saints also may feel and believe that they will be glorified before God, even though by the judgment of the world they may seem to have perished. But besides this Christ adds: The glory which I had with you before the world was made.

For He wishes to be glorified with His natural glory. That is, that the power of God which is natural to me, may again exert itself, for now I am weak. I have manifested Thy name to men. Namely, because the name of God is manifested to men by the Gospel alone. Whom Thou hast given me. That is, whom Thou hast chosen, separated from the world. They have kept My word, that is, the Gospel, namely those who are separated from the world. All things which Thou hast given me, are from Thee. The Gospel which I preach is Thy word, etc.

They have truly known. That is, they have believed the word, that Christ was sent to preach the remission of sins.

Latin:

Claritatem quam habui priusquam mun-dus fieret.

Glorificari vult Christus apud patrem, vel coram patre, id est ea gloria quae coram patre valet: haec est autem sapientia, iustitia, vita aeterna. Coram mundo crucifigitur ac damnatur Christus: sed coram patre glorificatur, ut & se sentiant & credant sancti glorificatum iri coram deo, tametsi mundi iudicio interisse videantur. Sed praeter hoc adiicit Christus: Gloriam quam habui coram te priusquam mundus fieret.

Vult enim glorificari sua naturali gloria. id est ut potentia dei quae mihi natura est, rursus exerat se, nunc enim infirmus sum. Manifestavi nomen tuum hominibus. scilicet quia solo Evangelio manifestatur nomen dei hominibus. Quos mihi dedisti. id est quos elegisti, separatos à mundo. Sermonem meum, id est Evangelium. servaverunt scilicet illi, qui à mundo separati sunt. Omnia quae dedisti mihi, abs te sunt. Evangelium quod praedico, verbum tuum est &c.

Cognoveruntueré. id est crediderunt verbo, Christum esse missum ad praedicandum remissionem peccatorum.

## 17:9
I pray not for the world, but for those whom.

See the horrible judgment of Christ concerning the world, when He denies that He prays for the world, and condemns whatever is of the world, however specious it may be. Then He separates from the world those for whom He prays, namely the elect: and teaches that He prays for them for this reason, because they are the elect. Moreover, the conscience is to be referred to this place, so that it may trust in the prayer of Christ. Above, Christ prayed for Himself; here, for all who were to believe until the very end of the world, as He says afterwards: I pray not for them only, but for those who shall believe through their word. And this is the priesthood of Christ, through which we have access to the Father. Moreover, this sentence also teaches how that Pauline saying is to be interpreted:

God wills all men to be saved. All my things are yours. That is, therefore they are saved. And your things are mine. That is to say, therefore I obtain what I ask. And I have been glorified in them. That is, I have been acknowledged, they believe in me.

And I am not in the world.

So that I may be able to console and confirm them by my bodily presence. And they are in the world. That is, they are alone.

I come to you. That is, I am glorified. And because I myself am glorified, my disciples also will be saved.

Latin:

Non pro mundo rogo, sed pro iis quos.

Vide horrendum iudicium Christi de mundo, cum negat se orare pro mundo, damnatque quicquid est mundi, quantumvis speciosum. Deinde separat à mundo pro quibus orat, nempe electos: docetque se ideo orare, quia electi sint. Est autem ad hunc locum referenda conscientia, ut confidat Christi orationi. Supra pro se rogavit Christus, hic pro omnibus qui credituri erant ad finem usque mundi, sicut postea inquit: Non pro eis tantum rogo, sed pro iis qui crediturisunt per verbum eorum. Atque hoc est sacerdotium Christi, per quod habemus accessum ad patrem. Docet autem & haec sententia quomodo sit interpretanda illa Paulina:

Deus vult omnes homines salvos fieri. Mea omnia tua sunt. id est ideo servantur. Et tua mea sunt. scilicet ideo impetro quod rogo. Et clarificatus sum in eis. id est agnitus sum, credunt mihi.

Etiam non sum in mundo.

Ut scilicet possim eos corporali praesentia consolari & confirmare. Et ii in mundo sunt. id est soli sunt.

Ego ad te venio. id est glorificor. Et quia glorificor ipse, servabuntur & discipuli mei.

## 17:11
Holy Father, keep them in your name.

That is, by the power of your name, as in Psalm 53. God, save me in your name, and judge me in your power. And often in the scriptures they cast out demons in the name of Christ, that is, in the power of the name of Christ. Thus Peter in Acts chapter 4. There is no other name under the sun, in which it behooves us to be saved, etc. So here he says: Keep them in your name. That is, cause that your name in which they believe may be powerful in them. For to save by the name of God is to trust in the name of God: which when it is done, the name of God itself is powerful and saves. Proverbs 16. The name of the Lord is a most strong tower; the just runs to it, and will be exalted. Do you see, moreover, what kind of consolation it is that the Father imparts to the saints?

Latin:

Pater sancte, serva eos in nomine tuo.

Id est potentia nominis tui, sicut Psalmo 53. Deus in nomine tuo salvum me fac, & in virtute tua iudica me. Et saepe in scripturis in nomine Christi eiiciunt daemonia, id est in potentia nominis Christi. Sic Petrus in Actis cap. 4. Non est aliud nomen sub sole, in quo oporteat salvos fieri &c. Ita hic ait: Serva eos in nomine tuo. id est effice ut nomen tuum cui credunt potens sit in eis. Nam salvare nomine dei, est nomini dei fidere: quod cum fit, ipsum nomen dei potens est & salvat. Proverbiorum 16. Turris fortissima nomen domini, ad ipsam currit iustus, & exaltabitur. Vides autem cuiusmodi sit consolatio, quam impertit pater sanctis?

## 17:11
That they may be one, even as we are.

That is, by the same spirit. Furthermore, the same spirit of faith produces unity, the unity of faith produces charity. Therefore Christ prays that the spirit of all may be the same: which cannot be agreed upon by minds without the holy spirit concerning faith, as appears in heresies. For where there is no light, darkness produces various opinions, as Isaiah calls it a spirit of dizziness. Furthermore, by this dissension, charity also is weakened. When I was with them, namely in bodily presence. I kept them in thy name, that is, I preached the Gospel to them, which they also believed: because it is the power of God. But now I come to thee, and I speak these things in the world, namely that their faith may be confirmed by a manifest word. That they may have my joy fulfilled, that is, with which I exhilarate them. I have given them thy word, I pray openly for them, that they may have the manifest word. I do not pray that they be taken out of the world, etc., lest they be tempted, lest they be afflicted. But that thou shouldst keep them from evil, that is, from temptations, lest thou allow them to succumb.

They are not of the world, even as I am not of the world, therefore the world will persecute them with hatred.

Latin:

Ut sint unum sicut & nos.

Id est eodem spiritu. Porrò idem spiritus fidei unitatem parit, fidei unitas parit charitatem. Itaque orat Christus, ut idem sit omnium spiritus: quod ingeniis citra spiritum sanctum non possit convenire de fide, sicut apparet in haeresibus. Nam ubi non est lux, tenebrae varias opiniones pariunt. sicut Esaias vocat spiritum vertiginis. Porrò hac dissensione labefit & charitas. Cum essem cum eis. scilicet corporali praesentia. Servabam eos in nomine tuo, id est praedicavi eis Evangelium, cui & crediderunt: quia potentia est dei. Nunc autem ad te venio, & haec loquor in mundo, scilicet ut manifesto verbo confirmetur fides eorum. Ut habeant gaudium meum impletum. id est quo ego exhilaro eos. Ego dedi eis sermonem tuum. palàm oram pro eis, ut habeant manifestum verbum. Non rogo ut tollantur de mundo &c. ne tententur, ne affligantur. Sed ut serves eos à malo. id est tentationibus ne sinas succumbere eos.

Non sunt de mundo, sicut & ego non sum de mundo, ideo odio mundus persequetur eos.

## 17:17
Sanctify them in truth.

To sanctify a thing is to separate it for offering, or otherwise for sacred uses, Exodus 13. Sanctify unto me every firstborn &c. And 30. Sanctify Aaron and his sons &c. So also here Christ feels that he sanctifies himself, that is, offers himself, so that we also may be sanctified, that is, separated for sacred uses: and as victims, just as Christ also was a victim. Furthermore, what he adds, sanctify them in truth, he distinguishes from the sanctifications of the law. Aaron was anointed with oil, he was distinguished also by clothing from others, which was his sanctification. But here Christ asks that we be sanctified in truth, that is, that they be sanctified not outwardly, but that they truly become divine instruments, that they become true victims, that is, which God truly approves: for he does not approve of cattle and similar things. Psalm 49. I will not rebuke you for your sacrifices. And Jeremiah 7. I did not speak with your fathers, and I did not command them concerning the word of burnt offerings, that is, I did not demand this worship: because those external rites were only memorials and signs.

Latin:

Sanctifica eos in veritate.

Sanctificare rem, est separare ad offerendum, vel alioquin ad sacros usus, Exodi 13. Sanctifica mihi omnem primogenitum &c. Et 30. Sanctificatis Aaron & filiis eius &c. Ita & hic sentit Christus, quod se sanctificet, id est immolet, ut simus et nos sanctificati, id est separati adsacros usus: & hostiae, sicut & Christus hostia fuit. Porrò quod addit, sanctifica eos in veritate, discernit à sanctificationibus legis. Aaron ungebatur oleo, discernebatur & vestitu ab aliis, quae eius sanctificatio fuit. Sed hic rogat Christus, ut in veritate sanctificemur, id est ut sanctificentur non extrinsecus, sed ut vere divina instrumenta fiant, ut verae hostiae fiant, hoc est quas vere deus approbet: nam pecudes & similia non approbat. Psalmo 49. Non in sacrificiis arguam te. Et Hieremiae 7. Non sum locutus cum patribus vestris, & non praecepi eis de verbo holocaustomatum, id est non exegi hunc cultum: quia ritus illi externi memorialia tantum et signa erant.

## 17:17
Your word is truth.

Moreover, Christ himself proceeds to explain how this sanctification happens. Your word is truth. That is, sanctify them truly. But you will sanctify them truly when you give your word to them, when you anoint them with your word, just as they were anointed with oil in the law. Now to be sanctified by the word is to believe. For one believes by the word, if all things are recalled to faith. And from these things it appears what the Christian victims are, namely we ourselves sanctified by the word. It appears also, that the sacrifices of the law, the righteousnesses of the flesh are nothing, but that they are truly sacrifices who believe: as Jeremiah 7 he says: This word I commanded them, saying: Hear my voice, and I will be your God. Sanctify, that is, separate for your uses, that they may become your true instruments. Your word is truth. That is, it teaches how it works in the saints: then they become truly instruments, when you will give your word to them, that they may believe. As you sent me into the world, so also I &c. namely for this reason they are sanctified, because they will preach the Gospel, that they may be also themselves sanctified in the truth. That is, that they may become truly instruments.

Latin:

Sermo tuus veritas est.

Pergit autem Christus ipse explicare quomodo fiat haec sanctificatio. Sermo tuus veritas est. id est sanctifica eos vere. vere autem sanctificabis, cum verbum tuum dabis eis, cum verbo tuo inunges eos, sicut oleo inungebantur in lege. Iam verbo sanctificari, est credere. Nam verbo creditur, si omnia revocantur ad fidem. Et ex iis apparet, quae sint hostiae Christianae, scilicet nos ipsi sanctificati verbo. Apparet item, hostias legis, iustitias carnis nihil esse, sed verè hostias esse qui credunt: sicut Hieremiae 7. ait: Hoc verbum praecepi eis, dicens: Audite vocem meam, ero vobis deus. Sanctifica, id est separa in tuos usus, ut fiant vera instrumenta tua. Sermo tuus veritas est. id est docet quomodo operetur in sanctis: tum fiunt vere instrumenta, cum tuum sermonem dabis eis, ut credant. Sicut tu me misisti in mundum, ita & ego &c. scilicet ideo sanctificentur, quia Evangelium praedicabunt, ut sint & ipsi sanctificati in veritate. id est ut fiant verè instrumenta.

## 17:20
I do not pray for these only, but also for those who are to believe &c.

Christ prays for the universal church, that all the members of the church may be one by faith with God and by charity among themselves. Also that they may be truly sanctified, that is, by the faith of the word: also that they may endure when they will be tempted. From which you will easily gather what Christianity is, or the church. For what sanctification may be, he himself explains, sanctify them truly. For he feels that sanctification is not some external observation, but that which is done by the word of God in the heart. Now he himself also explains what it is to be one.

That all may be one, as I in you, and you in me that is, just as we are one by one spirit. That they themselves may be one in us, that is, that they may have the brightness of Christ.

Latin:

Non pro eis tantum rogo, sed & pro eis qui credituri &c.

Precatur Christus pro universa ecclesia, ut unum sint fide cum deo & charitate inter se omnia ecclesiae membra. Item ut sanctificentur verè, id est fide verbi: item ut perdurent cum tentabuntur. Unde facilè colliges quid sit Christianismus, aut ecclesia. Quid enim sit sanctificatio, ipse exponit, sanctifica eos verè. Sentit enim sanctificationem non esse externam aliquam observationem, sed eam quae fit verbo dei in corde. Modò explicat & ipse, quid sit unum esse.

Ut omnes unum sint, sicut ego in te, & tu in me id est, sicut nos uno spiritu unum sumus. Ut ipsi in nobis unum sint, id est ut habeant claritatem Christi.

## 17:22
And I the brightness which you &c.

The term glory signifies brightness and light, just as Moses often uses it. Thus also 2 Corinthians 3 he calls the glory of the face of Moses, that is, the splendor or light. In that manner you will also understand here: I have given them glory, that is, light, that is, the word in which, having been illuminated, they have conceived the light of God, this is the wisdom and righteousness which God imparts, he establishes in our hearts, not hypocritical simulation, just as the Apostle also speaks 2 Cor. 3: We beholding the glory of the Lord with open face, as from the spirit of the Lord. That is, that we may become a spirit such as the spirit itself is. To this also pertain the prophecies Isaiah 60 The Gentiles shall walk in your light. Therefore, the cause which brings it about that we are one is the light by which we are illuminated by Christ. Furthermore, the first degree of unity is this: I in them, and you in me: which unity is the same faith. For Christ to be in the saints is for the saints to trust in Christ. And for the Father to be in Christ is likewise for the saints to acknowledge Christ to be the power of the Father. This unity of faith, this knowledge, Christ prays for the church, which knowledge is faith itself. And in me, that is, that I may reign and be glorified.

Latin:

Et ego claritatem quam tu &c.

Gloriae vocabulum claritatem & lumen significat, quemadmodum Moyses saepe utitur. Sic & 2. ad Corinthios 3. gloriam vultus Moysi. id est splendorem, seu lumen dixit. Ad eum modum & hic intelliges: Ego gloriam, id est lumen dedi eis, id est verbum in quo illuminati conceperunt lucem dei, hoc est sapientiam & iustitiam quam deus impertit, condit in cordibus nostris, non hypocriticam simulationem, quemadmodum & 2. Corinth. 3. loquitur Apostolus: Nos revelata facic speculantes gloriam domini, tanquam à domini spiritu. id est ut fiamus spiritus qualis et ipse spiritus est. Huc & prophetiae pertinent Esaiae 60. Ambulabunt gentes in lumine tuo. Proinde causa quae efficit ut unum simus, est lumen quo à Christo illuminamur. Porrò primus gradus unitatis hic est: Ego in eis, & tu in me: quae unitas eadem fides est. Nam Christum esse in sanctis, est sanctos fidere Christo. Et patrem esse in Christo, est itidem sanctos agnoscere Christum esse potentiam patris. Hanc unitatem fidei, hanc cognitionem Christus precatur ecclesiae, quae cognitio ipsa fides est. Et in me, id est ut regnem ego & glorificer.

## 17:23
That they may be perfected in one.

Perfection is from time to time to become more one, as Ephe. 4. Let us grow up in him in all things, who is the head, Christ, from whom the whole body is compacted and connected. Just as it was necessary for sacrifices to be whole, not diseased, not defective: so also it is necessary for the saints to be made whole, not to limp, to serve partly Baal, partly God, but to trust in Christ alone, not in the arm of flesh, or in merits. Nor likewise to be distracted into various opinions: for where Christ is not in the heart, there cannot be a constant and certain judgment concerning the word of Christ. And that the world may know, namely through the word and through the apostles. And you have loved them, namely those who believe through the word. Father, whom you have given me, I will that where I am. As I am glorified, so also they may be glorified. And that they may see my brightness. That is, that they may know me to be the one through whom they are saved. And they have known. That is, I revealing it, they have believed. And I have made known to them your name, namely through the apostles.

Latin:

Ut sint consummati in unum.

Perfectio est subinde magis unum fieri, sicut Ephe. 4. Crescamus in illo per omnia, qui est caput, Christus, ex quo totum corpus compactum & connexum est. Sicut oportebat hostias integras esse, non morbidas, non vitiosas: ita & sanctos oportet integros fieri, non claudicare, partim Baal, partim deo servire, sed fidere soli Christo, non brachio carnis, aut meritis. Nec item in varias opiniones distrahi: nam ubi non est in corde Christus, ibi non potest esse constans & certum iudicium de verbo Christi. Et cognoscat mundus, scilicet per verbum & per apostolos. Et dilexisti eos, scilicet qui per verbum credunt. Pater quos dedisti mihi, volo ut ubi ego sum. sicut ego glorificor, sic & illi glorificentur. Et videant claritatem meam. id est ut cognoscant me esse per quem salvi fiant. Et ii cognoverunt. id est me revelante crediderunt. Et notum feci eis nomen tuum, scilicet per apostolos.

## 17:26
That the love wherewith you have loved me, may be in them, and I in them.

He explains the same unity a little later in other words. That the love wherewith you have loved me may be in them, and I in them. That is, bring it about that they may feel themselves to be loved on account of me, so that that I may reign in them, that is, that they may know me reigning in them. Furthermore, this knowledge is faith itself, by which the Holy Spirit bears witness, when He consoles and pacifies the heart against every power of creatures. You have what the first degree of divinity is, the same and common faith, by which we are one with God. The second is charity: for common faith cannot but produce common charity, as the Apostle says to the Galatians: Through faith, charity.

Latin:

Ut dilectio qua dilexisti me, in eis sit, & ego in ipsis.

Eandem unitatem paulò post aliis verbis explicat. Ut dilectio qua dilexisti me in eis sit, & ego in ipsis. id est effice ut sentiant se diligi propter me, ut ego regnem in eis, id est me regnantem in ipsis cognoscant. Porrò haec cognitio ipsa fides, qua testimonium perhibet spiritussanctus, cum consolatur & pacificat cor adversus omnem vim creaturarum. Habes, qui sit primus gradus divinitatis, eadem & communis fides, qua cum deo unum sumus. Secundus est charitas: nam communis fides non potest non parere charitatem communem, sicut ad Galatas Apostolus: Per fidem charitatem.

## 18:1 Argument
CHAPTER EIGHTEENTH.

How the passion of Christ is to be meditated upon.

Truly to meditate on the passion of Christ is at the same time to crucify the oldness of our flesh. And to be terrified, or confounded, having seen sin: and again to believe that Christ was made a sacrifice for us, at Romans 6. Whoever of us have been baptized in Christ Jesus, have been baptized in His death. That is, that we may be mortified just as He was mortified, so that we may both bear the cross and perceive that it will be salutary for us through Christ. And this is clearly the power and energy of the death of Christ, to kill the oldness of our flesh. Besides This kind of meditation, reason devises another. It pretends that it will have honor for Christ by recounting the history of the passion of Christ. But these things are nothing to Christ.

Latin:

CAPUT DECIMUM-OCTAVUM.

Passio Christi quomodo sit meditanda.

Vere meditari Christi passionem, est simul crucifigi vetustatem carnis nostrae. Et expavescere, seu confundi conspecto peccato: & rursum credere, quod pro nobis hostia factus sit Christus, ad Romanos 6. Quicunque baptizati sumus in Christo Iesu, in morte eius baptizati sumus. id est ut mortificemur perinde atque ipse mortificatus est, ut & feramus crucem, & sentiamus salutarem nobis fore per Christum. Estque haec planè vis & energia mortis Christi, occidere vetustatem carnis nostrae. Praeter hoc meditandi genus, aliud ratio comminiscitur. Simulat se habituram honorem Christo, recensendo historiam passionis Christi. sed haec nihil ad Christum.

## 18:3
But Judas, having received a cohort.

It must be diligently considered who first harms Christ; it is Judas, one of the number of the apostles. By this thing it is signified that those who cloak themselves in the name of Christ and plead the word of God are also the leaders and most harmful enemies of the Gospel, of which sort impious teachers will always be. Now impious teachers are not only masters of ceremonies, but also those who make a certain civility out of Christianity, content with the appearance of human virtues. Furthermore, this is the nature of impious teachers, that when they are judged by the Gospel, they strive to defend themselves by human force, because they do not trust in God: this is what Judas does when he summons cohorts.

Latin:

Iudas autem cum accepisset cohortem.

Diligenter consyderandum est, quis primus laedat Christum, Is Iudas est unus de numero apostolorum. Qua re significatum est, quod sint et principes & nocentissimi hostes Evangelii, ii qui nomen Christi praetexunt, & verbum dei causantur, cuiusmodi semper erunt impii doctores. Sunt autem impii doctores non tantum ceremoniarum magistri, sed & ii qui ex Christianismo civilitatem quandam faciunt, contenti humanarum virtutum specie. Porrò haec est natura impiorum doctorum, ut cum ab Evangelio iudicantur, tueri se vi humana contendant, quia deo non fidunt: hoc est quod Iudas cohortes accersit.

## 18:6
Therefore, as he said I am, they went backward, etc.

And that human force can do nothing, he declares immediately when he strikes down the entire cohort with a word: thus also it is necessary that the hearts of the saints be confirmed by the word, so that they may feel that they are accepted by God, and that those others are going to fall: even though they may now seem to triumph. Furthermore, here he also lifts up the weak, when he takes care for the apostles that they may be dismissed: as Let us know that the power to inflict violence lies with the enemies: but it lies with the Father, who, according to his mercy, only then exposes his own to the enemies.

Latin:

Ut ergo dixit ego sum, abierunt re-trorsum &c.

Et vim humanam nihil posse, statim declarat cum verbo sternit universam cohortem: sic & verbo confirmari necesse est sanctorum corda, ut sentiant se deo acceptos esse, & illos casuros esse: tametsi iam triumphare videantur. Praeterea hic quoque sublevat imbecilles, cum cavet apostolis ut dimittantur: ut sciamus nos, esse penes hostes vim afferre: sed penes patrem esse, qui pro sua misericordia, tum demum obiieit suos hostibus.

## 18:11
Put the sword into the sheath, the cup which &c.

What, was it not permitted to defend him, upon whom unjust violence was being inflicted? Is it not a work of charity to defend another? &c. I answer: It is not permitted to use the sword, except to legitimate authority: because only magistrates carry the sword by ordination, Rom. 13. And in the law it is always said: You shall lead him to the elders of the city. Furthermore, to usurp the sword beyond this ordination is to violate the divine ordination: this is why Peter is rebuked here for having usurped the sword. For there is Matthaei 26. this reason applied: All who shall have taken the sword, shall perish by the sword. That is, those who usurp it themselves beyond legitimate ordination, not called by God, deserve to be killed by the sword: however just the cause may be, profane or sacred, for there is no distinction. It is not permitted to usurp the sword beyond legitimate ordination. Thus the prophets died, nor did they usurp the sword, although violence was being inflicted.

Latin:

Mitte gladium in vaginam, calicem quem &c.

Quid, an non licuit tueri eum, cui vis iniusta inferebatur? Nónne opus charitatis est tueri alterum? &c. Respondeo: Gladio uti non licet, nisi legitimae potestaeti: quia soli magistratus ordinatione gerunt gladium, Rom. 13. Et in lege semper dicitur: Duces eum ad seniores civitatis. Porrò praeter hanc ordinationem usurpare gladium, est violare divinam ordinationem: hoc est cur hic Petrus reprehenditur gladio usurpato. Est enim Matthaei 26. adhibita haec ratio: Omnes qui gladium acceperint, gladio peribunt. id est qui ipsi usurpant praeter legitimam ordinationem, non vocati à deo, merentur gladio occidi: quantumvis iusta causa sit prophana, sive sacra, non est enim discrimen. Non licet gladium usurpare praeter legitimam ordinationem. Sic prophetae mortui sunt, nec gladium usurpabant, quanquam vis afferretur.

## 18:11
The cup which the Father has given me, do you not wish that I drink it?

Faith brings it about that we resign vengeance to God: because the saints feel that they suffer by the good will of God, and that they are placed in this state in order that they may suffer. For it is not committed to them that they should avenge themselves; therefore, by the will of God, they obey Him, they yield the vengeance and the glory of fortitude, as in Psalmo 58. I will sing of your strength, etc. Thus faith brings it about that the cross becomes sweet, and we endure against all the force of the creature: if faith is lacking, it cannot be but that you succumb. Thus, therefore, Christ says: The cup which the Father has given me, do you not wish that I drink it? That is, because I know for certain that I have been called by God to these afflictions, I will sustain the will of God: and you, Peter, resist the will of God, when you arrogate to yourself the right of vengeance which is owed to another.

Latin:

Calicem quem dedit mihi pater, non-vis ut bibam illum?

Fides efficit, ut vindictam deo resignemus: quia enim sentiunt sancti se bona voluntate dei pati, & in hoc positas esse ut patiantur. Non est enim eis commissum ut vindicent se, ideo voluntate dei obtemperant illi, cedunt vindictam & fortitudinis gloriam, sicut Psalmo 58. Ego cantabo fortitudinem tuam &c. Ita fides efficit, ut crux dulcescat, duremusque adversus omnem vim creaturae: si defuit fides, fieri non potest quin succumbas. Sic ergo ait Christus: Calicem quem dedit mihi pater, non vis bibam? id est quia certo scio me à deo vocatum esse ad has afflictiones, sustinebo voluntatem dei: & tu Petre voluntati dei resistis, cum arrogas tibi ius vindictae quod alteri debetur.

## 18:20
I have spoken openly to the world, etc.

The world takes this upon itself, that it should judge concerning the word of God, when nevertheless the word of God judges reason, and in turn cannot be judged by reason. I have come into judgment, that is, that I myself might judge, not that I might be judged. Furthermore, because reason does not attain to the word, therefore it condemns. And here Annas condemns before he has found what he might accuse, before he has those who might testify concerning the doctrine. Thus the impious are accustomed to condemn things they have not known, nor to hear those who might testify concerning the doctrine. Just as Saul also killed Abimelech without a hearing, and Jehoiakim the prophet Uriah, Hieremiae 26. Thus Hieremiae 18. Come, let us strike with the tongue, and we do not pay attention, etc. Thus Satan precipitates the hearts of the impious with a spirit of dizziness. To this pertains what Christ says here: Because I spoke openly, therefore it was fitting for you to hear those testifying about the word, and afterwards to judge. But before you even know what you are condemning, or have heard those who can truly testify about the word, you have decreed to kill me. He therefore rebukes the fact that he condemns things unknown and without witnesses: that which must happen to all who despise the Gospel.

Latin:

Ego palàm locutus sum mundo &c.

Hoc sibi sumit mundus, ut de verbo dei iudicet, cum tamen verbum dei rationem iudicet, & vicissim à ratione iudicari non possit. Ego in iudicium veni, id est ut ipse iudicem, non ut iudicer. Porrò quia non assequitur verbum ratio, ideo damnat. Et hic Annas prius damnat quàm invenerit quid accuset, quàm habeat qui testificentur de doctrina. Sic solent impii ut damnent quae non cognorint, nec audire eos qui de doctrina testificentur. Sicut & Saul interemit Abimelech non auditum, & Ioachim prophetam Uriam, Hieremiae 26. Sic Hieremiae 18. Venite percutiamus cum lingua, & non attendamus &c. Sic praecipitat satan impiorum corda spiritu vertiginis. Huc pertinet quod hic Christus ait: Quia palàm locutus, ideo oportebat te audire testificantes de verbo, & postea iudicare. Verum priusquam vel scias quid damnes, vel audieris qui vere de verbo testificari possunt, occidere me decrevisti. Reprehendit itaque, quod damnet incomperta, & sine testibus: id quod necesse est accidere omnibus qui contemnunt Evangelium.

## 18:23
If I have spoken evil, bear witness.

In this place there are those who slander the history, because Christ treats the minister too harshly. I answer: Whoever suffers must never approve an unjust cause, but one must be instant in season and out of season, those who offer violence must be rebuked. Meanwhile, however, it is necessary to wait prepared for whatever the enemies are about to bring: just as here too Christ certainly turns the other cheek, and yet does not approve the unjust cause.

Latin:

Si male locutus sum, testimonium per-hibe.

Hoc loco sunt qui calumniantur historiam, quod Christus inclementius tractet ministrum. Respondeo: Nunquam probare debet iniustam causam quisquis patitur, sed instandum est opportune importune, increpandi sunt qui vim afferunt. Interim autem oportet paratum expectare quicquid illaturi sunt hostes: sicut & hic Christus certe obvertit alteram maxillam, & tamen iniustam causam non probat.

## 18:28
And they themselves did not enter into the praetorium.

True sanctification is that which is mentioned above in the next chapter, when, namely, the Father himself usurps hearts, so that he may renew and illuminate them with the knowledge of Christ. This sanctification the flesh does not acknowledge, and it conceives of a certain carnal sanctification, by which it abstains from certain foods and from the company of certain men: just as the Jews here abstain from the company of the Gentiles while they prepare themselves to eat the Passover, and those who trust in this sanctification cannot but persecute Christ. And the proposition here is what kind of people persecute Christ: namely, that which defends the righteousness of the flesh. If he were not a malefactor, we would not have delivered him to you. That is, because we are the high priests, therefore obey us. So that the word of Jesus might be fulfilled, which he spoke: namely, that the Gentiles were to crucify him.

Latin:

Et ipsi non introierunt in praetorium.

Sanctificatio vera est, cuius supra mentio fit proximo capite, cum scilicet pater corda ipse usurpat, ut renovet & illuminet cognitione Christi. Hanc sanctificationem non agnoscit caro, & sanctificationem quandam carnalem concipit, qua à cibis quibusdam, congressu quorundam hominum abstinet: quemadmodum hic Iudaei abstinent congressu gentium dum parant se ad edendum pascha, & qui hac sanctificatione fidunt, non possunt non persequi Christum. Estque hic propositum, cuiusmodi populus persequatur Christum: nempe is, qui iustitiam carnis tuetur. Si non esset hic malefactor, tibi non tradidissemus. id est quia pontisices sumus, ergo obtempera nobis. Ut sermo Iesu impleretur, quem dixit. scilicet quod gentes crucifixuri essent eum.

## 18:33
Are you the King of the Jews? Jesus answered: Do you say this of yourself &c.

Thus far the battle has been with the people of the law, here it is also conducted with the Gentiles. For this reason is pleaded against the Gospel, that such doctrine is of a kind that disturbs public tranquility and brings about contempt for civil laws: although the Gospel nevertheless commands charity, and therefore also forbids contempt for civil laws: therefore Christ here reproves Pilate for reciting the crimes, saying: Do you say this of yourself, or have others told you? That is, because you feel that I am aspiring to a corporal kingdom, I ask: do you yourself affirm it, or of this crime do you have witnesses, as if he were saying thus. Not otherwise than those who condemn without witnesses and with the cause unexamined, so do you also. You yourself cannot affirm that the public peace is disturbed by me, and yet you gratify the high priests by killing me. Am I a Jew? that is, do I have witnesses. What have you done? that is, have you forbidden tribute to be given to Caesar?

Latin:

Tu es rex Iudaeorum? Respondit Ie-sus: A' temetipso hoc &c.

Hactenus cum populo legis pugnatum est, hic et cum gentibus agitur. Nam haec causatur ratio adversus Evangelium, doctrinam eiusmodi esse, quae publicam tranquillitatem perturbet, & afferat legum civilium contemptum: cum tamen Evangelium charitatem praecipiat, adeoque & legum civilium contemptum vetat: ideo reprehendit hic Christus Pilatum recitantem crimina, dicens: An à temetipso hoc dicis, an alii tibi dixerunt? id est quia sentis me affectare corporale regnum, interrogo: ipsé ne affirmes, an huius criminis testes habeas, quasi sic dicat. Non aliter atque illi sine testibus & inexplorata causa damnant, sic tu quoque. Ipse affirmare non potes pacem publicam per me turbari, & tamen gratificaris pontificibus in me occidendo. Nunquid ego Iudaeus sum? id est habeo testes. Quid fecisti? id est prohibuisti censum dari caesari?

## 18:36
My kingdom is not of this world.

Concerning the kingdom of Christ enough has been said above: because my kingdom is the power of God, therefore I expect vengeance from God, and not from human force. Everyone who is of the truth. that is, who has been made truthful, and who has conceived the light, so that he may render to God what is God's etc. What is truth? as if he were saying: Is there any other truth than that which is conceived by men? Not this man but Barabbas, therefore Barabbas is preferred to Christ: because it was necessary for Christ to be diminished below the lowest of men, as Esaiae 53.

Latin:

Regnum meum non est de hoc mundo.

De regno Christi satis supra dictum est: quia meum regnum est potentia dei, ideo vindictam à deo expecto, & non ab humana vi. Omnis qui est ex veritate. id est qui est verax factus, & qui concepit lumen, ut deo tribuat quod est dei &c. Quid est veritas? quasi dicat: Num alia veritas est quàm quae ab hominibus concipitur? Non hunc sed Barrabam, ideo praefertur Barrabas Christo: quia extenuari oportebat Christum infra novissimum virorum, ut Esaiae 53.

## 19:1 Argument
CHAPTER NINETEEN.

Latin:

CAPUT DECIMUM-NONUM.

## 19:9
He said to Jesus, where are you from? But Jesus etc.

What is the reason why Christ does not answer here, when a confession is clearly necessary? I answer. Since Pilate asked about those crimes by which they wished it to be thought that the Roman majesty was harmed and Roman laws violated, Christ answers, accurately refuting the crime. For when they accused him of being the author of sedition and of aspiring to a kingdom, he answered: My kingdom is not of this world, etc. In which passage he teaches that the Gospel is not opposed to civil power. Furthermore, whether he is the son of God or not, that is nothing to the Roman laws; for that reason Christ did not answer Pilate.

Latin:

Dixit ad Iesum, unde es? Iesus autem &c.

Quid est, cur hic non respondeat Christus, cum confessio planè necessaria sit? Respondeo. Cum interrogaret Pilatus de iis criminibus, quibus Romanam maiestatem volebant laesam esse, & violatas Romanas leges: respondet Christus, accurate diluens crimen. Nam cum criminarentur eum esse seditionis autorem, affectare regnum, respondit: Regnum meum non est de hoc mundo &c. Quo loco docet Evangelium non adversari potestati civili. Porrò filium dei esse aut non esse, nihil ad Romanas leges, propterea non respondit Christus Pilato.

## 19:11
Jesus answered: You would have no power against me, etc.

Christ does not endure blasphemy when Pilate says that he has power, etc. But here he also confirms the consciences of the saints, so that they may feel that the kingdom of the world can do nothing against them, except that which the father admits out of his benevolence, so that we may experience his presence and our faith may be confirmed. Just as Zechariah 2 I will be to it, says the Lord, a wall of fire.

Latin:

Respondit Iesus: Non haberes pote-statem adversum me &c.

Non fert blasphemiam Christus, cum Pilatus ait se potestatem habere &c.
